## Confirmed (232)

### B17-1 — Split-duplicate merge runs on every setup and force-deletes tasks that share a consumable link

- **Severity:** critical (finder: critical)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/devices.py:346`  **Also reported as:** B01-1
- **Defect:** async_heal_split_device_ids calls store.async_merge_split_duplicates unconditionally on every setup/reload, and the merge groups tasks by ANY identical source namespace payload, including Home Keeper's own manual consumable link, so ordinary tasks that share a part are deleted and the survivor is moved to another device.
- **Failure scenario:** Battery Notes glue (documented flow) links each 'Replace battery' task to the AAA part with quantity 2, so every such task carries the identical source.part = {asset_id, part_id, manual: true, quantity: 2}. No HA split ever happened (canonical == {}). On the next restart or any reload (every asset edit reloads), the merge puts all of them in one group, keeps the one with most completions (ties: oldest), force-deletes every other one with zero completions (managed protection bypassed via force=True), and rewrites the survivor's device_id AND its glue namespace device_id to the newest group member's device. The same happens to two user tasks linked to one shared filter part. I ran the real function source against a stub store: with canonical={} two tasks were deleted and the survivor moved from dev_kitchen to dev_garage, including source.home_keeper_battery_notes.device_id. The glue then recreates the deleted tasks, which are deleted again next setup.
- **Suggested fix:** Skip the merge when canonical is empty, and only merge a group when at least one member's device_id (task or payload) is a key in canonical, i.e. the two copies really point at different halves of one split. Exclude Home Keeper's reserved namespaces (part, buy, problem_sensor, declarative_companion) from grouping. Never rewrite a survivor's device_id unless its current id was canonicalized.
- **Verifier fix note:** Direction is right, but 'skip when canonical is empty' is not enough, because a single real split anywhere turns it back on for every part group. Group only payloads whose device_id canonicalizes to a composite, merge only when the members carry at least 2 different raw ids from that composite, and skip the reserved namespaces (part, buy, problem_sensor, declarative). A grouped derived (non-manual) part task would also make delete_task raise TaskValidationError and abort the loop part-way. Add unit tests that run the real merge with an empty map and with 2 manual links to one part.
- **Verifier reason:** Same defect as B01-1, verified independently. The merge runs with no split: __init__.py:821 calls async_heal_split_device_ids on every async_setup_entry (restart, options save, every asset edit via devices.py:146), and devices.py:338-346 always calls store.async_merge_split_duplicates(canonical), with canonical == {} when no device has a composite_device_id. store.py:1306-1310 still groups every task by every source namespace, including Home Keeper's own manual 'part' link, whose payload store.py:574-584 builds as {asset_id, part_id, manual: True[, quantity]} with nothing per task. So 2 real tasks share a key whenever they link the same part with the same quantity, and real paths produce that: the panel Duplicate copies the link (forms.ts:1011 consumable_link), and the Battery Notes glue links every 'Replace battery' task to one part per battery type with the device's quantity (GLUE_INTEGRATIONS.md:59-60, INTEGRATING.md:852-855). Every non-survivor with no completions is force-deleted (store.py:1337-1339; delete_task lets a manual link go at store.py:828 and force skips managed protection), and the survivor's device_id and every payload device_id, the glue's own included, are rewritten to the newest member's device (store.py:1325-1331). A fresh low-battery task has no completions, so it is exactly what gets deleted. No unit test runs the real merge (test_device_heal.py:215 fakes it).
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B17/repro_b17_1.py (real devices.async_heal_split_device_ids + real store.py, registry with no composite ids, 3 tasks linked to one part with set_task_consumable): before [(Hallway, dev_hall), (Kitchen, dev_kitchen), (Bedroom, dev_bedroom)] -> after [(Hallway, dev_bedroom)]; home_keeper_task_deleted fired for Kitchen and Bedroom.

### B03-1 — delete_archived_completion is open to non-admins and echoes the full, unprojected appliance (costs, serials, warranty, part costs/vendors)

- **Severity:** high (finder: critical)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/websocket_api.py:709`  **Also reported as:** X01-2
- **Defect:** home_keeper/delete_archived_completion has no require_admin and replies with the raw asset dict, so any signed-in user can read every field card_projection withholds, and can also delete entries from an appliance's archived maintenance history.
- **Failure scenario:** A guest account (wall tablet, housemate) calls home_keeper/get_assets and gets every appliance id from card_projection. It then calls home_keeper/delete_archived_completion {asset_id: <id>, task_id: 'x', ts: 'x'}. assets.remove_archived_completion matches nothing and returns False, store.delete_archived_completion returns the stored asset unchanged, and the handler sends {asset: <full record>}: cost, serial_number, free-text and warranty metadata, each part's cost/vendor/part_number, and task_history. That is exactly what SECURITY.md says a guest must not read, and it bypasses the projection that test_admin_gates pins for get_assets and list_assets. With a real task_id/ts from the same leaked task_history, the guest can also delete appliance history. The service twin (no _verify_admin) allows the same deletion.
- **Suggested fix:** Add @websocket_api.require_admin to ws_delete_archived_completion (the only caller is the admin panel), await _verify_admin(call) in handle_delete_archived_completion, set admin_only=True in api_surface.py, and add a non-admin integration test that asserts the command is refused and never returns serial_number/cost.
- **Verifier fix note:** Suggested fix is right (require_admin on the ws command, _verify_admin in handle_delete_archived_completion, admin_only=True in api_surface, non-admin integration test). Note the same leak exists on a second route that the fix leaves open: the upload POSTs in manuals.py (_begin_upload manuals.py:532-560 checks only _uploader_is_a_real_user, no is_admin) let any signed-in user add a document (HomeKeeperDocumentView.post, manuals.py:586-666) or replace a part file (HomeKeeperPartFileView.post, manuals.py:699-767), and both reply with coord.store.get_asset(asset_id), the full unprojected asset. SECURITY.md:36 lists document and part-file changes as admin-only. Gate both POST handlers on request[KEY_HASS_USER].is_admin as part of the same fix.
- **Verifier reason:** websocket_api.py:689-709 has only websocket_command + async_response + _with_coordinator; no require_admin, and HA's require_admin decorator (core websocket_api/decorators.py:54-69) is the only admin gate a command gets, so any authenticated user can call it. The handler sends {asset: <stored dict>}; store.py:2396-2409 returns self._assets[asset_id] whether or not anything was removed, and assets.py:1855-1877 returns False without raising for an unknown task_id/ts. A non-admin gets every asset id from card_projection (assets.py:1783-1797, via get_assets/list_assets), so {asset_id: <id>, task_id: 'x', ts: 'x'} returns the full record: cost, serial_number, notes, all metadata, parts[].cost/vendor/part_number, task_history. This breaks the stated rule in .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md:43-45 ('A mutating command that echoes the full asset back is admin-gated instead') and SECURITY.md:19-24/69-71; tests/integration/test_admin_gates.py:614-626 states the same intent but only checks update_asset. With real task_id/ts from the leaked task_history, the guest can also delete archived history, and the service twin (__init__.py:1277-1285, no _verify_admin; api_surface.py:755-757 no admin_only) allows the delete too. The only frontend caller is the admin panel (panel-history.ts:97-105), so gating it breaks nothing. Downgraded from critical: needs an authenticated household account and exposes household appliance data, not credentials.
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (medium) — websocket_api.py:689-709 has websocket_command + async_response + _with_coordinator only; no require_admin, and _with_coordinator does no admin check. store.delete_archived_completion (store.py:2396-2409) returns the stored asset whether or not a row was removed, and the handler sends {asset: asset}. card_projection gives a non-admin every asset id (assets.py:1785), so {asset_id, task_id:'x', ts:'x'} returns the full record: cost, serial_number, warranty, free-text metadata, part cost/vendor/part_number, task_history. That breaks .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md:43-45 ('A mutating command that echoes the full asset back is admin-gated instead') and docs/SECURITY.md:19-23 and :69-70. api_surface.py:755-757 has no admin_only, and the service twin (__init__.py:1277-1285) has no _verify_admin, so any user can also delete archived history rows (ids come from the leaked task_history). Present in 0.27.0. Rated medium, not high: it needs a signed-in household account that crafts a websocket call on purpose, it exposes appliance costs and serials only, and SECURITY.md:19 itself calls these risks small. The deletion hits archived history of retired tasks only.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=pydeps python3 tmp-v-B03/archived.py -> 'remove_archived_completion(bogus) -> False'; stored asset keys include cost, serial_number, notes, metadata, parts; card_projection keys ['documents','id','metadata','parts'] (ids exposed). HA not installed, websocket leg traced from source.

### B04-1 — Import and dry-run crash with AttributeError on every Home Assistant before 2026.9

- **Severity:** high (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer_runner.py:71`  **Also reported as:** B17-3
- **Defect:** The runner iterates registry.devices directly and reads .id, but before HA 2026.9 that container is a UserDict keyed by device id, so iteration yields strings.
- **Failure scenario:** User on HA 2026.8 (or older) with Home Keeper 0.27.0 opens Settings > Import and export, pastes any document and presses Preview (or calls home_keeper.import_data). frozenset(device.id for device in registry.devices) raises AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'id' before the plan is even built, so both the dry run and the real import fail with unknown_error. CI does not see it because integration/e2e run HA_TAG=stable (2026.9) and mypy type-checks the 2026.9 Collection type.
- **Suggested fix:** Use `frozenset(d.id for d in device_compat.all_devices(registry))` (or resolve_device per stated id). Add a unit test for the runner helper with the UserDict-shaped fake that test_device_compat already has.
- **Verifier fix note:** device_compat.all_devices(registry) fixes the crash, but on 2026.9 both it and `registry.devices` list main devices only (child devices live in registry.child_devices, dr-2026.9.0.py:4294-4296), so a task stated on a child device is still treated as unknown and its device_id dropped. Checking each stated id with device_compat.resolve_device (registry.async_get answers both kinds) covers both problems. Add a runner unit test with the UserDict-shaped fake.
- **Verifier reason:** transfer_runner.py:71 `frozenset(device.id for device in registry.devices)` is the only direct iteration of registry.devices in the component (grep); every other read goes through device_compat.all_devices (device_compat.py:66-77), whose docstring says the container is a mapping keyed by id before 2026.9. Checked HA source: 2026.7.0 and 2026.8.0 declare `devices: ActiveDeviceRegistryItems` -> DeviceRegistryItems -> BaseRegistryItems(UserDict[str, ...]) (helpers/registry.py:19), so iteration yields id strings and `.id` raises AttributeError; only 2026.9.0 wraps it in _DeprecatedDeviceRegistryItemsView whose __iter__ yields entries. No minimum HA version is declared (hacs.json has no `homeassistant` key; manifest.json has none), and tests/upgrade/conftest.py:380-389 (hk_first_run) boots the WORKING-TREE Home Keeper on HA 2026.7, so pre-2026.9 is an explicitly supported target. The line dates from #309 (0db9983, 2026-09-09) and import has shipped in stable since 0.23.0, so every import and dry run on HA <= 2026.8 fails (websocket unknown_error; the service's _store_errors does not catch AttributeError). Only integration/e2e (HA stable = 2026.9) run this code. Loud failure, nothing written.
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (medium) — transfer_runner.py:71 `frozenset(device.id for device in registry.devices)` is the only direct iteration of registry.devices in the component; everything else goes through device_compat.all_devices, whose docstring (device_compat.py:69-72) says the container is a mapping keyed by id before HA 2026.9, so iteration yields id strings and .id raises AttributeError. It is computed unconditionally before plan_import, so every import and every dry run fails on HA <= 2026.8. Older HA is a supported target: hacs.json and manifest.json set no minimum, and tests/upgrade/conftest.py:61 and 383-389 boot the working-tree Home Keeper on HA 2026.7. The line came with #309 (0db9983) and is in stable 0.27.0 (git show 5496759:transfer_runner.py:71). Rated medium, not high: the failure is loud, nothing is written, no data is at risk, and upgrading HA clears it; a fresh-install restore normally runs on current HA.
- **Repro:** curl raw.githubusercontent.com/home-assistant/core/<tag>/homeassistant/helpers/device_registry.py: 2026.8.0:1442 `devices: ActiveDeviceRegistryItems`, registry.py:19 `class BaseRegistryItems[_DataT](UserDict[str, _DataT], ABC)`; 2026.9.0:1788 `devices: Collection[DeviceEntry]`, :1550 `def __iter__ ... return iter(self._devices.values())`

### B04-2 — Restore of nested appliances onto a new or empty install is refused: exported parent_asset_id is a uuid that planned records do not answer to

- **Severity:** high (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:1022`
- **Defect:** asset_refs registers a planned appliance only under its external_id and name, but the exporter writes parent_asset_id as the raw parent uuid (and a task's appliance falls back to the device_id), so the child cannot find a parent that is in the same document.
- **Failure scenario:** Store has HVAC (id P) with a subdevice Furnace (parent_asset_id P). Export, then import the file on a fresh install: _parent_id('P', asset_refs, {}) finds nothing (asset_refs has only 'HVAC'), so the plan gets the error 'no appliance called "P" is in this document or in Home Keeper ... List the parent before its children' and the whole import writes nothing, although the parent is listed first. Verified: plan.ok False. Same root cause: an existing-kind appliance with no name exports its tasks as `appliance: <device_id>`, which also cannot resolve on another install (verified: 'no appliance called "dev-old"', import refused).
- **Suggested fix:** Also register each planned appliance under its stated id and its final record_id (and its stated device_id) in asset_refs; for tasks, map a stated appliance/device reference to the planned record. Add a round-trip test with a parent/child tree and an unnamed existing-kind appliance onto an empty store.
- **Verifier fix note:** Register each planned appliance in asset_refs under its record_id (and stated id) too. For the task side, export the appliance's asset id (not the device id) as the last fallback, and let _appliance_device resolve a planned or stored asset id.
- **Verifier reason:** _asset_out (transfer.py:419) keeps parent_asset_id as the stored parent uuid; the parent keeps that uuid on a fresh install via _claim_id (1255-1257), but asset_refs registers only external_id and name (1022-1024), and _parent_id (1561-1566) then checks the empty store, so the child errors with 'no appliance called "<uuid>" ... List the parent before its children' although the parent is listed first; the error makes the whole plan fail, so nothing is imported. The user guide (docs/guide/automation/import-export.md table) promises parent_asset_id and appliance resolve 'by external_id, name, or id'. Same root: _task_out (375) falls back to the device_id for an unnamed existing-kind appliance (the panel makes name optional for that kind, forms.ts:1638), and that reference cannot resolve on another install, which refuses the whole import. Same-install re-import works (parent is in the store). No test restores a tree onto an empty store. Loud failure with a misleading message; breaks the documented move-to-another-HA restore.
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (high) — _asset_out (transfer.py:412-419) keeps parent_asset_id as the stored parent uuid, but asset_refs registers a planned appliance only under external_id and name (transfer.py:1022-1024), and _parent_id (transfer.py:1561-1566) then looks only in the store. On an empty install the child gets an error although its parent is listed first, and one error refuses the whole import. Second path, same root: _task_out (transfer.py:375) falls back to the device_id for an unnamed existing-kind appliance (name is optional for that kind), and _appliance_device (transfer.py:1635-1643) never matches a device id, so any task on such an appliance also refuses a cross-install import. Same-install re-import works. This breaks the documented move-to-another-install restore for 2 common data shapes. Loud, nothing written, but the error text ('List the parent before its children') misleads, and hand-editing the file is the only workaround.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r2_parent.py -> plan.ok: False; error appliances[1].parent_asset_id - no appliance called "890d9a75-..." (the HVAC id, planned in the same document); same-install plan.ok: True; unnamed existing: task appliance -> dev-old, plan.ok False 'no appliance called "dev-old"'

### B05-1 — An appliance update that does not send `parts` detaches every part's uploaded file (panel inline Notes edit, rename, partial import)

- **Severity:** high (finder: critical)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:1607`
- **Defect:** merge_update re-normalizes the stored parts when `parts` is absent from the update, and _normalize_part always writes file_name/file_content_type/file_size = None, but only the `"parts" in updates` branch calls _merge_parts to restore them.
- **Failure scenario:** A part has an uploaded receipt (set_part_file). The admin opens the appliance detail page and saves the inline Notes editor. The panel sends update_asset {notes} only. merge_update builds candidate['parts'] from existing parts, normalize_fields -> _normalize_part sets the 3 file fields to None, the `if "parts" in updates` branch is skipped, and merged.update(fields) writes the fileless parts. Every part on that appliance loses its attachment. The download view then 404s (manuals.py:322 needs file_name), and a later re-upload cannot delete the old blob because old_filename is read from file_name (manuals.py:730), so the blob is orphaned. The same happens for the update_asset service with only name/notes/area_id, and for an import record of a matched appliance that omits `parts` (transfer.py:1293 passes the record through, transfer.py:1338 calls merge_update). The same path hits managed appliances with `parts` locked. Reproduced: after merge_update(a, {'notes': 'x'}) the part's file_name is None; with {'parts': a['parts']} it is kept.
- **Suggested fix:** In merge_update, when `parts` is not in updates, keep existing['parts'] as stored (do not re-normalize), or always run _merge_parts(existing parts, fields['parts']). Add a unit test: set_part_file, then merge_update({'notes': ...}) and merge_update({'name': ...}) keep file_name/file_content_type/file_size.
- **Verifier fix note:** Prefer 'when parts is not in updates, keep existing["parts"] as stored' (do not re-normalize them). That also stops the B05-2 and B05-3 lockouts, because an unrelated edit no longer re-validates stored parts. 'Always run _merge_parts' keeps the files but leaves those lockouts in place. Also correct the panel.ts:989 comment, and add a unit test for {notes}, {name} and a managed appliance.
- **Verifier reason:** merge_update builds candidate['parts'] from the stored parts when the update has no 'parts' (assets.py:1595), normalize_fields re-normalizes them (assets.py:1410), and _normalize_part always writes file_name/file_content_type/file_size = None (assets.py:716-718). Only the 'parts' in updates branch runs _merge_parts, which is the one place those fields are restored (assets.py:1607-1608, 805-812). merged.update(fields) then overwrites the stored parts (assets.py:1634). No caller fills 'parts' in first: store.update_asset passes the update through unchanged (store.py:944-956), and so do ws_update_asset (websocket_api.py:777), the update_asset service (__init__.py:1456) and the import planner (transfer.py:1293, 1338). The appliance detail page wires the inline Notes editor (panel-detail.ts:1213), and it sends only {notes} (panel.ts:998). Its comment says merge_update carries every unmentioned field through, which is false for these 3 part fields. The full editor always sends parts (panel.ts:1342), so it is safe. Downstream: _part_with_file needs file_name (manuals.py:322), so the download fails, and a re-upload reads old_filename from file_name (manuals.py:729-730), so the old blob is not removed. Nothing re-links a part file from disk (the only writers of file_size are set_part_file and _merge_parts). The existing tests only send 'parts' (tests/unit/test_assets.py:739-805, tests/integration/test_part_files.py:158-183). Severity is high, not critical: the trigger is a routine edit and the loss is silent, but the blob stays on disk until the appliance is deleted (manuals.py:12 rmtree per asset), and nothing is exposed.
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (high) — assets.py:1595 fills candidate['parts'] from the stored parts when the update omits 'parts'; normalize_fields re-normalizes them and _normalize_part always writes file_name/file_content_type/file_size = None (assets.py:716-718). Only the 'parts' in updates branch (assets.py:1607-1608) calls _merge_parts, the one place that restores those fields (assets.py:805-812). merged.update(fields) then stores the fileless parts (assets.py:1634). store.update_asset passes the update straight to merge_update (store.py:954). The appliance detail page wires the inline Notes editor (panel-detail.ts:1213), which sends only {notes} (panel.ts:998); its comment (panel.ts:989) wrongly says merge_update carries every unmentioned field through. The update_asset service with name/area/notes and an import that omits parts hit the same path. Result: the part's file silently detaches, the download 404s (manuals.py:322 needs file_name), and the blob is orphaned on disk. Present in 0.27.0. High: routine edit, silent loss, recovery only by re-upload or file-system access.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro1.py -> after set_part_file: file_name 'receipt.pdf'; merge_update(['notes']) -> file_name None; merge_update(['name']) -> None; merge_update(['area_id']) -> None; merge_update(['parts']) -> 'receipt.pdf'; managed appliance (parts locked) merge_update(notes) -> None

### B06-1 — Upload views are not admin-gated and echo the full private asset to any signed-in user

- **Severity:** high (finder: critical)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/manuals.py:549`  **Also reported as:** X01-1, X03-1
- **Defect:** Both POST handlers only require a non-system user, so a non-admin can add documents, replace or destroy part files, and read back the unprojected asset (costs, serials, warranty dates, part vendors/costs).
- **Failure scenario:** A guest/non-admin account calls home_keeper/get_assets (card projection gives every asset id, part id, document id and filename), then POSTs a 1x1 PNG with its own bearer token to /api/home_keeper/part_document/<asset>/<part>. _begin_upload admits it (user is real, not system), set_part_file replaces the part's file, the old admin-uploaded blob is deleted (line 764-765), and the 200 response carries coord.store.get_asset(asset_id) unfiltered. The same works on /api/home_keeper/document/<asset>/<uuid> (adds a document). SECURITY.md lists 'Appliance documents and part files' as admin-only and says the narrowed view withholds costs and serial numbers.
- **Suggested fix:** In _begin_upload require `user.is_admin` (return 403 / raise Unauthorized) or decorate both post methods with homeassistant.components.http.decorators.require_admin; add a non-admin POST case to tests/integration/test_admin_gates.py for both routes, asserting 401/403 and no response body with asset data.
- **Verifier fix note:** Use homeassistant.components.http.require_admin (http/decorators.py; it raises Unauthorized, which request_handler_factory maps to 401) on both post methods. Keep _uploader_is_a_real_user as well. Leave GET open, because the card and the open sign_*_url commands depend on it. Add an admin flag to HttpViewSpec so test_api_surface pins the gate, add non-admin POST cases to test_admin_gates.py for both routes, and state in SECURITY.md that the upload endpoints are admin-only.
- **Verifier reason:** Exactly 2 HTTP views exist (manuals.py:563 HomeKeeperDocumentView 'api:home_keeper:document', manuals.py:671 HomeKeeperPartFileView 'api:home_keeper:part_document'; registered at manuals.py:777-778; api_surface.py:833-844 lists the same 2, GET+POST, and HttpViewSpec has no admin flag). The only other HTTP surface is the panel static path (panel.py:59-60). Both views set requires_auth = True (manuals.py:572, 682). HA core's request_handler_factory (helpers/http.py) checks only KEY_AUTHENTICATED; it never checks is_admin, and HA's auth middleware accepts a signature only for GET/HEAD (components/http/auth.py). Neither view uses @require_admin, and no code in manuals.py reads user.is_admin. The only identity gate on POST is _begin_upload -> _uploader_is_a_real_user (manuals.py:294-310, 549): user is not None and not system_generated. store.add_asset_document (store.py:1073) and store.set_part_file (store.py:1132) have no user check. What a non-admin CAN do: (READ, by design) GET any document or part file with a bearer token or a signed URL, because sign_document_url / sign_part_file_url are open to all users (websocket_api.py:1011-1090, SECURITY.md 'A signed URL is not admin-only'). (WRITE, not by design) get asset, document and part ids plus document filenames from home_keeper/get_assets card_projection (websocket_api.py:728-730, assets.py:1766-1797), then POST a 1x1 PNG to add a file document to any appliance (no admin check, bypassing the admin-gated add_asset_document service), or POST to a part to replace its file, which deletes the old admin-uploaded blob (manuals.py:764-765). (READ, not by design) every successful POST returns coord.store.get_asset(asset_id), the live unprojected record (store.py:927-928) with costs, serial numbers, warranty dates, part vendors/costs/part numbers and free-text metadata (manuals.py:664-666, 766-768). SECURITY.md lists 'Appliance documents and part files' as admin-only and says the narrowed view withholds costs and serial numbers; .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md:28-29 says documents and part files are 'Currently gated' and :43-45 that 'a mutating command that echoes the full asset back is admin-gated'. tests/integration/test_admin_gates.py never touches the HTTP views. Severity lowered from critical: it needs a signed-in HA account and exposes Home Keeper appliance data only, but it fully bypasses the documented privilege boundary.
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (high) — manuals.py has no is_admin or require_admin anywhere (grep). Both POST handlers admit a request through _begin_upload (manuals.py:549), whose only identity check is _uploader_is_a_real_user (manuals.py:309-310: user is not None and not system_generated). requires_auth=True (manuals.py:572, 682) admits any signed-in user. card_projection gives a non-admin asset ids and part ids (assets.py:1763, 1785, 1792-1795). So a non-admin can add a document to any appliance, or replace a part's file, which deletes the admin-uploaded blob (manuals.py:764-765). Each successful POST returns coord.store.get_asset(asset_id), the unprojected record (manuals.py:664-666, 766-768). SECURITY.md:36 lists appliance documents and part files as admin-only. Uploads are magic-byte sniffed to PDF/PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF (documents.py:21-41), so there is no stored-XSS escalation to admin; that keeps it below critical. High, not medium: unlike B03-1 it also writes and irreversibly destroys admin data.
- **Repro:** Traced, not run (no HA install): HA core dev helpers/http.py request_handler_factory: `if view.requires_auth and not authenticated: raise HTTPUnauthorized` (no admin check); components/http/auth.py auth_middleware: `request.method in ["GET", "HEAD"] and SIGN_QUERY_PARAM in request.query_string`; grep -n is_admin custom_components/home_keeper/manuals.py -> no match.

### B14-2 — Overlapping evaluation passes iterate stale task snapshots and re-complete clear_on_recover tasks another pass already cleared

- **Severity:** high (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_watcher.py:497`
- **Defect:** Every state change spawns an unserialized async_evaluate pass that iterates a dict snapshot across awaits; store.complete_task replaces the task dict, so an older pass still sees next_due set and completes the task again.
- **Failure scenario:** N availability tasks (clear_on_recover defaults to True) are armed during a Zigbee2MQTT/ZHA outage. The bridge comes back and all N entities turn available within a few ms. Pass P1 (device 1) clears t0 and suspends in the store save (Store.async_save awaits an executor write); P2..PN each clear their own task and suspend. P1 resumes with its snapshot, in which t1..tN are the OLD dicts with next_due set; each device now reads available -> _evaluate_edge(met=False) sees armed=True -> ACTION_CLEAR -> store.complete_task again. Result: N(N+1)/2 completions instead of N, duplicate home_keeper_task_completed events and notifications, and a part-linked task draws its consumable stock down once per duplicate (store.py:2023-2024). Reproduced by driving the real SensorTaskWatcher against a store stub with store.py's replace-on-complete semantics: 6 devices -> 21 completions ({t0:1, t1:2, ..., t5:6}); with events 50 ms apart -> 6.
- **Suggested fix:** Serialize passes (an asyncio.Lock around async_evaluate, or coalesce state changes into one pending pass with a Debouncer), and re-read the live task (store.get_task(tid)) immediately before applying ARM/CLEAR/REBASELINE, skipping the action when the live task no longer matches the decision (e.g. CLEAR on a task whose live next_due is None).
- **Verifier fix note:** Serialize passes with an asyncio.Lock plus a pending flag, as TodoSyncDriver.async_sync does, so a burst folds into one more pass. Re-read store.get_task(tid) before ARM/CLEAR and skip when the live task no longer matches. As a second guard, make complete_task with ORIGIN_SENSOR_RECOVER a no-op on a task that is already dormant.
- **Verifier reason:** Passes can overlap: each state change (sensor_watcher.py:494-497) and each hold timer (:478) starts async_evaluate with no lock and no coalescing. A pass holds the dicts from _sensor_tasks() (:325-331; store.get_tasks returns the live map, store.py:348-350) across awaits inside its loop (:513-567). CLEAR is level-triggered on that dict's next_due (sensor_tasks.py:543-548). store.complete_task replaces the dict (store.py:2008-2017) and awaits _save, and HA's Store.async_save really suspends (write lock plus executor job; upstream storage.py:464-477, 569-600). hass.async_create_task starts eagerly by default (upstream core.py:788-835), so each pass takes its snapshot at event time. An older pass then resumes holding the old armed dicts and completes each later task again with ORIGIN_SENSOR_RECOVER, which passes every gate. The trigger is realistic: a Zigbee2MQTT or bridge recovery, where every availability entity flips within one save window. Availability mode defaults clear_on_recover to True (models.py:403-409) and the panel's hold to 0 (forms.ts:866), and shipped presets with clear_on_recover (declarative_presets.py:94, 130, 182, 499-510) are exposed too. The result is quadratic duplicate completions in persisted history, duplicate completed events and automations, and extra stock draws for linked tasks (sensor-tasks.md:141-144).
- **Repro:** python3.13 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B14/race_v.py (real watcher, HA-style eager task start, locked executor save of 3 ms) -> 'N=6 gap=None: (21, {t0:1, t1:2, t2:3, t3:4, t4:5, t5:6})'; gap=0.001 -> 21; gap=0.02 -> 6. Finder's scripts re-run: race.py -> 21 completions; race_spaced2.py (50 ms apart) -> 6.

### F03-1 — Option cards re-send a stale full-options snapshot on every edit, reverting saved profiles, notifications and other cards

- **Severity:** high (finder: critical)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-settings.ts:372`
- **Defect:** Every Settings option card (General, Shopping, Problem sync, Skip/snooze) seeds its ha-form with the whole options object, and ha-form echoes its whole data object on each change, so the save sends render-time copies of profiles, notifications and every other card's fields, not only the edited field.
- **Failure scenario:** Open Settings. Add a notification, which renders the page. Then configure it: pick targets and turn on 'Auto-send when overdue'. Each change autosaves with render:false, so the option cards are not rebuilt. Then turn off Snooze on the Skip/snooze card, or change retention days, or pick a shopping list. That form emits {...renderTimeOptions, allow_snooze:false}, which includes notifications:[the blank new row]. saveOptions sends it, _normalize overwrites the notifications key with it, and the backend stores the blank row. The notification row still shows the configured values and the card says 'Saved'. After the next load the auto triggers are off and the notification never fires. The same path reverts profile renames, filters and to-do sync, and a notification saved by Test. It also reverts the other cards: turn problem sync on, then change retention days on General, and sync_problem_sensors:false is sent back. The reconcile then removes every synced problem task, while the Problem card still shows ON. The optimistic merge also puts the stale lists back into p._options, so the next debounced row save builds from them and reverts the other rows too. On a phone each section is a patch (_patchSettingsSection), not a render, so the snapshot lives for the whole Settings visit, and no event subscription re-seeds it.
- **Suggested fix:** In settingsCard, seed with pickFormData(opts, fields) and save pickFormData(raw, fields) (after coerce), so each card sends only its own schema keys. In shoppingCard, narrow form.data the same way. Add a test: edit a notification channel, then change a settings card, then assert the stored channel and the stored profiles are unchanged.
- **Verifier fix note:** Suggested fix is right. Narrow both the seed and the emitted value per form: p._makeForm(fields, pickFormData(opts, fields), raw => save(pickFormData(coerce ? coerce(raw) : raw, fields))). Pick after coerce (or rely on 'in', which keeps an undefined key) so a cleared shopping picker still sends ''. Also narrow shoppingCard's form.data at :515 and :544 (both spread the full object). Add tests for the intra-card case (sync on, then change an exclusion, assert stored sync stays true) and the cross-card case (edit a notification, then change retention, assert notification unchanged).
- **Verifier reason:** Full path traced. renderSettingsForm seeds opts = p._options (panel-settings.ts:277), which get_options fills with every key incl. profiles, notifications, dismissed_companions (options.py:86-97,144). settingsCard gives each ha-form { ...opts } (panel-settings.ts:372; the Problem card builds TWO forms, toggle and exclusions, each with the full copy, :395/:401). _makeForm forwards detail.value unfiltered (panel.ts:2148,2167-2169). ha-form emits its whole data object: the repo says so itself in forms.ts:771-775 (pickFormData doc: 'a section seeded with the whole form would re-assert a stale snapshot of every other section each time it changed'), and the notification editor narrows for that reason (panel-settings.ts:1478-1488). saveOptions merges and sends the whole value (:696,:699). Backend _normalize replaces every present key (options.py:176-203), so it is a full overwrite of profiles/notifications/bools/lists, not a merge of rows. Nothing re-seeds the forms: row saves are render:false (:1098), saveOptions -> _reload refreshes p._options but never renders (panel.ts:763-800, :705), the hass setter does not render (panel.ts:292-300), section moves are patched (panel.ts:345,379), no event subscription in the panel. profile_removals_in_use does not block it (ids unchanged). Simplest real path, inside one card: turn problem sync on, then add an exclusion -> exclusions form sends sync_problem_sensors:false; problem_sync._eligible returns {} when off (problem_sync.py:106-110) so the reload removes all synced tasks while the toggle still shows ON. Also: configure a notification, then touch any option card -> targets/auto reverted. Tests model ha-form the same way (settings-autosave.test.js:158-165,350-353) but assert only status text. Downgraded from critical: loss is of settings (re-enterable) and regenerable mirror tasks, admin-only surface, no security impact; but it is silent and easy to hit.
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (high) — renderSettingsForm passes the full p._options (incl. profiles, notifications) as opts (panel-settings.ts:277). settingsCard seeds each ha-form with { ...opts } (panel-settings.ts:372); the Problem card builds 2 forms (toggle and exclusions), each with its own copy. Only the Shopping card has a coerce, and it spreads ...raw (panel-settings.ts:534-540). _makeForm forwards detail.value unfiltered (panel.ts:2167-2169), and ha-form emits its whole data object, which the repo itself documents at forms.ts:770-775. saveOptions sends the whole value (panel-settings.ts:696-699), and options._normalize replaces every key present (options.py:176-203), including profiles and notifications. Nothing re-seeds the forms: row saves use render=false (persistOptionList, panel-settings.ts:1051), saveOptions -> _reload refreshes p._options only, the hass setter does not render (panel.ts:292-300), and the panel has no event subscription. Simplest path, inside one card: turn problem sync on, then add an exclusion; the exclusions form sends sync_problem_sensors:false, the reconcile removes synced tasks, and the toggle still shows ON. Also: configure a notification, then touch any option card, and its targets and auto triggers revert. profile_removals_in_use does not block (ids unchanged). High: silent and on the natural edit flow; not critical, because it is admin-only settings and regenerable mirror tasks.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-F03/repro.py (real options._normalize, babel stubbed; snapshot = render-time options, stored = sync on + configured notification, emitted = {...snapshot, one_off_retention_days: 30}) -> 'sync_problem_sensors: True -> False' / 'targets: [mobile_app_x] -> [] | auto: {overdue: True} -> {overdue: False}' / 'blocked by profile check: []'

### X04-1 — To-do list sync writes the due date in the stored offset, not HA local time (#250 again)

- **Severity:** high (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list.py:292`  **Also reported as:** B10-7
- **Defect:** desired_by_sync takes .date() of next_due in whatever offset it was stored with, so every UTC-stored due date (panel one-off due, panel snooze) syncs to the external to-do list one day off from the native to-do list and the panel.
- **Failure scenario:** HA zone America/Los_Angeles. At 19:00 on 2026-10-05 a user creates a one-off in the panel with the default due (now). The panel sends toISOString, so next_due is stored as 2026-10-06T02:00:00+00:00. todo.home_keeper_tasks shows due 2026-10-05 (as_local), the panel says today, but the synced Todoist/Google list gets due_date 2026-10-06. Reproduced with desired_by_sync: 'due': '2026-10-06'. The same happens for every panel snooze to an evening time, and in UTC+ zones for any panel date between local 00:00 and the offset (the item lands a day early).
- **Suggested fix:** Use `datetime.fromisoformat(task["next_due"]).astimezone(now.tzinfo).date().isoformat()` (now is dt_util.now() in the driver) and add a test with a +00:00 next_due near local midnight.
- **Verifier fix note:** The suggested astimezone(now.tzinfo) is right. Also consider re-homing one-off due and snooze until into HA's zone at storage time (models.py:729, websocket_api.py:523), so later consumers cannot repeat this.
- **Verifier reason:** Same defect as B10-7, but the severity is high, not low. B10-7's verifier called it rare because most completions use dt_util.now(). That misses the panel: a one-off due goes through haDateTimeToIso and toISOString (forms.ts:205, forms.ts:1160, which defaults to now) and every panel snooze sends until.toISOString() (defer-dialogs.ts:234). models.py:724-729 and websocket_api.py:519-523 qualify only naive values, so a normal panel save stores +00:00, and recurrence.py:377-382 hands that due back as next_due. todo_list.py:292 then takes .date() in UTC. Nothing re-homes it (grep for as_local/astimezone in store.py, models.py and todo_list.py finds only the #250 fix at store.py:2470). In US zones every one-off due or snooze after 16:00/17:00 local syncs to the external list one day late; in UTC+ zones anything between local midnight and the offset lands a day early. No test_todo_list case uses a +00:00 next_due.
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (medium) — The defect is real and I reproduced it. todo_list.py:292 does datetime.fromisoformat(next_due).date(), which gives the date in the stored offset. The driver passes the stored strings unchanged: todo_list_sync.py:166-173 calls notifier.effective_filter_tasks (notifier.py:75-110), which rewrites only labels and area_id. Nothing in todo_list.py, todo_list_sync.py, models.py or recurrence.py calls as_local on next_due. The only such fix is store.py:2470-2477, for a different path. The panel stores UTC. A new one-off defaults its due to now (forms.ts:844), and haDateTimeToIso sends toISOString (forms.ts:201-206, 1160). build_task qualifies only a naive value, so the aware UTC value stays UTC (models.py:717-729). compute_next_due returns that due (recurrence.py:377-382), and models.py:987 stores .isoformat(), which ends in +00:00. A panel snooze sends until.toISOString() (defer-dialogs.ts:234), and store.py:666 stores it unchanged (websocket_api.py:519-523). The native to-do list has the #250 fix (todo.py:92-99, as_local before date()), so the synced list disagrees with it. No refutation holds. I adjusted the severity down from high to medium. The shift is one day, and only on the external synced list. The stored data, the panel, the native to-do list and the calendar are correct. Nothing is lost. The value is also self-consistent, so there is no rewrite churn (the planner compares the item due with the same want: todo_list.py:556). Recurring tasks completed through HA 'now' carry the local offset and are not affected. It is common, though: every panel one-off or snooze whose local time is after 16:00/17:00 in US zones is one day late, and one in an early-morning hour in UTC+ zones is one day early. With the default overdue filter, an item that is overdue in the panel shows on the external list as due tomorrow.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-X04/t.py -> X04-1 stored 2026-10-06T02:00:00+00:00 sync due 2026-10-06 local date 2026-10-05

### X12-1 — Retention field autosaves every keystroke, and each partial number is stored and applied at once by the purge

- **Severity:** high (finder: critical)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-settings.ts:378`
- **Defect:** The General card saves one_off_retention_days on every value-changed with no debounce. Each save reloads the entry, and the first refresh of the reload runs _purge_expired_one_offs with the partial value.
- **Failure scenario:** Retention is 0 (keep forever, the default). The user types '30' into the box. The '3' keystroke calls set_options({one_off_retention_days: 3}). The backend runs async_update_entry and then async_reload. setup runs async_config_entry_first_refresh, and _async_update_data calls _purge_expired_one_offs with retention 3. Every completed one-off task completed more than 3 days ago is deleted for good. The '0' keystroke comes too late. The same happens with '14' (purge at 1 day), '365' (purge at 3 days), or one arrow-key step up from 0 (purge at 1 day).
- **Suggested fix:** Debounce the settings-card saves (the existing p._debounce with a per-card key), or save a number field on change/blur only. Also consider a backend guard: do not purge in the same reload that lowered the retention, or ask for confirmation when a lower retention would delete tasks.
- **Verifier fix note:** A 600ms debounce alone is not enough (a pause mid-typing still commits '3'). Commit the number on change/blur/Enter, not on input, and add a backend guard: do not purge in the reload that lowered retention, or require a confirmation that shows how many tasks a lower value deletes. Also serialize/retry option writes (X12-2), or a partial value can stay persisted.
- **Verifier reason:** Full path traced. panel-settings.ts:372-379 settingsCard onChange calls saveOptions directly; saveOptions (panel-settings.ts:690-711) has no debounce and no blur/commit. panel.ts:2167-2170 _makeForm forwards every ha-form value-changed. generalSchema is selNumber(0) with mode 'box' (forms.ts:73-76, 1542-1543); HA ha-selector-number binds @input on its textfield and fires value-changed per input event (HA frontend behavior; the repo itself documents per-keystroke ha-form saves at panel-host.ts:118-119 and panel-settings.ts:1076-1077, and debounces only profiles/notifications). Backend: ws_set_options -> options.async_set_options (options.py:325-341): merged != base, so async_update_entry then awaited async_reload; setup (__init__.py:808-830) runs async_config_entry_first_refresh; _async_update_data (coordinator.py:203-204) calls _purge_expired_one_offs first, which reads current_options(entry) and deletes every non-buy one-off where one_off_expired(task, 3) (recurrence.py:895-911: now >= last_completed + 3 days) via store.delete_task: permanent. ArrowUp from 0 gives 1 -> purge at 1 day. Aggravating: the follow-up '0' keystroke usually lands in the reload window and fails with not_loaded (see X12-2), so retention 3 can stay persisted and every periodic tick keeps purging at 3 days. Only a narrow race refutes a single case: if the second write lands while the old entry is still unloading (runtime_data still set) it updates the entry to 30 before the first purge reads it. Severity high, not critical: irreversible and deterministic on normal input, but it needs the user to opt in from the default 0 and it deletes only completed one-off records (which the user is configuring to auto-delete anyway, just sooner than intended).
- **Second opinion:** CONFIRMED (high) — Second, independent trace; every refutation route fails. FRONTEND: the General card is settingsCard(..., generalSchema(), opts) with no coerce (panel-settings.ts:296-305). Its onChange calls saveOptions directly (panel-settings.ts:370-379). saveOptions (panel-settings.ts:690-711) has no debounce, no blur or Enter commit, and no confirmation. _makeForm forwards every ha-form value-changed (panel.ts:2167-2170). The only debounce (_persistTimers, panel.ts:261-264; _debounce, panel-host.ts:118-120) covers profiles and notifications, not this card. generalSchema is selNumber(0) with mode 'box' (forms.ts:73-82, 1542-1543). HA FRONTEND: current home-assistant/frontend dev ha-selector-number.ts binds '@input=${this._handleInputChange}' on its ha-input and fires value-changed for each input event whose number differs from .value; there is no 'change' or blur path for the box. Box mode also shows spin buttons ('.withoutSpinButtons=${!isBox}'), so one up-arrow click from 0 saves 1. BACKEND: ws_set_options (websocket_api.py:1203-1228) is async_response, so a second keystroke is not queued behind the first. async_set_options (options.py:309-341) reloads for any effective change: no per-key exemption. _coerce_days keeps 3 as 3 (options.py:147-153). Setup runs async_config_entry_first_refresh (__init__.py:828-829). _async_update_data calls _purge_expired_one_offs first, with no first-refresh or reload guard (coordinator.py:203-204). The purge reads current_options(entry), which async_update_entry already set to 3 (coordinator.py:263-290). one_off_expired is now >= last_completed + N days, with no grace period (recurrence.py:895-911). store.delete_task removes the task for good (store.py:855-862). History is archived only when an appliance references the task (store.py:882-896), so a standalone one-off loses its history too. The only escape is a race: the next keystroke has to reach async_set_options while async_unload_entry is still running and runtime_data is still set (coordinator.py:421-434). Then options become 30 before the purge reads them. Otherwise the second write fails with integration_not_loaded (websocket_api.py:154-157), which leaves 3 stored while the box shows 30, or it lands after the purge ran. It is not only the 0 default: select-all and '6' to go from 30 to 60 purges at 6 days. Kept at high, not critical: the loss is permanent and needs only normal typing, but it hits only completed one-offs (not buy reminders), and only when the user edits this one setting.

### B01-4 — Completion history of declarative-companion and problem-sensor tasks is dropped without archiving on bulk delete

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:1812`
- **Defect:** delete_task, reconcile_part_tasks and reconcile_buy_tasks call _archive_task_history so a deleted task's completions survive on its appliance, but async_delete_declarative_companion, the 'deleted' ops of reconcile_declarative_companion_tasks, and reconcile_problem_sensor_tasks delete tasks with no archiving.
- **Failure scenario:** A companion materializes 'Replace HEPA filter' on the air purifier's device, which an appliance (kind existing) references; a year of completions accumulate. The user deletes the companion, narrows its selection, or the entity leaves the registry: the task is removed and its history is gone, while deleting the same task by hand would have kept it in the appliance's archived history. The same applies to a problem-sensor mirror deleted when the sensor is excluded or sync is toggled (its note is kept in _problem_notes, its completions are not).
- **Suggested fix:** Call self._archive_task_history(task) for every 'deleted' op in these three paths before assigning self._tasks, as the part/buy reconcilers do.
- **Verifier fix note:** As suggested: call _archive_task_history for every 'deleted' op in the 3 paths before self._tasks is replaced. For problem-sensor mirrors, decide explicitly whether their auto-clear entries belong in appliance history.
- **Verifier reason:** delete_task (store.py:857), reconcile_part_tasks (1549-1552) and reconcile_buy_tasks (1606-1610) call _archive_task_history. async_delete_declarative_companion (1812-1816), the 'deleted' ops of reconcile_declarative_companion_tasks (1913-1918; the orphan pass at declarative_companions.py:792-796 runs when an entity leaves the registry, is excluded, or the selection narrows) and reconcile_problem_sensor_tasks (1683-1696) do not. find_archiving_asset matches by device_id and related_device_ids (assets.py:1811-1817), so a managed task on an appliance's device loses its completions only on these paths. That breaks docs/guide/appliances/appliances.md:25-26: 'The history keeps the completions of tasks that were deleted while assigned to the appliance.' The companion delete dialog says 'Delete {name} and every task it created?' (en.json:629), which tells the user the tasks go but not their history. The orphan path is silent, for example when an integration is removed and added again (new registry id). The loss cannot be undone, but it needs an appliance on the same device: medium.

### B02-1 — Every entry reload removes all home_keeper.* services, so calls in the reload window fail with 'Action not found'

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:2006`
- **Defect:** async_unload_entry removes every service whenever no *loaded* entry remains, which is true on every reload of the single entry, and _register_services only re-adds them near the end of setup (after platform forwarding), so services disappear on each of the many routine reloads.
- **Failure scenario:** A reload is routine here: add_task/update_task/delete_task that changes the entity set, set_options, a problem sensor appearing, buy-task creation/removal (scheduled in the background by async_settle_buy_tasks), declarative reconcile, language change. Example: an automation completes a restock buy task via home_keeper.complete_task (buy task removed -> _async_reload_for_buy_tasks scheduled with async_create_task), then calls home_keeper.notify or adjust_part_stock. If the second call lands while the reload is between unload and line 866, HA raises ServiceNotFound ('Action home_keeper.notify not found') and the automation aborts. Concurrent callers (parallel automations, a companion seeding tasks with devices, each add_task awaiting a reload) hit the same window. An entry in SETUP_ERROR/SETUP_RETRY also has no services at all, so users never get the localized integration_not_loaded error that _coordinator() was written to raise.
- **Suggested fix:** Follow HA's action-setup rule: register the services once in async_setup (they already resolve the coordinator per call and raise integration_not_loaded when none is loaded) and drop the removal from async_unload_entry, or at minimum skip removal when the unload is part of a reload (e.g. only remove when entry.disabled_by is set or from async_remove_entry). Update the api_surface integration test accordingly.
- **Verifier fix note:** Registering once in async_setup (HA's action-setup rule) and keeping the services through a reload makes the gap smaller but does not remove it. HA deletes entry.runtime_data after async_unload_entry (config_entries.py:1055), and setup sets it again only at __init__.py:830. Calls in that gap get the localized integration_not_loaded error, and the gap no longer includes platform forwarding. If calls must survive a reload, _coordinator() can wait a short time for the entry to be LOADED again. Also correct the rule at architecture-and-code.md:1023-1024 and the expectation in tests/integration/test_api_surface.py:116.
- **Verifier reason:** Checked against HA core 2026.9.4, read from the wheel. ConfigEntries.async_reload (config_entries.py:2473-2508) runs async_unload and then async_setup under entry.setup_lock. ConfigEntry.async_unload sets UNLOAD_IN_PROGRESS (1044) before it awaits async_unload_entry (1046), and async_loaded_entries keeps only LOADED entries (2234). So __init__.py:2006-2008 removes every service on every reload of the single entry. ServiceRegistry.async_remove pops the handler (core.py:2798-2825), and async_call then raises ServiceNotFound (core.py:2894); nothing queues the call. The services come back only at __init__.py:866, after store load, the device heal, the first refresh, the reconciles and async_forward_entry_setups, so the gap includes platform setup. Several reloads start in the background and no caller awaits them: the buy-task reload after a completion, undo or stock change (coordinator.py:185-188, hass.async_create_task with eager start), the declarative reconcile on registry events (declarative_companion_sync.py:440-464), the problem-sensor sync (problem_sync.py:211) and the one-off purge on the tick (coordinator.py:306-308). A sequential automation (complete a buy task, then call a second home_keeper action) or any concurrent caller can land in the gap and stop with 'Action home_keeper.x not found'. An entry in setup error or setup retry has no services at all. The project rule at .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md:1023-1024 makes this choice on purpose ('re-registering them is invisible'), and HA core shows that premise is false. quality_scale.yaml:11-15 marks action-setup done, but async_setup (797-799) registers nothing.
- **Repro:** HA 2026.9.4 wheel (pip download --no-deps into SCRATCH/tmp-v-B02): config_entries.py:1044 '_async_set_state(hass, ConfigEntryState.UNLOAD_IN_PROGRESS, None)', 1046 'result = await component.async_unload_entry(hass, self)', 2234 'entry.state is ConfigEntryState.LOADED', core.py:2894 'raise ServiceNotFound(domain, service) from None'.

### B03-2 — delete_declarative_companion never reloads, so the removed tasks' device-page entities stay until an unrelated reload

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/websocket_api.py:1404`
- **Defect:** The store returns entity_set_changed and documents that the caller must reload, but the handler only echoes the flag to a client that ignores it, and the follow-up reconcile walks only the remaining specs, so nothing prunes the per-task button/sensor/binary_sensor entities.
- **Failure scenario:** An admin deletes a declarative companion (for example a battery companion) whose managed tasks sit on real devices. async_delete_declarative_companion removes the tasks and returns True. ws_delete_declarative_companion sends {ok, entity_set_changed: true} and does not reload. The SIGNAL_DECLARATIVE_SPECS_CHANGED reconcile iterates only the surviving specs, finds no entity-set change and only refreshes. Platforms prune orphans only in async_setup_entry, so every affected device page keeps a 'Mark done' button, a 'Next due' sensor and an 'Overdue' binary sensor for a task that no longer exists. Pressing the button raises KeyError from store.complete_task. They go away only on the next unrelated reload or restart. The service twin has the same gap.
- **Suggested fix:** In both ws_delete_declarative_companion and handle_delete_declarative_companion: if the store returns True, await hass.config_entries.async_reload(coord.entry.entry_id), else await coord.async_request_refresh(). Add an integration test that deletes a device-bound companion (the _device_battery_spec shape) and asserts its next-due sensor and button disappear.
- **Verifier fix note:** Suggested fix is right. Awaiting async_reload inside the ws handler is fine (ws_delete_task does it); the dispatcher-triggered reconcile may also schedule a reload, and DeclarativeCompanionSync._reload_scheduled only guards its own reloads, so expect at most one extra reload.
- **Verifier reason:** ws_delete_declarative_companion (websocket_api.py:1395-1405) only echoes entity_set_changed; the panel discards it (api.ts:936-944), and the service twin ignores it (__init__.py:1890-1893). store.async_delete_declarative_companion (store.py:1799-1830) removes the tasks, returns True when any removed task had device_id+enabled (store.py:109-116, 1823-1824), and its docstring says the caller must reload. The dispatcher reconcile (declarative_companion_sync.py:333-363) walks only the surviving specs, so it reports no entity-set change and only refreshes (440-458). Orphans are pruned only by prune_registry_entries at platform setup (entity.py:105-125, button.py:60), and coordinator.py:294-308 says the same thing ('the entity registry is cleaned by reloading the config entry'). Companion tasks carry the matched entity's device_id (declarative_companions.py:724), so a device-bound companion leaves Mark done/Next due/Overdue entities on every matched device page; the button calls store.complete_task on a gone id (button.py:77-78) and raises KeyError. No test deletes a device-bound spec (test_declarative_companion_sync.py:921-929 uses the device-less tank spec). Self-heals at the next reload or restart.

### B03-3 — import_data / export_data parse and emit YAML with pure-Python PyYAML on the event loop (seconds to tens of seconds of freeze)

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/websocket_api.py:1174`
- **Defect:** ws_import_data and ws_export_data await transfer_runner coroutines that call transfer.parse_document / document_to_yaml synchronously, and both use yaml.SafeLoader/SafeDumper subclasses (the pure-Python implementation, even when libyaml is installed), which blocks Home Assistant's event loop.
- **Failure scenario:** The panel's import flow sends the file text twice: once for the dry-run preview, once for the real import. Each call runs yaml.load in the loop. Measured locally with the same SafeLoader subclass shape: 0.2 MB (40 tasks x 50 completions) takes about 0.7 s to load and 0.3 s to dump. 2 MB takes about 6.1 s to load and 3.2 s to dump. The panel's 4 MiB websocket cap is about 12 s per parse. A Raspberry Pi is several times slower. For that whole time HA processes no automations, state changes or websocket frames, and the frontend can drop its connection. Export of a household with long history blocks the loop in the same way.
- **Suggested fix:** In transfer_runner, run the text parse (transfer.parse_document, or the whole pure plan_import step for a str document) and document_to_yaml through hass.async_add_executor_job, then apply the plan on the loop. Optionally prefer yaml.CSafeLoader/CSafeDumper bases when available. Extend test_event_loop_blocking to cover a large import/export.
- **Verifier fix note:** Run parse_document (or the whole pure plan_import) and document_to_yaml through hass.async_add_executor_job; plan_import only reads the passed-in snapshots, so pass copies (dict(...) of tasks/assets) rather than live store dicts to the executor. yaml.CSafeLoader cannot simply be subclassed with the Python compose_node override (the alias refusal), so keep the pure loader and move it off the loop.
- **Verifier reason:** transfer_runner.py:27-40 and 43-67 call transfer.document_to_yaml / transfer.plan_import on the loop; plan_import parses a str document with parse_document (transfer.py:934-936), which runs yaml.load with a pure-Python SafeLoader subclass (transfer.py:583, 693), and the dumper is a SafeDumper subclass (561, 636). The panel sends the raw file text (panel.ts:1485 -> api.ts:844-850), twice per import (dry run, then real). Measured with the real modules on this machine: 0.24 MiB document = 0.57 s load + 0.33 s dump; 1.18 MiB = 3.3 s load + 1.8 s dump; 4.69 MiB = 15.7 s load. ws cap is 4 MiB (const.py:86), service cap 8 MiB (const.py:71). ARM hardware (HA Green/Pi) is several times slower. Severity stays medium, not higher: admin-only and rare, and a typical store (well under 0.25 MiB) blocks for under a second.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../tmp-v-B20/pydeps python3 tmp-v-B03/yaml_block.py -> 'tasks=40 completions/task=50 yaml=0.24 MiB dump=0.33s load=0.57s'; 'tasks=100 completions/task=100 yaml=1.18 MiB dump=1.79s load=3.29s'; 'tasks=200 completions/task=200 yaml=4.69 MiB dump=7.71s load=15.69s'

### B04-3 — Tasks silently re-attach to the wrong appliance on restore when two appliances share a name

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:375`  **Also reported as:** X03-4
- **Defect:** The export names a task's appliance by external_id or name only; on another install the task's device_id is unknown, so the name decides, and asset_refs.setdefault keeps the first appliance with that name.
- **Failure scenario:** Two appliances named 'Smoke detector' (Hall, Bedroom), each with a monthly test task, no external_id. Export and import on a new install: both tasks resolve `appliance: Smoke detector` to the first planned appliance. Verified: both tasks -> the Hall appliance; the Bedroom appliance ends with no tasks; plan.ok True with no warning. Duplicate names (smoke detectors, toilets, ceiling fans) are common.
- **Suggested fix:** Export an unambiguous reference (the appliance id, which import keeps via _claim_id) when the name or key is not unique, register planned ids in asset_refs, and report an ambiguity problem when a document name maps to more than one planned appliance instead of taking the first.
- **Verifier reason:** _task_out (transfer.py:375) writes `appliance: <external_id or name>`; on an install that lacks the old devices the stated device_id is dropped (1438-1443) and _appliance_device returns asset_refs[name] (1635-1636), which setdefault (1024) fixed to the first same-named appliance. No ambiguity check for document-side names (the stored side raises AmbiguousName, the document side does not). Silent: plan.ok True, no warning. Needs duplicate appliance names plus a restore where the old device ids are gone (new install, or Home Keeper removed and re-added); same install with devices present is correct. Recoverable by re-pointing tasks, so medium rather than high.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r3_dupname.py -> exported refs ['Smoke detector', 'Smoke detector']; plan.ok True; task 'Test hall alarm' -> HALL, task 'Test bedroom alarm' -> HALL; devices-present case keeps dev-hall/dev-bed

### B04-4 — History replay on an update rewinds last_completed/next_due and, past the 500 cap, discards the newest stored completions

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:1511`  **Also reported as:** X03-3
- **Defect:** On a matched task, apply_history replays only the document's entries on top of the stored list: apply_completion stamps last_completed from each replayed entry and _record_entry appends out of order, so the cap trims newer stored entries.
- **Failure scenario:** (a) Documented re-run: a migration file with history up to 2026-03-01 is imported, the household completes the task on 2026-09-01, the same file is run again (matched by external_id). Verified: last_completed goes back to 2026-03-01 and next_due to 2026-06-01, so the task shows overdue although the 2026-09-01 completion is still in its history. (b) Backfill: a task with 400 completions (2025-26) gets 200 older entries (2015) from a document. Verified: 100 of the 400 recent entries are dropped, all 200 old ones are kept, last_completed becomes 2015-07-19 and next_due 2015-07-20.
- **Suggested fix:** For an update, merge stored and document entries, dedupe by ts, sort chronologically, cap to the newest 500, set last_completed to the max ts, and recompute next_due from the latest completion (or replay the merged, sorted list onto a copy with no completions).
- **Verifier fix note:** For an update, restate last_completed/next_due from the newest entry of the merged list (fixed tasks already restate next_due from the anchor at 832-840), and merge-sort the entries before capping so the newest 500 survive.
- **Verifier reason:** transfer.py:1510-1511 replays the document history onto the merged stored task; apply_completion (recurrence.py:529-530) stamps last_completed/next_due from each replayed entry and _record_entry (396-416) appends then keeps the tail. So a re-run (documented use of external_id in the user guide) after a newer completion rewinds last_completed/next_due while the newer entry stays in completions (inconsistent record), and a backfill past 500 entries drops newer stored entries. apply_history's own docstring says it folds onto a 'freshly built' task and warns about exactly this ordering hazard. No test covers history onto a stored task with newer completions. Due-date damage is visible and recoverable; the cap loss needs >500 entries, so medium.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r_rest.py -> B04-4a: before re-run last_completed 2026-09-01, re-run (update, external_id) last_completed 2026-03-01 next_due 2026-06-01, max ts in completions 2026-09-01; B04-4b: kept recent 300 of 400, old 200, last_completed 2015-07-19 next_due 2015-07-20

### B06-2 — Re-used document_id overwrites an existing document's blob before the store regenerates the id

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/manuals.py:617`  **Also reported as:** X01-3, X03-2, X07-2
- **Defect:** The client-chosen document_id is used as the storage key and the upload is os.replace'd onto <document_id>__<name> before add_asset_document detects the id collision, so an existing document with the same id and filename loses its bytes.
- **Failure scenario:** Asset has document id D, filename manual.pdf. A client (script, agent, retry tool, or a non-admin via B06-1) POSTs another PDF named manual.pdf to /api/home_keeper/document/<asset>/D. _move os.replace's the new bytes over D__manual.pdf; append_document sees id D is taken and mints a new uuid N; async_rename_document moves D__manual.pdf to N__manual.pdf. Document D now 404s and its original file is gone. On the rejection path (e.g. the asset already has 50 documents, or the name part is >200 chars) line 641 deletes D__manual.pdf outright. Because ids are free-form, the '__' delimiter also aliases: doc id 'A'+'B__C.pdf' and doc id 'A__B'+'C.pdf' share A__B__C.pdf (verified with documents.document_path), and doc id 'part_<partid>' aliases that part's file key.
- **Suggested fix:** Before streaming, reject (409) a document_id that already exists on the asset or is not a UUID (mirror transfer._is_uuid); or ignore the URL id and store under a server-minted uuid. Never move onto an existing path (check with os.link/O_EXCL or exists() inside the executor job).
- **Verifier fix note:** Check the collision before the move: if document_id is already on the asset (or is not a UUID), mint the new id first, move the blob onto the final id, then add the metadata with that id. That keeps the 'blob before metadata' order the comment at manuals.py:610-615 wants. A 409 is also fine. Do not move onto an existing path.
- **Verifier reason:** The path is as described: manuals.py:617 moves the upload onto <document_id>__<safe_name> with os.replace (manuals.py:145) before manuals.py:628 add_asset_document. assets.append_document (assets.py:352-354) then regenerates a colliding id, and manuals.py:647-651 renames the overwritten blob to the new id. Document D keeps its record but loses its bytes (GET 404). On the rejection path (50-document cap, assets.py:347-350) manuals.py:641 deletes D's blob outright. Only a reused id WITH the same sanitized filename destroys data. Severity lowered: the panel never reuses an id. It mints randomId() per upload (panel-asset-editors.ts:479) and retries only on a 401 (api.ts postUploadAuthed), which HA returns before the handler runs. So this needs a hand-written client, or a non-admin through B06-1 (card_projection gives every document id and filename, which turns B06-1 into 'destroy any document').
- **Repro:** python3 repro_b06.py -> 'B06-2 regen id differs: True'; 'B06-2 alias same path: True A__B__C.pdf' (doc id A + B__C.pdf vs doc id A__B + C.pdf); doc id 'part_<pid>' resolves to the part file key.

### B06-3 — Removing a part leaves its attached file on disk forever

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:944`  **Also reported as:** X03-10
- **Defect:** Parts are removed by an update_asset that omits them, and nothing deletes the dropped part's part_<id>__<file> blob, so it stays until the whole appliance is deleted.
- **Failure scenario:** User attaches a 40 MB PDF to a part, then clicks Remove part in the appliance editor and saves. The panel sends update_asset with the part filtered out; _merge_parts only keeps incoming parts; store.update_asset saves with no blob cleanup. The file stays in <config>/home_keeper/documents/<asset>/ (and in every HA backup) with no record pointing at it. The same happens when a managed appliance's owner drops an untracked part (apply_managed_parts) and when an import merge replaces the parts list.
- **Suggested fix:** In store.update_asset (and the apply_managed_parts / import write paths) diff the old and merged parts by id and, after _save, call manuals.async_delete_part_file for each dropped part that had file_name. Add an integration test: upload a part file, update_asset without that part, assert the blob is gone.
- **Verifier reason:** The panel's Remove part only filters the draft (panel-asset-editors.ts:826-827), and removePartFile is called only from the separate Remove file button (panel-asset-editors.ts:929-933). store.update_asset (store.py:944-963) calls merge_update and _save with no blob cleanup. _merge_parts (assets.py:799-814) iterates only the incoming parts. The only blob deletes are store.py:1107, 1169 and 1481 (delete_asset rmtree). The dropped part's part_<id>__<file> stays until the whole appliance is deleted, and it is in every backup. No test covers part removal (test_part_files.py covers preserve-on-update and delete-asset). It is a disk-only leak bounded by the appliance's lifetime, but it breaks the stated 'cannot orphan a blob' invariant on a normal UI action and never heals.

### B07-1 — Undo of a completion on an overdue fixed task drops the overdue occurrence

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:669`
- **Defect:** remove_completion rebuilds a fixed task's next_due as the next grid occurrence after now, so undoing an accidental Done on an overdue fixed task does not put the missed occurrence back; the task silently stops being overdue.
- **Failure scenario:** Fixed MONTHLY task anchored on the 1st at 09:00, next_due 2026-09-01 (overdue). On 2026-09-20 12:00 the user taps Done on the wrong row: next_due -> 2026-10-01. The user taps Undo (store.delete_completion -> recurrence.remove_completion): next_due stays 2026-10-01, is_overdue is False. The September occurrence is gone from every time surface. Reproduced with a standalone snippet.
- **Suggested fix:** Record the pre-completion next_due on the completion entry (like meter_start), and when the removed entry is the latest completion restore that value instead of recomputing from now.
- **Verifier fix note:** A pre-completion next_due stored on the entry is a new persisted field: it must round-trip through transfer.py or be named in EXCLUDED_* with a reason. Restore it only when the removed entry is the latest completion and no later skip exists.
- **Verifier reason:** recurrence.py:669-670 recomputes a fixed task's next_due with compute_next_due, which is next_fixed_occurrence(after=now) (recurrence.py:361-369). So undoing a completion (store.delete_completion store.py:2169, reached from the history trash button panel-history.ts:233 and the service) on an overdue fixed task cannot put the missed occurrence back, and the task stops being overdue. No caller guard. tests/unit/test_recurrence_fixed.py:254 states the intent ('an undo is meant to rewind ... today's occurrence returns') but covers only now < occurrence; :158 covers a task that was not overdue. Medium, not high: history is intact and the schedule continues; what is lost is the overdue state of 1 missed occurrence.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e12.py -> 'overdue before: True 2026-09-01T09:00:00-07:00' / 'after Done: 2026-10-01T09:00:00-07:00' / 'after Undo: 2026-10-01T09:00:00-07:00 overdue: False'

### B07-2 — Deleting or moving an older history row rewinds next_due and discards a later skip, snooze, due-today or early-completion advance

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:670`  **Also reported as:** B08-4
- **Defect:** remove_completion and move_completion always re-derive next_due from the log, even when the edited row is not the latest, so schedule state the log does not hold (skip, snooze, due today, a fixed task completed before its time of day) is thrown away.
- **Failure scenario:** (a) Fixed DAILY 10:00 task with a stray 2026-08-01 row. Done at 09:00 today -> next_due tomorrow 10:00 (#331 path). User deletes the unrelated August row -> next_due today 10:00, overdue at 10:01. (b) Floating monthly task: completed Jul 1 and Aug 1, skipped Sep 1 -> next_due Oct 1. User deletes the old Jul 1 row -> next_due Sep 1, overdue again. (c) Same task snoozed to Sep 20; user moves the Jul 1 row by a day (move_completion) -> next_due Sep 1. All reproduced.
- **Suggested fix:** Re-derive next_due only when the removed/moved entry is (or becomes) the latest completion and no later skip exists; otherwise leave next_due untouched.
- **Verifier fix note:** Gate the recompute on last_completed actually changing (the entry was, or becomes, the latest). This does not fix B07-1, which is the latest-entry case.
- **Verifier reason:** remove_completion (recurrence.py:669-670) and move_completion (recurrence.py:782-783, floating) recompute next_due for any entry, latest or not. store.delete_completion computes was_latest (store.py:2168) but uses it only for the meter. The panel offers delete and move on every history row (panel-history.ts:229-233). Reproduced: deleting or moving an unrelated old row discards a floating skip or snooze, puts a fixed task completed early (#331) back on today's done occurrence, and (extra case) clears the overdue state of a fixed task. No test pins next_due for a non-latest removal (test_skip_log.py:122 asserts last_completed only). Same defect as B08-4.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e5.py -> 'after deleting the Aug 1 row: 2026-09-30T10:00:00-07:00 overdue at 10:01? True' / 'floating after skip: 2026-10-01...' then 'after deleting the old July row: 2026-09-01T09:00:00-07:00' / 'snoozed to 9/20, after moving the July row by a day: 2026-09-01T09:00:00-07:00'. python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B07/v2.py -> 'overdue before: True 2026-09-23...' / 'after deleting the Sep 2 row: 2026-09-30T09:00:00-07:00 overdue: False'

### B07-3 — A back-dated completion older than the latest one rewinds last_completed and a floating task's next_due
