- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:530`  **Also reported as:** B08-3, X04-4
- **Defect:** apply_completion stamps last_completed and the floating next_due from whatever completed_at it is given, so logging a forgotten older completion makes the task look last done on the older date and puts it overdue.
- **Failure scenario:** Floating monthly task done 2026-09-20 -> next_due 2026-10-20. On 2026-09-30 the user logs a forgotten completion with Completed at = 2026-08-15 (panel completion dialog field, or complete_task completed_at). Result: last_completed 2026-08-15, next_due 2026-09-15, is_overdue True, while max(completions) is still 2026-09-20. remove_completion/move_completion use max(history), so the layers disagree.
- **Suggested fix:** In apply_completion set last_completed to the latest ts in the updated history, and compute the floating next_due from that latest ts rather than from completed_at.
- **Verifier fix note:** Also skip store.complete_task's _reset_usage_baseline when the new entry is older than the latest decision. For a fixed task, _advance_fixed_schedule consumes the showing occurrence for any completed_at; decide whether an older back-fill should move the schedule at all.
- **Verifier reason:** apply_completion sets last_completed = completed_at (recurrence.py:530) and measures a floating next_due from completed_at (recurrence.py:533-539) with no check against the existing latest completion, while remove/move use max(history) (recurrence.py:656, 773). The panel Done dialog accepts any Completed at value (panel-dialogs.ts:351-387, no minimum) and store.complete_task passes it through (store.py:1986, 2008). transfer.apply_history documents the hazard and sorts to avoid it (transfer.py:788-792); the live path does not. sensor_tasks.latest_decision_ts reads last_completed, so a usage task's time backstop also re-anchors to the older date. docs/BACKDATE_COMPLETION_PLAN.md does not consider an entry older than the latest. Same defect as B08-3.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e6.py -> 'after logging Aug 15: last_completed 2026-08-15T09:00:00-07:00 next_due 2026-09-15T09:00:00-07:00 overdue: True' with 'max(history): 2026-09-20T09:00:00-07:00'

### B07-4 — Floating next_due recomputed from a stored offset drifts one hour across DST, and a day near midnight and at a season start

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:354`  **Also reported as:** X04-3
- **Defect:** compute_next_due parses last_completed from ISO (fixed UTC offset) and adds the interval in that offset; the fixed-task path re-homes to HA's zone (_regrid) but the floating path does not, so undo, move, interval/season edits and imports give a different due time than the live completion did.
- **Failure scenario:** LA zone. Monthly floating task done 2026-02-20 23:30 PST: apply_completion (ZoneInfo now) gives 2026-03-20 23:30 PDT. An accidental Done then Undo on Mar 20 re-derives 2026-03-20T23:30-08:00 = Mar 21 00:30 PDT, so the to-do date (dt_util.as_local(due).date()) moves to Mar 21. Season case: task done 2026-07-15 (PDT) with season 12-01..02-28; apply_completion gives Dec 1 00:00 PST, but compute_next_due (any edit of interval/season, undo, import) gives 2026-12-01T00:00-07:00 = Nov 30 23:00 PST, outside the season. Also applies in apply_completion when completed_at comes from the panel or a service with an explicit offset.
- **Suggested fix:** Re-home last_completed (and completed_at in apply_completion) with .astimezone(now.tzinfo) before add_interval, as _regrid does for anchors, so both paths do wall-clock arithmetic in HA's zone.
- **Verifier fix note:** Re-home completed_at and last_completed to now.tzinfo before add_interval, and evaluate in_season and _next_season_start on the local date. The same fixed-offset pattern is in sensor_tasks.backstop_due (sensor_tasks.py:203) and reconcile.py:319.
- **Verifier reason:** compute_next_due parses last_completed as a fixed offset (recurrence.py:354), and add_interval and _next_season_start (recurrence.py:126-141, 185) do wall-clock math in that offset; only fixed anchors are re-homed (_regrid, recurrence.py:271). The live Done path uses dt_util.now() (ZoneInfo), so undo/move (recurrence.py:670, 783), interval or season edits (models.py:1128), seeds and imports (transfer.py:829) give a different time. Broader than claimed: a back-dated panel completion arrives as UTC (websocket_api.py:408 parses toISOString()), so a seasonal floating task clamps to midnight UTC, which is the previous local day west of UTC. todo.py:99 shows as_local(due).date(), so the shown date moves. No floating DST test exists.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e1.py -> 'after undo next_due: 2026-03-20T23:30:00-08:00 -> local 2026-03-21 00:30:00-07:00'. python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B07/v4.py -> 'live Done: 2026-12-01T00:00:00-08:00 local date 2026-12-01' / 're-derived: 2026-12-01T00:00:00-07:00 local date 2026-11-30 in season (local)? False' / 'panel back-dated: 2026-12-01T00:00:00+00:00 local 2026-11-30 16:00:00-08:00 in season (local)? False'

### B07-5 — Due today (or an early snooze) then Done on a fixed task hands back the occurrence the user just did

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:481`  **Also reported as:** X04-2
- **Defect:** _advance_fixed_schedule treats an off-grid next_due as no occurrence on the board and advances only past now, so a task pulled forward with Due today, or snoozed before its time and done early, returns to the same original occurrence (#331 again).
- **Failure scenario:** Fixed WEEKLY task, Saturdays 10:00, showing 2026-10-03 10:00. Wednesday 12:00 the user selects Due today (next_due = now) and then Done: next_due -> 2026-10-03 10:00, the same occurrence. Monthly task showing Oct 28: Due today on Oct 10 and Done -> still Oct 28. Due-soon notification Friday: Snooze 24h, then Done Saturday 09:00 -> next_due Saturday 10:00, overdue an hour later. The guide says Due today is for a task a user wants to do now rather than on its usual date, and the panel offers it only on tasks not yet due.
- **Suggested fix:** When snooze_task/set_due_today move next_due, keep the grid occurrence they displaced (e.g. a deferred_from field), and have _advance_fixed_schedule advance past max(now, deferred_from).
- **Verifier fix note:** A deferred_from field is a new persisted storage field (one-way door): it must round-trip through transfer.py or be excluded with a reason, and be cleared whenever the schedule changes.
- **Verifier reason:** _advance_fixed_schedule drops an off-grid next_due and advances past now only (recurrence.py:481-483). set_due_today writes now (store.py:719), and deferVerbs offers Due today only on a task that is not yet due (defer.ts:66), so on a fixed task the shown occurrence is always still ahead, and Done hands that same occurrence back. The guide says Due today is for a task a user wants to do now rather than on its usual date (docs/guide/tasks/snooze-and-skip.md:19-20). The pinning test (test_recurrence_fixed.py:354) sets due-today after the occurrence, a state the UI does not offer; commit 1536444 added _is_occurrence for the snooze case.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e3.py -> 'showing: 2026-10-03T10:00:00-07:00' / 'after due-today + done on Wed: 2026-10-03T10:00:00-07:00' / 'monthly after due-today + done on 10th: 2026-10-28T09:00:00-07:00' / 'after snooze(24h) Fri, done Sat 09:00: 2026-10-03T10:00:00-07:00'

### B08-1 — merge_update compares due/anchor as raw strings, so a panel rename resurrects a completed one-off and cancels snoozes

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/models.py:1123`
- **Defect:** recurrence_changed compares the stored ISO string with the re-normalized one, but the panel always re-sends due/anchor as UTC toISOString() at second precision, so any task whose stored value has another offset or sub-second digits counts as a schedule change on its first panel save.
- **Failure scenario:** 1) add_task {name: 'Take out bins'} stores due = now.isoformat() = '2026-09-28T14:23:45.123456-07:00'. Complete it (next_due None). Rename it in the panel: payload due = '2026-09-28T21:23:45.000Z' -> normalized '2026-09-28T21:23:45+00:00' != stored -> next_due = due, so the done task is back and overdue (to-do list, notifications). Reproduced: one_off_completed True -> False, is_overdue True. The same happens on a panel-only flow: converting a task to one-off with a blank picker sends new Date().toISOString() with milliseconds, and the next rename drops them. 2) A fixed DAILY 09:00 task created by the service with a naive anchor (stored '-07:00'), completed at 08:05 (next_due tomorrow per #331), then renamed: next_due goes back to today 09:00. 3) A snoozed fixed/one-off task loses its snooze on rename. A service-written season '4-1' re-sent by the panel as '04-01' triggers the same recompute.
- **Suggested fix:** In merge_update, compare due/anchor by parsed instant (and season windows by (month, day) tuples), and keep the stored string when the instant is unchanged. Alternatively canonicalize at normalize time (e.g. drop microseconds and store one offset form). Add a test that uses the panel's UTC `.000Z` form against a '-07:00' stored value.
- **Verifier fix note:** Compare due and anchor as parsed instants, and season windows as (month, day) tuples. The regression test payload must be haDateTimeToIso(stored) for a '-07:00' stored value with microseconds.
- **Verifier reason:** merge_update compares raw strings (models.py:1123-1126). The panel keeps the stored ISO in its edit state (panel-task-form.ts:461) and re-sends it through haDateTimeToIso (forms.ts:201-206, 1160, 1163), which returns toISOString(): UTC 'Z' with milliseconds. A stored value with a non-UTC offset or more than 3 fractional digits reads as changed on the first full-form save. Such values come from the service and integration paths: build_task defaults due to now.isoformat() (models.py:911), and a naive due or anchor gets HA's offset (models.py:723-729, 771-778). Reproduced with the exact Node output: a completed service-created one-off comes back overdue, and an overdue service-created fixed task stops being overdue. Panel-created tasks round-trip unchanged (control). After that save the string is UTC, so each task is hit once, silently. tests/unit/test_models.py:825-850 sends the stored string back, not the panel form. Correction: a plain rename keeps milliseconds (the edit state holds the stored ISO); the 'next rename drops them' sub-case needs an edit in the schedule section.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B08/v1.py -> '1 stored due 2026-09-28T14:23:45.123456-07:00 | panel sends 2026-09-28T21:23:45.123Z' / '1 after rename next_due 2026-09-28T21:23:45.123000+00:00 completed? False overdue? True' / '2 control (panel-created) completed still? True' / '3 overdue before? True 2026-09-23T09:00:00-07:00' / '3 after rename next_due 2026-09-30T09:00:00-07:00 overdue? False'

### B08-2 — sensor.state accepts a YAML boolean: 'state: on' is stored as 'True' and the task never arms

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/models.py:379`
- **Defect:** normalize_sensor stringifies the state with str(), so an unquoted YAML on/off (read as a bool by Home Assistant's YAML 1.1 loader and by the import document) becomes 'True' or fails with a misleading 'sensor.state is required'.
- **Failure scenario:** An automation, script or Developer Tools call writes add_task with sensor: {entity_id: binary_sensor.garage_door, mode: state, state: on}. HA's loader yields state=True; the service schema passes the dict through (`sensor: dict`); normalize_sensor stores state='True'. evaluate_state compares the entity state 'on' == 'True' -> never met, so the task silently never arms. With `state: off` the call fails with 'sensor.state is required', which hides the cause. The same applies to a hand-written import document. Reproduced with yaml.safe_load + normalize_sensor.
- **Suggested fix:** Run sensor.state (and attribute/unit) through _reject_boolean, or map True/False to 'on'/'off' explicitly for state mode, so the failure is loud or the intent is kept.
- **Verifier fix note:** Use _reject_boolean, the project's precedent for text fields, rather than mapping True to 'on': YAML also reads yes/no as booleans, so a mapping would guess.
- **Verifier reason:** normalize_sensor stores str(data.get('state') or '') (models.py:379). HA's YAML loader and the import parser keep the YAML 1.1 boolean resolver (transfer.py:600-603), so an unquoted 'state: on' arrives as True and is stored as 'True'; sensor_tasks.py:636 compares 'on' == 'True' and the task never arms. 'state: off' fails as 'sensor.state is required'. The service schema passes the dict through (__init__.py:149, 199). _reject_boolean (models.py:457-479) exists for this trap and is not applied here. The panel sends a string, so only hand-written YAML (automations, scripts, import documents) is hit, but on/off are exactly the values this mode is for.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B08/v2b.py -> "parse_document state: True -> stored 'True'"; python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B08/sim3.py -> 'state: off -> TaskValidationError sensor.state is required'

### B09-1 — resolve_tracked binds a vanished uid to an older ticked-off record, so deleting an item completes the task (and restocks a part)

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_items.py:84`
- **Defect:** When the captured uid is no longer on the list, resolve_tracked falls back to a summary match and, with no open match, returns the first completed item with that text, which both planners then read as the household's tick.
- **Failure scenario:** To-do list sync, vanish_as_completed OFF, two_way ON, list that keeps completed items (local_todo, CalDAV, Google Tasks). Last cycle's 'Clean filter' (uid U1) is ticked and stays on the list, which todo_list.py says is intended history. This cycle's item U2 is bound (uid captured). The household deletes U2. Next pass: resolve_tracked(uid='U2') finds no U2, falls back by summary, finds only U1 (completed), returns it; plan_sync takes the 'ticked off' branch and emits CompleteOp -> store.complete_task records a completion nobody did and advances the schedule. Documented behaviour for this toggle is 'treated as deleted and recreated'. Same for the shopping mirror: shopper deletes this episode's 'Buy filter' line while last episode's ticked 'Buy filter' is still on HA's Shopping List -> CompleteOp -> _stamp_buy_restock adds restock_quantity to stock, although the code promises 'leave it deleted'. Reproduced with the pure planners: todo_list.plan_sync -> [CompleteOp(key='prof1:t1')] with the old record present, AddOp without it; shopping.plan_sync -> [CompleteOp(key='a1:p1')] with the old line present, nothing without it.
- **Suggested fix:** In resolve_tracked, when a non-empty string uid was captured and no live item carries it, fall back to OPEN summary matches only (a re-created line); never return a completed item with a different uid. Let the planners treat that as 'vanished'. Add planner tests with a stale completed record beside a deleted bound item.
- **Verifier fix note:** The suggested fix is sound: when a non-empty string uid was captured and no live item carries it, fall back to OPEN summary matches only. test_an_unmatched_uid_still_falls_back_to_the_summary still passes because its recreated line is open. Add the stale-completed-record case to test_todo_list.py and test_shopping.py. With vanish_as_completed on, the vanished-item branch still completes by design.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced. After a bind, the entry holds the uid and added_at=None (todo_list.py:576 _entry; shopping.py:414-418). When that uid is gone, todo_items.py:84-93 falls back to summary and returns the first COMPLETED match when no open one exists. Last cycle's ticked record is unclaimed by then (its key was dropped at todo_list.py:515-516 / 533-536, shopping.py:364-370, 381-387), so it is the normal steady state on lists that keep completed items (local_todo, HA Shopping List, CalDAV). todo_list.py:494-516 then emits CompleteOp (the added_at hold at :500 does not apply to a bound entry), and shopping.py:364-369 does the same, so store.complete_task restocks via _stamp_buy_restock (store.py:2025-2026) with no guard. This contradicts todo_list.py:44-47 ('treated as deleted and recreated') and shopping.py:351-356 ('leave it deleted'). No test covers a deleted bound item beside a stale completed record (test_todo_items.py:135 uses an OPEN recreated line; test_todo_list.py:789 and test_shopping.py:461 use an empty list). Trigger needs a household to delete (not tick) an open synced line, with toggle vanish_as_completed off and two_way on. The effect is a false completion that can be undone, so medium, not high.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-B09 && python3 sim_sync.py -> 'todo_list (vanish_as_completed OFF) complete: [CompleteOp(key=prof1:t1, task_id=t1)] add: []'; control with empty list -> 'complete: [] add: [AddOp(...)]'. python3 sim_shop.py -> 'shopping (old ticked line present) complete: [CompleteOp(key=a1:p1, task_id=b2)]'; control -> 'complete: []'.

### B09-2 — Shopping mirror has no unconfirmed-add hold: a new reminder binds to last episode's ticked line and is completed at once, restocking the part

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/shopping.py:344`
- **Defect:** An uid-less shopping entry (just added) is resolved by summary through resolve_tracked, which returns the previous episode's completed line when the new line is not readable yet; the planner reads it as 'bought'.
- **Failure scenario:** Shopping list on CalDAV (HA's CalDAV todo entity refreshes after add_item in a fire-and-forget task and returns completed VTODOs). Episode 1: 'Buy filter' was ticked and stays on the list. Part goes low again -> reconcile_buy_tasks creates reminder B2 -> pass adds 'Buy filter' and tracks {uid: None}. The coordinator refresh that async_settle_buy_tasks requests schedules a forced sweep right away; that pass reads the list before CalDAV shows the new line, resolve_tracked(uid=None) returns the old completed line, plan_sync emits CompleteOp(b2), complete_task restocks the part and the reconciler removes the reminder. The household never sees the reminder and stock is inflated. Reproduced: shopping.plan_sync(tracked={'a1:p1': {'uid': None, 'summary': 'Buy filter'}}, items=[old completed 'Buy filter']) -> [CompleteOp(key='a1:p1', task_id='b2')]. todo_list.py fixed exactly this case with the added_at hold; shopping.py did not.
- **Suggested fix:** Port the todo_list hold to shopping.plan_sync: stamp added_at on AddOp entries, and while an uid-less entry is unconfirmed, do not read a completed summary match as a tick (hold the entry until an open line shows or the grace expires). Alternatively give resolve_tracked a flag that excludes completed items for never-bound entries.
- **Verifier fix note:** Port todo_list's hold: stamp added_at on shopping AddOp entries, and while an uid-less entry is unconfirmed, do not read a completed summary match as a tick (carry the entry until an open line shows or the grace expires). A flag on resolve_tracked that excludes completed items for never-bound entries is the smaller change and also serves todo_list.
- **Verifier reason:** Planner reproduced, and the race is traced end to end. shopping.py:460-467 tracks a fresh add as {uid: None} with no added_at. On the next pass resolve_tracked returns last episode's ticked line when the new line is not readable yet (todo_items.py:90-93). shopping.py:364-369 then emits CompleteOp, and store.complete_task (store.py:1979-2026) restocks with no guard. HA's CalDAV to-do entity really does refresh fire-and-forget (upstream homeassistant/components/caldav/todo.py:149 'self.hass.async_create_task(self.async_update_ha_state(force_refresh=True))') and returns completed VTODOs ('include_completed=True', :69). A forced pass follows the add at once: async_settle_buy_tasks -> async_request_refresh (coordinator.py:195) -> _async_update_data -> shopping_sync.async_schedule_sweep (coordinator.py:253-254). When the buy task owns device entities, the reload's initial forced sync does the same. todo_list.py:500-509 documents and fixes exactly this predecessor case, and shopping.py lacks the hold. Limited to CalDAV-like lists: HA Shopping List, local_todo and Todoist make the add visible or drop completed items. So medium, not high.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-B09 && python3 sim_shop2.py -> 'shopping (unconfirmed add, old ticked line) complete: [CompleteOp(key=a1:p1, task_id=b2)]'. Upstream check: curl caldav/todo.py | grep -n async_create_task -> 149, 197, 235 (fire-and-forget refresh).

### B09-3 — Editing an existing wear part's 'Last replaced' date never moves its maintenance task

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/reconcile.py:674`
- **Defect:** reconcile_part_tasks anchors a floating task to part.last_replaced only when it creates the task; the update branch ignores last_replaced, so a date set or corrected later is stored on the part but the task schedule stays where it was.
- **Failure scenario:** User adds wear item 'Filter' (6 months) without a date -> task due now. Later they open the part editor and set Last replaced = 2026-08-01 (the field is offered for existing parts, and the editor preview then says 'First one due 2027-02-01'). update_asset -> reconcile_part_tasks: no name/interval/device change, `changed` False; the task keeps next_due = creation time and reads overdue indefinitely, while the appliance page says 'Last replaced 2026-08-01 · a maintenance task tracks the next one'. Same when a counted part is switched back to months: merge_update recomputes from last_completed (None -> due now) and ignores last_replaced. Reproduced with the pure reconciler: after the edit changed=False, last_completed None, next_due unchanged.
- **Suggested fix:** In the update branch, for a floating derived task, re-anchor when `anchored` is later than the task's last_completed (or last_completed is None): set last_completed = anchored and recompute next_due. A completion stamps last_replaced with its own local date, so its local-midnight instant is never later than the completion and real completions are not reverted (keeps test_completing_derived_part_task_survives_reconcile green). Consider recording it as a history entry through the store instead.
- **Verifier fix note:** The suggested re-anchor rule (anchored later than last_completed, or last_completed None, floating role only) is safe for real completions, because a completion stamps last_replaced with its local date and local midnight is never after the completion. Also fire task_updated for it, because reconcile edits are otherwise silent (store.py:1555-1559).
- **Verifier reason:** reconcile.py:674 computes 'anchored', but only the create branch uses it (:707-717). The update branch (:720-783) never reads last_replaced, and nothing else does either: store.update_asset (store.py:944-963) and devices.async_apply_asset_change only reconcile. assets._merge_parts keeps an explicitly sent last_replaced (assets.py:803-804). The part editor offers the field for every wear part, not only new ones (forms.ts:1844-1846), and its preview shows 'First one due' from it (forms.ts:2118-2130, 2224-2226). The guide says the date 'can be set to a past date so that the schedule starts from the real date' (appliances.md:65-66). The _merge_parts docstring mentions only seeding on add, so the edit case is an oversight rather than a stated rule. The workaround is to log a back-dated completion on the task, and nothing is lost, so medium, not high. Not checked: the counted->months conversion variant.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B09/v3.py -> 'created: True None 2026-09-30T10:00:00+00:00' then 'after edit: False None 2026-09-30T10:00:00+00:00' (last_replaced 2026-08-01 set on the existing part; task unchanged).

### B09-4 — A user task manually linked to a consumable is silently left out of export

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:306`
- **Defect:** is_portable_task drops every task whose source has a 'part' key, but reconcile treats a manual link (part.manual = True) as a user-owned task, not a reconciler-owned one.
- **Failure scenario:** User creates 'Descale coffee machine' and links it to consumable 'Descaler tablets' (set_task_consumable writes source.part with manual: True). Export/backup: is_portable_task returns False, so the task and its whole completion history are missing from the document, and the envelope's `skipped` does not count it. Importing into a new install (migration/restore) loses the task with no message. Reproduced: transfer.is_portable_task({'source': {'part': {..., 'manual': True}}}) -> False.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat a manual part link as portable: export the task, and either carry the link as a portable reference re-resolved on import or strip only source.part (and report it in `skipped`). Add a round-trip test for a manually linked task.
- **Verifier fix note:** Import refuses any 'source' (UNPORTABLE_TASK_KEYS, transfer.py:189), so carrying the link needs an import path too. The minimal fix: export the task with only source.part stripped, count the stripped links in the envelope's 'skipped', and add the case to import-export.md and test_transfer_roundtrip.
- **Verifier reason:** transfer.py:305-307 drops any task whose source has a 'part' key (_RECONCILER_SOURCES, :170-177). The rationale there (:166-169, :295-303) is 'regenerated by a reconciler', which is false for a manual link: reconcile.py:125-137 says a manual link is user-owned and invisible to the reconciler. store.set_task_consumable (store.py:498-560) puts it on an arbitrary user task. The envelope's 'skipped' counts only file documents (transfer.py:524-525). The guide lists only tasks 'that another part of Home Keeper owns' (import-export.md:127-128). Neither docs nor tests intend the omission: every reserved-source test uses a non-manual part (test_transfer.py:251-291, test_transfer_properties.py:230-250). The task and its whole history are silently missing from a migration document. The source install still has them, so medium, not high.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B09/v4.py -> 'tasks exported: [] envelope: None'; same task with source None -> 'unlinked exported: 1'.

### B10-1 — Deleting the current line completes the task (vanish switch off) because a uid-bound entry falls back to last cycle's ticked record

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list.py:448`
- **Defect:** When a uid-bound entry's item is gone, resolve_tracked falls back to summary matching and can land on an older completed line with the same text, which plan_sync then reads as a fresh household tick and completes the task, even with vanish_as_completed off.
- **Failure scenario:** local_todo list, profile with two_way on and 'Treat removed items as completed' OFF (what the guide tells local_todo users). Last cycle the household ticked line u1 'Water the plants' (it stays as the record). This cycle Home Keeper added u2 and bound it by uid. The household deletes u2 (skip it, clean up). Next pass: no item has uid u2, the summary fallback returns u1 (completed), completed_since(L1, L1) is False, two_way -> CompleteOp. The task is completed nobody did it: history row, recurrence advances, a part-linked task consumes a spare. Guide says a removed item is re-added instead. With two_way off the same fallback freezes the entry, so the deleted chore is never re-added (guide says it is). Verified with the real planner: CompleteOp with an old record present, AddOp without one.
- **Suggested fix:** When the entry holds a uid and no live item carries it, do not let the summary fallback return a completed item (restrict the fallback to open items, or skip it entirely for uid-bound entries) so the item reads as vanished and the vanish_as_completed / re-add rules apply.
- **Verifier fix note:** For a uid-bound entry whose uid is not found, restrict the summary fallback to OPEN items rather than removing it: a provider that changes uids would otherwise lose its binding and duplicate the line. Fix once in todo_items.resolve_tracked (shared with shopping).
- **Verifier reason:** Same root defect as B09-1 and B11-1: todo_items.resolve_tracked (todo_items.py:79-93) falls back to summary matching even when a uid was captured and no live item carries it, and returns a ticked-off item when no open one exists. In todo_list.plan_sync the stale completed record then reads as a fresh household tick (todo_list.py:494-516) and emits CompleteOp. It matters only with vanish_as_completed OFF (with it on, a removed item is a completion by design) and on lists that keep completed items (local_todo, CalDAV); that is the setting the guide recommends for local_todo (docs/guide/views/todo-sync.md:63-66). One fix in resolve_tracked covers B09-1, B10-1 and B11-1.
- **Repro:** cd tmp-v-B10 && PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 v.py -> vanish=False old record present: complete=[CompleteOp('p1:t1','t1')] add=[] ; vanish=False no old record: complete=[] add=['p1:t1']

### B10-2 — Moving a profile's sync to a new list stalls forever when the old list is gone or cannot delete

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list.py:444`
- **Defect:** One bookkeeping key holds one entry, and an entry still pointing at the old list is carried forward (old list unreadable) or restored (remove failed), so the desired loop never adds, adopts, or tracks the task on the new target.
- **Failure scenario:** (a) User renames the list entity (todo.family -> todo.family_list), re-adds the to-do integration (new entity id), or removes a broken integration, then points the profile at the new list. Every previously synced task: the old entity has no state, _read_lists skips it silently (it is not a target), plan_sync carries the entry forward, and `key in plan.tracked` blocks the add. Those chores never reach the new list; if they are already there (entity rename) they are never adopted, renamed, or re-dated, and a household tick on the new list completes nothing. needs_pass stays True forever, so every task event reads every list. (b) Old list lacks DELETE_TODO_ITEM or its remove keeps failing: _apply restores the old entry each pass, the new line is added but never persisted, a tick on the new list does not complete the task and a fresh duplicate line is added instead. Both verified with the real planner (b with a mirror of _apply).
- **Suggested fix:** When entry.entity_id differs from the profile's current target, keep the old-list cleanup separate from the task's binding: e.g. move the stale entry to a separate orphan key (or orphan list) so the desired loop can add/adopt on the new target, and drop orphans whose entity no longer exists in the state machine and entity registry.
- **Verifier fix note:** Also drop (or orphan-track) an entry whose old list is not a target and has no state at all; carry-forward is right only for the current target or an unavailable (not missing) list.
- **Verifier reason:** lists_to_read includes the old entity from tracked (todo_list.py:634-637); a state-less non-target list is skipped silently (todo_sync_driver.py:203-210); plan_sync carries the entry forward when items is None (todo_list.py:443-446) before the entity_id != target check (:537-540) can run, and the desired loop skips any key in plan.tracked (:594). Nothing else rewrites todo_list_items when a profile target changes (only the sync writes it, store.py:379-393). Result: the profile's tasks never reach, get adopted on, or complete from the new list, and needs_pass stays True. Sub-case (b) is traced in _apply (todo_list_sync.py:305-306 restores the old entry over the new-list entry) but needs a list without DELETE support or a persistently failing remove, which is rare. Medium: silent and permanent, but only after the old list entity disappears.
- **Repro:** cd tmp-v-B10 && PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 v2.py -> pass 0..2: add=[] update=[] tracked={'m1:t1': 'todo.old'} ; tick on new list -> complete: [] add: [] ; needs_pass forever: True

### B10-3 — Two profiles holding one task complete it twice in one pass (skipped occurrence, double spare use)

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list_sync.py:229`
- **Defect:** plan_sync emits one CompleteOp per bookkeeping key, and keys are per profile, so a task ticked (or vanished) on two profiles' lines in the same snapshot yields two CompleteOps, and _complete_tasks calls store.complete_task for each with no dedupe.
- **Failure scenario:** Profiles 'Mom' and 'Dad' both sync 'Take out the trash' (weekly fixed schedule) to their Todoist projects (vanish on). Dad ticks his, Mom ticks hers a minute later to clear it; both land in one Todoist poll, so one pass sees both. Result: two completions. Verified with the real recurrence: next_due goes 06-22 -> 06-29 -> 07-06 (a whole week skipped), two history rows, two task_completed events and notifications, and a part-linked task consumes two spares. Same on one list with two overlapping profiles (duplicate lines by design) or after a restart/reload whose first pass sees both ticks. A second trigger: `desired` is snapshotted before the list reads, so a completion made in Home Keeper while a tick-driven pass is reading/applying is not seen and the CompleteOp completes the task again.
- **Suggested fix:** Dedupe plan.complete by task_id, and in _complete_tasks re-read the live task and skip the completion when its last_completed is already newer than the want snapshot the op was planned from.
- **Verifier fix note:** Dedupe plan.complete by task_id, and add the completed_since(entry.last_completed, want.last_completed) guard to the vanish branch as the ticked branch already has.
- **Verifier reason:** Profiles are independent filters, so two profiles can hold one task; keys are profile_id:task_id (todo_list.py:148-150), so each profile's line yields its own CompleteOp (todo_list.py:471, 515). _complete_tasks calls store.complete_task per op with no dedupe (todo_list_sync.py:229-231), and complete_task has no idempotency gate (store.py:1979-1985 checks only existence, synced-problem, completion_blocked, scan). Both the ticked and the vanished variants double-complete in one pass. Additional path found: the vanish branch (todo_list.py:462-471) has no completed_since check, so a line that vanishes after Home Keeper already recorded the completion completes the task again in a later pass (the ticked branch is guarded). Needs overlapping profiles plus ticks in one snapshot, so medium.
- **Repro:** cd tmp-v-B10 && PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 v3.py -> same pass, both vanished: [CompleteOp('m1:t1','t1'), CompleteOp('m2:t1','t1')] ; both ticked: same two ops ; cross pass, Dad vanished after task already completed: [CompleteOp('m2:t1','t1')]

### B11-1 — Deleting a mirrored shopping line binds to last episode's ticked-off record and completes + restocks the reminder

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_items.py:84`
- **Defect:** When the uid Home Keeper captured for its line is gone (the shopper deleted the line), resolve_tracked falls back to an exact-summary match that may be a *completed* line from an earlier low episode, and plan_sync reads that stale tick as 'bought'.
- **Failure scenario:** Episode 1: 'Buy Anode rod' mirrored, ticked at the shop; the ticked line stays on HA's shopping list (completed items stay until 'Clear completed'). Weeks later the part is low again: a new line is added and its uid 'new' is captured on the next forced pass. The shopper deletes the new line (the case the planner documents as 'not this one, thanks'). The deletion changes the list count, the state listener forces a pass, resolve_tracked finds no uid 'new', falls back to summary, returns the old completed line, and plan_sync emits CompleteOp. Home Keeper completes the reminder and _stamp_buy_restock adds restock_quantity to stock although nothing was bought; home_keeper_task_completed fires with origin shopping_list. Reproduced with the pure planner: tracked={uid:'new'}, items=[{uid:'old',status:'completed'}] -> complete=[CompleteOp('asset1:part1','t2')]; with items=[] the same input keeps the bookkeeping and completes nothing. The window stays open for the whole low spell, because the deleted-item bookkeeping is kept and re-resolved by summary on every 5-minute sweep.
- **Suggested fix:** When a string uid was captured and no live item carries it, fall back only to OPEN summary matches (or treat the entry as deleted). E.g. give resolve_tracked a flag the shopping planner sets, or in plan_sync treat a completed item reached through the summary fallback despite a captured uid as 'deleted by hand'. Add a planner test: tracked uid 'new', items=[completed 'old' with the same summary] -> no CompleteOp, bookkeeping kept.
- **Verifier fix note:** Fix it once in resolve_tracked (a captured uid that is gone falls back to OPEN summary matches only). That also fixes the to-do list sync (B09-1). Add a test in both planners.
- **Verifier reason:** Same root cause as B09-1: todo_items.resolve_tracked (todo_items.py:79-93). When the captured uid is no longer on the list, the summary fallback returns a completed item if no open one exists. The list is read with both statuses (todo_sync_driver.py:217-220), and HA's Shopping List keeps ticked lines until they are cleared. plan_sync then takes the ticked branch (shopping.py:364-369) and emits CompleteOp, not the 'deleted, leave it' branch (shopping.py:351-359). store.complete_task -> _stamp_buy_restock adds restock_quantity to stock (store.py:2025-2026, 2547-2567). Reproduced the whole sequence with the real planner. Medium, not high: it needs the last episode's ticked line with the same text still on the list, and the shopper has to delete (not tick) the new line. The effect is one phantom restock and a completed reminder.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B11/repro_b11_1.py: pass A -> AddOp; pass B binds uid 'new'; pass C (new line deleted, old ticked line present) -> complete=[CompleteOp('asset1:part1', 't2')], tracked={}; control with no old line -> complete=[], bookkeeping kept

### B11-2 — Calendar projects fixed tasks from the anchor grid and ignores next_due: completed-early, skipped, snoozed and due-today occurrences are wrong

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/calendar.py:91`  **Also reported as:** X04-5
- **Defect:** Every other time surface (to-do, next-due sensor, overdue sensor) reads next_due, but the calendar's event property and async_get_events never read next_due for a fixed task, so completing, skipping, snoozing or due-today on a fixed task does not move its calendar event.
- **Failure scenario:** Daily fixed task 'Meds' anchored 10:00. Marked done at 09:00: apply_completion moves next_due to tomorrow 10:00 (the #331 fix), but calendar.event still returns today 10:00-11:00 (simulated: next_due=2026-10-01T10:00, calendar start=2026-09-30T10:00). At 10:00 calendar.home_keeper_upcoming_tasks turns 'on' and HA calendar triggers (event: start) fire for an occurrence that is already done. Skip gives the same result. Snooze of a fixed task to Saturday: the event stays at the original time, and the snoozed time never appears on the calendar. A floating task in the same situation moves correctly. 'Due today' on a fixed task does not show today on the calendar.
- **Suggested fix:** For a fixed task, start the projection at next_due: in _next_start return next_due when it is later than now-EVENT_DURATION (it is on-grid or a deferral), and in _collect_events drop grid occurrences in [now-EVENT_DURATION, next_due) and add an event at next_due when it is off-grid (snooze or due today). Add unit tests for complete-early, skip and snooze.
- **Verifier fix note:** The suggested fix is sound. Keep a next_due in the past (overdue) out of the drop rule, so an overdue occurrence still shows.
- **Verifier reason:** calendar.py:91-117 (event) and 139-153 (async_get_events) project a fixed task from the anchor grid only and never read next_due. The to-do list (todo.py:63-75), the next-due sensor (sensor.py:191) and the overdue sensor all read next_due. Reproduced with the real store, recurrence and calendar: a daily 10:00 task done at 09:00 has next_due 2026-10-01T10:00, but calendar.event is 2026-09-30T10:00, so the entity goes on at 10:00 and calendar triggers fire for an occurrence that is done. Snoozed to 2026-10-03T18:00, the event is still today 10:00 and 18:00 never appears. Skip and due today change only next_due too (store.py:666, 719). DESIGN.md:40 ('fixed = expanded') intends the grid for future occurrences, but no doc says a done, skipped or snoozed occurrence stays on the calendar, and the snooze guide says snooze moves the due date. test_calendar.py builds fixed tasks without next_due. Medium: the calendar is a secondary surface, and it agrees with next_due when a fixed task is done on time.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B11/repro_b11_2.py: 'done 09:00 next_due: 2026-10-01T10:00-04:00 | calendar.event: 2026-09-30T10:00-04:00'; 'snoozed next_due: 2026-10-03T18:00-04:00 | calendar.event: 2026-09-30T10:00-04:00'

### B12-1 — Disabling an entity, its device or its integration deletes the companion task and all its completion history

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_companions.py:413`
- **Defect:** A disabled entity stops matching, so the orphan pass hard-deletes its task (completions, usage baseline, per-task entity ids); re-enabling makes a brand-new empty task, although the same history-loss was explicitly designed away for a disabled companion.
- **Failure scenario:** A Roborock 'parts near end of life' companion has made a filter task with a year of completions. The user disables the vacuum device (or disables the Roborock config entry to troubleshoot; HA sets disabled_by=device/config_entry on every entity). The next registry event runs a pass: _entity_matches returns False for the disabled entity, the orphan pass pops the task and fires home_keeper_task_deleted. On re-enable, a new task with a new id, 0 completions, a restarted usage meter and new per-task entity ids (breaking automations/dashboards on the old ones) is created. The same happens when an integration is removed and re-added: HA restores the entity's registry id from deleted_entities (entity_registry.py: `entity_registry_id = deleted_entity.id`), so the dedupe key (spec_id, entity_registry_id) would have matched, but the task was already deleted by the immediate first pass of the debouncer.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat a disabled (and a just-removed) entity like a paused companion: switch the task off with a marker in source (reuse the pause_spec_tasks 'paused' idea per entity) instead of deleting, and resume it when the same entity_registry_id matches again; only delete after an explicit spec narrowing or after a grace period / when the registry id is gone from deleted_entities.
- **Verifier fix note:** Separate 'registry entry present but disabled' from 'entry gone': in the orphan pass, switch a disabled entity's task off with the per-task paused marker (reuse pause_spec_tasks semantics) and let the existing resume path turn it back on when the same entity_registry_id matches again. A grace period for removed entries is optional and separate.
- **Verifier reason:** HA keeps disabled entries in ent_reg.entities (RegistryEntry.disabled = disabled_by is not None) and a device disable sets disabled_by=DEVICE on each entity (HA entity_registry.py:1906), which fires EVENT_ENTITY_REGISTRY_UPDATED -> _trigger_reconcile. _project_entry passes disabled (declarative_companion_sync.py:92), _entity_matches returns False (declarative_companions.py:413-414), and the orphan pass pops the task (declarative_companions.py:791-796); store fires task_deleted (store.py:1913-1918). No grace period and no orphan protection exist for a present-but-disabled entity; the only pause path is for a disabled spec (pause_spec_tasks, .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md:223). test_expand_skips_disabled_entities (test_declarative_companions.py:538) pins non-match only, not what happens to an existing task. Re-rated medium: real, irreversible history loss, but it needs a user to disable an entity, device or config entry; the removed-and-re-added-integration sub-case is weaker because removal is a genuine orphan.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=pylib python3 tmp-v-B12/probe.py -> DISABLED entity ops: [('deleted', 1)] tasks left: 0 ; RE-ENABLED ops: [('created', False, 0)]

### B12-2 — A name template that renders empty for one entity aborts every reconcile pass, and at the next restart Home Keeper fails to set up

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_companion_sync.py:355`
- **Defect:** _build_task raises TaskValidationError for an empty rendered name; _reconcile_all guards only expand_spec, so the error escapes the pass (starving every later spec) and escapes async_initial_reconcile, which async_setup_entry awaits unguarded.
- **Failure scenario:** An admin saves a companion over update.* with name template `{% if state == 'on' %}Update {{ friendly_name }}{% endif %}` or `{{ attributes.title or '' }}` (the preview samples only 10 entities and shows a blank name without complaint). One matched entity renders ''. store.reconcile_declarative_companion_tasks -> reconcile_declarative_tasks -> _build_task -> models.build_task raises 'missing required field: name'. The exception leaves _reconcile_all: no task of that spec is created, every spec after it in dict order is never reconciled again, and each registry event logs an unhandled task exception. On the next HA restart the same raise comes out of async_initial_reconcile (__init__.py:849), async_setup_entry fails, and Home Keeper (tasks, to-do, calendar, and the panel, registered only at __init__.py:853) is down until .storage/home_keeper is hand-edited. Separately, on the UPDATE path an empty render is written straight into task['name'] with no validation, so an attribute-only template stores empty task names after every restart (states are absent at setup).
- **Suggested fix:** In _reconcile_all wrap the per-spec render+store call in try/except (log once, continue). In reconcile_declarative_tasks skip (and report) a match whose rendered name is blank instead of raising for the whole spec, and on the update path keep the stored name when the render is blank. Optionally validate at save time by rendering the name for all matches.
- **Verifier fix note:** Agree with the suggested fix. Guard per spec in _reconcile_all (try/except around render+store, log once, continue), and in reconcile_declarative_tasks skip a blank-name match on create and keep the stored name on update. A setup-time raise must never escape async_setup_entry.
- **Verifier reason:** _render_one returns '' for a template that renders empty (declarative_companion_sync.py:~275-296, only TemplateError falls back to source). Create path: _build_task -> models.build_task -> _require raises TaskValidationError for '' (models.py:436-437). _reconcile_all guards only expand_spec (declarative_companion_sync.py:344-350); store.reconcile_declarative_companion_tasks at :355-360 is unguarded, so the pass aborts and every later spec is skipped on every pass. At setup, async_initial_reconcile is awaited with no try (__init__.py:849), before panel/websocket registration (:853-857), so async_setup_entry fails (SETUP_ERROR, no retry). Update path writes '' into task['name'] with no validation (declarative_companions.py:853-856). Shipped presets cannot render empty (friendly_name falls back to entry name/original_name/entity_id in template_context.py:152), so a user-authored template is needed; re-rated medium for that reason, though the consequence when it happens is severe.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B12/probe.py -> CREATE path raises: TaskValidationError missing required field: 'name' ; UPDATE path stored name: '' updated

### B12-3 — Setup-time reconcile renders names and notes before target entities have state, renaming tasks on every restart and later forcing an entry reload

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_companion_sync.py:153`
- **Defect:** async_initial_reconcile runs during bootstrap, before most target integrations have written a state, so friendly_name falls back to the registry name, state is None and attributes are empty; the pass persists these degraded renders over the good ones, and the restore on the next registry event changes entity_set_key and reloads the whole entry.
- **Failure scenario:** Firmware update available preset (`Update {{ friendly_name }}`) with 6 ESPHome devices. HA restarts; Home Keeper sets up before ESPHome connects. template_variables finds no state, so friendly_name = original_name 'Firmware': all 6 tasks are renamed 'Update Firmware' (or 'Update update.xxx' when original_name is None), store saved, 6 home_keeper_task_updated fired. Integration-preset notes (`{{ friendly_name }}: {{ state }}`) become 'Filter: None', wiping the arm-time reading that async_refresh_task_notes wrote. The sync never listens to state, so the wrong names stay until an unrelated registry event; that pass restores the names, name is part of entity_set_key, so Home Keeper reloads itself (and may degrade again if some integrations are still loading). A template error while the state is missing (e.g. `| float` on None) persists the raw Jinja source as the task name (_render_one returns source). The same no-state render also happens at runtime for new entities, because the eager debounced pass runs inside the registry 'create' event before the entity's state is written.
- **Suggested fix:** On the update path, do not overwrite stored name/notes when the bound entity has no state (or is unavailable/restored) or when the render failed; re-render such tasks when the entity first reports a state, or defer the re-render part of the initial pass to async_at_started. Never persist the raw template source as a name.
- **Verifier fix note:** Prefer skipping the name/notes rewrite on the update path when the bound entity has no state (keep stored values), rather than deferring the whole initial pass, because the initial pass must still create tasks before platforms forward.
- **Verifier reason:** async_initial_reconcile runs inside async_setup_entry (__init__.py:848-849); template_variables reads hass.states.get (template_context.py:144) and falls back to entry.name/original_name when no state (:152), state None, attributes {}. Existing tasks take the update path, which rewrites owned name/notes unconditionally (declarative_companions.py:841-856). name is in entity_set_key (task_entities.py:87-93), so the next registry-event pass with live states restores the name, store sets entity_set_changed (store.py:1936-1943) and _async_reconcile_and_maybe_reload reloads the entry (declarative_companion_sync.py:440-452). Preset notes quote {{ state }} (declarative_presets.py:459-463) and become 'X: None'. Raw-source-as-name on TemplateError is real (:296) but needs a user template. Effect is renames, extra events and one reload per restart for integrations that load after Home Keeper; no data loss, so medium.

### B14-1 — Startup baseline runs before other integrations load; a missing entity is baselined as not-met, so every restart re-arms dealt-with tasks

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_watcher.py:373`
- **Defect:** async_baseline runs inside async_setup_entry, and a bound entity that is not in the state machine yet is recorded as condition_met=False, so its first real state after a restart reads as a fresh rising edge.
- **Failure scenario:** State task 'water tank low' (binary_sensor from a Zigbee2MQTT/ZHA/Z-Wave/cloud integration, state 'on') armed, user completed it, sensor still 'on' (the documented vacuum case). HA restarts; Home Keeper sets up before MQTT discovery/ZHA radio finishes, so hass.states.get() is None at baseline -> read_sensor_state None -> condition_met=(None=='on')=False. The integration then adds the entity with state 'on' -> _handle_state_change -> _evaluate_edge(met=True, condition_met_prev=False) -> crossed_at=now -> ARM (after for_seconds if set, since the condition stays true). Same for threshold (met = reading is not None and ...) and template (result is True). The task the user dealt with is re-opened and home_keeper_task_triggered fires on every restart. Simulated with the real evaluators: baseline-with-entity-present -> None, baseline-with-entity-missing -> 'arm'.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat a missing/unreadable reading at baseline as 'unknown' (e.g. condition_met=None) and make the first definite reading after an unknown baseline a silent baseline (no crossing) unless the task is in the just-made set; alternatively re-run the edge baseline from async_at_started and treat state.attributes['restored'] placeholders as missing. Add an integration test that creates the bound entity only after Home Keeper setup.
- **Verifier fix note:** Re-running the baseline at EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_STARTED is not enough, because entities can arrive later (MQTT discovery, ConfigEntryNotReady retries). Record an unknown baseline (condition_met None) for a missing or indeterminate reading, and treat the first definite reading after unknown as a silent baseline (except for the just-made set). This also fixes B14-4.
- **Verifier reason:** The baseline runs inline in async_setup_entry (__init__.py:906-907); only the list syncs wait for async_at_started (:910-933). The manifest's after_dependencies are only lovelace and tag, and HA sets stage-2 config entries up concurrently (upstream bootstrap.py STAGE_2_TIMEOUT=300, START fires after stage 2). So MQTT discovery, Z-Wave JS, ZHA, cloud pollers and retrying entries can add their entities after Home Keeper takes its baseline. HA does not restore the state machine as a whole: an entity is absent until its integration adds it. At EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_START the entity registry writes an 'unavailable' placeholder with restored: True for registered entities still absent (upstream entity_registry.py:2826-2836 and 476-503), and EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_STARTED comes later still. A missing entity is baselined as condition_met=False (sensor_watcher.py:373-415). The placeholder reads as indeterminate and keeps False (sensor_tasks.py _evaluate_indeterminate). The real state then reads as a rising edge and arms (sensor_tasks.py:558-566). This breaks the guide's promise 'If the sensor is already in the state when Home Assistant starts, the task does not arm again' (docs/guide/tasks/sensor-tasks.md:135) and the comment at sensor_watcher.py:383-385. The integration test (tests/integration/test_sensor_watcher.py:622-648) only reloads with the entity already present. Only tasks that are dormant while their condition is still true are affected, and completion-blocked companion tasks stay armed. The effect is a visible re-arm and triggered event on restart with no data loss, so medium, not high.
- **Repro:** python3.13 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B14/v14_boot.py (real SensorTaskWatcher; baseline with entity absent -> START placeholder -> real state) -> "state 'on', sensor still on after restart: (([], []), (['t'], []))": no action on the placeholder, ARM when the real 'on' arrives.

### B14-3 — Meter-reset debounce counts evaluation passes, not readings, so any unrelated pass confirms a one-off dip and corrupts the baseline

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_tasks.py:386`
- **Defect:** The 'second consecutive below-baseline reading' is any later pass (another bound entity's state change, the 5-minute tick, any requested refresh), so a transient dip is re-read from the unchanged state and persisted as the new baseline.
- **Failure scenario:** Usage task: odometer baseline 45,000 km, reading 48,000, target 10,000. A cloud hiccup reports 0 for one poll. Pass 1 (the odometer's own state change): reset_candidate=0. Seconds later any other bound sensor changes state (or a panel action requests a refresh): pass 2 reads the same 0 -> ACTION_REBASELINE baseline=0, persisted. The odometer returns to 48,000 -> 48,000-0 >= 10,000 -> ARM: a spurious 'oil change due', and the 3,000 km of real progress is gone. Simulated with the real evaluate_usage: {'action': None, 'reset_candidate': 0.0} -> {'action': 'rebaseline', 'baseline': 0.0} -> {'action': 'arm'}. The more sensor tasks a home has, the shorter the effective debounce window.
- **Suggested fix:** Key the candidate to the bound entity's own updates: store (reading, state.last_updated or last_reported) and confirm the reset only on a later update of that entity, or require the dip to persist for a minimum duration.
- **Verifier fix note:** Key the candidate to the bound entity's own update: store the state's last_updated or last_reported with the candidate, and do not count a pass that sees the same one as a second reading. A minimum duration would also work.
- **Verifier reason:** evaluate_usage confirms a reset on the second below-baseline evaluation (sensor_tasks.py:382-392). The watcher keeps the candidate per task across passes (sensor_watcher.py:586-592), and a pass runs for any bound entity's state change (:494-497), each hold timer (:478) and each coordinator refresh (coordinator.py:215-216, which many services and panel actions request). So an unrelated pass re-reads the unchanged glitch value and re-anchors. The docstring intends 'persist for two ticks'. Reproduced through the real watcher. It needs a below-baseline glitch that is not 'unavailable' (a one-poll 0, or a template sensor with float(0) during an outage). The persisted baseline and progress are lost and a spurious arm follows, but set_task_meter can repair it, so medium, not high.
- **Repro:** python3.13 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B14/v14_3.py -> 'after dip pass: baseline writes []' / "after unrelated pass: baseline writes [('oil', 0.0)] baseline now 0.0" / "odometer back: armed ['oil']".

### B14-4 — Availability mode treats HA's restored 'unavailable' startup placeholder as a real outage, arming and then auto-completing tasks on restart

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_watcher.py:174`
- **Defect:** read_availability_status ignores the restored attribute, so the placeholder the entity registry writes at EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_START for not-yet-loaded entities reads as a live available-to-unavailable transition.
- **Failure scenario:** Availability task (panel defaults: for_seconds 0, clear_on_recover True) on a Z-Wave JS / ZHA / MQTT entity. Restart: at baseline the entity is absent -> 'missing' -> condition_met False. At EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_START the entity registry writes state 'unavailable' with restored: true -> state_changed -> status 'unavailable' -> rising edge -> ARM (home_keeper_task_triggered, notifications). When the integration finishes loading the entity is available -> ACTION_CLEAR -> a real completion with origin sensor_recover is recorded. Every restart adds a fake outage and a fake completion to the history. The docstring promises the opposite: 'missing — ... isn't in the state machine yet (restored on boot / never seen). Indeterminate.'
- **Suggested fix:** Return AVAILABILITY_MISSING when state.attributes.get('restored') is True (HA's marker for a not-yet-loaded entity), matching the docstring and problem_sync's 'not-yet-restored is indeterminate' rule.
- **Verifier fix note:** The suggested restored check is necessary but not sufficient. An MQTT entity created before its availability message arrives writes a real 'unavailable' state with no restored flag, and the same arm and clear follow. The unknown-baseline fix in B14-1 covers both cases. Keep the restored check as well.
- **Verifier reason:** read_availability_status (sensor_watcher.py:171-175) returns 'unavailable' for HA's START placeholder: state 'unavailable' with restored: True, written by the entity registry at EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_START for registered, enabled entities not yet loaded (upstream entity_registry.py:2826-2836, 476-503). The watcher's listener is subscribed during setup (__init__.py:909), before START. A missing entity is baselined as condition_met False (:398-403). So the placeholder is a rising edge and arms at once with the default 0 hold; then the real 'available' state clears the task and records a real completion with origin sensor_recover. That contradicts the docstring's 'a boot-time gap can never fabricate a spurious arm or clear' (:152-157). It needs an entity that is not loaded by START (slow or retrying config entries, late MQTT discovery). Each restart then adds a spurious triggered event and a completion, so medium.
- **Repro:** python3.13 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B14/v14_boot.py -> "availability, device online after restart: ((['t'], []), (['t'], ['t']))": armed on the placeholder, completed when the real state arrives.

### B14-5 — A crossing seen while the task is armed stays unconsumed, so completing the task re-arms it at once

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_tasks.py:558`
- **Defect:** _evaluate_edge records crossed_at on a rising edge even when the task is already armed and never consumes it, so the first pass after the user completes the task arms it again on that old crossing.
- **Failure scenario:** Threshold task 'battery < 20' (no clear_on_recover, the default). Battery reads 19 -> armed. While armed it flaps 21 -> 19 (common for battery percentages): crossed_at=t1 is stored, action None because armed. The user replaces the battery and presses Done; the Zigbee sensor has not reported yet and still reads 19. The next pass (any bound entity change or the 5-minute tick): not armed, crossed_at=t1, held >= for_seconds -> ARM immediately; the task the user just finished reappears. Same after editing an armed task's condition (the fingerprint retires the edge and a standing condition becomes an unconsumed crossing). Simulated: after completion, next pass -> 'arm'. The unit test comment says the opposite: 'An armed task cannot arm again on this crossing' (tests/unit/test_sensor_tasks.py:1120); test_threshold_armed_stays_armed_no_action asserts only the action, not crossed_at.
- **Suggested fix:** When the task is armed, return crossed_at=None (the armed state already represents the condition), so only a fresh false->true crossing after completion arms it again.
- **Verifier fix note:** Return crossed_at None from _evaluate_edge while the task is armed, because the armed state already stands for the condition. Extend the unit test to assert crossed_at.
- **Verifier reason:** _evaluate_edge records crossed_at on a false->true edge and keeps it while the task is armed (sensor_tasks.py:558-568). Nothing consumes it: hold_due_at returns None for an armed task (:464), complete_task does not touch the watcher's in-memory edge, and the condition fingerprint does not change on completion. So after the user completes the task while the condition still holds, the next pass (any bound change or the 5-minute tick) arms it at once. This contradicts the guide ('After a completion the task arms again only after the sensor returns to normal and reaches the state again', sensor-tasks.md:133-134) and the test comment at test_sensor_tasks.py:1120. test_threshold_armed_stays_armed_no_action (:310-317) asserts only the action. It needs a flap across the limit while armed plus a completion before the sensor reports the fix, which is common for jittery battery sensors. The task reappears once and nothing is corrupted, so medium.
- **Repro:** python3.13 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B14/v14_5.py -> 'flap down while armed: None 2026-09-01 14:00:00+00:00' / 'next pass after Done: arm' / 'control (no flap): None'. The finder's sim.py shows the same.

### B14-6 — Every state change of any bound entity re-evaluates every sensor task, rendering every template on the event loop (quadratic with the shipped last_seen preset)

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_watcher.py:494`
- **Defect:** _handle_state_change starts a full async_evaluate pass (uncoalesced) per event, and the pass renders the Jinja template and rebuilds the registry projection for every template-mode task, although only the changed entity's tasks can have changed.
- **Failure scenario:** The shipped device_stopped_reporting preset matches every sensor.*_last_seen entity in template mode; Zigbee2MQTT updates last_seen on every device message. With 150 devices sending ~20 messages/s in total, the watcher runs 20 full passes/s x 150 template renders (plus template_variables copying each entity's attributes and device/area lookups) = ~3,000 renders/s on the event loop, growing with the square of the device count; with a larger mesh the loop saturates and all of HA lags. A mass arm/clear in one pass also writes the whole store once per task (Store default serializes on the loop).
- **Suggested fix:** Keep an entity_id -> task ids index and, on a state change, evaluate only the tasks bound to event.data['entity_id'] (the periodic tick keeps the full pass for time-based templates); coalesce bursts with a Debouncer.
- **Verifier fix note:** Index entity_id -> task ids and, on a state change, evaluate only the changed entity's tasks. Keep the full pass for the periodic tick and hold timers, because a template can depend on now(). Folding bursts into one pass (see B14-2) also cuts the pass count.
- **Verifier reason:** The design is as described: _handle_state_change ignores the event and starts a full pass (sensor_watcher.py:494-497). The pass visits every enabled sensor task (:513-567), and for each template task it builds registry, device and area variables with a copy of the attributes (template_context.py:126-174) and renders on the event loop (sensor_watcher.py:243-245). The compiled template is cached; the variables and the render are not. With eager start (upstream core.py:788-835) the pass runs synchronously inside the state write of the entity that changed. The device_stopped_reporting preset binds every *_last_seen entity in template mode (declarative_presets.py:155, 178), so the cost grows as bound tasks x bound-entity update rate. The finder's magnitude (about 3,000 renders/s, a saturated loop) is an estimate that I did not measure. It matters only for large preset-driven installs, so medium.

### B15-1 — Companion-name entity prefix makes identical entity names when one declarative companion matches several entities on one device (shipped presets do this)

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/task_entities.py:62`
- **Defect:** entity_name_prefix returns only the companion name for every declarative task, so all tasks one companion opens on the same device get the same Next due / Overdue / Mark done names, which is the collision HomeKeeperTaskEntity exists to prevent.
- **Failure scenario:** Enable the shipped Brother printer preset (percent_low, keys black/cyan/magenta/yellow_ink_remaining) or the Dyson preset (hepa_filter_life + carbon_filter_life). One task per matched entity is created, all with device_id = the printer/purifier device and managed_by.display_name = 'Brother printer: parts and supplies running low'. On the device page there are now 4 sensors all named '<device> Brother printer: parts and supplies running low: Next due' and 4 identical '...: Overdue' binary sensors; Home Assistant gives them entity_ids ..._next_due, ..._next_due_2, _3, _4 in store order. Nobody can tell which cartridge an entity is for from its name or id, and the overdue binary sensor carries no task name attribute. The preset author explicitly switched to _NAME_BY_ENTITY task names (declarative_presets.py:454-458, 542-545) so these tasks are distinguishable, but the device-page prefix throws that name away.
- **Suggested fix:** Use the companion name only when it is unique among the enabled tasks on that device; otherwise fall back to (or append) the task name with the device name stripped, e.g. compute prefixes per device in the platform setup and disambiguate collisions. Add a unit test with two companion tasks on one device.
- **Verifier fix note:** Simplest correct fallback: when another enabled task of the same companion shares the device, use the task name with the device name stripped (the _NAME_BY_ENTITY name is unique). entity_set_key already holds name and companion name, and a new companion task reloads the entry, so names recompute. Entity_ids already registered keep their _2/_3 suffixes; the fix changes friendly names only.
- **Verifier reason:** task_entities.py:62-64 returns the companion name for every declarative task, and entity.py:59-64 builds the whole prefix from that label. declarative_companions.py:724 gives each matched entity's task that entity's device_id, and :627 stamps the same spec name as managed_by.display_name on all of them. Presets group duties by shape (declarative_presets.py:527-547), so the Brother printer (4 ink keys + maintenance box, all percent_low) and Dyson (hepa + carbon) presets open several tasks on one device. strings.json names are '{task_name}Next due' / '{task_name}Overdue' / '{task_name}Mark done', so the names are identical and HA suffixes the entity_ids _2, _3 in store order. The preset author switched the task name to _NAME_BY_ENTITY for exactly this case (declarative_presets.py:452-458, 544), and the prefix throws it away. Only the next-due sensor carries a task_name attribute (sensor.py:199-200); the overdue binary sensor has task_id/due_soon/next_due only (binary_sensor.py:112-118). No unit test covers two companion tasks on one device (tests/unit/test_task_entities.py:71-77). Medium: every multi-key preset hits it on first enable, and the ambiguous entity_ids are kept by the registry after any later fix.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 tmp-v-B15/b15_1.py -> 'Replace ink or toner: Printer black_ink_remaining' -> prefix 'Brother printer: parts and supplies running low'; 'Replace ink or toner: Printer cyan_ink_remaining' -> prefix 'Brother printer: parts and supplies running low' (built with the real declarative_companions._build_task)

### B15-3 — Stock can pass the spares number's max (MAX_INTERVAL) through adjust/restock, after which the appliance cannot be edited at all

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/number.py:73`
- **Defect:** The number entity and the asset normalizer cap stock at 10,000, but assets.adjust_part_stock (service, websocket, buy-task restock) has no upper clamp, so stock goes above the cap and every later update_asset re-validates the stored part and fails.
- **Failure scenario:** A part measured in ml (the seeded 'Descaling solution' style) at stock 9,500 with restock_quantity 1,000: completing its Buy reminder or calling home_keeper.adjust_part_stock delta=1000 sets stock to 10,500. The spares number now reports 10,500 above its own native_max_value 10,000. Then renaming the appliance (or any update_asset / panel save) fails: merge_update copies existing parts into the candidate and normalize_fields -> _normalize_stock raises 'stock must be <= 10000'. Reproduced with the pure module: adjust_part_stock(part, 1000) -> 10500; merge_update(asset, {'name': 'Pool pump'}) -> AssetValidationError.
- **Suggested fix:** Clamp assets.adjust_part_stock (and consume/restock) to MAX_INTERVAL, or reject the adjustment with a translated error, so the store can never hold a value its own validator and the number entity refuse; add a unit test.
- **Verifier fix note:** Clamp in assets.adjust_part_stock (and the buy-restock path) to MAX_INTERVAL, or raise a translated error there, so the store cannot hold a value its own validator refuses; add max=MAX_INTERVAL to the panel stepper input as well.
- **Verifier reason:** assets.adjust_part_stock clamps only at zero (assets.py:1367-1369). The service and websocket accept any delta (__init__.py:569, websocket_api.py:873 vol.Coerce(float)), and the panel stock stepper has no max either (panel-detail.ts:924-960, utils.ts:473-476 snapStock), so typing a large value in the panel is enough. merge_update copies the stored parts into normalize_fields (assets.py:1595, 1605), and _normalize_stock raises above MAX_INTERVAL (assets.py:457-458), so every later appliance edit fails, including a rename. Worse than reported: the export carries the value and plan_import reports it as an 'error' problem (transfer.py:1353-1355), which blocks the whole import (transfer.py:208-209), so a backup holding it cannot be restored as is. Recoverable by lowering the stock, and the message names the field. Needs a unit-measured part near 10,000.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 tmp-v-B15/b15_3.py -> adjust +1000 -> none stock 10500; merge_update {'name': 'Kettle 2'} FAILED: AssetValidationError stock must be <= 10000; parts edit FAILED; import problems: [('error', 'appliances[0]', 'stock must be <= 10000')]

### B16-1 — Auto triggers fire for crossings of tasks outside the notification's profile

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:311`  **Also reported as:** B18-1
- **Defect:** async_send_auto receives only the set of kinds that fired, so every notification with a matching auto flag re-sends its whole queue whenever ANY task in the home crosses overdue/due-soon, not only a task in its own profile.
- **Failure scenario:** Two per-person walk notifications with auto.overdue on: 'Mom - kitchen' (profile area=kitchen) and 'Dad - garage' (area=garage). 'Clean fridge' in the kitchen has sat overdue for a week. A garage task becomes overdue -> fired_kinds={'overdue'} -> both notifications send. Mom's phone re-buzzes with the week-old 'Clean fridge' card (same tag replaces it, and no alert_once is sent, so Android re-alerts) although nothing in her profile crossed. This repeats on every crossing of every task in the house. The user guide promises 'a notification is sent when a task in the profile becomes overdue or due soon'.
- **Suggested fix:** Pass the fired (kind, task_id) pairs to async_send_auto, run effective_filter_tasks + profiles.matches_filter on the crossed tasks per notification, and send only when at least one crossed task matches that notification's profile filter.
- **Verifier fix note:** Carry (kind, task_id) out of the coordinator (the fired payloads already hold task_id), enrich the crossed tasks with effective_filter_tasks, and send a notification only when a crossed task passes profiles.matches_filter for its profile.
- **Verifier reason:** coordinator.py:228-234 reduces the fired events to a set of kinds, and notifier.async_send_auto (notifier.py:311-326) sends every notification whose auto flag matches a kind. Nothing checks that the task that crossed is in that notification's profile. _send (notifier.py:232-236) then sends the head of the profile's whole queue whenever it is non-empty. This goes against the design (docs/ACTIONABLE_NOTIFICATIONS_PLAN.md:204-207: 'matches the fired kind **and** whose filter matches the task') and the user guide (docs/guide/views/notifications.md:33-35, 114-116: 'when a task in the profile becomes overdue or due soon'). The payload sets no alert_once (only a tag, notifications.py:512-564), so each re-send re-alerts. No test covers async_send_auto (tests only stub it, test_coordinator_purge.py:58-61). Severity lowered: repeated pushes of an unchanged head task are noise, with no data harm, and only notifications with auto on and a non-empty queue are affected.

### B16-2 — Snooze and Skip taps skip the freshness-token gate (stale taps corrupt the schedule)

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:487`  **Also reported as:** X04-6
- **Defect:** Only the complete branch checks is_current_action; a stale Snooze moves next_due EARLIER after the task was already done, and a stale Skip advances a fixed task one more occurrence.
- **Failure scenario:** Fixed monthly 'Change HVAC filter', next_due Sep 1, walk card on the phone. User completes it from the to-do list or panel -> next_due Oct 1; the card stays on the phone (walk-advance runs only on a notification tap). Later the user taps 'Snooze 24h' to clear it -> snooze_task sets next_due = now+24h, so a task done for the month is due again tomorrow. Or taps 'Skip' -> skip_occurrence advances past max(now, Oct 1) to Nov 1, silently losing October and logging a bogus skip. Same with two cards for one task (the case test_two_notifications_for_one_task_complete_it_once guards only for Mark done). The button carries the exact token that would reject this.
- **Suggested fix:** Fetch the task and apply notifications.is_current_action(task, due_token, tokenless_ok=...) before snooze_task and skip_task too, keeping the check and the mutation free of yield points as in the complete branch.
- **Verifier fix note:** Do not reuse the complete branch's tokenless_ok=is_overdue: a due_soon or all walk card offers Snooze and Skip on a task that is not yet overdue, so a legacy 4-field action would be refused. Use tokenless_ok=True (or overdue-or-due-soon) for these 2 verbs. Keep get_task, the check and the mutation free of awaits: snooze_task assigns before its first await, and skip_task assigns self._tasks[task_id] before its first await (store.py:783-784).
- **Verifier reason:** Yes, the freshness-token check really is missing for Snooze and Skip. notifications.is_current_action is called only in the ACTION_COMPLETE branch (notifier.py:447-486).
