The Snooze branch (notifier.py:487-505) and the Skip branch (notifier.py:506-509) check only _verb_allowed and then mutate. store.snooze_task sets next_due = until unconditionally (store.py:666) and rejects only a dormant task. store.skip_task rejects only problem-sensor and completion-blocked tasks, and recurrence.skip_occurrence advances a fixed task from max(now, next_due) (recurrence.py:582-586, 602-603). is_current_action's own docstring (notifications.py:164-169) says a tap after the task was 'completed, snoozed, skipped or rescheduled ... is stale and must not act on the task again'. docs/MINOR_BUGS_FIX_PLAN.md:63-64 left snooze/skip out on purpose as 'idempotent-ish ... worth a follow-up note', so this is a known gap and not a design decision. A walk card stays on the phone after a completion from another surface: no clear or replace is sent then, because the only sends are auto, walk-advance on a tap, and the service. Severity lowered: it needs a stale walk card and a Snooze or Skip tap, and the result is a visible, recoverable schedule shift, not lost records.
- **Repro:** python3 repro_b16_2.py (fixed MONTHLY task due 2026-09-01, completed 09-03) -> 'card token: 2026-09-01T00:00:00+00:00' / 'after completion next_due: 2026-10-01T00:00:00+00:00' / 'is_current_action(stale token): False' / 'stale snooze -> next_due: 2026-09-04T14:00:00+00:00 (earlier than 2026-10-01...)' / 'stale skip -> next_due: 2026-11-01T00:00:00+00:00 | skips logged: 1'

### B16-3 — Injected Snooze on a completion-blocked task is a dead button when allow_snooze is off

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:494`
- **Defect:** actions_for deliberately injects Snooze on a completion-blocked task even with allow_snooze off (#248), but the tap handler rejects every snooze while the switch is off and returns before the walk-advance, so the walk is stuck on that task forever.
- **Failure scenario:** allow_snooze = False, sync_problem_sensors on, a walk notification covering a problem-sensor mirror task. The mirror is the most overdue task, so it is the head of every send; its card shows only 'Snooze' (+Open) because Done/Skip are withheld. The user taps Snooze -> _verb_allowed returns False -> `return`: nothing snoozes, no refresh, no walk-advance. Every later auto or manual send shows the same task again, and the tasks behind it are never reached -- the exact outcome test_a_blocked_task_keeps_snooze_even_when_the_switch_is_off says the injection exists to prevent. The comment 'by this point the tap has already advanced it' is wrong: the advance at :529-537 is skipped by the return.
- **Suggested fix:** In the snooze branch, allow the tap when the task is completion-blocked (notifications.is_completion_blocked(task)), mirroring the actions_for exception; keep rejecting it for ordinary tasks.
- **Verifier fix note:** Allow the snooze tap when notifications.is_completion_blocked(task) is true, and correct the comment.
- **Verifier reason:** actions_for injects Snooze on a completion-blocked task even with allow_snooze off (notifications.py:429-436, pinned by tests/unit/test_notifications.py:380-389: ['snooze','open']). The tap handler returns at notifier.py:494-495 when allow_snooze is off, before snooze_task and before the walk-advance at notifier.py:529-546. The comment at notifier.py:491-493 ('by this point the tap has already advanced it') is wrong: the walk advances only after a successful action. The settings help text promises 'A problem-sensor task always offers Snooze, because nothing else can move such a reminder on' (locales/en.json:577), and the button is dead. The walk stays stuck on the mirror task while the problem lasts. It needs allow_snooze off plus problem-sensor sync plus a walk.

### B16-5 — due_soon/all walks re-send the task just acted on, with fresh buttons that complete it again

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:529`
- **Defect:** The walk-advance rebuilds the queue from the profile filter without excluding the tapped task; under status due_soon (or all) a task snoozed <=72h or a task with an interval <=3 days is still in the queue and is re-sent at the head.
- **Failure scenario:** Walk on a due_soon profile; daily task 'Feed the cat' (due 08:00) is the only task. 08:30 tap 'Mark done' -> next_due tomorrow 08:30, still inside the 3-day window -> walk-advance re-sends 'Feed the cat - Due soon.' with a new token. The user thinks the tap failed and taps 'Mark done' again -> the fresh token matches, so the task completes a second time and jumps to the day after tomorrow (tomorrow's feeding is lost). 'Snooze 24h' likewise brings the same card straight back. The walk never reaches 'All caught up'. Verified with the pure modules: due_queue still returns the task after apply_completion and after a 24h snooze.
- **Suggested fix:** Pass the actioned task_id into the walk-advance send and drop it from the queue for that advance (or only walk tasks not acted on since the walk started).
- **Verifier reason:** The walk-advance (notifier.py:532-537) rebuilds the queue from the profile filter, and under status due_soon or all a task whose new next_due is inside DUE_SOON_WINDOW stays in it (profiles.py:277-280). So the task just completed is sent again with a fresh token that a second tap will accept. For a fixed daily task that second completion skips the next occurrence. For a floating task it only adds a duplicate completion record. The walk never reaches 'All caught up', although the guide says 'Each Mark done, Snooze, or Skip then sends the next due task'. It needs a due_soon or all walk and short-interval tasks, or a snooze of 72 h or less.
- **Repro:** python3 repro_b16_5.py -> 'floating after 1st complete next_due 2026-09-02T08:30 | still in due_soon walk queue: [Feed the cat]'; 'fixed after 1st complete next_due 2026-09-02T00:00 | still in due_soon walk queue: [Feed the cat]'; 'fixed after 2nd complete (fresh-token tap) next_due 2026-09-03T00:00:00+00:00 | completions: 2'

### B18-2 — Renaming a problem sensor's entity_id deletes and recreates its mirror, losing labels, history and the saved note

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/problem_tasks.py:164`  **Also reported as:** X10-2
- **Defect:** Mirrors are keyed only by the current entity_id and the registry-update handler ignores old_entity_id, so an entity_id rename is treated as delete-old plus create-new.
- **Failure scenario:** User renames binary_sensor.node_5_problem to binary_sensor.sump_pump_problem. The entity-registry 'update' event triggers a reconcile: the old mirror is an orphan and is deleted (task_deleted), a new mirror with a new task id is built with no labels, an empty completions log and notes='' (the note is stored under the old entity_id in _problem_notes and is never re-hydrated or pruned), then the entry reloads. A Profile/notification that selected the mirror by label (the integration test's own pattern) silently stops matching, automations keyed on the task_id break, and the problem history is gone.
- **Suggested fix:** In _handle_registry_update, when action=='update' and 'old_entity_id' is present, rewrite the mirror's source.problem_sensor.entity_id and move the _problem_notes key before reconciling (or match mirrors by registry unique id).
- **Verifier reason:** Mirrors are keyed only by the current entity_id (problem_tasks.py:157-168): after a rename the old mirror is an orphan and is deleted, and a new one is built with a new id, no labels, an empty history and notes from notes_by_entity.get(new_id) (problem_tasks.py:171-185). _handle_registry_update (problem_sync.py:184-196) reads only event.data['entity_id']; nothing in the component reads old_entity_id. The note stays under the old key (store.py:476-486). The declarative path avoids this by keying on entity_registry_id (declarative_companions.py:729). Home Assistant's device rename dialog offers to rename the entity IDs as well, so this is ordinary housekeeping. Labels on a mirror are user-editable (only name/recurrence_type/device_id/area_id are locked, problem_tasks.py:39), so a Profile that routes the mirror by label silently stops matching.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 tmp-v-B18/b18_problem.py -> B18-2 seed ops: ['cleared'] | completions: 1; B18-2 rename ops: ['deleted', 'created'] | same id: False | labels: [] | notes: '' | completions: 0

### B19-1 — Unbounded one_off_retention_days overflows datetime math and fails every coordinator refresh, including setup

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/options.py:147`  **Also reported as:** B21-6
- **Defect:** _coerce_days clamps only the low end, so a retention of about 2.92 million days or more is stored, and the coordinator's purge then raises OverflowError on every refresh. The first refresh at setup fails the same way, so the entry sits in SETUP_RETRY.
- **Failure scenario:** An admin types 9999999 in Settings > General > One-off retention (the panel field has min 0 and no max), or an automation calls home_keeper.set_options with one_off_retention_days: 9999999 (the schema is Coerce(int) + Range(min=0) with no max). async_set_options stores it and reloads. When a completed one-off exists (common), the coordinator's _async_update_data -> _purge_expired_one_offs -> recurrence.one_off_expired computes completed + timedelta(days=9999999), which raises 'OverflowError: date value out of range'. async_config_entry_first_refresh converts that into ConfigEntryNotReady, so Home Keeper never finishes setup. Every entity goes unavailable, the panel answers integration_not_loaded, and set_options no longer exists because services are removed on unload. The only way out is the Configure dialog, whose NumberSelector (max 3650) rejects the stored default until the user edits it. The same thing happens later with no reload if the first one-off is completed after the value is saved. Checked: 2,920,000 days from 2026 overflows, and 1e9 fails in timedelta itself.
- **Suggested fix:** Clamp in the single coercion point: `_coerce_days` returns min(days, 3650) (a MAX_ONE_OFF_RETENTION_DAYS constant shared with config_flow and services.yaml). Add vol.Range(min=0, max=MAX) to SET_OPTIONS_SCHEMA and max to the panel's selNumber. Optionally, also guard one_off_expired against OverflowError (treat it as not expired).
- **Verifier fix note:** Clamp in _coerce_days (the one coercion point every read and write shares), so an already-stored huge value also reads back clamped and the entry recovers on the next load without user action.
- **Verifier reason:** Every input path checked. Bounded: only the options flow (config_flow.py:92-99, NumberSelector min 0 max 3650). Unbounded: the set_options service (__init__.py:764-766, Coerce(int)+Range(min=0); the services.yaml max 3650 is a UI selector hint only), the panel websocket home_keeper/set_options (websocket_api.py:1206-1207, `options: dict`, no per-key schema), and the panel General card (forms.ts:1543 selNumber(0), no max). All reach options._coerce_days (147-153), which clamps only the low end, and async_set_options stores and reloads (options.py:323-340). The purge is the first line of _async_update_data (coordinator.py:204) with no guard, and recurrence.one_off_expired (911) adds timedelta(days=N) to last_completed: >= ~2.92M days overflows the year-9999 limit and >= 1e9 overflows timedelta itself. The first refresh (__init__.py:829) runs before services are registered (866), and unload removed them (2007-2008), so a reload leaves the entry in SETUP_RETRY with no set_options service and the panel answering integration_not_loaded; the Configure dialog still recovers it once the value is edited under 3650. Admin-only and needs an absurd value (~8000 years), but the outcome is a full outage, so medium.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B19 && python3 r1_retention.py -> 2900000 -> one_off_expired False; 2920000 -> RAISES OverflowError: date value out of range; '9999999' -> stored 9999999 -> RAISES OverflowError; 1e9 -> stored 1000000000 -> RAISES OverflowError: days=1000000000; must have magnitude <= 999999999

### B20-1 — Diagnostics leak completion and skip notes: TO_REDACT names 'notes' but history entries store free text under 'note'

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/diagnostics.py:28`  **Also reported as:** B20-2, B21-1
- **Defect:** Every completion and skip note in the store is written verbatim into the diagnostics dump, although the module promises to redact free-form notes before the file is posted publicly.
- **Failure scenario:** A user records a completion with note 'Paid Bob 120 cash, gate code 4471' (or a skip with 'Away at 12 Elm St until Sunday'), then downloads config-entry or device diagnostics to attach to a GitHub issue. async_redact_data matches keys exactly, so 'note' is not matched by 'notes'; the dump carries the note text in clear under tasks[].completions[].note and tasks[].skips[].note while 'who' next to it is redacted. Reproduced with a faithful copy of HA's redaction logic (scratchpad tmp-B20/redact_demo.py).
- **Suggested fix:** Add 'note' to TO_REDACT (keep 'notes'). Add a unit test that builds a task with models.build_task, records a completion and a skip with note/who/photo, runs the diagnostics functions, and asserts no free-text value survives.
- **Verifier fix note:** Add 'note' to TO_REDACT (keep 'notes'). The test should cover archived task_history on an asset as well as live completions and skips.
- **Verifier reason:** TO_REDACT = {'serial_number', 'notes', 'who', 'photo'} (diagnostics.py:28). HA's async_redact_data (core diagnostics/util.py, fetched from dev) recurses into every Mapping and list and redacts only on an exact 'key in to_redact' match, so 'note' is not matched by 'notes'. Completion and skip entries store free text under 'note' (models.py:108-110 via normalize_completion_metadata; recurrence.py:593-595 for skips), and archived history copies the same entries onto assets (assets.py:1821-1837 build_archived_history -> task_history[].completions[].note). Result: task-level notes and who/photo are redacted while every completion/skip note, including archived ones on appliances, is dumped in clear, contrary to the module docstring (diagnostics.py:4-8). No test under tests/ mentions diagnostics. Downgraded from high: the user generates and chooses to post the file, and most completion notes are mundane.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=pydeps python3 tmp-v-B20/redact_check.py (HA redact body copied verbatim) -> completion: {'note': 'Paid Bob 120 cash, gate code 4471', 'who': '**REDACTED**', 'cost': 120.0, 'photo': '**REDACTED**'}; skip: {'note': 'Away at 12 Elm St until Sunday', 'who': '**REDACTED**'}; asset.task_history[0].completions[0].note: Paid Bob 120 cash, gate code 4471

### F02-2 — Clearing the optional Attribute or Hold box on an existing sensor task is silently ignored

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:1079`
- **Defect:** Home Assistant's text and number selectors emit undefined for an emptied non-required box, and buildTaskPayload then falls back with ?? to the stored nested sensor binding, so the old attribute or hold is re-sent.
- **Failure scenario:** A state/threshold task is bound to sensor.car attribute 'battery_level' with a 600 s hold. The user edits it, empties 'Attribute (optional)' so it reads the entity state, and empties 'Hold (seconds)', then saves. ha-selector-text converts '' to undefined when !required (and ha-selector-number emits undefined for ''); ha-form spreads {sensor_attribute: undefined}; the panel merges it into the edit state that still holds the loaded `sensor` object; `sd.sensor_attribute ?? task.sensor?.attribute` yields 'battery_level' and `sd.sensor_for ?? task.sensor?.for_seconds` yields 600. The saved binding still reads the attribute with the hold, while the form showed both empty. isBinarySensorBinding and sensorLive also keep reading the stale attribute, so the on/off picker never comes back.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat a flat key that is present in the edit state as authoritative: `const attrRaw = 'sensor_attribute' in sd ? sd.sensor_attribute : task.sensor?.attribute;` (same for sensor_for, and in isBinarySensorBinding/sensorLive/taskFormData). Add a test with the key present and undefined next to a nested binding.
- **Verifier fix note:** The same fallback also blocks a clear of sensor_unit (forms.ts:868, 1083). Apply the 'key in sd' rule to every optional flat sensor key in buildTaskPayload, taskFormData (forms.ts:867-869), isBinarySensorBinding (forms.ts:330) and sensorLive (forms.ts:1459), so the four readers agree.
- **Verifier reason:** HA ha-selector-text changes '' to undefined when the field is not required (fetched source tmp-F02/text.ts:120-125). ha-number emits undefined for an empty box (tmp-F02/number.ts:156-157). ha-form then spreads {name: undefined} into its data (tmp-F02/form.ts:281-288), and the panel merges that into the edit state, which still holds the nested sensor from _openEdit (panel.ts:879). buildTaskPayload then reads sd.sensor_attribute ?? task.sensor?.attribute (forms.ts:1076-1080) and sd.sensor_for ?? task.sensor?.for_seconds (forms.ts:1125), so the stored values come back. The form shows both boxes empty, and the save keeps the old attribute and hold. The hold has a workaround (type 0). The attribute has none.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types tmp-v-F02/repro2.mjs (edit state with sensor_attribute: undefined, sensor_for: undefined) -> 'F02-2 payload.sensor = {"entity_id":"sensor.car","mode":"threshold","attribute":"battery_level","comparison":"<=","value":20,"for_seconds":600}'

### F04-1 — Rendered notes lose single line breaks: ha-markdown is emitted without `breaks`

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/markdown.ts:99`
- **Defect:** markdownBlock emits <ha-markdown> without the `breaks` flag, so marked (CommonMark) folds every single newline into a space, while the escaped fallback keeps them with pre-wrap.
- **Failure scenario:** A user stores a plain multi-line note such as 'Filter: 16x25x1\nBrand: Filtrete\nBuy at: Costco'. On a cold deep-link (fallback path) it shows as 3 lines. After ha-markdown registers (any dashboard visit, and always inside the card) the same note renders as one run-on paragraph 'Filter: 16x25x1 Brand: Filtrete Buy at: Costco' on the task/appliance/part detail, history rows and the card. The author is not warned: createPreview hides the preview because looksLikeMarkdown() is false for plain prose (markdown.ts:182), and the comment at markdown.ts:111-113 assumes plain prose 'renders identically'. HA's own Markdown card passes `breaks` for the same kind of user text.
- **Suggested fix:** Emit `<ha-markdown breaks class=...>` (boolean attribute maps to the `breaks` property), matching HA's markdown card and the fallback; add a test that the emitted element carries it.
- **Verifier fix note:** Adding the breaks attribute does not break the existing markdown.test.js assertions. Add a test that the emitted tag carries breaks.
- **Verifier reason:** markdown.ts:99 emits <ha-markdown class data-md> without breaks, and nothing in frontend/src sets breaks (grep). HA's ha-markdown defaults breaks=false, so marked folds single newlines into spaces. No panel style gives .hk-md a pre-wrap: only .hk-md-plain has it (panel-styles.ts:1551-1552), so a multi-line plain note loses its line breaks once ha-markdown is registered. The comment at markdown.ts:111-113 ('plain prose renders identically') and card.ts:321-322 both show the line breaks are meant to survive. Tests only check toContain('<ha-markdown') (markdown.test.js:70), so nothing pins either behavior. Medium: it affects every multi-line note on detail pages, history and the card, but no data is lost.

### F05-1 — Card filter 'soon' (Due soon) never shows a task on its due day

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card-filter.ts:505`
- **Defect:** matchesFilter('soon') compares against statusBucket with the card default today:true, so every task due later today is bucketed 'today' and dropped from a Due soon card.
- **Failure scenario:** Card config filter: soon. A floating task completed a month ago at 18:00 is due today 18:00. At 09:00 statusBucket returns 'today' (not 'soon'), so the row is filtered out; at 18:00 it becomes 'overdue', still filtered out. The task is on the card the day before its due day and vanishes on the day it is due. The panel's own Due soon pill includes it (scopeMatches uses PANEL_BUCKETS, today:false), so the two surfaces disagree.
- **Suggested fix:** Use statusBucket(task, now, { today: false }) === 'soon' in matchesFilter (the section split stays for group_by: status), and add a filterTasks test with a task due later today.
- **Verifier fix note:** Suggested fix is right: statusBucket(task, now, { today: false }) === 'soon'. With group_by: status the card still splits a Today section, which is fine. Add a filterTasks test with a task due later today.
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced. card-filter.ts:504-505 matchesFilter('soon') calls statusBucket(task, now) with the card default today:true (card-filter.ts:150), and card-filter.ts:186 puts any task due before midnight in 'today', so a Due soon card drops a task on its due day from 00:00 until its due time (then it is 'overdue'). The panel pill uses PANEL_BUCKETS {today:false} (panel-types.ts:161, panel-controls.ts:299) and keeps it. This contradicts SOON_DAYS' own doc (card-filter.ts:106 'due within this many days (and not overdue)') and the rule stated at card-filter.ts:501-502 that the card filter and the panel pill 'must not disagree'. Editor label is plain 'Due soon' (card.ts:141). Only tasks whose next_due carries a time later than now are affected (typical for floating tasks; a 00:00 due date is overdue on both surfaces). No filterTasks test uses filter 'soon' (test/card-filter.test.js). Not high: a wrong list on an opt-in card filter, no data effect.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types f1.mjs (copy of src with i18n stub, now=09:00, task due 18:00 today): card bucket=today, panel bucket=soon; filter 'soon' at 09:00 -> ['due_tomorrow_18h'] (today's task missing); same filter the day before -> ['due_today_18h','due_tomorrow_18h']

### F05-3 — Card Create button has no in-flight guard; a second tap during a device-linked add creates a duplicate task

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:710`  **Also reported as:** X12-4, F01-2
- **Defect:** _submitForm neither disables Create nor ignores re-entry, and add_task for a device-linked task waits for a full entry reload before it replies.
- **Failure scenario:** A user adds 'Replace filter' with a device selected from the card's + form and taps Create. ws_add_task awaits hass.config_entries.async_reload before send_result (1-3 s). During the reload the card's signal-driven refresh gets not_loaded and the body shows 'Couldn't load tasks' while the form is still open with a live Create button. The user taps Create again: a second identical task (and a second reload) is created.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep a _submitting flag: return early while set, disable the Create button during the call, and clear it in finally.
- **Verifier reason:** Same defect as X12-4 (whose verified reason already cites card.ts:720-723). card.ts:710-727 _submitForm has no busy flag and card.ts:1244 leaves Create live; websocket_api.py:283-289 awaits hass.config_entries.async_reload before send_result for a device-linked task, so the window is the whole reload and a second tap runs api.addTask again, giving a duplicate task plus a second reload.

### F06-1 — Clear on recover = off does not round-trip: the edit dialog shows it on, and any trigger edit saves it on

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-declarative.ts:880`
- **Defect:** The backend stores clear_on_recover only when true, and the dialog reads a missing key as true, so a companion saved with auto-clear off reopens ticked and is silently switched on by the next edit in the Trigger section.
- **Failure scenario:** Admin adds a state-mode (or threshold/template) companion, unticks Clear on recover, saves. models.normalize_sensor stores the trigger as {mode:'state', state:'on'} with no clear_on_recover key. Admin reopens Edit: the checkbox shows ticked. Admin changes only For seconds (or State, or Attribute) and saves: ha-form echoes its whole seed, which holds clear_on_recover:true, so line 892 writes trig.clear_on_recover=true. Every materialized task now clears itself on recovery, a change the admin never made.
- **Suggested fix:** Seed the form with `clear_on_recover: trig.clear_on_recover === true` for state/threshold/template (missing means off there), and keep the availability default separately; or have normalize_sensor store clear_on_recover explicitly as a bool in every edge mode so a missing key is unambiguous.
- **Verifier fix note:** Mirror forms.ts:862-865: seed `clear_on_recover: trig.clear_on_recover ?? mode === 'availability'`. The availability half only becomes correct once F06-2 makes the backend keep an explicit False. Add a test that reopens a spec with no clear_on_recover key and edits for_seconds.
- **Verifier reason:** Full trace. models.py:371-372 (threshold), 389-390 (state), 430-431 (template) store clear_on_recover only when truthy, so an unticked box is stored as a missing key. ws_list_declarative_companions (websocket_api.py:1280-1295) returns the stored spec as-is; openDeclarativeForm (panel-declarative.ts:380-381) deep-copies it into the draft. The trigger section seeds `clear_on_recover: trig.clear_on_recover !== false` (panel-declarative.ts:880), so a missing key shows ticked. _makeForm (panel.ts:2167-2170) forwards ha-form's whole echoed data, pickFormData keeps clear_on_recover because it is in the schema for every non-usage mode (panel-declarative.ts:858-861), and line 892 then writes trig.clear_on_recover=true on any edit in the Trigger section (hold, state, value, attribute). Saving the whole draft re-enables auto-clear, and build_managed_by (declarative_companions.py:624) then sets completion_blocked on every materialized task. Without a trigger edit the stored value stays off, but the box misreports it. The task form already does this correctly: forms.ts:862-865 seeds `task.sensor?.clear_on_recover ?? mode === 'availability'`. No frontend test reopens a spec whose trigger lacks the key (every fixture in panel-declarative.test.js carries clear_on_recover: true). Severity lowered from high: it needs a non-default choice plus a later trigger edit, and the effect is a behaviour flip, not data loss.
- **Repro:** python3 tmp-v-F06/repro12.py -> state: spec.trigger {'mode': 'state', 'state': 'on'}; threshold: {'mode': 'threshold', 'comparison': '>=', 'value': 5.0}; template: {'mode': 'template', 'template': '{{ true }}'} (input had clear_on_recover False; the key is gone, and panel-declarative.ts:880 reads the missing key as true)

### F06-2 — Availability mode: an unticked Clear on recover can never be stored; the checkbox has no effect

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/models.py:408`
- **Defect:** normalize_sensor drops an explicit clear_on_recover:false in availability mode and treats the missing key as true on the next pass, so the dialog's checkbox (and the service field) cannot turn auto-clear off.
- **Failure scenario:** Admin makes an availability companion and unticks Clear on recover so an offline alert stays until acknowledged. The panel sends clear_on_recover:false; normalize_sensor returns {mode:'availability'} (key dropped). The reconciler builds each task through models.build_task, which normalizes again and gets clear_on_recover:True (missing -> default True). Tasks close themselves when the device comes back, and on reopen the dialog shows the box ticked.
- **Suggested fix:** In availability mode store `result["clear_on_recover"] = bool(clear_on_recover) if clear_on_recover is not None else True`, i.e. keep an explicit False, and add a round-trip unit test.
- **Verifier fix note:** Suggested fix is right: store `bool(clear_on_recover)` explicitly in availability mode (True when missing). Update test_models_sensor.py:787-795 to assert `cfg['clear_on_recover'] is False` and add a normalize(normalize(x)) == normalize(x) check. Plain availability tasks share the root cause (forms.ts:1133 sends a real False that the backend drops); that path was not traced here.
- **Verifier reason:** models.py:407-409 drops an explicit False in availability mode, and a second normalize reads the missing key as True, so normalize_sensor is not idempotent there. The declarative spec stores {mode:'availability'} (declarative_companions.py:326). expand_spec builds match['sensor'] = {**trigger, entity_id} (declarative_companions.py:569). _build_task passes it through models.build_task (line 737), which normalizes again and stamps clear_on_recover True. The next reconcile pass rewrites the binding from match['sensor'] (line 849, merge_sensor_binding) without the key, and the watcher reads it raw (sensor_tasks.py:548 `cfg.get('clear_on_recover')`, sensor_config at :101 has no mode default), so auto-clear is then off. Result: a fresh task auto-clears until the next registry-driven pass, while build_managed_by (line 624) already reports completion_blocked False for it; the reopened dialog shows the box ticked (F06-1 seed). The unit test test_models_sensor.py:787-795 pins the single-pass drop ('clear_on_recover' not in cfg) and never normalizes twice, so it passes on the bug.
- **Repro:** python3 tmp-v-F06/repro2.py -> spec.trigger: {'mode': 'availability'}; pass1 ops: ['created'] sensor: {'entity_id': 'sensor.a', 'mode': 'availability', 'clear_on_recover': True} completion_blocked: False; pass2 ops: ['updated'] sensor: {'mode': 'availability', 'entity_id': 'sensor.a'}

### F07-3 — Clearing the stock stepper input and leaving it sets the part's stock to 0

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-detail.ts:962`
- **Defect:** The change handler commits Number(input.value); an empty field is Number('') === 0, which snapStock keeps as 0, so a cleared field becomes an adjust_part_stock call of -stock.
- **Failure scenario:** Part has stock 12. User selects the number, deletes it to type a new one, then taps elsewhere (or the phone keyboard closes). change fires with value '' -> commit(0) -> delta -12 -> stock 0. That fires the low-stock transition and can create an auto-buy task the user did not ask for.
- **Suggested fix:** In the change handler, if input.value.trim() === '' or !Number.isFinite(Number(input.value)), restore input.value = String(committed) and return without committing.
- **Verifier fix note:** Also treat input.validity.badInput (non-numeric text in a number field also reads as '') the same way: if input.value.trim() === '' or !Number.isFinite(input.valueAsNumber), restore input.value = String(committed) and return.
- **Verifier reason:** The stock input is type=number (panel-detail.ts:906-908). Clearing it and blurring fires change with value ''; the handler commits Number('') = 0 (panel-detail.ts:962); snapStock(0, step) = 0 (utils.ts:473-476); delta = -committed; api.adjustPartStock is called (panel-detail.ts:941-950). The backend clamps at 0, saves, and fires the edge-triggered low/out-of-stock event (store.py:2605-2608); the coordinator then runs reconcile_buy_tasks (coordinator.py:182), which can mint an auto-buy task. The only feedback is a 'stock set to 0' toast after the fact. The busy flag and change guard do not catch an empty value.
- **Repro:** node r.js (copies of snapStock + delta math): F07-3 Number("")= 0 next= 0 delta= -12

### F07-5 — 'Remove orphaned tasks' bulk-deletes with no confirmation, and 'orphaned' includes owners that are only retrying or not yet known

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-lists.ts:147`
- **Defect:** One tap deletes every managed task whose config entry is not in state 'loaded', without the confirmation every single delete gets; the banner says the integration is 'no longer installed'.
- **Failure scenario:** After a restart a companion integration sits in setup_retry (cloud down). The banner says '3 tasks are managed by integrations that are no longer installed'. One tap on the danger button deletes all 3 and their history (backend agrees: state is not LOADED). Also, if config_entries/get soft-fails, _loadedEntryIds is an empty Set, so every managed task with a config_entry_id reads as orphaned; non-protected ones are deleted until the first protected one fails.
- **Suggested fix:** Route the button through openConfirmDialog naming the count; only treat an entry as gone when it is absent or disabled (keep setup_retry as 'offline'); do not show the banner when the loaded-entry fetch failed.
- **Verifier fix note:** Keep the backend orphan definition unless it changes too (store.managed_task_orphaned + models.deletion_blocked), or the panel and store disagree. Minimal fix: openConfirmDialog naming the count, banner copy that covers 'offline or removed', and a null sentinel (not an empty Set) when getLoadedEntryIds fails so the banner hides.
- **Verifier reason:** cleanupOrphans (panel-lists.ts:147-157) deletes every isManagedOrphan task in a loop, wired directly to the banner button (panel-lists.ts:424-426) with no openConfirmDialog, while a single delete always confirms (panel-detail.ts:1167). isManagedOrphan is 'config_entry_id not in state loaded' (panel-chips.ts:52-55, api.ts:886), so setup_retry/setup_error owners count; the backend agrees and allows the delete (store.py:862-880). That is a deliberate backend definition (docstring: 'uninstalled, disabled, or is failing to set up'), but the banner copy says 'no longer installed' (en.json:363-364) while the chip says 'Integration offline' (en.json:72). A soft-failed config_entries/get yields an empty Set (panel.ts:784, 799), so every managed task with an entry id shows as orphaned; the backend still blocks protected ones, so the loop deletes non-protected managed tasks until the first refusal. Deleted standalone tasks lose their completion history (store.py:883-897). The code comment calls it 'one-click' on purpose (panel-lists.ts:128-132), which explains but does not justify a bulk irreversible delete without confirmation.

### F08-1 — Failed document add/edit/remove and part-file removal: the error goes only to the banner at the bottom of the drawer, and the re-render clears the typed link

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-asset-editors.ts:419`
- **Defect:** mutateDocuments and removePartFile report a failure only through setAssetError, which is the form-level banner below every section. The re-render that shows it also rebuilds the add-link form (and the inline link editor) from scratch, so the name and URL the user typed are lost.
- **Failure scenario:** On a saved appliance, the user types name 'Manual' and URL 'www.acme.com/manual.pdf' (no scheme) and presses Add link. The backend rejects it ('document url must be an http(s) URL', assets.py:193-195). The catch calls setAssetError and _render. renderDocumentAdd then rebuilds the form with an empty draft, so both fields clear and nothing is added. The only error is the banner under the metadata, parts and related-devices sections, and there is no toast. This is the 'nothing happened' symptom of issue #159. The same thing happens when Edit > Save sends a bad URL or a name over 200 characters: the inline editor re-seeds from the stored document and loses the correction. Any other render also discards a link that was typed but not yet added: Add part, a metadata seed button, Edit on another document, or an upload that finishes.
- **Suggested fix:** Send document and part-file failures through a keyed inline error plus a toast plus the one-shot scroll (reuse failUpload with key 'document' or 'part:<id>'). Keep the add-link and edit drafts on _assetEdit so that a render re-seeds them. Optionally check isHttpUrl on the client before the call.
- **Verifier fix note:** failUpload is module-private in panel-upload.ts (:179), so export a general keyed-failure helper (inline alert + toast + _scrollToError) and do not reuse failUpload by name. Keep the add-link and inline-edit drafts on _assetEdit and clear them only on success. A client isHttpUrl check before the call also removes the most common trigger.
- **Verifier reason:** Real defect, and the error is visible only if the user scrolls. mutateDocuments (panel-asset-editors.ts:418-422) and removePartFile (:938-941) report a failure only through setAssetError (panel-upload.ts:30-33), which renderAssetForm draws as one banner after the documents, metadata, parts and related-devices sections (panel-asset-form.ts:194-196). There is no toast and no scroll flag. _render rebuilds everything (panel.ts:1595-1610) but restores the drawer scrollTop, so the view stays on the documents area. Metadata and parts sections are open by default when they have rows (panel-history.ts:138), so the banner is often below the fold, and on a phone almost always. renderDocumentAdd creates a new empty draft and form on each render (:283-289), so the typed name and URL clear on failure. On an edit failure done() does not run, editingDocId stays set, and renderDocumentEdit re-seeds from the stored d (:242), so the correction is lost. The trigger is realistic: a URL without a scheme passes the client check (:426 checks only non-empty) and _normalize_http_url rejects it (assets.py:193-195). This breaks the written rule in .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md 'A failed action reports itself where the user is looking', which names the bottom banner as not sufficient. No frontend test covers document add, edit or remove. Downgraded from high: no stored data is lost, and the message does appear in the banner.

### F08-3 — Removing a document or a part's file deletes the stored file at once, with no confirmation, and the drawer's Cancel does not restore it

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-asset-editors.ts:215`
- **Defect:** The document trash icon and the part-file Remove call the backend directly, and the backend deletes the blob. Metadata and part removals ask for confirmation first, even though they change only the local draft until Save.
- **Failure scenario:** The user opens the editor and taps the trash icon beside Open on an uploaded warranty scan by mistake. removeAssetDocument runs at once and the blob is deleted. Pressing the drawer's Cancel to discard the edit does not bring the file back, and there is no undo. A part's file behaves the same way.
- **Suggested fix:** Put removeDocument and removePartFile behind openConfirmDialog (confirm.removeNamed with the document label or file name), as the other destructive rows are.
- **Verifier reason:** The trash icon on a document card calls removeDocument directly (panel-asset-editors.ts:213-216). fileCard wires remove.onClick with no confirm (:118-120). For a saved appliance mutateDocuments calls the backend at once (:414-417). store.remove_asset_document deletes the blob inside the mutation (store.py:1100-1110). The part-file Remove does the same (:873-876 -> ws remove_part_file -> store.py:1152-1169 async_delete_part_file). The drawer's Cancel is only _closeAssetForm (panel.ts:1317-1320) and restores nothing. In the same drawer, metadata and part removals, which change only the local draft, ask openConfirmDialog first (:159, :820-834), as do appliance, task and profile deletes. Documents are live by design (panel.ts:1336-1339), so the missing step is the confirmation, not the live call. Medium: one mis-tap next to Open or Edit deletes an uploaded file for good, with no undo.

### F09-1 — Card subscribes to a custom bus event that HA refuses for non-admin users, and retries on every hass update (ERROR log flood, no live refresh)

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:534`
- **Defect:** The dashboard card calls connection.subscribeEvents(..., 'home_keeper_task_completed'); Home Assistant core only lets non-admin users subscribe to events in its SUBSCRIBE_ALLOWLIST, so for every non-admin viewer the subscription fails, and because the failure is not remembered the card re-sends subscribe_events on every `set hass`.
- **Failure scenario:** A wall tablet or a household member logged in as a non-admin user opens a dashboard with the Home Keeper card (an intended audience: get_assets returns card_projection precisely for non-admins). HA's handle_subscribe_events logs `ERROR ... Refusing to allow <user> to subscribe to event home_keeper_task_completed` and raises Unauthorized. The card's catch swallows it, leaves `_unsub` undefined and resets `_subscribing`, so the next hass push (every state_changed batch in the instance, often several per second) calls `_subscribe()` again: one failed websocket round trip and one ERROR line in home-assistant.log per hass update, indefinitely. The instant cross-surface refresh the card relies on for completions never works for these users (see F09-3 for why the fallback does not cover it).
- **Suggested fix:** Add a Home Keeper websocket subscription command (e.g. `home_keeper/subscribe_tasks`, not require_admin) that forwards task events to any authenticated connection via connection.subscriptions, and use it from the card. At minimum, remember a rejected subscription (per connection) and stop retrying, falling back to a timed refresh.
- **Verifier fix note:** The dedicated non-admin subscription command (e.g. home_keeper/subscribe_task_events, not require_admin, forwarding a fixed allowlist of task events through connection.subscriptions) also fixes F09-3. Whatever the fix, remember a refused subscription per connection object (Unauthorized has code 'unauthorized') so the card stops retrying; must be declared in api_surface.WEBSOCKET_COMMANDS.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced both ends. HA core websocket_api/commands.py handle_subscribe_events (read from the HA source already fetched under SCRATCH/tmp-v-B02/src, lines 188-201): `if event_type not in SUBSCRIBE_ALLOWLIST and not connection.user.is_admin: _LOGGER.error('Refusing to allow %s to subscribe to event %s', ...); raise Unauthorized`. SUBSCRIBE_ALLOWLIST is a fixed set of core event types with no registration hook, so home_keeper_task_completed is refused for every non-admin. Card side: card.ts:440 `void this._subscribe()` runs on every `set hass`; card.ts:531 guard is only `this._unsub || this._subscribing`; card.ts:546-550 catch swallows the rejection and finally resets _subscribing, and nothing records the failure, so the next hass push re-sends subscribe_events (home-assistant-js-websocket rejects the promise and deletes the command on an error result). Non-admin card use is an explicit design goal: websocket_api.py:723-726 and docs/SECURITY.md 'Open to any signed-in user' lists the dashboard task card. No repo code or test covers a refused subscription (grep for subscribeEvents/SUBSCRIBE_ALLOWLIST: only card.ts:534 and types.ts:291). Result per non-admin client: one refused round trip plus one ERROR line in home-assistant.log per hass update, indefinitely, and no instant completion refresh. Downgraded from high: log flood and a lost live-refresh path, no data loss or privilege issue.

### F09-2 — Non-admin card never shows counted wear-item progress: card_projection strips replace_interval/use_noun/carried_uses that countedProgress reads

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:1763`
- **Defect:** get_assets returns assets.card_projection for non-admin users, whose part whitelist is only id/name/url/stock/reorder_at/stock_unit, but the card's status pill for a counted wear item's use task is computed by utils.countedProgress, which needs part.replace_interval (the target), part.use_noun and part.carried_uses.
- **Failure scenario:** An admin adds a rain jacket with a wear part `replace_unit: uses, replace_interval: 25, use_noun: wears`. A household member (non-admin) taps Done on the generated use task from the dashboard card each time they wear it. For the admin the card row reads "17 of 25 wears"; for the non-admin `part.replace_interval` is undefined, countedProgress returns null, and statusChipHtml falls back to dueLabel -> "Counting" forever. The person who records the uses is exactly the one who can never see the count or that the target was reached.
- **Suggested fix:** Add replace_interval, replace_unit, use_noun and carried_uses to _CARD_PART_FIELDS (none is cost/serial/admin data), and add a card test that feeds a card_projection-shaped payload.
- **Verifier fix note:** Only replace_interval, use_noun and carried_uses are read (replace_unit is not needed by countedProgress). docs/SECURITY.md 'Open to any signed-in user' enumerates the part allowlist, so update that list in the same change, and extend the tests/unit/test_assets.py projection test and add a card test fed a card_projection-shaped payload.
- **Verifier reason:** Full path traced. card.ts:569-570 fetches api.getAssets when any task has source.part (every use task has); websocket_api.py:729-730 returns assets.card_projection for a non-admin; assets.py:1763 `_CARD_PART_FIELDS = ('id','name','url','stock','reorder_at','stock_unit')` and assets.py:1792-1795 keep only those keys. card.ts:929 passes countedProgress(task, this._assets, this._tasks); utils.ts:894-895 `const target = part?.replace_interval; if (!part || typeof target !== 'number' || target <= 0) return null;` so for every non-admin the result is null, statusChipHtml (utils.ts:1009) skips the counted chip and dueLabel returns t('due.counting') (utils.ts:943). carried_uses (utils.ts:869) and use_noun (utils.ts:916) are also stripped. The panel is admin-only, so for a non-admin the card is the only surface that could show the count. Deterministic for every non-admin user; the reminder itself still arms at the target, so this is a missing display, not a lost reminder.

### F10-1 — Editing a logged completion or skip fails once the task no longer records readings

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-defer.ts:64`
- **Defect:** The edit dialogs send back the reading stored on the entry, and the backend refuses any reading when the task is not a numeric sensor task now, so no field on those entries can be edited.
- **Failure scenario:** A usage sensor task logs completions and skips with a reading. The user changes the task to a floating task (or the sensor mode to state). Later the user opens Edit on an old completion or skip to fix the note and presses Save. The dialog sends reading=<old value> (the field is not even shown, because taskRecordsReading/sensorLive is false). store.update_completion / update_skip call normalize_completion_metadata(allow_reading=False), which raises 'reading is only valid for a sensor task with a numeric binding'. The error alert shows and the edit can never be saved. The user has no field to clear, because the reading field is hidden.
- **Suggested fix:** In openSkip/openCompletionEdit (or at submit), drop `reading` when taskRecordsReading(task) is false. Alternatively make the backend edit path accept (or silently drop) an unchanged reading that equals the stored one.
- **Verifier fix note:** Dropping `reading` in the frontend is not neutral: recurrence.update_completion/update_skip pop every key in COMPLETION_ENTRY_FIELDS/SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS that the metadata omits (recurrence.py:701-704), so the save would silently erase the historical reading. Prefer the backend: in store.update_completion/update_skip, when task_records_reading(existing) is False, accept a reading equal to the entry's stored value (or drop it before normalize and carry the stored value through), and reject only a changed one. Add a unit test that edits a note on a completion with a reading after a sensor->floating change.
- **Verifier reason:** Full path traced and reproduced at the model layer. panel-defer.ts:64 (openSkip) and panel-dialogs.ts:57 (openCompletionEdit) copy the stored entry.reading into the dialog data. The reading field is shown only when taskRecordsReading(task) (defer-dialogs.ts:280, panel-dialogs.ts:364), but the value stays in s.data / c.data and api.ts:241-251 metadataMsg sends any non-null reading. store.py:2074-2076 and :2272-2274 call normalize_completion_metadata(allow_reading=task_records_reading(existing)), and models.py:101-106 raises when allow_reading is False. models.merge_update does not strip readings from history (no pop of 'reading' anywhere in models/store/recurrence), so after a sensor task becomes floating, or its sensor mode becomes state, every old completion and skip keeps its reading and every panel edit of those entries fails. The history Edit button is shown for every live task (panel-history.ts:207,223). No test covers the case (tests/unit/test_completion_metadata.py tests only the direct rejection). Real but needs a type or mode change, so medium, not high.
- **Repro:** python3 tmp-v-F10/run.py tmp-v-F10/repro1.py (stub babel, loads tests/conftest.py) -> 'records reading (sensor usage): True' / 'after change to floating: type floating records reading: False' / 'stored completion still has reading: [{... 'reading': 55.0, 'note': 'old'}]' / 'REJECTED: TaskValidationError reading is only valid for a sensor task with a numeric binding' / 'mode->state records reading: False completions: [{... 'reading': 55.0 ...}]'

### X01-5 — Appliance serial numbers reach non-admins through the HA device registry, contrary to SECURITY.md

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/devices.py:461`
- **Defect:** Home Keeper copies each appliance's serial_number onto its HA device, and HA's config/device_registry/list websocket command (no require_admin) returns serial_number to every signed-in user.
- **Failure scenario:** A guest account on a wall tablet sends {type: 'config/device_registry/list'}; each Home Keeper appliance device carries serial_number (and manufacturer/model). SECURITY.md promises the narrowed view withholds serial numbers and claims HA restricts the device registry to admins; only the registry mutations are admin-only.
- **Suggested fix:** Stop pushing serial_number into the device registry (keep it in the admin-only asset record), or make it an explicit opt-in and correct SECURITY.md's claim that HA's device registry is admin-only.
- **Verifier fix note:** Also correct SECURITY.md's claim that HA restricts device-registry reads; manufacturer and model reach non-admins the same way.
- **Verifier reason:** devices.py:460-461 seeds serial_number (and manufacturer/model/name) on the HA device, and devices.py:470-475 keeps it in sync. HA core components/config/device_registry.py:74-99 websocket_list_devices ('config/device_registry/list') has @callback + @websocket_command only; only update/remove carry require_admin (lines 150, 276, 293). helpers/device_registry.py:627 dict_repr includes serial_number. So any signed-in user reads every appliance serial number. This contradicts SECURITY.md:15-17 and :44-47 (HA reserves the device registry for admins) and :69-70 (narrowed view withholds serial numbers), and makes the card_projection withholding of serial/manufacturer/model (test_assets.py:1621) moot.
- **Repro:** curl raw HA core dev components/config/device_registry.py: grep require_admin -> lines 11,150,276,293 only; list command at line 74-99 has none

### X03-8 — 2026.8 split repair leaves related_device_ids and profile device filters on dead ids

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:1261`  **Also reported as:** B17-4
- **Defect:** The shared dead->live mapping is applied to task device_id, source copies and existing-asset device_id only, not to asset related_device_ids or the profile filter devices/exclude_devices lists.
- **Failure scenario:** Before upgrade, task T is attached to a Z-Wave device that appliance X lists in related_device_ids (and a profile filters on that device). After the heal, T.device_id is the live split id but X.related_device_ids still holds the composite id: task_relates_to_asset(T, X) is False, so X's page and report drop T and deleting T no longer archives its history onto X; the profile's devices filter matches nothing (exclude_devices stops excluding).
- **Suggested fix:** Apply the same mapping to every asset's related_device_ids and, via options, to each profile's filter devices/exclude_devices in async_heal_split_device_ids.
- **Verifier reason:** Same defect as B17-4. devices.py:312-324 collects ids only from task device_id and existing-asset device_id and calls only async_repoint_device_ids and async_repoint_asset_device_ids; store.py:1252-1264 rewrites only asset['device_id']; related_device_ids is not mentioned in store.py or devices.py at all (grep), and profile filter devices live in options, which the heal never touches. After the 2026.8 split heal, task_relates_to_asset (assets.py:1749-1751) and profile device filters compare against dead ids.
- **Repro:** grep -n related_device_ids custom_components/home_keeper/store.py custom_components/home_keeper/devices.py -> no output

### X04-7 — Panel reads and writes times in the browser zone, not HA's zone

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:203`  **Also reported as:** F04-6
- **Defect:** haDateTimeToIso/isoToHaDateTime, dueLabel, statusBucket 'today' and every other panel date calculation use the browser's local zone and never read hass.config.time_zone or the user's 'server time zone' setting, while the backend and HA services read the same naive selector value in HA's zone.
- **Failure scenario:** HA in America/Los_Angeles, admin browser in America/New_York. The user sets a fixed monthly anchor '2026-01-01 00:30'. The panel sends 05:30Z; the backend regrids it to 2025-12-31 21:30 PST, so the schedule is 'day 31 at 21:30' in HA, clamps to Feb 28 and then stays on the 28th (Mar 28, Apr 28...) instead of the 1st. Calling home_keeper.snooze_task with the same naive until from HA's UI means HA time, but the panel's custom snooze means browser time. The card's Today section and the to-do list disagree about which day a task is due.
- **Suggested fix:** Convert selector strings and compute day boundaries in hass.config.time_zone (or honour hass.locale.time_zone), e.g. with Intl.DateTimeFormat timeZone offsets, or send the naive selector string and let the backend qualify it in HA's zone.
- **Verifier fix note:** Sending the naive selector string unchanged is the smallest fix, because models.py already qualifies naive input in HA's zone. Day-boundary maths (card-filter.ts, utils.ts startOfDay) still needs the HA zone separately. See also F04-6.
- **Verifier reason:** A grep for time_zone, timeZone, hass.locale and config.time in frontend/src finds nothing. haDateTimeToIso (forms.ts:201-206) parses the selector string in the browser zone and sends UTC, for fixed anchors too (forms.ts:1163). models.py:766-776 keeps an aware anchor as it is, and the recurrence regrid moves it into HA's zone, so the wall-clock hour (and near midnight, the day) moves when the browser zone is not HA's zone. The same naive selector value sent to a home_keeper service is read in HA's zone. No doc says browser-zone input is intended. Medium, not high: it needs a browser zone that differs from HA's (remote admin, travel). When that happens, every time typed in the panel is stored shifted. The 'stays on the 28th' clamp detail was not checked.

### X06-4 — PR jobs that run npm ci hold contents:write, and Dependabot PRs receive that write token

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:22`
- **Defect:** release (on pull_request), walkthrough-preview and docs-preview install and run PR dependencies in the same job that holds contents:write. Because these workflows set the `permissions` key, the read-only default that GitHub gives Dependabot-triggered runs no longer applies.
- **Failure scenario:** Dependabot proposes a compromised version of a transitive npm package in one of the lockfiles. On the PR, before anyone reviews it, `npm ci` runs the package's install script inside the `release` job, which has `contents: write` even though the tag and release steps are skipped on PRs. The script can use the token to create a GitHub Release with a malicious home_keeper.zip, which HACS offers to users, or to push tags or branches. walkthrough-preview and docs-preview run `npm ci` under the same token scope.
- **Suggested fix:** Split release.yml into a read-only validate/build job for PRs and a write job that runs only on push and does the tag and release. For the previews, build in a job with `contents: read` and upload an artifact, then publish from a separate job that runs no PR code. Or skip the previews when `github.actor == 'dependabot[bot]'`.
- **Verifier fix note:** The release.yml part of the evidence is overstated (its npm ci only runs on a PR that bumps to an untagged version, which is a maintainer PR). The fix belongs in the two preview workflows: build with contents: read (and persist-credentials: false) and publish from a separate job that runs no PR code, or skip them for github.actor == 'dependabot[bot]'. `npm ci --ignore-scripts` also removes the install-script path for most packages.
- **Verifier reason:** Confirmed for walkthrough-preview.yml and docs-preview.yml; weak for release.yml. walkthrough-preview.yml:18-24 runs on every same-repo PR, Dependabot's included (the fork guard at :34 passes for Dependabot branches), with workflow-level contents: write + pull-requests: write, and runs `npm ci` for the frontend (:46) and tests/e2e (:54) after actions/checkout persisted the token (:37, default persist-credentials). The token really is writable on Dependabot runs: run 32544935239 (branch dependabot/github_actions/actions/github-script-9) has 'Publish / remove preview' (a gh-pages push, no continue-on-error) and the sticky comment both 'success'. docs-preview.yml:30-32 is the same for website/** changes, which Dependabot's /website npm ecosystem produces (dependabot.yml:55-56). release.yml's `npm ci` (:88-90) is gated by `steps.check.outputs.release == 'true'`, and on a Dependabot PR main's manifest version is already tagged, so it does not run in practice: the 'release' check on that Dependabot PR passed in 31 s with no build. A compromised npm release that Dependabot proposes can read the persisted token in its install script and create tags or releases before review. Severity stays medium: a supply-chain precondition, but the blast radius is a GitHub Release that HACS would offer.
- **Repro:** list_workflow_jobs 32544935239: head_branch dependabot/github_actions/actions/github-script-9; steps 'Install frontend deps' success, 'Install e2e deps' success, 'Publish / remove preview' success, 'Sticky comment with the walkthrough' success.

### X06-5 — Nightly HA-beta integration job runs 13.3 of its 15-minute cap, and a timeout files no regression issue

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** ci
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/ha-beta.yml:28`
- **Defect:** The integration tier already raised its PR cap to 20 minutes, but the nightly cap is still 15. A job that hits timeout-minutes ends as 'cancelled', and the report job checks only `failure()`, so the early-warning nightly goes quiet with no issue filed.
- **Failure scenario:** The integration tier grows by about 1.7 minutes more, or an HA beta slows it down. The nightly integration job hits 15 minutes and is cancelled. `report` has `if: failure() && ...`, and a cancelled need does not count as a failure, so no ha-beta-regression issue is filed. The scheduled run shows grey 'cancelled' every night, and HA beta breakage, the #183 class of bug, goes unreported.
- **Suggested fix:** Set the nightly integration cap at or above the PR lane's cap, for example 25. Make the report job fire on cancelled needs too: `if: always() && github.event_name == 'schedule' && (contains(needs.*.result, 'failure') || contains(needs.*.result, 'cancelled'))`.
- **Verifier fix note:** Prefer `if: ${{ !cancelled() && github.event_name == 'schedule' && (contains(needs.*.result, 'failure') || contains(needs.*.result, 'cancelled')) }}` over the suggested `always()`: always() also files a false regression issue when someone cancels the scheduled run by hand, and !cancelled() stays true when only a job timed out.
- **Verifier reason:** ha-beta.yml:28 caps the integration job at 15 min. Run 36577564225 (2026-09-29): 'Integration (HA beta)' 13:46:19-13:59:37 = 13m18s, 'Run integration tests' 11m38s, so 1m42s of margin, and whole-run time rose from about 7 min (late August) to 13.3 min now. A job that exceeds timeout-minutes ends with conclusion 'cancelled' (seen directly on job 96961624897). report (:191-192) is `if: failure() && github.event_name == 'schedule'`, and job-level failure() is true only when a needed job's result is 'failure', so a timed-out integration job with the other 3 green files no ha-beta-regression issue. integration.yml:13-18 already raised its PR cap to 20 for the same growth. Medium is fair: the cap is close to being hit, and when it is hit, the only early warning for the #183 class goes quiet.
- **Repro:** list_workflow_jobs 36577564225: Integration (HA beta) started_at 13:46:19Z completed_at 13:59:37Z; step 'Run integration tests' 13:47:55Z-13:59:33Z. list_workflow_runs ha-beta.yml: run durations 7 min (2026-08-22) -> 13m24s (2026-09-29).

### X08-1 — Remove orphaned deletes one task per websocket call, and each device-attached delete reloads the whole config entry and rewrites the whole store

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-lists.ts:152`
- **Defect:** The bulk orphan cleanup loops over single delete_task calls, and ws_delete_task does a full config-entry reload for every task that owns per-task entities, so N orphans cost N sequential entry reloads plus N full-document saves.
- **Failure scenario:** A glue integration (for example a battery-replacement companion) that created 60 device-attached managed tasks is removed. The user taps Remove orphaned. The panel awaits 60 sequential home_keeper/delete_task calls; each one runs store.delete_task (full JSON save of the whole document, one task_deleted event, one to-do sync pass) and then awaits hass.config_entries.async_reload, which unloads and re-creates every Home Keeper entity (about 2000 at the target scale), re-runs registry pruning, the sensor-watcher baseline and the declarative reconcile. The panel shows nothing for minutes, every Home Keeper entity flaps unavailable 60 times, and a failure halfway leaves the rest (the loop stops at the first throw). One reload after the batch is all that is needed.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a batch delete path (a store method that deletes a list of ids with one _save, events per task, and one reload if any deleted task owned entities), exposed as a service plus a websocket command, and call it once from cleanupOrphans.
- **Verifier fix note:** The fix is right. Minimum version without a new contract: keep the per-task websocket loop but add a no-reload flag, or better a batch store method (one _save, per-task task_deleted events, one reload if any deleted task owned entities) exposed as a service first (AGENTS.md: every data action is a service) with a websocket twin. Also continue past a single failure and report the count, instead of stopping at the first throw.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced end to end. panel-lists.ts:147-157 cleanupOrphans awaits api.deleteTask once per orphan, in sequence, inside one try (the first throw stops the rest). api.ts:190-192 is a plain home_keeper/delete_task call. websocket_api.py:336-341 runs store.delete_task (store.py:855-862: one full _save plus one task_deleted event per task) and then AWAITS hass.config_entries.async_reload for every task where coordinator.task_has_entities is true (coordinator.py:66-77: device_id set and enabled; it does not check that the device still exists). No batch delete exists in the websocket API or services, and no test in tests/ references cleanupOrphans or #cleanup-orphans-btn. The project itself treats stacked reloads as a defect elsewhere (coordinator._buy_reload_scheduled, and the purge loop sets reload_needed and reloads once, coordinator.py:286-300), so this path breaks its own convention. Re-rated scale: the finder's 60 tasks / 2000 entities is inflated; a realistic glue removal leaves tens of device-attached orphans and a few hundred entities, so N reloads cost seconds to tens of seconds, and every reload flaps all Home Keeper entities unavailable and back N times (logbook/recorder rows, and state-trigger automations without a from: filter can fire again on each unavailable->on). Rare, one-off, user-initiated, outcome eventually correct: medium, not high.

### X10-1 — Sensor-task bindings are keyed by entity_id and never follow a rename, so a renamed source sensor silently kills the task

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_watcher.py:115`
- **Defect:** A threshold/usage/state sensor task stores only the bound sensor's entity_id; nothing listens for entity-registry renames, so after a user renames that entity_id the task reads None forever, never arms, and nothing tells the user.
- **Failure scenario:** User has a usage task 'Oil change every 10,000 km' bound to sensor.car_odometer. They rename the device 'Car' to 'Tesla' and accept Home Assistant's 'also rename entity IDs?' prompt (a normal HA flow), so the entity becomes sensor.tesla_odometer. The task still holds cfg.entity_id = 'sensor.car_odometer': _raw_reading returns None (hass.states.get is None), the state listener tracks an id that no longer exists, the task never arms and the oil-change reminder never fires. The next-due sensor also silently drops its usage_consumed/usage_remaining/usage_percent attributes (sensor.py:237). No repair issue, log warning or panel badge surfaces it. Declarative companion tasks do not have this problem because they key on the rename-stable entity_registry_id and re-merge the sensor binding each pass (declarative_companions.py:836-853); hand-made sensor tasks get no equivalent.
- **Suggested fix:** Listen for er.EVENT_ENTITY_REGISTRY_UPDATED with action 'update' and 'entity_id' in changes, and rewrite sensor.entity_id from old_entity_id to entity_id on every bound task (one store write, then resubscribe). Alternatively store the entity registry id beside the entity_id and resolve it on read, as declarative companions already do. Raise a repair issue when a bound entity is missing from both the registry and the state machine.
- **Verifier fix note:** Rewrite every task whose sensor.entity_id equals old_entity_id, including disabled tasks (_sensor_tasks() at sensor_watcher.py:325-331 skips enabled=False, so a watcher-only rewrite leaves a stale id to surface on re-enable). Do it in one store write through the store chokepoint so home_keeper_task_updated fires, then call _resubscribe_state(). Filter on action == 'update' and 'old_entity_id' in event.data. If an entity_registry_id is stored beside entity_id instead, that is a new persisted field: it must round-trip through transfer.py (test_transfer_roundtrip.py) and is a one-way door for the PR body. A missing-entity cue should cover all modes in panel-detail.ts sensorProgress, not only availability.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced in full. A hand-made sensor task stores only sensor.entity_id (sensor_tasks.py:109-112 bound_entity_id; no entity_registry_id anywhere in sensor_tasks.py/models.py/sensor_watcher.py/store.py). The watcher reads hass.states.get(entity_id) and returns None when it is gone (sensor_watcher.py:115-119), builds its tracked set from the stored ids (sensor_watcher.py:333-339) and subscribes only to state_changed for them (sensor_watcher.py:480-491). Nothing rewrites the binding: the only EVENT_ENTITY_REGISTRY_UPDATED listeners in the component are problem_sync.py:81 (binary_sensor problem mirrors only; its handler at :184-195 reads just event.data['entity_id'] and reconciles mirrors) and declarative_companion_sync.py:170 (managed tasks only, re-merged from entity_registry_id at declarative_companions.py:836-853). store.py and __init__.py have no registry listener, and old_entity_id appears nowhere in custom_components/home_keeper. HA itself does not rewrite integration storage on an entity_id rename, and its device-rename dialog offers to rename entity IDs, so this is an ordinary flow. After the rename: a threshold task never arms on a None reading (sensor_watcher.py:560-566), a state task holds its edge on a missing state (sensor_watcher.py:518-526), a template task renders with its bound state as None (sensor_watcher.py:259-279), so a template written against the bound reading goes stale, and a usage task without an also_every backstop is skipped every pass (sensor_watcher.py:556-557); usage attributes drop off the next-due sensor (sensor.py:237-246). No test covers it (no test references old_entity_id or EVENT_ENTITY_REGISTRY_UPDATED), and no doc says a rename needs a re-bind. Panel warning: only availability-mode tasks get one, on the task detail page ('{entity}: not found', panel-detail.ts:178-179 via locales/en.json:776). The other modes fall back to the bare 'sensor.x (cond)' line, the same text as a merely unavailable reading (panel-detail.ts:168-212), and the edit form's reading helper returns {} silently (forms.ts:1457-1458). No repair issue exists (no issue_registry use in the component) and no log line fires for a missing bound entity (the only watcher warning is for template render errors, sensor_watcher.py:695). Mitigations that keep it at medium, not high: a usage task with also_every still comes due by time, and availability tasks show 'not found' on the detail page.
- **Repro:** grep -rn 'old_entity_id' custom_components/home_keeper -> (no output); grep -rn 'EVENT_ENTITY_REGISTRY_UPDATED' custom_components/home_keeper -> problem_sync.py:81, declarative_companion_sync.py:170 only; grep -rln 'old_entity_id\|EVENT_ENTITY_REGISTRY_UPDATED' tests/ -> (no output)

### X11-1 — Confirm-delete scrim has no dialog semantics, no initial focus, no focus containment and no focus return

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** accessibility
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-dialogs.ts:191`
- **Defect:** The panel's only destructive confirmation (delete task, appliance, profile, part, metadata row, declarative companion) is a bare div appended to document.body that never takes focus, is not announced as a dialog, and leaves the whole panel tabbable behind it.
- **Failure scenario:** A keyboard user on a task page presses Enter on Delete (panel-detail.ts:1167). The scrim paints, but focus stays on the Delete ha-button behind it. A screen reader announces nothing, because the modal has no role=alertdialog/dialog, aria-modal or aria-labelledby. Tab walks through every remaining panel control under the scrim (nothing is made inert) before it reaches Cancel/Delete, which sit at the end of document.body. Enter pressed again on the still-focused opener only re-opens the dialog. After Cancel, the scrim is removed and focus falls to <body>. At a 320px viewport the content-box modal is 280px + 2x24px padding = 328px, so it overflows the screen.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the modal role=alertdialog, aria-modal=true, and aria-labelledby/aria-describedby ids for the h2 and p. On open, remember the deep active element (shadowRoot.activeElement), focus Cancel after the append, and keep Tab inside the modal (or set inert on the panel host while it is open). On close, restore focus to the opener if it still exists. Use box-sizing:border-box and max-width:calc(100vw - 32px). Or build it on ha-dialog, as the other panel dialogs are.
- **Verifier fix note:** Setting inert on the panel host alone does not cover the HA shell (sidebar, header) that sits before the scrim in document order. Focus Cancel on open (or Delete-less Close for openBlockedDialog) and restore focus to the opener via the deep activeElement. Moving to ha-dialog (as the other panel dialogs use) gives role, aria-modal, focus trap and focus return in one step.
- **Verifier reason:** panel-dialogs.ts:186-266 renderConfirmDeleteDialog builds a plain div scrim and modal. No role, no aria-modal, no aria-labelledby, and no .focus() call anywhere in the function. The scrim is appended at the end of document.body (panel-dialogs.ts:265), so Tab walks the whole HA shell and the panel before it reaches Cancel/Delete. Nothing is made inert: the only inert logic is the drawer sheet (panel.ts:1841-1865), and closeConfirmDialog (panel-dialogs.ts:176-184) only gives the drawer's Escape handler back. teardownOverlay (panel-dialogs.ts:140-153) removes the scrim and restores no focus. Escape does close it (document keydown, panel-dialogs.ts:215-220), so it is not a keyboard trap, but a keyboard user must Tab past everything to reach Delete, and a screen reader hears nothing. The only e2e check (tests/e2e/tests/a11y.spec.ts ~274-279) clicks Cancel with the mouse. Width claim holds: content-box min-width 280px + 2x24px padding = 328px, which overflows only a 320px viewport (edge case).

### X11-3 — Dashboard card rebuilds its whole shadow tree (open create form and Snooze/Skip dialogs included) on every refresh, and never manages focus

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** accessibility
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:587`
- **Defect:** Any Home Keeper state change or task-completed event while the card's '+' form or a Snooze/Skip/note dialog is open replaces shadowRoot.innerHTML. The focused field is destroyed, and the following keystrokes go to Home Assistant's global hotkeys.
- **Failure scenario:** A user taps '+' on the card and types 'Replace filter'. Mid-word, someone else completes any task (to-do list, automation, another dashboard), which fires home_keeper_task_completed and bumps todo.home_keeper_tasks. _refresh() then ends in _render(), which rewrites the whole shadow root and rebuilds the form from _edit.task. The text field is gone and focus falls to <body>, so the next letters 'e', 'c', 'a' open HA's entity search, command palette and Assist. The panel's own form-focus.test.js documents this exact consequence and guards the panel against it; the card has no guard. An open Snooze/Skip dialog is torn down and re-created by the same path, so it re-animates and focus resets. Cancel, Create and the note dialog's Close also re-render with no focus target, so focus is dropped to <body>. card.ts contains no focus() call at all.
- **Suggested fix:** While a form or dialog is open, repaint only .hk-body (the list) and leave #hk-form-host and the dialog nodes in place, or defer the full _render until the overlay closes. After _closeForm, _closeNote and the defer dialogs close, move focus back to #hk-add or to the row control that opened them.
- **Verifier fix note:** Deferring the whole _render while an overlay is open would also freeze the list; the better fix is to repaint only .hk-body when _edit.open or a defer dialog is open, or to keep the #hk-form-host and dialog nodes and re-append them after the innerHTML write. Re-appending a detached node still loses focus, so save and restore document.activeElement / shadowRoot.activeElement around the write if nodes are moved.
- **Verifier reason:** Full path traced. hass setter card.ts:448-451 calls _refresh() whenever any home_keeper entity's last_updated changes (_stateSignal card.ts:508-520), and the home_keeper_task_completed subscription (card.ts:534-537) calls it on any completion anywhere. _refresh (card.ts:553-587) ends in an unconditional this._render() with no check of _edit.open, _snooze.open or _skip.open. _render (card.ts:742-773) replaces shadowRoot.innerHTML, then _hydrate re-creates the form via _renderForm (card.ts:1039, 1201-1210 builds a new ha-form each time) and re-creates the Snooze/Skip dialogs (card.ts:1127-1136). The typed text survives (value-changed copies it into _edit.task, card.ts:1214-1227), but the focused field node is destroyed and focus falls to body. HA global hotkeys then take the next keys, which the panel's own test documents (frontend/test/form-focus.test.js:16-21). card.ts has 0 matches for 'focus'. No card test covers a refresh with the form open (frontend/test/card.test.js). Needs another Home Keeper state change during the few seconds the form is open, so it is occasional, not constant.
- **Repro:** grep -c focus src/card.ts -> 0; grep refresh+form in test/card.test.js -> no match

### X12-2 — Option writes are not serialized, so a second write during the reload from the first fails with not_loaded and is lost

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-settings.ts:699`
- **Defect:** Each set_options that changes something waits for a full entry reload. runtime_data is set only after store.load and the first refresh. Any set_options that arrives in that window gets integration_not_loaded, and no write path retries.
- **Failure scenario:** (a) The user turns off Snooze and then Skip within a second on the Skip/Snooze card.
