The second saveOptions reaches the backend during the first reload. find_coordinator returns None, so the backend sends a not_loaded error. The card shows 'Save failed' plus a raw toast. The form still shows Skip off, but the _reload() from the first save puts p._options back to the server state (Skip on), so the UI and the store now disagree. The change is gone on the next load. (b) The user renames two profile rows within 600ms. The per-row debounce timers fire less than one reload apart, so the second write fails. The rename that the #255 fix protects is lost anyway. (c) The user edits a notification and presses Test within 600ms. Test saves the list and starts a reload. The armed row debounce then fires into that reload and reports 'Not saved' for a value that is saved.
- **Suggested fix:** Send option writes through one queue per panel, so each write waits for the one before it. Retry a write that fails with not_loaded after the same RELOAD_RETRY_MS back-off that _reload uses. Cancel the row's pending debounce when Test saves the row.
- **Verifier fix note:** Queue option writes per panel and retry not_loaded after RELOAD_RETRY_MS, as suggested; cancel the row debounce when Test saves the row.
- **Verifier reason:** ws_set_options is wrapped by _with_coordinator (websocket_api.py:153-157, 1206-1228); find_coordinator (coordinator.py:421-434) returns None once HA drops runtime_data at unload until __init__.py:830 sets it after the first refresh. options.py:338-339 awaits the full reload per changed write. saveOptions (panel-settings.ts:698-710) and persistOptionList (1036-1071) have no queue and no not_loaded retry; only the read path retries (panel.ts:828-829). So a second option write in that window gets not_loaded. Timing-dependent: a write that lands during the unload phase still succeeds and waits on the entry setup lock. Downgraded to medium because the failure is visible ('Save failed' + toast) and the optimistic list write is re-sent by the next save of that list; no silent loss unless the user ignores the error.

### X12-3 — Panel Done, completion dialog, Skip and Snooze have no in-flight guard: a double-click records two completions or skips two occurrences

- **Severity:** medium (finder: high)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:1096`  **Also reported as:** F01-1
- **Defect:** The card guards Done against re-entry. The panel list Done, the detail Done, the completion-details Save and the shared Skip/Snooze dialogs do not. The backend does not dedupe completions.
- **Failure scenario:** The user double-clicks Done on a task row, or taps it again on a phone because the first tap looks slow. Two complete_task calls go out. The history gets two rows, a linked consumable is drawn down twice (which can fire a false low-stock or reorder event), and home_keeper_task_completed fires twice, so automations and notifications run twice. A double-click on Skip in the skip dialog, which the card also uses, advances the schedule by two occurrences and logs two skips.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a per-task in-flight set to PanelHost._complete, like the card's _completing. Disable the dialog primary button (footerButtons, the completion dialog, move-date) on click and enable it again only on error.
- **Verifier reason:** panel.ts:1096-1117 _complete awaits api.completeTask with no in-flight guard and no synchronous disable/re-render before the await; wired from panel-lists.ts:474-478 (.done-btn). submitCompletion (panel-dialogs.ts:108-124, wired at 435) and submitSkip (defer-dialogs.ts:312-327 via footerButtons 146-157, primary never disabled) close only after the await, so a double click sends two writes. store.complete_task (store.py:1979-2015) has no dedupe: each call appends a completion, draws down linked stock and fires the event. The card has the guard (card.ts:386, 632, 654-660), which shows the intent. Medium, not high: the extra history row and stock can be corrected, although duplicate automation/notification runs cannot be undone.

### X13-1 — Device-registry calls deprecated in HA 2026.9 (removed 2027.8) sit on the unguarded setup path

- **Severity:** medium (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/devices.py:463`
- **Defect:** devices.py uses four device-registry APIs that HA 2026.9 (current stable) deprecates with breaks_in_ha_version 2027.8: async_get_device, async_get_or_create(via_device=...), async_update_device(remove_config_entry_id=...) and DeviceEntry.config_entries. Each logs a 'custom integration home_keeper ... will stop working in 2027.8' warning today, and two of them run unguarded inside async_setup_entry.
- **Failure scenario:** Today (HA 2026.9.x): any install with a sub-appliance (parent_asset_id) passes via_device on every setup, and any install with an orphaned task device reaches remove_config_entry_id in async_prune_orphaned_devices. Both log a deprecation warning that points at Home Keeper. On HA 2027.8, where the kwargs.pop('via_device') shim goes, async_get_or_create rejects the unexpected kwarg with TypeError. _reconcile_virtual -> async_reconcile_assets (__init__.py:833, no try/except) then raises, so setup fails for every user with a sub-appliance. async_prune_orphaned_devices (__init__.py:864) raises after async_forward_entry_setups, so the entry goes to SETUP_ERROR with its platforms still loaded. A later reload does not unload SETUP_ERROR platforms (HA config_entries.py:1029-1032), and the re-forward fails with 'already been setup' until HA restarts. async_remove_asset_device (devices.py:600) breaks the delete-asset service in the same release.
- **Suggested fix:** Route these through device_compat.py with a capability check like devices._supports_kwarg. Use via_device_id=parent_device_id when async_get_or_create accepts it, else via_device. Use async_get_device_by_identifier / async_get_device_by_connection when present, else async_get_device. Use async_remove_device for a device our entry owns alone, and remove_config_entry_id only on pre-2026.8 cores. Use device.config_entry_id when present, else config_entries. Wrap async_prune_orphaned_devices / async_detach_legacy_merged_devices in try/except (they run after the platforms forward), and update docs/INTEGRATING.md:92.
- **Verifier fix note:** Corrections: (1) _resolve_by_snapshot looks up ANOTHER integration's device, so async_get_device_by_identifier/by_connection do not fit (both need the owner's config_entry_id). Use async_get_devices(identifiers=..., connections=...) there and keep the prefer_not_entry pick. Use async_get_device_by_identifier(identifier, entry.entry_id) only in async_remove_asset_device (pass the entry in), or resolve asset['device_id'] with async_get. (2) Never pass via_device and via_device_id together (HA raises HomeAssistantError, :2295-2299). Pass via_device_id only when parent_device_id resolves, because an unresolvable via_device_id raises DeviceInfoError (:2404-2416) and would fail setup in a new way. (3) In prune on 2026.8+, the devices from async_entries_for_config_entry are ours alone, so use async_remove_device; keep remove_config_entry_id only where DeviceEntry has no config_entry_id (pre-2026.8). Gate async_detach_legacy_merged_devices the same way. (4) Read device.config_entry_id when present, else config_entries. Also wrap the post-forward device maintenance in try/except, as suggested.
- **Verifier reason:** Checked against an independently downloaded homeassistant-2026.9.4 wheel (byte-identical to the finder's tmp-X13 copy). Three of the four calls are deprecated with breaks_in_ha_version 2027.8.0 and log a custom-integration warning today (report_usage, custom_integration_behavior LOG, once per run): async_get_device (device_registry.py:1981-1989), the via_device kwarg of async_get_or_create (:264-271 table, popped at :2260, then any leftover kwarg raises TypeError at :2261-2265), and remove_config_entry_id on async_update_device (:3750-3765). DeviceEntry.config_entries (:396-403) is a shim with NO warning today; only the dict_repr comment (:533-535) dates it to 2027.8, so 'each logs a warning' is overstated for that one. device_compat.py wraps none of them: it covers only async_get (resolve_device), registry.devices (all_devices) and child-device connections (device_compat.py:45-90). Setup would really fail after removal: (a) via_device goes into create_kwargs on every setup for a sub-appliance (devices.py:462-464), so a TypeError leaves async_reconcile_assets, which runs unguarded at __init__.py:836 -> SETUP_ERROR before the platforms forward; the same error hits every asset change (devices.py:140). (b) remove_config_entry_id is an explicit parameter (no **kwargs), so removal gives TypeError in async_prune_orphaned_devices (devices.py:424-426), which runs unguarded after async_forward_entry_setups (__init__.py:859-864). That path runs whenever a self-owned task device lost its entities (task delete, problem-sync off, and the 2026.8 split heal, devices.py:283-285), so it reaches most installs. HA then runs _async_process_on_unload but leaves the forwarded platforms loaded (config_entries.py:912-914); a later reload skips unload for a non-LOADED entry (:1029-1032), and the re-forward is logged per platform as 'already been setup' (caught by the forwarded entry's setup, not raised). (c) async_get_device removal gives AttributeError in _resolve_by_snapshot: unguarded via _reconcile_existing only when an existing-device asset's device is gone (devices.py:515-518), guarded via the heal (__init__.py:820-826), and in async_remove_asset_device (devices.py:600), which breaks virtual-asset delete after the store delete already ran (__init__.py:1988-1994). async_detach_legacy_merged_devices never reaches its remove_config_entry_id call on 2026.8+ (config_entries is a singleton, so 'others' is empty). docs/INTEGRATING.md:92 does show async_get_device. Medium: nothing breaks until 2027.8, but the break is dated, deterministic and broad, and users see the 'will stop working' warnings now.
- **Repro:** pip download homeassistant==2026.9.4 --no-deps --ignore-requires-python; unzip; grep -> device_registry.py:270 "via_device": ("2027.8.0", "via_device_id"); :2260 via_device = kwargs.pop("via_device", UNDEFINED); :2262 raise TypeError(...unexpected keyword arguments); :1988 breaks_in_ha_version="2027.8.0" (async_get_device); :3764 breaks_in_ha_version="2027.8.0" (remove_config_entry_id). diff -q against tmp-X13 copy: identical.

### B01-3 — Undoing or moving a wear-part replacement leaves the part's last_replaced at the undone date

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:2184`
- **Defect:** complete_task stamps part.last_replaced, and delete_completion gives back stock_drawn but never restores last_replaced; move_completion never re-stamps it either.
- **Failure scenario:** Furnace air filter part has last_replaced 2026-08-01. User taps Done on 'Replace Air filter' by mistake on 2026-09-30, then undoes it. Stock is returned and next_due re-derived, but the part still says last_replaced 2026-09-30 (reproduced: 'last_replaced after undo: 2026-09-30 (was 2026-08-01)'). The appliance page and export show the wrong date; for a counted wear item whose only replace completion was undone, cycle_start becomes None and replacement_backstop_due falls back to the wrong last_replaced, so the time backstop reminder comes late; a part task recreated later (interval toggled) anchors to the wrong date. Back-dating via move_completion leaves last_replaced at the original day.
- **Suggested fix:** Record the previous last_replaced on the completion entry (like meter_start/stock_drawn) and restore it in delete_completion when the removed entry was the latest replacement; in move_completion re-stamp from the latest remaining completion.
- **Verifier fix note:** After delete or move, derive last_replaced again from the latest remaining replace completion. Fall back to a prior value recorded on the entry (like meter_start) when no completion remains.
- **Verifier reason:** store.py:2484 stamps part.last_replaced in complete_task. delete_completion (2169-2185) restores only the meter baseline and stock_drawn, and move_completion (2217-2224) never stamps it again. But the schedule of an existing part task comes from its own completions: reconcile never re-anchors an existing task from last_replaced (reconcile.py:720-783), so next_due is correct after the undo. The stale date shows on the appliance page and in the export. It also changes the counted-wear backstop, but only when the replace task has no completion or skip left (reconcile.py:309-313), and the anchor of a part task created again later (reconcile.py:674, 707-717). The panel has no undo toast, so the undo is the history-row delete. Real but narrow: low.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B01/repro_undo.py -> 'last_replaced after completion: 2026-09-30', 'last_replaced after undo: 2026-09-30 (was 2026-08-01)'.

### B01-5 — Undoing a buy-reminder completion keeps the restock it added

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:2547`
- **Defect:** _stamp_buy_restock adds restock_quantity to stock but records nothing on the completion, and delete_completion only reverses stock_drawn, so the undo leaves the count inflated.
- **Failure scenario:** Filter part: stock 1, reorder_at 3, restock_quantity 1, auto-buy on. The 'Buy Filter' reminder is completed by mistake: stock becomes 2, still low, so the completed reminder stays. The user undoes it in the panel (home_keeper/delete_completion) or the service: stock stays 2 (reproduced: 'stock after undoing the buy completion: 2 (expected 1)'). The inventory reports a spare that was never bought, and the next low/out-of-stock crossing comes one unit late.
- **Suggested fix:** Stamp the restock delta on the completion entry (for example stock_added) in _stamp_buy_restock and subtract it in delete_completion, emitting the stock transition.
- **Verifier reason:** _stamp_buy_restock (store.py:2547-2567) adds restock_quantity and writes nothing on the completion entry. delete_completion reverses only stock_drawn (2184, 2529-2545). The case is reachable: the buy reconciler keeps a completed reminder while the part stays low (reconcile.py:828-831, 849-855), and the panel history row calls home_keeper/delete_completion (api.ts:402). It needs restock_quantity too small to lift the part above reorder_at: otherwise the reminder is removed at once and there is nothing to undo. docs/EVENTS.md:178 promises a give-back only for consumption. The result is one unit too many in the count.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B01/repro_undo.py -> 'stock after buy completion: 2', 'stock after undoing the buy completion: 2 (expected 1)'.

### B02-2 — delete_declarative_companion ignores entity_set_changed, so device-page entities of the deleted tasks linger

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:1890`
- **Defect:** The store deletes a spec's managed tasks and returns whether per-task entities were removed (the caller must reload), but the service handler (and its websocket twin) discards that flag and neither reloads nor refreshes.
- **Failure scenario:** An admin deletes a device-backed companion (e.g. a battery preset matching entities on real devices; declarative tasks copy the entity's device_id, declarative_companions.py:724). store.async_delete_declarative_companion removes the tasks and returns True. The handler returns without reloading. The follow-up SIGNAL_DECLARATIVE_SPECS_CHANGED reconcile only walks the remaining specs, finds no change, and just refreshes. The Mark done button, next-due sensor and overdue binary sensor of each deleted task stay on the device page (orphans are pruned only at platform setup, entity.py:105-125) until some unrelated reload or restart; pressing the stale button calls complete_task on a missing task and errors.
- **Suggested fix:** In the service handler (and ws_delete_declarative_companion) reload the entry when the store returns True, else request a refresh - the same shape handle_delete_task uses.
- **Verifier reason:** __init__.py:1890-1893 drops the store's return value. websocket_api.py:1404-1405 returns it, and api.ts:936-944 ignores it. The SIGNAL reconcile that follows walks only the remaining specs (declarative_companion_sync.py:333-362) and then only refreshes (440-458). Declarative tasks copy the entity's device_id (declarative_companions.py:724), so they own per-task entities. HomeKeeperTaskEntity._task returns {} for a missing task (entity.py:67-68), and the Mark done button calls complete_task, which raises KeyError (button.py:77-78). Only platform setup prunes orphans (entity.py:105-125). By contrast, handle_delete_task reloads (__init__.py:1208-1216). Low: the stale entities go at the next reload or restart, which is routine here, and the only failure is the stale button.

### B02-3 — Declarative companion services leak raw TaskValidationError/KeyError instead of localized ServiceValidationError

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:1877`  **Also reported as:** B21-2
- **Defect:** add/update_declarative_companion take a pass-through schema and leave every field check to the normalizer, but the handlers do not translate its TaskValidationError (a ValueError) or the KeyError for an unknown id, while the websocket twins map both to translated errors.
- **Failure scenario:** An automation or integration calls home_keeper.add_declarative_companion with a bad field, a duplicate id, or past MAX_DECLARATIVE_COMPANIONS; or update_declarative_companion with an unknown id. HA treats the exception as unexpected: it logs a full traceback ('Unexpected exception' / 'Error executing script') and returns an unknown_error with raw English text (or just "'abc'" for the KeyError) instead of the translated invalid_declarative_companion / declarative_companion_not_found messages. The quality scale claims action-exceptions: done.
- **Suggested fix:** Wrap both calls: except TaskValidationError -> ServiceValidationError(translation_key='invalid_declarative_companion', placeholders={'error': str(err)}); except KeyError -> ServiceValidationError(translation_key='declarative_companion_not_found', placeholders={'spec_id': spec_id}).
- **Verifier reason:** The handlers at __init__.py:1877 and 1887 do not use _store_errors. The store raises TaskValidationError (store.py:1733-1741; DeclarativeCompanionValidationError is a subclass, declarative_companions.py:69) and KeyError (store.py:1766-1767). In HA 2026.9.4, websocket call_service catches ServiceNotFound, vol.Invalid, ServiceValidationError and HomeAssistantError, and anything else reaches 'connection.logger.exception("Unexpected exception")' and ERR_UNKNOWN_ERROR with str(err) (commands.py:296-350). So a KeyError reads "'abc'", with a traceback in the log. The call still fails as it should, so this is error quality only. strings.json invalid_declarative_companion is only '{error}', so for the validation case the gain is the error class (no traceback), not a translation.

### B02-4 — update_task accepts and documents `source`, but the store silently drops it

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:217`
- **Defect:** UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA and services.yaml offer `source` on update_task, yet models.merge_update never reads updates['source'] and store.update_task has no other path for it, so the call succeeds with no effect.
- **Failure scenario:** An integration keeps provenance in its source namespace (INTEGRATING.md tells it to embed its own schedule_id and to match on source). It moves a schedule and calls home_keeper.update_task(task_id, source={'my_integration': {'schedule_id': new}}). No error, no task_updated event, the stored source keeps the old id. Its next list_tasks match on the new schedule_id finds nothing, so it re-creates the task (duplicate) or loses track of it. Reproduced: build_task(source={'acme':{'v':1}}) then merge_update(t, {'source':{'acme':{'v':2}}}) returns source {'acme': {'v': 1}}.
- **Suggested fix:** Either apply it (merge the caller's namespaces into the stored source, rejecting the reserved part/buy/problem_sensor/declarative_companion keys as add_task does) or remove `source` from UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA and services.yaml so the call fails loudly.
- **Verifier fix note:** Removing source from UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA and services.yaml (create-only, as for appliances) is the smaller change, but it is a one-way door: callers that pass source today and succeed would start to fail. Either way, keep strings.json and api_surface in parity and write the rule in INTEGRATING.md.
- **Verifier reason:** UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA accepts source (__init__.py:217), and services.yaml:383-388 shows it in the UI. The candidate set in models.merge_update has no source (models.py:1011-1033), and store.update_task (458-496) has no other path for it. The call succeeds with no change and no event. The panel already relies on this (panel.ts:950-951: 'update_task doesn't touch' source). INTEGRATING.md:779 makes an appliance's source create-only but says nothing for a task. Low: a silent no-op on an opaque field, and no stored data is harmed.
- **Repro:** python3 (SCRATCH/tmp-v-B01 harness, real models.py/store.py) -> 'merge_update source: {acme: {v: 1}}', 'store.update_task source: {acme: {v: 1}} events: []'.

### B02-5 — An owning integration cannot change its own locked task fields via update_task, contrary to INTEGRATING.md

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:1191`
- **Defect:** merge_update strips managed_by.locked_fields from every update_task payload regardless of caller, and there is no owner door for tasks (assets have update_managed_asset), so the documented 'reconciler renames its locked task' flow is a silent no-op.
- **Failure scenario:** A companion creates a task with managed_by.locked_fields=['name'] and, as INTEGRATING.md instructs, calls home_keeper.update_task(task_id, name='Give Rex his medicine') when the pet is renamed. merge_update removes 'name' from the payload, nothing changes, no event fires, no error is raised; the task keeps the old name forever. Related drift: the same doc says update_task 'ignores' managed_by, but UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA has no managed_by key, so voluptuous rejects the call with 'extra keys not allowed'.
- **Suggested fix:** Add an owner path for tasks mirroring update_managed_asset (e.g. an update_managed_task service, admin/integration only, that writes locked fields), or correct INTEGRATING.md; accept-and-ignore managed_by in UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA or fix the doc.
- **Verifier reason:** models.py:1001-1006 strips managed_by.locked_fields from every update_task payload. handle_update_task (__init__.py:1190-1205) and store.update_task pass no owner flag, and only appliances have an owner door (update_managed_asset, services.yaml:1166). So INTEGRATING.md:357-359 promises a flow that is a silent no-op, with no event and no error. INTEGRATING.md:353 ('The update_task service ignores it') is also wrong: UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA (__init__.py:188-220) has no managed_by key, so voluptuous rejects the call. Home Keeper's own reconcilers write directly and are not affected (declarative_companions.py:61). Low: it affects only a third-party integration that locks a field and later needs to change it.

### B02-6 — remove/update_asset_document services report 'Asset not found' for an unknown document

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:1575`  **Also reported as:** X09-1
- **Defect:** The store raises KeyError(document_id) for a missing document, but the handlers wrap it in _store_errors(asset_id=...), which maps any KeyError to asset_not_found with the appliance id.
- **Failure scenario:** home_keeper.remove_asset_document(asset_id='Dishwasher', document_id='Manaul') on an existing appliance: _document_ref hands the typo back unchanged (as its docstring intends, so the not-found path can quote it), the store raises KeyError('Manaul'), and the user gets 'Asset not found: 3f2c...' - pointing at the wrong field and never naming what they typed. The websocket twins answer unknown_document.
- **Suggested fix:** Check the asset first and map a KeyError from these two calls to unknown_document with document_id, as the websocket twins do.
- **Verifier reason:** _document_ref returns an unmatched key unchanged (__init__.py:1053-1056, 1025-1029). store.remove_asset_document and update_asset_document raise KeyError(document_id) for an unknown document (store.py around 1105 and 1127). The handlers wrap the call in _store_errors(asset_id=asset_id) (__init__.py:1575, 1583), which maps any KeyError to asset_not_found with the appliance id (1147-1152). The websocket twins answer unknown_document, and strings.json has 'Unknown document: {document_id}'. The message is wrong only.

### B02-7 — delete_task and delete_asset silently succeed for an unknown id or misspelled name

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:1210`  **Also reported as:** X09-3
- **Defect:** _ref returns an unmatched name unchanged on the promise that the caller's not-found path will raise, but store.delete_task and store.delete_asset treat a missing id as a no-op, so there is no not-found path.
- **Failure scenario:** An automation calls home_keeper.delete_task(task_id='Clean gutter') where the task is 'Clean gutters' (or delete_asset with a misspelled appliance). The call returns success, nothing is deleted, no event fires; the automation and the user believe the task/appliance is gone. Other handlers (complete_task, archive_asset) do raise task_not_found / asset_not_found for the same input.
- **Suggested fix:** In the two service handlers, raise task_not_found / asset_not_found when the resolved id is not in the store before deleting.
- **Verifier fix note:** Do not raise for every unknown key, because that breaks the INTEGRATING.md section 5 cleanup loop. Raise only for a key that did not resolve and does not look like a stored id (uuid), or document that cleanup must catch task_not_found.
- **Verifier reason:** store.delete_task (826-862) and store.delete_asset (1444-1446) do nothing for a missing id. The handlers pass no ids to _store_errors (__init__.py:1210-1213), and _delete_asset returns early (1988-1990). So a misspelled name, which _ref hands back unchanged (1025-1029), succeeds silently, and the not-found path that the _ref docstring expects does not exist for deletes. But the no-op on an unknown id is also what integrator cleanup relies on: INTEGRATING.md:259 and 346 tell an integration to call delete_task for every id it stored, and the user may already have deleted some of them.

### B02-8 — set_task_meter stores a NaN/Infinity baseline, bypassing the finite-number invariant

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:231`
- **Defect:** SET_TASK_METER_SCHEMA uses vol.Coerce(float), which accepts 'nan'/'inf', and the handler passes the value to store.set_sensor_baseline, which writes it raw; every other stored numeric field goes through models._finite_float.
- **Failure scenario:** home_keeper.set_task_meter(task_id=..., baseline: 'inf' or .nan, e.g. from a template that renders a non-finite float). With inf the usage task can never arm (reading - inf < target). With NaN every comparison is false, and on the JSON round-trip NaN becomes null, so after a restart the watcher silently re-anchors the meter to the live reading and the user's progress is lost - the exact junk state models._finite_float documents it exists to prevent.
- **Suggested fix:** Validate the explicit baseline with math.isfinite (or models._finite_float) in the handler or in set_sensor_baseline and raise invalid_task.
- **Verifier reason:** vol.Coerce(float) at __init__.py:231 calls float(v), which accepts 'nan' and 'inf'. The handler passes float(baseline) (1329), and set_sensor_baseline writes it without a check (store.py:815-816), while models._finite_float guards the same field on add and update (models.py:343). HA storage writes with orjson (helpers/json.py:66, storage.py:592-597), which stores NaN and Infinity as null, so after a restart the baseline is None and the watcher anchors again on the live reading. The input must come from YAML or a template, because the UI number selector cannot give a non-finite value.

### B02-9 — set_task_meter and add_asset_document raise hard-coded English error text

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:1311`
- **Defect:** Three service errors pass English sentences as the {error} placeholder of invalid_task/invalid_asset, so non-English users get English text, while the websocket twin of add_asset_document uses the translated link_documents_only key.
- **Failure scenario:** A German-language instance calls add_asset_document with kind 'file', or set_task_meter on a non-usage task or with no live reading: the error reads 'only link documents can be added via this service; upload files from the panel' / 'set_task_meter is only valid for a usage sensor task' in English.
- **Suggested fix:** Use dedicated translation keys (reuse link_documents_only; add keys for the two set_task_meter cases).
- **Verifier fix note:** Reuse link_documents_only for add_asset_document and add 2 keys for set_task_meter. The general English-in-{error} pattern needs its own decision.
- **Verifier reason:** The 3 placeholders are English sentences (__init__.py:1311-1313, 1322-1327, 1559-1562), and every translation of invalid_task and invalid_asset is only '{error}' (for example translations/de.json). The websocket twin of add_asset_document uses link_documents_only (websocket_api.py:929-931). But these 3 are a small part of a general pattern: every store TaskValidationError that _store_errors maps to invalid_task also carries English text.

### B03-5 — skip_task websocket accepts cost and photo in metadata and stores them on the skip entry; update_skip can never clear them

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/websocket_api.py:547`  **Also reported as:** F09-4
- **Defect:** The websocket takes `metadata: dict` and passes it straight to store.skip_task, whose normalizer accepts cost/photo, so a skip entry can carry fields the data model and the service schema forbid.
- **Failure scenario:** Any signed-in user sends home_keeper/skip_task {task_id, metadata: {cost: 50, photo: 'https://x/y.jpg'}}. normalize_completion_metadata keeps cost and photo, and recurrence.skip_occurrence does entry.update(metadata), so the skip entry stores them. The history list then renders a cost chip and a photo on a 'Skipped' row. update_skip only touches SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS (note/who/reading), so the stray cost/photo can never be edited away, only removed by deleting the skip. Export silently drops them, so the store and the export disagree. The service path cannot do this because SKIP_TASK_SCHEMA is strict and _skip_metadata lifts only SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS.
- **Suggested fix:** Filter to SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS in the handler ({k: v for k, v in (msg.get('metadata') or {}).items() if k in SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS}), or better, filter in store.skip_task so every caller gets the rule. Optionally tighten the schema to vol.Schema({vol.Optional('note'): str, vol.Optional('who'): str, vol.Optional('reading'): vol.Coerce(float)}).
- **Verifier fix note:** Prefer filtering in store.skip_task (keep only SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS after normalizing) so every caller gets the rule; a handler-only filter leaves the store contract loose.
- **Verifier reason:** ws skip_task schema is vol.Optional('metadata'): dict (websocket_api.py:547) and passes it through (559). store.skip_task normalizes with normalize_completion_metadata (store.py:760-763), which keeps cost and photo (models.py:111-131), and recurrence.skip_occurrence does entry.update(metadata) (recurrence.py:593-595). update_skip only touches SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS (recurrence.py:787-812, store passes fields=SKIP_ENTRY_FIELDS), so the stray keys cannot be cleared, and export drops them (transfer.py:383-386). The service path is strict (SKIP_TASK_SCHEMA __init__.py:255-265, _skip_metadata 1224-1226). Needs a hand-crafted websocket call; values are still validated (finite cost >= 0, http(s)/site-relative photo), so it is a data-model inconsistency, not a security issue.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../tmp-v-B20/pydeps python3 tmp-v-B03/skip_meta.py -> stored skip entry: {'ts': ..., 'note': 'away', 'cost': 50.0, 'photo': 'https://x/y.jpg'}; after update_skip clearing all: {'ts': ..., 'cost': 50.0, 'photo': 'https://x/y.jpg'}

### B04-10 — plan_import raises instead of reporting a problem for an ambiguous stored name or an unhashable id

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:1306`  **Also reported as:** X03-5
- **Defect:** _parent_id and _appliance_device call resolve.match_by_name outside any try, and the matcher and device check use `in` on dict/set with raw values, so AmbiguousName and TypeError escape the planner.
- **Failure scenario:** Store holds two appliances named 'Smoke detector'; a document task says `appliance: Smoke detector` (or an appliance says `parent_asset_id: Smoke detector`). Verified: plan_import raises resolve.AmbiguousName; `id: [1]`, `id: {x: 1}` or `device_id: [1]` raise TypeError: unhashable type. _store_errors does not translate these, and ws_import_data has no wrapper, so the panel preview shows a generic unknown_error with no record path, against the promise to report every problem with a path.
- **Suggested fix:** Catch resolve.AmbiguousName around both lookups and report it at the parent_asset_id/appliance path; reject non-string id/device_id/external_id values with a problem row before using them as keys.
- **Verifier reason:** _parent_id (called at 1306, outside the try) and _appliance_device (1445, outside the try) call resolve.match_by_name, which raises AmbiguousName for two stored appliances with one name; a list id or device_id raises TypeError at `record_id in available` (739) or `stated_device in known_devices` (1439-1441). _store_errors (__init__.py:1124-1175) translates neither, so the panel/service get a generic unknown error instead of a problem row with a path. No data effect.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r_rest.py -> appliance ambiguous -> RAISES AmbiguousName; parent ambiguous -> RAISES AmbiguousName; list id -> RAISES TypeError unhashable type: 'list'; list device_id -> RAISES TypeError

### B04-11 — archived is applied only when an appliance is created; on an update it is silently ignored

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:1362`
- **Defect:** The payload drops `archived`, and only the create branch stamps archived_at, so a matched appliance neither archives nor unarchives, and no warning says so.
- **Failure scenario:** Re-importing an export where 'Old fridge' has `archived: true` onto the same install where it is live: verified the update keeps archived_at None, no problem. The reverse (document live, store archived) keeps it archived.
- **Suggested fix:** Apply `archived` on the update branch too (set archived_at when true and absent, clear it when explicitly false), or report a warning that it was not applied.
- **Verifier fix note:** Apply an explicit archived true/false on update; an absent key should keep the stored value (documented merge rule).
- **Verifier reason:** The payload drops `archived` (transfer.py:1293) and only the create branch stamps archived_at (1362-1363); the update branch (1336-1352) never reads it. The user guide says an update changes the fields the file states and lists `archived: true` as a field, so `archived: true` on a matched appliance is silently ignored. The reverse case (file omits archived, store archived) is consistent with the documented 'fields it leaves out keep the value', because _asset_out writes archived only when true; so only the explicit direction is a defect.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r_rest.py -> update archived_at None problems []; store archived + doc without archived -> archived_at kept

### B04-5 — Two records that state the same id pass the external_id collision check and duplicate on every re-run

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:1119`
- **Defect:** _colliding_external_ids assumes a stated uuid is kept, but _claim_id silently mints a fresh id when the stated one is already claimed, so the second record has no stable key at all.
- **Failure scenario:** A user copies a task block in the YAML to make a second similar task, keeping `id: X` and `external_id: filter`. Run 1: two creates (X and a new id). Run 2 of the same file: the first updates X, the second (id X claimed, ladder short-circuits on a stated id) creates again. Verified: store grows 2 -> 3 -> 4 tasks, all with external_id 'filter', and a later keyed-only import fails with '"filter" matches several tasks'. The same duplication happens with no external_id. No problem is reported.
- **Suggested fix:** Report an error when two records in one section state the same id (or treat a claimed stated id as a collision), and in _colliding_external_ids require the stated ids to be distinct as well as uuids.
- **Verifier reason:** Matcher.match returns (None, None) for a stated id that is already claimed (739-742), _claim_id mints a fresh uuid (1255-1258), and _colliding_external_ids skips the group because both raw ids are uuids (1119-1120). Each re-run of the file adds one more copy, with no problem reported. Trigger is a hand edit that copies a block with its id (natural when editing an export); the first run gives the intended result, only re-runs duplicate, and the duplicates are visible in the list, so low.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r_rest.py -> run 1: create, create (store 2); run 2: update, create (store 3); run 3: update, create (store 4); problems=[] on every run

### B04-6 — A stated area_id is kept without checking it exists, so the resolvable area name is ignored on another install

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:1222`
- **Defect:** _resolve_area returns any stated area_id verbatim and only falls back to the area name when area_id is absent, and it warns only for an unknown name, never for an unknown id.
- **Failure scenario:** Old install: area id 'living_room' renamed to 'Lounge'. Export writes `area_id: living_room` and `area: Lounge`. New install has 'Lounge' with id 'lounge'. Verified: task and appliance get area_id 'living_room' (does not exist), no warning, although 'Lounge' resolves. Area ids differ across installs whenever an area was renamed, created in another language, or de-duplicated (kitchen_2).
- **Suggested fix:** Keep a stated area_id only when it is in areas.values(); otherwise fall back to `area` by name, and warn when neither resolves (mirror the device_id handling at 1438-1472).
- **Verifier reason:** _resolve_area (transfer.py:1222-1223) returns any stated area_id verbatim, and every export writes area_id (not excluded) beside area. The export comment (366-371) says the name 'makes the file mean something on an install that has never seen that id', which the code does not do; the user guide says only 'A stated area_id wins' (unconditional, unlike device_id's 'if that device is on this Home Assistant'), so the behavior is half-documented. Result on a new install whose area ids differ: tasks/appliances filed under a dead area id, no warning, area-scoped profiles miss them. No data loss; area ids only differ after renames/other-language creation, so low.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r_rest.py -> exported {'area_id': 'living_room', 'area': 'Lounge'}; with areas {'Lounge': 'lounge'} planned area_id: living_room, problems: []

### B04-7 — An existing-kind appliance's device_id is never checked, and its tasks inherit the dead id past the task-level check

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:1293`  **Also reported as:** X03-7
- **Defect:** Tasks validate a stated device_id against device_ids, but an existing-kind appliance keeps its document device_id verbatim, and a task resolved through `appliance` gets that id via the placeholder, so neither the plan nor the runner's unattached warning fires.
- **Failure scenario:** Export an existing-kind 'Washer' (device dev-old) with a task, import on a new install whose washer device is dev-new. Verified: appliance created with device_id dev-old, task payload -> hk-planned-asset placeholder -> runner substitutes dev-old (truthy, so no 'imported without its appliance' warning); problems []. Result: the appliance and task point at a device that does not exist; the task falls back to a self-owned device and nothing tells the user to re-point the appliance.
- **Suggested fix:** Pass device_ids into _plan_asset and warn (or refuse) when an existing-kind appliance names a device that is not on this install; have the runner warn when a resolved placeholder device_id is not a live device.
- **Verifier reason:** _plan_asset keeps the document device_id (payload at 1293; assets.normalize_fields 1399 requires it for kind existing) with no known-device check, and the task resolves to the placeholder and then to that dead id in the runner (transfer_runner.py:102-106 warns only when it is empty). The only signal is a HA log warning from devices._reconcile_existing (devices.py:517-524), and snapshot recovery cannot help because identifiers/connections are excluded from the export. Every existing-kind appliance on a cross-install move needs re-pointing anyway; the defect is that the report does not say so. No data loss, so low.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r_rest.py -> appliances create device_id dev-old; tasks create device_id hk-planned-asset:<id>; problems: [] (device_ids={'dev-new'})

### B04-8 — YAML tags and oversized integers reach the store through pass-through fields and make every later save fail

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:618`
- **Defect:** The loader drops only the implicit timestamp resolver; SafeLoader still constructs !!binary (bytes), !!set (set) and explicit !!timestamp values, and free-form fields (a foreign task source, an existing-kind device_id, sensor.for_seconds) store them verbatim, so HA's orjson Store encoder cannot write the file.
- **Failure scenario:** Admin imports a shared template containing `source: {acme: {blob: !!binary aGVsbG8=}}` on a task, or `device_id: !!binary ...` on an existing-kind appliance, or `sensor: {..., for_seconds: 99999999999999999999999}`. Verified: plan.ok True, no problem, payload holds b'hello' / a set / a date / a >64-bit int. After apply, Store.async_save logs 'Error writing config' (SerializationError) and returns normally; the bad record stays in memory, so every later save of the store also fails and all changes since the import are lost at the next restart.
- **Suggested fix:** Use a loader whose constructors are limited to JSON types (remove binary/set/omap/pairs/timestamp constructors), and before applying, check every planned payload with a strict JSON encode (orjson or json.dumps with allow_nan=False plus a 64-bit int check), reporting failures as problems with the record path.
- **Verifier fix note:** Removing the non-JSON constructors from _Loader covers the tags; bound for_seconds in models._normalize_for_seconds (it is shared with add_task/update_task) instead of only in transfer.
- **Verifier reason:** The loader only drops the implicit timestamp resolver (transfer.py:618-621); SafeLoader still constructs explicit !!binary/!!set/!!timestamp. models.validate_source (models.py:800) only checks that source is a mapping, and _normalize_for_seconds (models.py:220-228) has no upper bound. HA's Store (store.py:200, default orjson encoder) cannot encode bytes or ints above 64 bits; Store._async_handle_write_data logs 'Error writing config' and returns, so later saves fail too while the record is in memory. This breaks the loader's own stated rule ('the document's scalars are JSON's scalars', 592-598). The trigger needs explicit YAML tags or an absurd integer in an admin-supplied file; the big for_seconds also enters through add_task, so it is not import-specific. Severe consequence but contrived trigger: low.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B04 && python3 r_rest.py -> payload source: {'acme': {'blob': b'hello', 'tags': {'b','a'}, 'when': datetime.date(2026, 1, 1)}}, json.dumps fails: TypeError bytes; state-mode sensor for_seconds 99999999999999999999999 planned ok, fits int64: False

### B04-9 — Parsing and planning run synchronously in the event loop; a large document freezes Home Assistant for tens of seconds

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer_runner.py:62`
- **Defect:** async_import_document calls transfer.plan_import (pure-Python PyYAML SafeLoader parse, validation and history replay) directly in the event loop, and the panel does it twice (preview, then import).
- **Failure scenario:** A 7.9 MB document (under MAX_IMPORT_BYTES, which const.py says 'holds a very large migration') measured 22.4 s to parse and 7.2 s to plan on this machine. During that time the whole HA event loop is blocked: automations, websocket heartbeats and the UI stall. The panel's preview-then-import doubles it; the 4 MiB panel limit still blocks about 15 s per call.
- **Suggested fix:** Run parse_document and plan_import in `hass.async_add_executor_job` (they are pure and work on a snapshot copy of the store maps), or cap history entries per record and total entries per document.
- **Verifier reason:** transfer_runner.py:62-74 calls plan_import (which parses with a pure-Python SafeLoader subclass, transfer.py:583, 691-693) directly inside the async handler, so parsing blocks the event loop. Measured here: a 3.4 MB export (2000 tasks x 20 completions) parses in 9.6 s and plans in 0.5 s; the panel runs preview then import. Real HA-guideline violation, but only large one-off admin migrations reach multi-second stalls; a typical household export is a few hundred KB, so low.
- **Repro:** timed inline script under tmp-v-B04 -> bytes 3371105, parse 9.64s plan 0.51s ok True

### B05-2 — Stock can pass MAX_INTERVAL through restock, undo or adjust_part_stock, and then every edit of that appliance fails

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:1368`
- **Defect:** adjust_part_stock clamps only at zero, but _normalize_stock refuses stock > 10000 and merge_update re-validates every stored part on every edit, so an over-cap count locks the appliance.
- **Failure scenario:** A part is counted in ml, with stock 8000 and restock_quantity 5000 (both valid, <= 10000). Completing its Buy task runs _stamp_buy_restock -> adjust_part_stock(part, 5000) -> stock 13000. After that, the panel's appliance save, the inline Notes edit and update_asset {name} all fail with 'stock must be <= 10000', because merge_update re-normalizes the stored parts even when the update does not touch them. The stock number entity (native_max_value 10000) also reports a state above its own max, and exporting then re-importing the appliance fails validation. The same happens through the adjust_part_stock service/websocket (delta is a bare vol.Coerce(float), no Range), and through _return_stock_drawn when a completion is deleted. Reproduced: stock 9995 + restock 10 -> 10005, then merge_update({'name': 'renamed'}) raises AssetValidationError.
- **Suggested fix:** Clamp the new count to MAX_INTERVAL in adjust_part_stock (applied_delta already reports the real move), and bound delta in both schemas. Separately, do not re-validate stored parts that an update did not send (see B05-1).
- **Verifier fix note:** A silent clamp at MAX_INTERVAL loses part of a real restock. For the service and websocket paths, refuse an adjustment that would pass the cap with a localized error. For the automatic restock and return paths, clamp. The B05-1 fix (do not re-validate stored parts that the update did not send) removes the lockout either way.
- **Verifier reason:** adjust_part_stock clamps only at zero (assets.py:1367-1369). _normalize_stock refuses a count > MAX_INTERVAL (assets.py:457-458), and merge_update re-normalizes every stored part on every update (assets.py:1595, 1605). The paths that raise the count past 10000 are real: the buy-task restock (store.py:2566-2567), a deleted completion returning its draw (store.py:2544), and the service and websocket delta, which is a bare vol.Coerce(float) (__init__.py:565-571, websocket_api.py:873). Reproduced: after a restock, every appliance update fails. Severity is low, not medium. The count must pass 10000, which needs a part counted in fine units (ml, g) with a large restock. The user can recover: the stock number entity writes through adjust_part_stock with a delta (number.py:148-155), so setting it to a value <= 10000 unlocks the appliance, and the error text names the field.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro2.py -> stock 8000 + restock 5000: transition none, stock now 13000; merge_update(name|notes|parts): AssetValidationError: stock must be <= 10000

### B05-3 — A future-dated completion stamps a future last_replaced, and every edit of that appliance then fails until the date passes

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:748`
- **Defect:** The completion path writes part['last_replaced'] directly, with no future check, but _normalize_part refuses a future last_replaced and runs on every stored part on every merge_update.
- **Failure scenario:** On 2026-09-30, a script calls home_keeper.complete_task on a wear part's replacement task with completed_at: 2026-10-01T08:00. complete_task has no future guard (store.py:1986), so _stamp_part_replacement sets last_replaced = '2026-10-01'. Until 2026-10-01, the appliance cannot be renamed, its notes cannot be edited, and its parts cannot be saved: each attempt raises 'last_replaced must not be in the future'. The panel shows a generic save failure with no hint of the cause. Reproduced for {notes}, {name} and {parts} updates.
- **Suggested fix:** Clamp the stamped date to today in _stamp_part_replacement (or reject a future completed_at for any task), and apply the future check only to a last_replaced the caller changed, not to the stored value carried through an unrelated edit.
- **Verifier fix note:** Clamping the stamp to today in _stamp_part_replacement is enough for this path. With the B05-1 fix, an unrelated edit also stops re-checking the stored date.
- **Verifier reason:** complete_task takes completed_at with no bound (store.py:1985-1991). The service uses cv.datetime (__init__.py:331) and the websocket uses parse_datetime (websocket_api.py:406-410). The panel's 'Completed at' selDateTime picker has no max either (panel-dialogs.ts:351). _stamp_part_replacement writes last_replaced from that date with no future check (store.py:2476-2484). _normalize_part refuses a future last_replaced (assets.py:748-751) on every stored part in every merge_update. Reproduced for {notes}, {name} and a panel-style {parts} update. Nothing un-stamps it: last_replaced is written only in complete_task (store.py:2024). Severity is low, not medium. It needs a future-dated completion of a wear-part task, and it clears when the date passes. The full editor shows the backend message and has a last_replaced date field (forms.ts:1846), so a user can correct the date there. Sending that part without last_replaced does not help, because _merge_parts carries the stored value (assets.py:803-804).
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro2.py -> last_replaced 2026-10-01 with now 2026-09-30: merge_update(notes|name|parts as stored): AssetValidationError: last_replaced must not be in the future

### B05-4 — A generic documents write can give a link the id of a stored file; deleting the link then deletes the file and its blob

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:333`
- **Defect:** _normalize_documents de-duplicates ids only inside the incoming list, and _merge_documents then prepends the stored file entries with no cross-check, so the merged list can hold two entries with one id.
- **Failure scenario:** A script reads an appliance and sends update_asset documents: [{id: <stored file doc id>, kind: link, url: https://...}] (for example, replacing a receipt entry with a link and keeping its id). The result holds file F and link F. remove_asset_document(F), meant for the link, removes the first match, the file, and store.remove_asset_document deletes its blob. update_asset_document(F, {url}) also edits the file entry, so the url change is ignored. Reproduced.
- **Suggested fix:** In _merge_documents, regenerate the id of any incoming link that collides with a stored file id, as append_document already does (assets.py:353-354).
- **Verifier reason:** _normalize_documents de-duplicates ids inside the incoming list only (assets.py:262-266). _merge_documents prepends the stored file entries with no id cross-check (assets.py:333-335). remove_document and update_document act on the first id match (assets.py:366-371, 386-395). store.remove_asset_document deletes the blob of a removed file entry (store.py:1103-1108). The service document schema accepts a caller id (__init__.py:465). Reproduced. Low: the panel never sends documents for a saved appliance (panel.ts:1340-1343), and the trigger is a service caller that deliberately gives a link the id of a stored file.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro4.py -> merged docs: [(True, 'file'), (True, 'link')]; remove_document removed kind: file; update_document edited kind: file url field: None

### B05-5 — adjust_part_stock accepts a non-finite delta: NaN silently zeroes the stock, and infinity raises a raw OverflowError

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:1368`
- **Defect:** max(0.0, old + nan) returns 0.0, so a NaN delta sets stock to 0; with infinity, _round_stock calls int(inf) and raises OverflowError.
- **Failure scenario:** An automation passes delta from a sensor whose state is 'nan' (vol.Coerce(float) accepts it). The part's stock drops to 0, out_of_stock fires when a reorder point is set, and an auto-buy task opens, with no error. delta: inf instead fails the service with an unlocalized OverflowError traceback. _normalize_stock guards the same inputs with math.isfinite (assets.py:453), but this path does not.
- **Suggested fix:** Reject a non-finite delta with a localized validation error (in adjust_part_stock or with a finite check in both schemas).
- **Verifier reason:** adjust_part_stock computes max(0.0, old + float(delta)) (assets.py:1368). With delta NaN, max returns 0.0, so the stock goes to 0 and the transition is 'out'. With delta inf, _round_stock calls int(inf) (assets.py:436), which raises OverflowError. Both schemas are a bare vol.Coerce(float), which accepts 'nan' and 'inf' (__init__.py:570, websocket_api.py:873). The number entity path fails first in round(nan / step), so the silent zero comes only from the service or websocket. Low: the input must be non-finite.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro2.py -> adjust nan: OK -> ('out', 0); adjust inf: OverflowError: cannot convert float infinity to integer; adjust -inf: OK -> ('out', 0)

### B05-6 — An infinite integer field raises a raw OverflowError, which escapes the import planner's error collection

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:415`
- **Defect:** int()/math.floor() on float('inf') raise OverflowError, which the normalizers do not catch (only TypeError/ValueError), and transfer.plan_import catches only AssetValidationError.
- **Failure scenario:** An import document (YAML keeps the float resolver, so `.inf` loads as a float) contains `replace_interval: .inf`, `carried_uses: .inf`, `replace_also_every: {interval: .inf}` or a document `size: .inf`. The dry run and the import both fail with an unhandled 'cannot convert float infinity to integer' traceback. They do not list a problem with its path, so the 'report every problem at once, each with a path' contract breaks. The task model has the same gap (models.py:741-743 with transfer.py:1527), so the two are consistent, but both are wrong.
- **Suggested fix:** Add OverflowError to the caught exceptions (or check math.isfinite first) in _normalize_interval, _normalize_carried_uses, _normalize_replace_also_every and the document size, and do the same in models.
- **Verifier reason:** int(inf) and math.floor(inf) raise OverflowError, and the normalizers catch only TypeError/ValueError (assets.py:414-417, 571-573, 597-602, 307-310). The import loader drops only the timestamp resolver (transfer.py:618-621), so '.inf' loads as a float. _plan_asset catches only AssetValidationError (transfer.py:1354), and async_import_document has no broader handler (transfer_runner.py:62). The error is a generic failure with no problem path. Nothing is written, because planning comes before any write. Low.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro2.py -> build part replace_interval=inf / carried_uses=inf / replace_also_every.interval=inf: OverflowError: cannot convert float infinity to integer

### B05-7 — _normalize_cost accepts NaN and infinity, so the appliance report totals read NaN

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:177`
- **Defect:** Asset and part cost go through float() with only a negativity check; NaN passes because nan < 0 is False.
- **Failure scenario:** update_asset with cost: 'nan' (the service schema is vol.Coerce(float)), or an import with `cost: .nan`, stores NaN. export_appliance_report then sums total_cost += NaN, and total_cost and grand_total come out as NaN. On the next restart the orjson store write turns NaN into null, so the cost silently becomes unset. _normalize_stock already refuses these values for this reason (assets.py:451-454).
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse a non-finite cost with 'cost must be a number', the same way as _normalize_stock.
- **Verifier reason:** _normalize_cost does float() plus a < 0 check only (assets.py:177-183), so NaN and inf pass. The asset schema is vol.Coerce(float) (__init__.py:491). appliance_report._num returns float(value) (appliance_report.py:28-33), and total_cost += NaN makes total_cost and grand_total NaN (appliance_report.py:106, 115-117). Low: the input must be non-finite.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro2.py -> asset cost nan: OK -> nan; asset cost 'nan' str: OK -> nan; asset cost inf: OK -> inf; part cost nan: OK -> nan

### B05-8 — A metadata text entry stores a null value as the text 'None' and a YAML boolean as 'True'/'False'; a null label is accepted as 'None'

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/assets.py:241`
- **Defect:** raw.get('value', '') returns None when the key is present with null, str(None) is 'None', and label and value skip the _reject_boolean guard that the other text fields use.
- **Failure scenario:** An import document has `- {type: text, label: Warranty, value: }` (a blank YAML value is null). The appliance shows the value 'None'. `value: no` is stored as 'False', which is the bug _reject_boolean was added to fix for part_number. `label: ~` becomes the label 'None' where it should fail with 'metadata label must not be empty'. Reproduced.
- **Suggested fix:** Use `raw.get('value') or ''` and `raw.get('label') or ''`, and pass both through _reject_boolean.
- **Verifier reason:** _normalize_metadata_entry uses str(raw.get('label', '')) and str(raw.get('value', '')) (assets.py:227, 241). A key that is present with null gives 'None', and a boolean gives 'True' or 'False'. _reject_boolean is not called here, but normalize_fields calls it for the other text fields (assets.py:1403). Date entries are safe, because _normalize_date maps None to '' (assets.py:160, 236). Low: an import with a blank YAML value is the realistic trigger.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B05/repro2.py -> value None: 'None'; value False: 'False'; label None: 'None'; label True: 'True'; date value None: ''

### B06-10 — Part upload keeps a same-name blob when metadata is rejected, orphaning it

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/manuals.py:759`
- **Defect:** The same-name exemption assumes the old file is still on disk, but os.replace already overwrote it, and the only rejection (KeyError) means no record references the blob any more.
- **Failure scenario:** A same-name re-upload of receipt.pdf runs while the appliance is deleted (or the part removed) in another tab or by an automation. delete_asset rmtree's the directory; _move's mkdir recreates it and drops the new blob in; set_part_file raises KeyError; because safe_name == old_filename the blob is kept. Nothing references <deleted-asset>/part_<id>__receipt.pdf, and no later rmtree will run for that asset id. The document view has the same recreated-empty-directory residue.
- **Suggested fix:** On KeyError delete the new blob unconditionally (the record it belonged to is gone); if the asset no longer exists, rmtree its directory too.
- **Verifier reason:** The trace holds. old_filename comes from the asset dict captured at admission (manuals.py:729-730). _move recreates the per-asset directory (manuals.py:141-145). store._mutate_asset raises KeyError for a deleted asset (store.py:1060-1062), and set_part_file raises it for a removed part (store.py:1143-1146). The same-name branch (manuals.py:759) then keeps a blob that no record references. It needs a race between a same-name re-upload and an appliance delete or part removal during streaming, so it is rare. The part-removal variant leaks anyway through B06-3.

### B06-4 — Cancelled upload leaks its temp file (CancelledError bypasses except Exception)

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/manuals.py:496`
- **Defect:** The temp-file cleanup in _stream_to_temp and _parse_upload catches only Exception, so a client abort that cancels the handler strands a partial upload of up to 100 MB.
- **Failure scenario:** User starts a 90 MB manual upload and presses the panel's Cancel upload button (xhr.abort()), closes the tab, or loses mobile signal. Home Assistant runs aiohttp with handler_cancellation enabled, so the handler task gets CancelledError at `await part.read_chunk()`. CancelledError is a BaseException: `except Exception` does not run, the partial temp stays in documents/.incoming/, and post() calls _parse_upload outside its try/finally. async_cleanup_temp_uploads runs only at entry setup and only removes files older than 24 h, so repeated cancels pile up and are included in backups until a later restart.
- **Suggested fix:** Use try/finally with a success flag (or `except BaseException`) in _stream_to_temp and _parse_upload, and run the unlink so a second cancel cannot skip it (e.g. schedule it with hass.async_add_executor_job without awaiting, or asyncio.shield). Add an integration test that aborts a large upload mid-body and asserts .incoming is empty.
- **Verifier fix note:** Use try/finally with a success flag (or except BaseException). For the unlink during cancellation, call the tiny sync unlink directly or shield the executor job, so a second cancel cannot skip it.
- **Verifier reason:** HA runs aiohttp with handler_cancellation=True (components/http/server.py:540-541 on dev), so a client abort cancels the handler task with CancelledError, which is a BaseException. The cleanup in _stream_to_temp (manuals.py:496) and _parse_upload (manuals.py:453) catches only Exception, and post() calls _parse_upload outside its try/finally (manuals.py:595, 713). The partial temp stays in documents/.incoming until a setup runs more than 24 h later (manuals.py:194-207, documents.purge_stale_temps). Severity lowered: disk only, up to 100 MB per cancelled upload, and it heals at the next restart or reload once the file is 24 h old.
- **Repro:** python3 repro_b06_4.py (aiohttp 3.14.3 = HA's pin, AppRunner(handler_cancellation=True), handler shaped like _stream_to_temp, raw client aborts mid-body) -> "exception seen by handler: {'base': 'CancelledError'} | temp files left: ['upload-km8v985q']"

### B06-6 — Non-Latin upload filenames collapse to underscores and become the document's display name

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/documents.py:59`
- **Defect:** safe_filename keeps only ASCII [A-Za-z0-9._-], and the panel uploads without a name, so the sanitized filename is what users see.
- **Failure scenario:** A Russian user uploads 'Инструкция.pdf' and 'Гарантия.pdf' to one appliance; a Chinese user uploads '说明书.pdf'. They are stored and listed as '__________.pdf', '________.pdf' and '___.pdf' (verified by running safe_filename). The two Russian documents are indistinguishable in the panel and on the card; a part's file (which has no separate display name) always shows the mangled name. German names lose every umlaut ('Kühlschrank' -> 'K_hlschrank').
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the ASCII segment only for the on-disk key; when no name part is sent, store the original client filename (NFC-normalized, control characters stripped, capped at 200) as the display name. Serve with an RFC 6266 `filename*=UTF-8''...` parameter so downloads keep the real name.
- **Verifier reason:** documents.safe_filename (documents.py:52-62) replaces every non [A-Za-z0-9._-] character. The panel uploads with no name (panel-asset-editors.ts:482), so the display name is the sanitized filename (assets.py:313). Severity lowered: a document can be renamed in the panel (updateAssetDocument, panel-asset-editors.ts:455). Only a part's file name, which has no rename, stays mangled.
- **Repro:** python3 repro_b06.py -> 'Инструкция.pdf -> __________.pdf', 'Гарантия.pdf -> ________.pdf', '说明书.pdf -> ___.pdf', 'Kühlschrank.pdf -> K_hlschrank.pdf'

### B06-7 — Non-finite costs (nan/inf) poison the appliance report totals

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/appliance_report.py:31`
- **Defect:** _num returns float('nan')/inf unchanged, and asset/part costs accept 'nan' and 'inf', so one bad value makes total_cost and grand_total NaN.
- **Failure scenario:** An automation or script calls home_keeper.update_asset with cost: 'nan' (or YAML .nan). vol.Coerce(float) and _normalize_cost accept it (nan < 0 is False). build_report then returns total_cost=nan and grand_total=nan (verified), the CSV TOTAL row prints 'nan', and the JSON totals serialize as null in the panel; 'inf' gives 'inf'.
- **Suggested fix:** Reject non-finite values in assets._normalize_cost/_normalize_stock with math.isfinite, and make _num return 0.0 for non-finite input so the report can never total to NaN.
- **Verifier reason:** assets._normalize_cost (assets.py:174-183) accepts 'nan' (nan < 0 is False), and appliance_report._num (appliance_report.py:28-33) passes NaN through, so total_cost and grand_total become NaN and the CSV TOTAL row prints 'nan'. It needs an unusual input (an automation or YAML .nan). HA's orjson storage writes NaN as null, so the value disappears after a restart.
- **Repro:** python3 repro_b06_7.py -> "stored cost: nan" / "totals: {'asset_count': 2, 'total_cost': nan, 'spares_value': 0.0, 'grand_total': nan}" / "TOTAL,,,,,nan,0.0,"

### B06-9 — Path.resolve() filesystem calls run in the event loop on every document GET and write

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/documents.py:138`
- **Defect:** resolve_under_root calls Path.resolve() (realpath, one lstat per path component) and is reached from async code outside the executor.
- **Failure scenario:** Every signed-URL GET, upload, rename and delete does two realpath walks on the loop thread. On an SD-card or USB-disk install under I/O pressure (backup, recorder purge) each lstat can block the loop for tens of milliseconds, stalling all of Home Assistant.
- **Suggested fix:** Compute and verify the path inside the executor job (pass asset_id/document_id/filename into _move/_unlink/_rmtree/is_file helpers that call documents.document_path themselves).
- **Verifier reason:** resolve_under_root calls Path.resolve() twice (documents.py:138-139). It runs on the event loop from _document_path at manuals.py:176, 184-185, 214, 220 and 521. The 'stall all of HA' impact is speculative: these are lstat calls on hot, dentry-cached paths, and HA's blocking-call detector does not list Path.resolve or os.path.realpath (block_async_io.py). It is a best-practice gap, not a practical stall.

### B07-7 — Skipping a one-off strands it: no completion, never auto-deleted, and deleting the skip does not re-arm it

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:608`
- **Defect:** skip_occurrence sends a one-off dormant without a completion, so one_off_completed is False and retention never removes it, and remove_skip never re-arms it even though a one-off's due date is known.
- **Failure scenario:** Armed one-off (panel/card offer Skip, defer.ts:65). User taps Skip: next_due None, last_completed None. one_off_expired(task, 7, now=+1 year) is False, so it never auto-deletes. User deletes the skip from history (guide: 'removes an entry and undoes the skip'): next_due stays None. Snooze and Due today refuse a dormant task, so only editing the due date to a different value re-arms it.
- **Suggested fix:** In remove_skip (or store.delete_skip) re-arm a one-off with no completions to its due date, mirroring remove_completion; optionally let one_off_expired count a skipped one-off from its skip ts.
- **Verifier reason:** skip_occurrence sends a one-off dormant with no completion (recurrence.py:604-608). one_off_completed needs last_completed (recurrence.py:888-892), so retention never deletes it (coordinator.py:284), the to-do list drops it (todo.py:84), and it is not in the panel Completed section. delete_skip never re-derives next_due (recurrence.py:825, store.py:2318-2357), although the guide says delete_skip 'undoes the skip' (docs/guide/tasks/snooze-and-skip.md:48) and a one-off's due is known. Skip is offered on an armed one-off (defer.ts:65). Low: Done still shows in the panel list, and completing it makes it eligible for retention.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e9.py -> 'after skip: None completed? False expired in a year (retention 7d)? False' / 'after delete_skip (undo): None skips []'

### B07-8 — Fixed task on the DST spring-forward day: completing or skipping before a gap-hour occurrence does nothing

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:431`  **Also reported as:** X07-1
- **Defect:** An occurrence in the skipped hour is stored as e.g. 02:30-05:00 and re-read as 03:30 EDT, which _is_occurrence does not match against the grid's nonexistent 02:30, so the advance falls back to now and returns the same occurrence.
- **Failure scenario:** America/New_York, fixed DAILY or WEEKLY (Sunday) anchored 02:30. next_due 2026-03-08T02:30-05:00. Done at 01:00 on 2026-03-08, or Skip the evening before: next_due stays 2026-03-08T02:30-05:00. _is_occurrence returns False for it. Reproduced.
- **Suggested fix:** Compare instants in _is_occurrence (e.g. both .astimezone(timezone.utc)) or normalise grid occurrences through a UTC round trip before comparing.
- **Verifier reason:** A gap-hour occurrence is stored as 02:30-05:00 and re-homed to 03:30 EDT (recurrence.py:474). _is_occurrence compares by wall clock in one tzinfo (recurrence.py:431-435), and the grid steps to the nonexistent 02:30, so it answers False and the advance falls back to now (recurrence.py:481-483), which returns the same occurrence. Only anchors inside the DST gap hour, on the transition day. Property test R7 uses a fixed offset.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e2.py -> 'DAILY after completing at 01:00 on gap day: 2026-03-08T02:30:00-05:00' / 'DAILY after skipping the evening before: 2026-03-08T02:30:00-05:00' / '_is_occurrence(gap occurrence): False' (same for WEEKLY)

### B07-9 — Fixed season clamp can return an out-of-season date; calendar shows a different, in-season date

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/recurrence.py:163`
- **Defect:** _clamp_season takes the first grid occurrence after the season start without checking it is in season, while calendar._next_start walks forward until in_season, so next_due and the calendar disagree for a sparse grid and a short season.
- **Failure scenario:** Fixed WEEKLY interval 2 anchored Fri 2026-01-09, season 12-01..12-10. compute_next_due on 2026-09-30 gives 2026-12-11 (out of season); the to-do list and panel show it due Dec 11, while the calendar's first in-season date is 2027-12-10. Reproduced for 4 of 14 anchor weekdays.
- **Suggested fix:** Loop in _clamp_season (bounded like the calendar) until the occurrence is in season, and share one helper with calendar.py.
- **Verifier fix note:** Share one bounded walk with calendar.py. When the bound runs out, both surfaces need the same answer (the calendar returns None today).
- **Verifier reason:** _clamp_season (recurrence.py:157-165) returns the first grid occurrence after the season start with no in_season check, while calendar._next_start (calendar.py:102-117) walks until in_season and states it never invents an out-of-season date. For a sparse grid and a short season the to-do list and panel show an out-of-season next_due that the calendar does not. test_recurrence_properties.py R4 excludes fixed tasks only for a grid that never meets its season; a grid that meets it in a later year is not covered.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B07/e11.py -> 'anchor 2026-01-09 next_due 2026-12-11 (out of season) ; calendar's first in-season: 2027-12-10' (4 of 14 anchors)

### B08-5 — Usage baseline is carried across a binding that reads a different quantity, or from a stale block

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/models.py:1051`
- **Defect:** The baseline carry-forward only compares entity_id, so changing the attribute (a different meter on the same entity) or re-converting a task that kept a stale sensor block keeps an unrelated baseline and the task can arm at once.
- **Failure scenario:** Usage task on vacuum.robo attribute cleaning_time, baseline 1200. The user switches the binding to attribute total_cleaned_area (reads 48,000): the merged binding keeps baseline 1200 (reproduced), so 48,000-1,200 >= target and evaluate_usage arms it on the next tick. Second path: sensor task (baseline 1000) converted to floating keeps its stale sensor block; months later converted back to a usage binding on the same entity without a baseline -> baseline 1000 is carried (reproduced) instead of anchoring at the live reading as a new binding should, and the task arms immediately.
- **Suggested fix:** Carry the baseline only when the existing task is a sensor task in usage mode and entity_id and attribute are both unchanged.
- **Verifier fix note:** The panel path bypasses this carry-forward because it sends the old baseline explicitly, so the form also has to clear sensor_baseline when the entity or attribute changes.
- **Verifier reason:** The carry-forward (models.py:1051-1059) checks entity_id only, so a service update that changes the attribute, or re-binds a task that kept a stale sensor block, keeps the old baseline. evaluate_usage has no jump guard and arms when reading - baseline >= target (sensor_tasks.py:375-411). Low: the panel shows and sends the baseline box on edit (forms.ts:591-594, 1091-1093), so a panel user sees the old number; the silent path is update_task from a service.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B08/sim2.py -> "attr change -> sensor: {... 'attribute': 'total_cleaned_area', 'target': 5000.0, 'baseline': 1200.0}" / "back to sensor: {... 'baseline': 1000.0} next_due None"

### B08-6 — INTEGRATING.md says the owner can change a locked field through update_task, but merge_update strips it for every caller

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/models.py:1002`
- **Defect:** locked_fields are removed from every update_task payload with no owner bypass, so an integration that follows the documented rename pattern gets a silent no-op.
- **Failure scenario:** An integration locks 'name' (as the docs example suggests) and later calls update_task(task_id, name='Buddy medicine') when the pet is renamed, as INTEGRATING.md instructs. merge_update drops 'name', the service returns success, and the task keeps the old name forever. Home Keeper's own reconcilers needed private bypasses (store.async_set_declarative_notes, async_repoint_device_ids) for exactly this reason; outside integrations have none.
- **Suggested fix:** Either fix the docs (owners cannot change locked fields through update_task; tell them to delete and re-add, or not to lock fields they must change) or add an explicit owner path (e.g. an origin/config_entry match) that skips the strip.
