- **Verifier fix note:** The docs fix is the safe option. An owner bypass is a new service contract (one-way door) and needs a privilege story, because any caller can claim to be the owner.
- **Verifier reason:** merge_update strips locked fields from every update_task payload with no owner path (models.py:1002-1006), and store.update_task adds none (store.py:458-472). docs/INTEGRATING.md:357-359 tells integrators the opposite. The repository's own code says the lock drops the owner's writes (store.py:1232-1238, declarative_companion_sync.py:381-383). Low: a documentation contract error; the failed update is silent but loses no data, and an integrator's first test shows it.
- **Repro:** Code trace: tests/unit/test_models.py:397-422 shows locked name and device_id ignored by merge_update for any caller; nothing identifies the owner.

### B08-7 — Stale schedule fields survive a type change and are reused on a later conversion back

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/models.py:1024`
- **Defect:** merge_update starts from dict(existing) and normalize_fields never removes fields the new type does not use, so the candidate later reads the stale due/anchor/sensor instead of the documented defaults.
- **Failure scenario:** One-off due 2026-01-10 converted to floating keeps due. Months later a service call update_task {recurrence_type: one-off} (no due) reuses due 2026-01-10 instead of the documented 'defaults to now', so the task is instantly overdue with an old date (reproduced). The stale sensor block (with baseline) is also exported and fed into B08-5.
- **Suggested fix:** On a recurrence_type change, drop the fields the new type does not use from merged, and build the candidate without the old type's schedule fields.
- **Verifier reason:** merge_update starts from dict(existing) (models.py:1008) and reads the old due (models.py:1024). The 'now' default applies only when no due exists (models.py:1037-1038), so a service conversion back to one-off without a due reuses a stale date and is overdue at once, against the code's own comment at models.py:1034-1036. The panel shows the stale due in its picker (forms.ts:844), so only the service path is silent. A stale sensor block is a known state (utils.ts:346-347 guards on recurrence_type).
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B08/sim2.py -> 'floating keeps due: 2026-01-10T09:00:00-08:00' / 'back to one-off due: 2026-01-10T09:00:00-08:00 next_due 2026-01-10T09:00:00-08:00 overdue True'

### B08-8 — update_task accepts and documents 'source', but merge_update silently ignores it

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/models.py:1011`
- **Defect:** The service schema and services.yaml list source as an update_task field, yet the candidate never reads it, so the call succeeds and changes nothing.
- **Failure scenario:** An integration calls update_task(task_id, source={'my_integration': {'schedule_id': 'new'}}) to repoint its provenance. The call returns success, the stored source is unchanged, and the integration's later list_tasks match on the new id fails, so it creates a duplicate task.
- **Suggested fix:** Remove source from UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA and services.yaml (or reject it with a clear error). Do not start honoring it without the reserved-namespace check store.add_task applies.
- **Verifier fix note:** Removing source from the schema makes existing callers that send it fail validation, which is a contract change. The least disruptive fix is to say in services.yaml that update_task ignores it, as INTEGRATING.md:779 already says for update_asset.
- **Verifier reason:** UPDATE_TASK_SCHEMA accepts source (__init__.py:217) and services.yaml documents it for update_task, but neither handle_update_task (__init__.py:1191-1200), store.update_task (store.py:458-496) nor merge_update (models.py:1011-1033) reads it: the call succeeds and changes nothing. The frontend relies on merge_update never merging source (utils.ts:337-338), so ignoring it is intended; the defect is the advertised field.
- **Repro:** Code trace only (no key named 'source' is read after the schema on the update path).

### B09-5 — Renaming a generated or locked task from the HA to-do card silently fails or reverts later

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo.py:143`
- **Defect:** The rename path calls store.update_task without checking ownership: a locked name is stripped with no error and snaps back, and a wear-part/use task name is accepted and then rewritten by the next reconcile with no event.
- **Failure scenario:** (a) Problem-sensor mirror or declarative companion task (managed_by.locked_fields has 'name'): user renames it in the to-do card; merge_update strips 'name', update_task saves, fires no event, the refresh restores the old name, and the card shows no error. (b) Wear-part replacement or use task: rename persists, then the next reconcile_part_tasks (any appliance edit, every restart/reload) rewrites the generated name; reconcile edits fire no task_updated event, so automations and synced lists are not told. The entity docstring promises 'a rename must actually persist rather than silently revert'. The panel hides Edit for derived tasks, so the to-do card is the surface where this looks like it worked. Reproduced: renamed derived task -> reconcile_part_tasks returns the generated name with changed=True.
- **Suggested fix:** In async_update_todo_item, refuse a rename of a task whose name is owned (non-manual part source, or 'name' in managed_by.locked_fields) with a translated HomeAssistantError that points to the owner (part editor / integration). Better: have store.update_task raise TaskValidationError for a changed locked field from user surfaces, as it already does for a part-owned tag.
- **Verifier fix note:** For (b), refuse a name change on a non-manual part-owned task in store.update_task with the same pattern as the tag guard (a translated TaskValidationError that points to the part editor). Do not change the locked_fields contract for (a) without updating INTEGRATING.md.
- **Verifier reason:** Only part (b) is a defect. (a) Silently dropping a locked 'name' is the documented contract: docs/INTEGRATING.md:313 says 'User edits are silently ignored by update_task', and models.merge_update (models.py:1001-1006) implements it. The to-do card reverting is therefore by design, although todo.py:116-118's docstring overstates it. (b) Traced: todo.py:142-151 -> store.update_task accepts a rename of a non-manual wear-part/use task and fires task_updated (store.py:458-496). Only a tag change is refused (_is_part_owned_tag_update, :466-471). The next reconcile_part_tasks (setup, reload, any asset change) rewrites the generated name (reconcile.py:726-727) and fires no update event (store.py:1555-1559). Buy reminders keep a typed name (_name_is_ours, reconcile.py:874-878); wear-part tasks do not. The rename is lost at the next restart, nothing else is.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B09/v5.py -> 'after rename: Swap the fridge filter' / 'after reconcile: Replace Filter (Fridge) changed: True' / 'locked rename: Replace Filter (Fridge)'.

### B09-6 — Open buy reminders never follow the appliance's device or area change

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/reconcile.py:870`
- **Defect:** reconcile_buy_tasks updates only the name of an existing reminder; device_id and area_id stay at their values from the time the reminder was created.
- **Failure scenario:** Part is low, 'Buy Filter' exists on device devOld/area areaOld. User re-links the appliance to another device or moves it to another area. The wear-part task moves (reconcile.py:738-741) but the buy reminder keeps devOld/areaOld: its per-task entities stay on the old device (or fall back to a self-owned device with a warning if devOld is gone), and area-filtered profiles, cards and to-do syncs mis-file it until the episode ends. Reproduced: after the move the wear task shows devNew/areaNew, 'Buy Filter' shows devOld/areaOld.
- **Suggested fix:** For an open reminder, also diff device_id/area_id against the asset and include them in the merge_update (and have store.reconcile_buy_tasks report an entity-set change when device_id moves).
- **Verifier fix note:** Diff device_id/area_id for an open reminder in the existing-key branch. The asset-change path already reloads the entry, so the entity move takes effect there. store.reconcile_buy_tasks's entity_set_changed only matters for the coordinator path.
- **Verifier reason:** reconcile.py:870-885 updates only the name of an existing reminder. The docstring (:836-839) says the name is its only update but gives no reason for leaving device/area. The part-task branch does follow the asset (:738-741). devices.async_apply_asset_change (devices.py:130-145) reconciles both and then reloads, so the reminder's per-task entities stay on the old device and its area filters stay stale until the low episode ends. A reminder lives only for one episode, and moving an appliance during one is rare, so low.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B09/v6.py -> 'buy after move: devOld areaOld changed: False'.

### B10-6 — A ':' in a profile id makes every pass remove and re-add each line, and ticks never complete

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list.py:414`
- **Defect:** Keys are split on the first ':' on the assumption that profile ids are uuid hex, but normalize_profile accepts any caller-supplied id, so a set_options call with id 'kids:weekly' makes every entry look like it belongs to a deleted profile.
- **Failure scenario:** Automation or developer-tools call home_keeper.set_options with profiles [{id: 'kids:weekly', sync: {entity_id: todo.kids}}]. Key 'kids:weekly:t1' partitions to profile 'kids' -> by_id miss -> deleted-profile branch removes the line, then the desired loop adds it again: remove+add every pass, forever (needs_pass always True). A household tick drops the entry without completing the task and a fresh line is added. Verified with the real planner.
- **Suggested fix:** Reject or sanitize ':' in profile ids in normalize_profile (ids are minted as uuids anyway), or store profile_id and task_id inside the entry instead of parsing the key.
- **Verifier reason:** normalize_profile keeps any caller-supplied id (profiles.py:122) and set_options accepts a bare list (__init__.py:791); keys are split on the first ':' (todo_list.py:414, 660). An id 'kids:weekly' parses as profile 'kids', so the entry takes the deleted-profile branch (remove) and the desired loop adds again every pass. Only reachable with a hand-written id through the service or an edited import document; the panel mints uuid hex.

### B10-8 — Sweep and task events run a pass before Home Assistant has started, logging a false, latched 'list does not exist' warning

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list_sync.py:147`
- **Defect:** The first pass is deferred to async_at_started so other integrations' lists exist, but the setup-time coordinator refresh (and problem/sensor task events) call async_schedule_sweep / async_sync during startup, bypassing that deferral.
- **Failure scenario:** HA restart where the Todoist/Google Tasks integration finishes setup more than ~10 s after Home Keeper. The debounced refresh requested at __init__.py:949 runs _async_update_data -> async_schedule_sweep -> forced pass; the target has no state yet (restored unavailable states are written only at START), so _read_lists warns 'Home Keeper's synced to-do list ... does not exist; its tasks are not being kept in step'. The _warned latch then suppresses the warning for a real missing list until the next reload. The pass itself does no harm.
- **Suggested fix:** Gate async_schedule_sweep and _handle_task_event (or async_sync) on hass.state being running / a started flag set by async_initial_sync.
- **Verifier reason:** async_schedule_sweep has no started check (todo_list_sync.py:140-153); the coordinator calls it on every refresh (coordinator.py:260-261), and setup requests a refresh after the sync exists (__init__.py:949), which the refresh debouncer can run before HA starts. A target of a not-yet-loaded integration has no state before EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_START, so _warn_once logs 'does not exist' and latches it (todo_sync_driver.py:203-209, 281-285). The pass itself is harmless; the effect is a false startup warning and a suppressed later real one.

### B10-9 — User guide says a Home Keeper completion ticks the line off, but the default overdue profile deletes it

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list.py:524`
- **Defect:** plan_sync checks `want is None` (remove) before completed_since (tick off), so on overdue/due_soon profiles and for one-off tasks the line is removed, contradicting the guide and the planner's own comment.
- **Failure scenario:** Default profile (status overdue) synced to Todoist. User taps Done in the panel: the task reschedules out of the filter, want is None, remove_item deletes the Todoist task instead of completing it, so it never shows in the project's completed history. Guide line 34 promises 'the item is marked complete on the list'. Integration tests deliberately pin removal for overdue profiles, so this is doc/behavior drift rather than an accident; the unit test at test_todo_list.py:624 is named 'only_ticks_the_item_off' but asserts a RemoveOp.
- **Suggested fix:** Either tick off when the live task's last_completed is newer than the entry snapshot even if it no longer matches (pass last_completed for all tracked tasks into the planner), or correct the guide and the misnamed test.
- **Verifier fix note:** Fix the guide and the test name. Do not change the planner.
- **Verifier reason:** Doc drift only; the behavior is intended. tests/integration/test_todo_list_sync.py:26-31 pins removal for an overdue profile, and the guide's third bullet already says a rescheduled task's open item is removed, which contradicts its second bullet (docs/guide/views/todo-sync.md:34). The unit test at tests/unit/test_todo_list.py:624 is named only_ticks_the_item_off but asserts a RemoveOp.

### B11-3 — A shopper's edit to a description Home Keeper wrote is reverted on the next sweep

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/shopping.py:405`
- **Defect:** user_described is true only when Home Keeper never recorded a description, so a note the shopper types over the amount Home Keeper wrote is overwritten on the next forced pass.
- **Failure scenario:** List with SET_DESCRIPTION_ON_ITEM; the mirror wrote '500 ml' (tracked description '500 ml'). The shopper edits the line's description to '500 ml, the blue Lenor bottle'. A description edit does not change the list count, but the 5-minute sweep forces a pass: current != entry description, user_described = bool(current) and not entry.get('description') = False, so UpdateOp(description='500 ml') replaces the note. Each later edit is reverted again. A title edit in the same place is kept (user_named). Reproduced with the pure planner.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat a live description that differs from both what Home Keeper last recorded and what it wants now as the shopper's (the same rule as user_named): set user_described and do not write the amount while it stands.
- **Verifier fix note:** Use the same rule as user_named: a live description that is neither the recorded one nor the wanted one belongs to the shopper. A list that normalizes text or shows an update late would then freeze the amount, so test that case.
- **Verifier reason:** shopping.py:405 sets user_described only when the tracked entry has no description. A shopper's edit of a description Home Keeper wrote therefore reads as Home Keeper's, and shopping.py:406-413 writes the amount back on the next forced pass (the 5-minute sweep: coordinator.py:47, 253-254; shopping_sync.py:107-109). Reproduced. This breaks the plan_sync docstring (shopping.py:312-313: a note Home Keeper did not write is not replaced). test_shopping.py:1130-1136 pins only the case where the live note still equals what Home Keeper wrote. Low: it affects only lists with SET_DESCRIPTION_ON_ITEM (HA's own Shopping List has none, core shopping_list/todo.py:36-39), and what is lost is the shopper's note text.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B11/repro_b11_3.py: edited note -> UpdateOp(description='500 ml'); the same note on an entry with no recorded description -> no update, user_described=True

### B11-4 — Failed remove plus failed add for the same key (target switch) drops the old list's bookkeeping; its line is orphaned forever

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/shopping_sync.py:250`
- **Defect:** On a target change the plan holds a RemoveOp (old list) and an AddOp (new list) under one key and one tracked slot; _apply's restore for the failed remove is then clobbered by the add's bookkeeping or popped by the add's failure.
- **Failure scenario:** Target moved from todo.a to todo.b (options save, reload). Pass: RemoveOp(K, todo.a) and AddOp(K, todo.b); plan.tracked[K] points at todo.b. (1) The remove fails (todo.a cloud API error or no DELETE feature) and the add fails too: settled[K] is restored to the todo.a entry, then `settled.pop(add.key)` deletes it. The todo.a line has no bookkeeping and is never removed or synced (the orphan the SyncPlan docstring says must not happen). (2) The remove fails and the add succeeds: the restore overwrites the todo.b entry, so the new line on todo.b is untracked. If the shopper ticks it before a later pass re-adopts it, find_open skips a ticked line, a duplicate is added, and the reminder is not completed.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep separate bookkeeping for a key's in-flight move (for example track the old-list entry under a secondary slot), or have the planner not emit the AddOp for a key until its RemoveOp on the old list has succeeded (add on the next pass). At minimum, in _apply do not pop a key on a failed add when a failed remove for that key restored it.
- **Verifier fix note:** Do not emit a key's AddOp in the same pass as its RemoveOp (add after the remove lands), or keep the pending remove in a second slot. At minimum, do not pop a key on a failed add when a failed remove restored it.
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced against the real ShoppingListSync._apply. On a target switch the plan holds RemoveOp(K, old) and AddOp(K, new) for one key (shopping.py:372-376, 460-467). If the remove fails and the add fails, settled is {} (shopping_sync.py:222-223 restores the entry, then 250-251 pops it). The old line is then never read or removed again, because lists_to_read follows only tracked entries (shopping.py:471-485). If the remove fails and the add succeeds, the old entry overwrites the new line's bookkeeping. The add's own state change normally re-adopts the open line through find_open on the next pass. With a permanent remove failure (an old list without DELETE), the new line is never tracked, a tick on it is ignored, and another line is added. Low: it needs a target switch and a failed remove.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B11/repro_b11_4.py: remove fails + add fails -> {}; remove fails + add works -> {'a1:p1': {'entity_id': 'todo.a', ...}}

### B11-5 — Armed sensor and triggered tasks are never on the calendar, contrary to the user guide

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/calendar.py:89`
- **Defect:** calendar.py drops REC_SENSOR and REC_TRIGGERED tasks in both event and async_get_events, while the guide says an armed sensor task 'is on the to-do list and the calendar' and describes triggered and declarative tasks leaving the calendar when they go dormant.
- **Failure scenario:** A mileage sensor task (or a declarative-companion task such as 'Firmware update available') arms: next_due is set to the arming time, and it is on the to-do list and overdue sensor. It is absent from calendar.home_keeper_upcoming_tasks and from calendar.get_events, so a calendar card or calendar automation built on the documented behaviour never sees it. A one-off task with the same kind of single next_due is shown.
- **Suggested fix:** Decide one contract. Either show an armed sensor/triggered task as a single event at its next_due (the floating branch already does this), or correct the three guide pages to say these tasks never appear on the calendar.
- **Verifier fix note:** Pick one contract. To show armed tasks: one event at next_due (the arming time) falls off one hour later under the floating rule, so a 'due now' event needs its own window. Otherwise fix the 3 guide pages.
- **Verifier reason:** calendar.py:86-90 and 137-138 always drop REC_SENSOR and REC_TRIGGERED tasks, armed or not. But sensor-tasks.md:49-50 says an armed sensor task 'is on the to-do list and the calendar', triggered-tasks.md:12-13 says a completed triggered task 'leaves ... the calendar', settings.md:59-61 says the same about declarative tasks, and SENSOR_TASKS_PLAN.md:129-130 planned it. The code comment says the exclusion is deliberate, so the docs and the code disagree. Low: no data effect, and the task shows on every other surface.

### B11-6 — First mirror pass with a measured or multi-restock part loads Babel CLDR data on the event loop

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/shopping.py:161`
- **Defect:** buy_tasks_by_part runs in _sync_once on the loop and calls part_restock_label -> format_quantity -> decimal_mark, whose first call per language does Locale.parse/get_decimal_symbol (open() of Babel's global.dat and locale pickles); nothing warms it in an executor.
- **Failure scenario:** HA starts, no notification has been built yet (the notifier runs Babel in an executor, which would warm the cache). A part with stock_unit 'ml' or restock_quantity 4 is low; the initial mirror pass formats '500 ml' / 'x4' on the loop, and HA's blocking-call detector logs 'Detected blocking call to open ... inside the event loop by custom integration home_keeper' (the #150/#247 class). tests/integration/test_event_loop_blocking.py reads the error log before test_shopping_sync.py (alphabetical order) runs its restock_quantity=4 part, so it cannot catch this.
- **Suggested fix:** Warm decimal_mark(hass.config.language) (and 'en') in the executor next to backend_i18n.preload in async_setup_entry, or compute the desired map in async_add_executor_job.
- **Verifier fix note:** Warm decimal_mark(hass.config.language) in the executor next to backend_i18n.preload. Also note that users with no mirror target hit this path, because desired is built before the target check.
- **Verifier reason:** shopping_sync._sync_once builds desired with buy_tasks_by_part on the event loop before it checks the target (shopping_sync.py:117-133). async_initial_sync runs it with force=True after every HA start, whether the mirror is on or off (__init__.py:914-920, todo_sync_driver.py:117-124). For a part with a stock unit or restock_quantity > 1, part_restock_label -> format_quantity -> decimal_mark (assets.py:1117-1130, 1174, 1190-1195) calls Locale.parse and get_decimal_symbol. The first call in a process opens Babel data files. A spy on the real code saw global.dat, root.dat and en.dat opened in the first pass and nothing in the second. Nothing warms this in an executor: backend_i18n.preload loads only JSON, and the notifier runs Babel in an executor only when it sends. HA's detector logs a blocking open by the custom integration. Low: once per start, a few ms.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B11/repro_b11_6.py: first pass [exists en.dat, open global.dat, open root.dat, open UTC, open en.dat]; second pass []

### B11-7 — Fixed-task range query returns an occurrence that ended exactly at the window start (floating does not)

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/calendar.py:148`
- **Defect:** expand_fixed_occurrences is half-open [start-1h, end), so an occurrence starting at start-1h (ending exactly at start) is returned, while the floating branch requires due + 1h > start.
- **Failure scenario:** Daily fixed task at 06:00. An automation at 07:00 calls calendar.get_events for [07:00, 22:00) to list today's remaining chores: the 06:00-07:00 occurrence is returned although it does not overlap the window (verified: ['2026-10-02T06:00:00-04:00']). A floating task at the same instant is excluded. An automation that queries [00:00, 24:00) also gets yesterday's 23:00 chore.
- **Suggested fix:** Filter the expanded occurrences with `occ + EVENT_DURATION > start_date` (or expand from start_date - EVENT_DURATION + 1 microsecond).
- **Verifier fix note:** Filter occ + EVENT_DURATION > start_date, as suggested.
- **Verifier reason:** calendar.py:144-150 expands the half-open range [start-1h, end) (recurrence.py:307-314). An occurrence that starts at start-1h, and so ends exactly at start_date, is returned. The floating branch requires due + 1h > start (calendar.py:159). HA returns the entity's list without a filter from both the HTTP view and the get_events service (core calendar/__init__.py:826-838, 1121-1129). Reproduced: the window [07:00, 22:00) returns the fixed 06:00-07:00 event and not the floating one. Low: boundary only.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B11/repro_b11_7.py: [('Fixed 06:00', '2026-10-02T06:00:00-04:00', '2026-10-02T07:00:00-04:00')]

### B11-8 — Season walk in calendar.event is quadratic for a 29th-31st monthly anchor whose grid never lands in season (~170 ms, 3x per state write)

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/calendar.py:109`
- **Defect:** Up to MAX_EXPAND_ITERATIONS (500) calls of next_fixed_occurrence, and each re-steps from the anchor in _fast_forward when the anchor day stays above 28, so one misconfigured task blocks the event loop on every calendar state write.
- **Failure scenario:** Fixed task 'every 12 months' anchored Jan 31, active season Apr-Sep (the grid never enters the season). Measured with the real engine: 166 ms per event access. HA's CalendarEntity reads self.event in state, state_attributes and _async_write_ha_state, so each coordinator update (every 5 min and on every task change) blocks the loop for about 0.5 s.
- **Suggested fix:** Step the grid incrementally inside the walk (recurrence._step from occ) instead of re-solving from the anchor, and/or cache the per-task next start until the coordinator data changes.
- **Verifier fix note:** Step from occ inside the walk (recurrence._step) and stop re-solving from the anchor, or stop early when the grid's months repeat without entering the season.
- **Verifier reason:** calendar.py:109-116 calls next_fixed_occurrence up to 500 times. For an anchor day above 28 on a grid that never reaches a short month (every 12 months from Jan 31), recurrence._fast_forward (recurrence.py:233-241) steps from the anchor on every call, so the walk is quadratic. Nothing checks that a fixed grid can enter its season (models.py:604-645). Measured with the real code: anchor Jan 31, every 12 months, season Apr-Sep -> event None in 216.6 ms (Jan 28: 4.0 ms). HA's CalendarEntity reads self.event 3 times per state write (core calendar/__init__.py:573-629), which blocks the loop for about 0.5-0.65 s on each coordinator update. Low: it needs a contradictory seasonal task with an anchor on day 29-31.
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B11/repro_b11_8.py: 'anchor Jan 28 ... in 4.0 ms'; 'anchor Jan 31 ... in 216.6 ms'

### B12-4 — Every registry event re-renders state-quoting templates for all tasks: event storm with changed_fields [], one full-store write per spec, and a full reload when a name quotes state

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_companions.py:844`
- **Defect:** Passes run on any entity/device/area registry event anywhere in HA, and owned notes/names are re-rendered from the current reading, so a pass almost always changes state-quoting tasks; the store then fires home_keeper_task_updated with an empty changed_fields per task and saves the whole store once per spec.
- **Failure scenario:** A user has 5 integration-preset companions (all notes use `{{ state }}`) with 40 tasks whose readings drift (filter hours, ink %). Renaming any unrelated entity fires one registry event; the pass rewrites up to 40 notes, fires up to 40 home_keeper_task_updated events whose changed_fields is [] (api_surface declares it as 'which stored fields actually changed', so an automation filtering on 'notes'/'name' never matches and one reacting to any update is spammed), and does up to 5 full JSON store writes. If a user name template quotes state (e.g. `{{ friendly_name }} ({{ state }}%)`), each such event also changes entity_set_key and reloads the entire Home Keeper entry. The listeners have no filter, so Home Keeper's own entity events and option-only updates trigger passes too.
- **Suggested fix:** Return the changed field names from reconcile_declarative_tasks and pass them as changed_fields; collect all specs' ops and save once per pass; skip registry events for platform home_keeper and for changes that cannot affect selection or rendering; consider rendering state-quoting notes only at creation and on arm (async_refresh_task_notes), not on unrelated registry events.
- **Verifier fix note:** Returning the real changed field names from reconcile_declarative_tasks is the important part (fixes the event contract); a single save per pass is a secondary optimization.
- **Verifier reason:** Registry listeners are unfiltered (declarative_companion_sync.py:409-433); owned notes are re-rendered each pass (declarative_companions.py:844-856); store fires task_updated with changed_fields [] (store.py:1931-1934) although api_surface.py:340 documents it as the fields that changed; _save writes the whole store once per changed spec (store.py:316-326, 1902). Steady-state registry events are infrequent and bursts are debounced to 2 passes, and shipped preset names do not quote state, so the reload sub-case needs a user template. Real contract inaccuracy and write amplification, low impact.

### B13-1 — LG ThinQ alert preset also matches the fridge's fresh-air-filter percentage entity, which can never reach state 'replace'

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_presets_catalog.py:1255`
- **Defect:** Upstream lg_thinq gives translation_key 'fresh_air_filter' to both an ENUM sensor and a PERCENTAGE sensor on every refrigerator, so the lg_thinq_alert_replace preset makes a second task per fridge that is bound to a number and can never arm.
- **Failure scenario:** User with an LG refrigerator installs 'LG ThinQ: service alerts'. Selection {target_integration: lg_thinq, domain: sensor, translation_keys: ['fresh_air_filter']} matches two entities on the fridge device: the ENUM mode sensor (states auto/off/power/replace/smart_on...) and the FRESH_AIR_FILTER_REMAIN_PERCENT sensor (state e.g. '37', unit %). Both get a state-mode trigger {state: 'replace'}. The percentage task is permanently dormant (a number never equals 'replace'), so the percentage reading is never evaluated by any preset (fresh_air_filter is not in lg_thinq_percent_low). Because the preset has one key, it uses _NAME_BY_DEVICE, so both tasks are named 'Replace the filter: <Fridge>' and the user cannot tell the dead one from the live one. The picker 'matches' count also over-reports.
- **Suggested fix:** Give shapes a way to pin the entity kind: e.g. add an optional device_class ('enum') to the alert duty and put it into selection.device_class, or add a per-duty unit guard. Then add fresh_air_filter to the percent_low duty of lg_thinq (the '%' variant) so the percentage reading is watched. Add a unit test that no alert preset key is shared with a numeric entity where upstream is known to reuse keys.
- **Verifier fix note:** Setting selection.device_class 'enum' works here, because the snapshot falls back to original_device_class (declarative_companions.py:427). But the preset group key is (shape, platform, state) (declarative_presets.py:529), so a per-duty device_class also needs to be part of that key. Do not just add fresh_air_filter to lg_thinq percent_low. That preset would then match the enum entity too, and threshold mode treats 'auto' as indeterminate (sensor_tasks.py:116-126, 594), so it would make the same dead task in the other preset. Guard it with a unit check, as life_low does, or pin the kind there as well.
- **Verifier reason:** The lg_thinq alert duty selects translation key fresh_air_filter with state 'replace' (declarative_presets_catalog.py:1252-1257). _integration_presets puts no device_class or unit into the selection (declarative_presets.py:568-578), and the result has no device_class. _entity_matches supports a device_class filter (declarative_companions.py:425-429), but the preset does not use it. I fetched upstream home-assistant/core dev lg_thinq/sensor.py myself. FRESH_AIR_FILTER (ENUM) and FRESH_AIR_FILTER_REMAIN_PERCENT (%) both use translation_key fresh_air_filter (sensor.py:223-232), and DeviceType.REFRIGERATOR maps both (sensor.py:539-541). thinqconnect has both properties in the one 'refrigeration' resource. expand_spec matches both entities and binds the % entity to {mode: state, state: 'replace'} (reproduced). That task is created dormant (declarative_companions.py:713-741) and can never arm. It has the same name as the live task (one key, so _NAME_BY_DEVICE). No other lg_thinq preset watches fresh_air_filter. Two corrections: the % entity exists only when the device profile has the property (get_active_idx, sensor.py:669-683), so the claim 'every refrigerator' is too strong. The notes template shows the state and unit (declarative_presets.py:459-463), so the % task reads '37 %' and the user can tell the two apart. Severity is low, not medium: the live alert still fires, the dead task never nags, and exclude_entity_ids removes it.
- **Repro:** PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B13/repro.py -> preset lg_thinq_alert_replace device_class None; matched: ['sensor.fridge_fresh_air_filter', 'sensor.fridge_fresh_air_filter_2']; sensor binding of % entity: {'mode': 'state', 'state': 'replace', ...}; name_template {{ task_name }}: {{ device_name or friendly_name }}

### B13-2 — Name-by-device heuristic ignores one key on many entities of one device: QNAP volumes get identical task names

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_presets.py:542`
- **Defect:** _integration_presets picks _NAME_BY_ENTITY only when two keys share a duty name; a single key that the integration creates once per volume on the same device (QNAP volume_percentage_used) still gets _NAME_BY_DEVICE, so every volume task has the same name.
- **Failure scenario:** QNAP NAS with volumes DataVol1 and DataVol2, both above 85%. The qnap_reading_high preset matches two entities with translation_key volume_percentage_used on the one NAS device. Both tasks render 'Free up storage: <NAS name>' on the task list, to-do list, calendar and notifications; only the notes say which volume. This is exactly the 'device alone cannot tell the tasks apart' case the code comment says it avoids.
- **Suggested fix:** Add an optional per-integration or per-duty flag (e.g. "per_instance": true) in the catalog for keys an integration repeats on one device (qnap volume_percentage_used; check mos pool_usage and unifi_unas_rest storage_usage too) and use _NAME_BY_ENTITY for those presets. Alternatively always use _NAME_BY_ENTITY for reading_high storage presets.
- **Verifier reason:** The name template is _NAME_BY_ENTITY only when two keys share a duty name (declarative_presets.py:542-545). qnap has one key (declarative_presets_catalog.py:884-890), so qnap_reading_high uses _NAME_BY_DEVICE. In the upstream qnap/sensor.py I fetched, every QNAPSensor, volumes included, sets DeviceInfo(identifiers={(DOMAIN, unique_id)}) for the NAS (sensor.py:323-331), and one volume sensor is created per volume (sensor.py:292-298). device_name is the device's name (template_context.py:153-167), so every volume task renders 'Free up storage: <NAS>'. This goes against the comment at declarative_presets.py:453-456. Low: the notes carry the per-volume friendly name, and the to-do sync de-duplicates same-summary items through its claimed set (todo_items.py:63-110), so nothing merges. The effect is on the name only.
- **Repro:** PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B13/repro.py -> qnap name_template {{ task_name }}: {{ device_name or friendly_name }}; qnap matched: 2 -> both render Free up storage: <NAS>

### B13-3 — Saved companion stops following the HA language after any catalog change to its preset's task_names table

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_presets.py:399`
- **Defect:** localized_task_template treats task_names as preset text only when the whole stored table equals one current per-language table, so a release that adds or renames a key, or corrects one duty translation, makes every existing companion from that preset look user-edited and freezes its task names in the language it was saved in.
- **Failure scenario:** A household saves 'Brother printer: parts and supplies running low' in French. A later release fixes the French duty text for one duty (e.g. the shared 'Remplacer le desodorisant' wording), or adds a key to the brother percent_low duties (the weekly key check exists to drive such edits). The stored task_names dict no longer equals any value in PRESET_TASK_TEXT[id]['task_names'], so the whole table is returned as written. When the household then switches Home Assistant to English, every task of that companion keeps its French task name, while a newly installed copy of the same preset renders in English.
- **Suggested fix:** Localize task_names per key: for each stored key, replace the value when it equals that key's name in some language of the current table (or of any duty name in DUTY_NAMES), and keep user-edited entries. Add a unit test where the stored table has one extra or one reworded entry.
- **Verifier fix note:** Per-key localization is right. _pick returns the catalog's own dict for task_names, and today localized_default_spec hands out that shared object (my first probe mutated PRESET_TASK_TEXT through it). A per-key rewrite must build a new dict and never edit the picked table in place.
- **Verifier reason:** localized_task_template replaces task_names only when the whole stored table equals one current per-language table (declarative_presets.py:397-400). The saved spec keeps its own copy (_normalize_task_names, declarative_companions.py:197-225, 283-285). Nothing migrates a stored spec when the catalog changes: preset_id is used only in normalize, events and api_surface. Reproduced: a French-saved brother_percent_low spec renders in English today, but after a key is added to the catalog tables it stays French. This is latent. The integration presets are new in HEAD f016cf5 (0.28.0b2), so no shipped catalog edit has triggered it yet. ci/check_preset_keys.py exists to drive such edits. Low: it needs a catalog change and then a language switch, or a translation fix that then never reaches companions saved in that language.
- **Repro:** PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B13/repro.py -> today, fr spec rendered in en: 'Replace the ink or toner'; after a key is added, fr spec rendered in en: "Remplacer l'encre ou le toner"

### B13-4 — check_preset_keys.py exits 0 when a domain argument matches nothing

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** ci
- **Location:** `ci/check_preset_keys.py:76`
- **Defect:** A mistyped or removed domain on the command line filters the list to empty, prints '0 integrations checked, 0 with a problem.' and returns success.
- **Failure scenario:** Maintainer runs `python ci/check_preset_keys.py robrock` (typo) or a planned scheduled job passes a domain that was renamed in the catalog. entries becomes [], problems stays 0, exit status 0, so the check reports green without checking anything.
- **Suggested fix:** Fail (exit 2) when any argv domain is not in the catalog, listing the unknown names.
- **Verifier reason:** main() filters entries by argv and does not report names that matched nothing (ci/check_preset_keys.py:75-76). With an empty list it prints the summary and returns 0 (ci/check_preset_keys.py:92-93). tests/unit/test_check_preset_keys.py does not cover main(). Low: this is a manual tool and not a PR gate. The weekly workflow is only planned (docs/INTEGRATION_PRESET_GAPS_PLAN.md:117) and would run with no arguments.
- **Repro:** PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -B ci/check_preset_keys.py robrock -> '0 integrations checked, 0 with a problem.' exit=0

### B14-8 — A unit change on the bound entity is not detected; the unitless baseline and threshold silently change meaning

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_watcher.py:122`
- **Defect:** The watcher never reads unit_of_measurement, so when a user changes the entity's display unit (km->mi, degC->degF, Wh->kWh) HA converts the state and Home Keeper compares the new number with a baseline/limit stored in the old unit.
- **Failure scenario:** Odometer switched from km to mi in the entity settings: 48,000 -> 29,826 < baseline 45,000 -> treated as a meter reset after two passes -> baseline 29,826, 3,000 km progress lost and the 10,000 target now counts miles (60% late). The reverse (mi->km) arms at once. A freezer task 'temperature > -10' arms immediately when the sensor is switched to degF (14 > -10).
- **Suggested fix:** Record the entity's unit_of_measurement in the binding when it is saved and, when it differs at evaluation, convert with HA's unit converters (or treat the reading as indeterminate and log once) instead of comparing raw numbers.
- **Verifier fix note:** Store the entity's unit at bind time. When it differs at evaluation, treat the reading as indeterminate and log once, or convert with HA's converters.
- **Verifier reason:** The backend never reads a binding's unit_of_measurement (grep finds only number.py and preset templates), and the watcher compares the raw state (sensor_watcher.py:105-122). HA converts a numeric sensor's state to the unit the user picks (upstream sensor/__init__.py:757, 'UNIT_CONVERTERS.get(device_class)'), so the stored baseline and limit change meaning after a unit change. A km->mi switch then follows the reset path in B14-3, and a degC->degF switch can arm a threshold. It needs a deliberate unit change after the task is bound, so low.

### B14-9 — parse_reading accepts NaN and infinity from live states and attributes

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_tasks.py:125`
- **Defect:** float('nan')/float('inf') pass through as readings, although stored values are guarded with _finite_float and usage_intervals drops non-finite readings.
- **Failure scenario:** A sensor or attribute reports 'nan': a threshold '!= 0' task arms (nan != 0 is True); a fresh usage task stamps baseline NaN, after which reading-NaN >= target and reading < NaN are always False, so the meter never arms or re-anchors until restart. An 'inf' reading arms a usage task at once and is written as the completion reading and new baseline. Simulated: '!= 0' on 'nan' -> arm; usage without baseline on 'nan' -> rebaseline to nan; usage on 'inf' -> arm.
- **Suggested fix:** Return None from parse_reading when the value is not finite, so a non-finite reading is indeterminate like 'unavailable'.
- **Verifier fix note:** Return None when not math.isfinite(value), so a non-finite reading is indeterminate.
- **Verifier reason:** parse_reading accepts 'nan' and 'inf' (sensor_tasks.py:115-127). Stored values are guarded (models._finite_float, models.py:60-73), and so is usage_intervals (sensor_tasks.py:285). The reach is narrow: HA's SensorEntity rejects non-finite states for any sensor with a device class, state class, unit or precision (upstream sensor/__init__.py:747-753, 'non-finite value'), so meters and battery sensors cannot deliver them. Only attribute bindings and untyped sensors (an MQTT or template sensor with no unit) can.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-B14/sim.py -> "parse_reading('nan') = nan  parse_reading('inf') = inf" / "threshold != 0 on 'nan' -> arm" / "usage w/o baseline on 'nan' -> {'action': 'rebaseline', 'baseline': nan, ...}".

### B15-2 — Mark done button is created for require_tag_scan tasks and every press raises an unwrapped, unlocalized TaskValidationError

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/button.py:41`
- **Defect:** The button platform skips problem-sensor and completion-blocked tasks but not scan-only tasks, and async_press calls store.complete_task without converting TaskValidationError (a ValueError) to a translated HomeAssistantError.
- **Failure scenario:** Give an appliance wear part a tag with 'require tag scan' (assets.py:676-682); reconcile copies require_tag_scan onto the derived task, which carries the appliance device_id, so the device page gets a 'Mark done' button. Pressing it: ButtonEntity stamps the button state as pressed, then complete_task -> _reject_scan_required raises models.TaskValidationError (ValueError). It is not a HomeAssistantError, so the websocket call_service handler logs 'Unexpected exception' with a traceback and returns unknown_error with the raw English text. The button can never succeed, and toggling require_tag_scan does not add/remove it because entity_set_key ignores the flag. The to-do entity wraps the same error in HomeAssistantError(translation_key='complete_failed'); a deleted-before-reload task gives a raw KeyError here too.
- **Suggested fix:** Skip tasks where not tags.completion_allowed(task, None) in button setup and add require_tag_scan to entity_set_key; also wrap KeyError/TaskValidationError in async_press as HomeAssistantError(translation_domain=DOMAIN, translation_key='complete_failed'/'task_not_found'), as todo.py does.
- **Verifier fix note:** Wrap TaskValidationError -> HomeAssistantError(translation_domain=DOMAIN, translation_key='complete_failed') and KeyError -> 'task_not_found', as todo.py does. If the button is dropped for scan-only tasks, add require_tag_scan to entity_set_key so update_task reloads when it flips; the wear-part path already reloads on every asset edit (devices.async_apply_asset_change).
- **Verifier reason:** button.py:41-46 filters only problem-sensor and completion_blocked tasks, so a device-attached require_tag_scan task (task-level or copied from a wear part, reconcile.py:468-481) gets a Mark done button. async_press (button.py:77-78) has no try/except; store.complete_task -> _reject_scan_required (store.py:164-179, 1984) raises models.TaskValidationError (a ValueError). HA core websocket_api handle_call_service catches that in its generic 'except Exception' branch: logger.exception('Unexpected exception') with a traceback and ERR_UNKNOWN_ERROR with the raw English text. ButtonEntity._async_press_action writes the pressed state before awaiting async_press, so the button state changes although nothing was completed. todo.py:126-133 wraps the same error as HomeAssistantError(translation_key='complete_failed'), and quality_scale.yaml:69-71 and :147-151 claim action-exceptions/exception-translations done. Rejecting the press is intended (docs/guide/tasks/nfc-tags.md:10-16 lists 'device button' among the surfaces the toggle blocks), so the defect is the unwrapped, unlocalized error and the always-failing button. Low: nothing is written; log noise and a poor error. The KeyError case needs a press between a delete and its immediate reload.

### B15-4 — Device-page Mark done on an already completed one-off records a duplicate completion

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/button.py:77`
- **Defect:** A completed do-once task stays enabled and device-attached, so its button remains, and async_press completes it again; the to-do entity explicitly ignores this case.
- **Failure scenario:** One-off task 'Replace door seal' attached to a fridge device is marked done. next_due becomes None but the button stays on the device page (entity set unchanged). A second press (or a dashboard button tap) appends another completion, moves last_completed, and fires home_keeper_task_completed again, so automations run twice for work done once.
- **Suggested fix:** In async_press return early (or raise a translated HomeAssistantError) when recurrence.one_off_completed(self._task); optionally mark the button unavailable for a done one-off.
- **Verifier reason:** coordinator.device_attached_task_ids (coordinator.py:310-316) keeps a completed one-off (enabled + device_id), button.py:41-46 does not filter it, and completion does not change entity_set_key, so the button stays indefinitely (until a retention purge). store.complete_task (store.py:1979-1984) has no one-off guard and recurrence.apply_completion appends another history entry and moves last_completed (recurrence.py:524-529), then home_keeper_task_completed fires again. Every other human surface withholds it: todo.py:123-125, panel-lists.ts:285 and panel-detail.ts:414 hide Done for a completed one-off, and docs/guide/tasks/one-off-tasks.md:24-26 says it leaves the active surfaces. Low: needs a second press on finished work.

### B15-5 — adjust_part_stock on an untracked part starts tracking but its spares number and low-stock sensor do not appear until an unrelated reload

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/coordinator.py:185`
- **Defect:** adjust_part_stock deliberately begins tracking a part whose stock was None, which flips part_tracks_stock/part_has_reorder, but the settle step only reloads when a buy task's entity set changed.
- **Failure scenario:** A script calls home_keeper.adjust_part_stock delta=2 on a part with no stock (reorder_at set). Stock becomes 2 and the part is now tracked, but number.*_spares and binary_sensor.*_low_stock are only created in platform setup; async_settle_buy_tasks just calls async_update_listeners/async_request_refresh, so the device page shows no stock entities until some other change reloads the entry or HA restarts.
- **Suggested fix:** In the adjust_part_stock service/websocket handlers (or in async_settle_buy_tasks), reload the entry when the part's part_tracks_stock/part_has_reorder state changed across the call.
- **Verifier reason:** adjust_part_stock starts tracking from zero (assets.py:1361-1369), which flips part_tracks_stock/part_has_reorder (assets.py:998-1014). The number and low-stock entities are built only in platform setup (number.py:41, binary_sensor.py:62). handle_adjust_part_stock (__init__.py:1525-1539) and ws_adjust_part_stock (websocket_api.py:885-895) call async_settle_buy_tasks, which reloads only when a buy task's entity set changed (coordinator.py:182-195). Reach is narrow: the panel stepper is wired only for tracked parts (panel-detail.ts:931) and the number entity exists only for tracked parts, so only a script or Developer Tools call starts tracking this way. Any appliance edit reloads the entry (devices.async_apply_asset_change) and fixes it.

### B15-6 — Renaming a task from the to-do card skips the entity_set_key reload, so device-page entity names (and a self-owned device name) stay stale

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/task_entities.py:79`
- **Defect:** entity_set_key includes name because HA caches entity names and a reload is the only rename path, but todo.py applies name edits with only a coordinator refresh.
- **Failure scenario:** Edit a device-attached task's summary in the HA to-do card from 'Clean coils' to 'Vacuum coils'. The store is updated, but the Mark done/Next due/Overdue entities keep the 'Clean coils:' prefix, and a self-owned task device keeps the old name, until the next unrelated reload or restart.
- **Suggested fix:** In todo.async_update_todo_item compute entity_set_key before and after update_task and reload the entry when it changed, as handle_update_task does.
- **Verifier reason:** todo.async_update_todo_item applies the summary as a name update and then only calls async_request_refresh (todo.py:143-158). The prefix is fixed in _attr_translation_placeholders at construction (entity.py:59-64), and a self-owned fallback device takes the task name (coordinator.py:410-417). The service and websocket update paths compare entity_set_key and reload (__init__.py:1191-1206, websocket_api.py:310-314). Names stay stale until the next reload or restart; cosmetic.

### B15-7 — last_completion_* attributes pick the latest completion by string order of ts, not by instant

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor.py:214`
- **Defect:** max(completions, key=ts string) orders by local wall-clock text, which differs from the instant order when entries carry different UTC offsets; recurrence and sensor_tasks parse the timestamps.
- **Failure scenario:** A completion logged by an automation with completed_at in UTC ('2026-03-01T20:00:00+00:00') and a later button completion in Australia/Sydney ('2026-03-02T05:00:00+11:00', which is earlier in absolute time, 18:00 UTC) or two completions inside the DST fall-back hour: the sensor reports the note/cost/who/reading of the wrong completion, while undo re-derives last_completed with parsed datetimes and disagrees.
- **Suggested fix:** Order by parsed datetime (datetime.fromisoformat(c['ts'])) with a guard for bad values, matching recurrence.remove_completion.
- **Verifier reason:** sensor.py:214 takes max by the ts string. Mixed offsets are normal, not exotic: the panel's dated completion sends toISOString() (forms.ts:201-206), websocket_api.py:406-414 parses it as UTC and apply_completion stores completed_at.isoformat() (recurrence.py:524), while a plain Done uses dt_util.now() in the local zone (store.py:1985-1986). Two completions less than |UTC offset| hours apart can then sort wrong, and the last_completion_* attributes publish the other completion's note/cost/who/reading. Only those attributes are affected; the schedule is not.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 tmp-v-B15/b15_7.py (America/Los_Angeles, -07:00) -> ts values: ['2026-03-02T03:00:00+00:00', '2026-03-01T21:00:00-07:00']; sensor (string max) picks: backdated | instant max: latest | last_completed: 2026-03-01T21:00:00-07:00

### B16-10 — Regional HA languages (es-419, de-CH, pt, zh-Hant) get English notification text

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifications.py:459`
- **Defect:** _notification_strings loads only the exact <lang>.json and falls back straight to English, while the panel's i18n falls back to the base language first.
- **Failure scenario:** hass.config.language = 'es-419'. The panel shows Spanish (i18n.ts resolves es-419 -> es), but every notification reads 'Mark done', 'Overdue by 3 days.' because notification_strings/es-419.json does not exist and the lookup never tries es.json. Same for de-CH -> de, pt -> pt-BR.
- **Suggested fix:** Try the exact tag, then the base language (lang.split('-')[0]), then English, in _t and _tn.
- **Verifier fix note:** Put the base-language fallback in one shared helper and use it in notifications and backend_i18n. Mapping pt to pt-BR needs an explicit alias, not a base fallback.
- **Verifier reason:** _notification_strings loads only <lang>.json and _t/_tn fall back to English (notifications.py:459-497), while the panel falls back to the base language (i18n.ts:28-39). Two of the finding's examples are wrong: 'pt' has no base-language match in the panel either ('pt' is not 'pt-BR'), and 'zh-Hant' falls to English in both. The real gap is es-419, de-CH and similar tags. backend_i18n.py (resolve_exception, resolve_string) uses the same exact-or-English rule.
- **Repro:** python3 (pure modules) -> "key action_complete | es: Marcar como hecho | es-419: Mark done | de-CH: Mark done | de: Als erledigt markieren"

### B16-11 — Legacy non-mobile targets log a WARNING on every options read, indefinitely

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifications.py:313`
- **Defect:** normalize_notification runs on the read path (current_options), so a stored target the allowlist rejects is warned about on every read and the cleanup is never persisted.
- **Failure scenario:** An install from before 0.21 saved targets: ['telegram_family']. After upgrade, each coordinator refresh (_purge_expired_one_offs), each to-do sync sweep, each shopping sync and each notifier read calls current_options -> the same WARNING several times every 5 minutes until someone re-saves Settings, flooding the log.
- **Suggested fix:** Warn only on the write path (async_set_options / merge_flow_input), or migrate the stored options once at setup and log once.
- **Verifier reason:** current_options normalizes the stored options on every read (options.py:144), and normalize_notification logs a WARNING for each rejected target (notifications.py:312-319). Nothing writes the cleaned list back until a user saves Settings (options.py:338). The coordinator reads options on every refresh (coordinator.py:272, SCAN_INTERVAL 5 min, coordinator.py:47), and the to-do and shopping syncs read them as well, so the warning repeats forever. The allowlist shipped in 0.14.0 (CHANGELOG.md:1482-1487, 'with a log warning when a stored one is dropped'), not 0.21.

### B16-12 — Walk-advance sends 'All caught up' when the notification's profile no longer exists

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:538`
- **Defect:** async_send_for_notification returns (0, None) for a dangling profile_id, and the tap handler reads matched == 0 as an empty queue.
- **Failure scenario:** A notification restored from a backup names a profile that is gone (a designed, readable state per options.py:281-282). The user taps Mark done on an old card -> the task completes, the send is skipped as misconfigured, and the phone gets 'All caught up - No tasks due right now' although tasks may be overdue.
- **Suggested fix:** Return a distinct signal for the misconfigured case (e.g. matched=None) and skip the all-clear then.
- **Verifier reason:** async_send_for_notification returns (0, None) for a dangling profile_id (notifier.py:297-304), and the tap handler reads matched == 0 as an empty queue and sends 'All caught up' (notifier.py:538-546). It is rare: a save cannot delete a profile that a notification references (options.py:274-282), so a dangling id comes only from a backup restore or import, and the phone must still hold a card sent before that.

### B16-4 — Walk offers 'Mark done' on require_tag_scan tasks that the store always refuses

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifications.py:421`
- **Defect:** actions_for filters Done/Skip only for managed_by.completion_blocked, not for scan-required tasks, so the button is dead: the store raises, the handler swallows it at debug level and the walk does not advance.
- **Failure scenario:** Task 'Refill water softener salt' has tag_id + require_tag_scan. A walk notification shows it with 'Mark done'. The user taps it -> complete_task -> _reject_scan_required raises TaskValidationError -> notifier catches it, logs at debug and returns. The card is dismissed by the tap, the task is still overdue, nothing tells the user a scan is needed, and the walk does not move on (with a [complete, open] button set it is stuck on this task). The panel and card treat the same task as blocked (card.ts:1001), so the surfaces disagree.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat tags.completion_allowed(task, ORIGIN_NOTIFICATION_ACTION) == False as blocking ACTION_COMPLETE in actions_for (keep Skip/Snooze, which the store accepts), and add a unit test.
- **Verifier reason:** actions_for (notifications.py:421-428) blocks only managed_by.completion_blocked, and notifications.py never reads require_tag_scan. store.complete_task (store.py:1946, 1984) calls _reject_scan_required, which refuses ORIGIN_NOTIFICATION_ACTION (tags.py:31-33, 51-61). notifier.py:512-519 swallows the refusal at debug level and skips the walk-advance. The card treats the same task as blocked (card.ts:1001). Severity lowered: require_tag_scan is an opt-in per-task flag, nothing changes on the task, and with the default buttons [complete, snooze, open] (notifications.py:58) Snooze still advances the walk. It is stuck only with a [complete, open] button set.

### B16-6 — A snooze (or completing a short-interval task) immediately fires a due-soon auto push

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:323`
- **Defect:** Snoozing moves next_due, which re-arms the edge state, and now+snooze_hours (default 24h) is inside the 3-day window, so the very next refresh fires home_keeper_task_due_soon and async_send_auto pushes every auto.due_soon notification right after the user asked to be reminded later.
- **Failure scenario:** Notification 'Chores' has auto.due_soon on. User taps 'Snooze 24h' on an overdue task -> snooze_task -> _handle awaits async_request_refresh (immediate debouncer) -> detect_transitions sees a new next_due, resets flags, is_due_soon is true -> due_soon fires -> async_send_auto pushes 'Chores' (and every other auto.due_soon notification), then the walk-advance pushes again. Every completion of a daily/every-2-days task does the same. Verified with transitions.detect_transitions: snoozed and daily-completed tasks both fire home_keeper_task_due_soon at once. snooze_task's own docstring says 'a fresh reminder fires when the snooze lapses'.
- **Suggested fix:** Do not treat a due-soon edge caused by a snooze/completion (next_due moved by a user action to a time already inside the window) as a crossing for auto notifications, e.g. baseline due_soon_fired=True when the new next_due is already within the window at the moment it changed, or skip auto sends for origin=notification/snooze.
- **Verifier reason:** The mechanism reproduces. A snooze or completion changes next_due, and transitions re-arms both flags (transitions.py:62-66). A new date inside the 3-day window fires home_keeper_task_due_soon at the next refresh, and HA's request_refresh debouncer runs the first refresh at once (coordinator.py:5). The auto.due_soon notifications then send (coordinator.py:228-234), and for a walk the walk-advance sends again straight after (notifier.py:526-537). Severity lowered: re-arming on a moved next_due is the documented event contract, and the new occurrence really is due soon, so this is extra pushes for users of the opt-in 'Send when due soon' trigger, with no data impact. The noise is larger because of B16-1.
- **Repro:** python3 repro_b16_5.py -> 'floating B16-6 events after snooze 24h: [home_keeper_task_due_soon]', 'floating B16-6 events after completion: [home_keeper_task_due_soon]' (same for fixed)

### B16-7 — Ad-hoc home_keeper.notify (profile: + target:) gets a random id per call: cards stack and the walk never advances

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:399`
- **Defect:** With no saved notification, normalize_notification mints uuid4() as the id, so the tag differs on every call and the action strings name a notification that never resolves.
- **Failure scenario:** Automation every 2 hours calls home_keeper.notify with profile: Chores, target: mobile_app_phone. Each call builds tag home_keeper_<new uuid>, so by evening the phone holds 6 stacked copies of the same task instead of one replaced card (the guide promises replacement). Tapping Mark done on one completes the task but resolve_notification(<uuid>) is None, so no next task is sent; the other 5 cards stay, each with Skip/Snooze buttons that B16-2 lets act again.
- **Suggested fix:** Give ad-hoc sends a deterministic id derived from the profile id (e.g. f"adhoc_{profile['id']}") so the tag is stable, and let the tap handler rebuild an ad-hoc walk from that id.
- **Verifier reason:** With no saved notification, notif_raw has no id (notifier.py:399), so normalize_notification mints uuid4 (notifications.py:321) and the tag home_keeper_<id> is new on every call. A tap then gets None from resolve_notification (notifier.py:442-444), so no walk-advance runs (notifier.py:532). The guide's 'replaces the previous one' promise (notifications.md:197-199) is about a saved notification, but the service description says a walk 'advances as you action it' (services.yaml:904-906), and a profile-only call defaults to the walk style.

### B16-8 — Walk-advance after a target-override send goes to the saved targets, not the tapping device

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:532`
- **Defect:** notification: X + target: override keeps X's id in the action strings, and the tap handler advances with the saved notification's own targets.
- **Failure scenario:** Saved 'Walk my chores' targets Dad's phone. An automation sends notification: Walk my chores, target: mobile_app_kid. The kid taps Mark done -> the next task is pushed to Dad's phone, and the kid's card is not replaced. If the saved notification has no target, the kid's walk stops and a WARNING 'matched N task(s) but has no notify target' is logged on every tap.
- **Suggested fix:** Carry the delivery target in the action (or key the walk state by the override) so the advance goes back to the device that tapped; at minimum document that overrides do not walk.
- **Verifier reason:** notification: X plus a target: override keeps X's id (notifier.py:399, 414-416), so the action strings name X. The tap handler resolves the saved X (notifier.py:442-444) and advances to X's saved targets (notifier.py:535-546). If X has no targets, _send logs the 'no notify target' WARNING and returns matched > 0, so the walk just stops (notifier.py:237-254).

### B16-9 — Delivery failures are logged only at debug and the service still reports the task as sent

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/notifier.py:152`
- **Defect:** A removed or renamed mobile_app target raises ServiceNotFound synchronously; _send_payload swallows it at debug level and _send returns (matched, sent_id), so home_keeper.notify and the panel's Test button report success for a push that never left.
- **Failure scenario:** A phone is re-registered and its service becomes notify.mobile_app_pixel_8_2. The stored target mobile_app_pixel_8 no longer exists. Every auto, walk and service send fails; the notify response says {matched: 1, sent: <task_id>}; at the default log level nothing is written.
- **Suggested fix:** Log a missing service at warning level (once per target), and count successful targets so the response can report delivery (or raise notify_invalid_target when every target is missing).
- **Verifier reason:** HA core ServiceRegistry.async_call looks up the handler and raises ServiceNotFound before its blocking check (homeassistant/core.py async_call, dev), so a removed or renamed mobile_app target raises even with blocking=False. _send_payload swallows it at debug level (notifier.py:149-153), and _send / async_run_notify still return matched and sent (notifier.py:266, 281, 431).

### B17-5 — Runtime asset-device recovery moves the asset but strands its tasks on the dead id

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/devices.py:527`
- **Defect:** _reconcile_existing re-points an existing-kind asset to a snapshot-matched device without re-pointing tasks on the old id; when this runs from async_apply_asset_change (before the reload's heal), the heal later has no snapshot left that names the old id, so the tasks never follow.
- **Failure scenario:** The user removes and re-adds an integration (or re-adopts an ESPHome device) while HA runs; the device gets a new id with the same identifiers. Before any restart the user edits any appliance in the panel. async_apply_asset_change -> async_reconcile_assets -> _reconcile_existing: resolve_device(old) is None, _resolve_by_snapshot finds the new device, asset['device_id'] = new and it is persisted. The reload then runs async_heal_split_device_ids: tasks on the old id have no snapshot (unresolved), and the asset now names the live id, so the mapping is empty. The tasks stay on the dead id and fall back to self-owned devices forever, which is the 'asset on the live device, task left on the dead id' split the heal docstring says must not happen. A plain restart instead of the edit would have healed both.
- **Suggested fix:** When _reconcile_existing recovers a device, return the (old, new) pair and have async_reconcile_assets call store.async_repoint_device_ids({old: new}) in the same pass (or route recovery through the shared heal mapping).
- **Verifier fix note:** Return the (old, new) pair from _reconcile_existing and call store.async_repoint_device_ids({old: new}) in the same pass, so both paths agree.
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced with the real devices.py and store.py. A device is re-created under a new id with the same identifier. An appliance edit (async_apply_asset_change -> async_reconcile_assets -> _reconcile_existing, devices.py:516-528) moves the asset to the new id. The reload's heal then finds no snapshot that names the old id (devices.py:241, 312-320), so the task stays on the dead id on every later setup too. A plain restart heals both, because the heal runs before reconcile (__init__.py:821 vs 836). Low: HA restores the old device id when an integration re-creates a device with the same identifiers (deleted_devices, or an orphan of the same domain; core device_registry.py 2026.9:2414-2460), so a new id with the same identifiers is uncommon (for example, after the deleted entry was purged).
- **Repro:** tmp-v-B17/repro_b17_5.py: edit before restart -> asset 'dev_new', task 'dev_old'; plain restart -> asset 'dev_new', task 'dev_new'

### B17-8 — Backend accepts an existing-device appliance as a parent, then reconcile references a via_device that never exists

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/devices.py:441`
- **Defect:** _validate_parent does not require the parent to be virtual (the panel does), so add_asset/update_asset can nest a virtual appliance under an existing-kind one; _reconcile_virtual then passes via_device=(home_keeper, asset_<parent>), an identifier no device carries.
- **Failure scenario:** An automation or companion calls home_keeper.update_asset with parent_asset_id set to an existing-kind appliance. On every setup _reconcile_virtual calls async_get_or_create(via_device=(home_keeper, asset_<parent>)); no such device exists, so HA reports a non-existing via_device (a report_usage warning naming the custom integration, announced to break), and the update loop then sets via_device_id to the parent's foreign device id, a hierarchy the panel explicitly refuses to create.
- **Suggested fix:** Reject a non-virtual parent in store._validate_parent, and in _reconcile_virtual only pass via_device when the parent is ASSET_KIND_VIRTUAL.
- **Verifier fix note:** Reject a non-virtual parent in _validate_parent as suggested. Also pass via_device and via_device_id only when the parent is virtual, which removes the setup-failure path for data that is already stored.
- **Verifier reason:** store._validate_parent (store.py:1176-1191) checks only that the parent exists and that there is no cycle, and assets.py:1394-1395 accepts any parent id for a virtual child. So add_asset/update_asset (a name or id, services.yaml:1073-1076) can nest a virtual appliance under an existing-kind one, which the panel never offers (panel-asset-form.ts:36-55). _reconcile_virtual then passes via_device=(home_keeper, asset_<parent>) (devices.py:440-446, 462-464). HA 2026.9 logs that as a non-existing via_device (report_usage, core device_registry.py 2026.9:2517-2525). Then devices.py:490-492 sets via_device_id to the parent's foreign device. Escalation found: if that foreign device is removed later, HA clears the child's via link, the next reconcile asks for via_device_id=<dead id>, and async_update_device raises 'Can't link device to unknown via device' (2026.9:3068-3080). async_reconcile_assets at __init__.py:836 has no guard, so Home Keeper setup fails. Rare: it needs a service-only input and then a device removal.

### B17-9 — Area set on a Home Keeper appliance device in HA is reverted on every reconcile

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/devices.py:483`
- **Defect:** _reconcile_virtual unconditionally writes the asset's area_id (None when unset) over the device's area, so an area the user assigns on HA's device page is wiped at the next setup or asset edit.
- **Failure scenario:** A user creates an appliance in the panel without an area, then assigns the new device to 'Garage' on HA's device page (the usual HA flow). The next reload (any asset edit, options change, or restart) runs _reconcile_virtual: area_id None != 'garage', so updates['area_id'] = None and the assignment disappears; the change is never read back into the asset.
- **Suggested fix:** Only push area_id when the asset's area changed since the last push (store the last pushed value), or read a device-side change back into the asset.
- **Verifier fix note:** Push area_id only when the asset's area changed since the last push (keep the last pushed value), or copy a device-side change into the asset.
- **Verifier reason:** devices.py:480-484 writes the asset's area_id (None when unset) over device.area_id on every reconcile (setup, every asset edit, options reload). Nothing reads an area set on the device page back into the asset (only declarative_companion_sync.py:176 listens to device-registry updates). The comment at devices.py:476-477 intends the clear for a clear made in the panel. No guide page tells users to set the area only in the panel. Low: cosmetic, and the area set in the panel works.

### B18-10 — Problem sync ignores device-registry changes, so area/label exclusions and the mirror's area go stale

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/problem_sync.py:79`
- **Defect:** Eligibility and area_id read the device's area and labels, but only entity-registry and state events trigger a reconcile.
- **Failure scenario:** User moves the sump pump device into an area listed in the problem-sensor area exclusions (or adds an excluded label to the device). No entity-registry event fires, so the mirror is not removed and keeps its old area_id; area-filtered profiles and notifications keep including it until an unrelated reconcile or reload.
- **Suggested fix:** Also listen to dr.EVENT_DEVICE_REGISTRY_UPDATED (filtered to devices of tracked sensors) and reconcile.
- **Verifier reason:** _eligible reads device.area_id and device.labels (problem_sync.py:136-142), but the sync listens only to entity-registry updates and tracked state changes (problem_sync.py:78-84, 101-103). A device area or label change fires only dr.EVENT_DEVICE_REGISTRY_UPDATED, so an exclusion by area or label and the mirror's area_id wait for the next state flip of any tracked sensor, a binary_sensor registry event or a reload. The declarative sync listens to the device and area registries for the same kind of rule (declarative_companion_sync.py:168-185). An exclusion that applies late; low.

### B18-4 — Mirror metadata drift (rename, re-home) fires no task_updated event and never reloads when the entity-set key changes

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/problem_tasks.py:188`  **Also reported as:** X10-3
- **Defect:** The problem reconciler rewrites name/device_id/area_id as 'silent churn', so the store neither announces the change nor reloads the entry, unlike the declarative reconciler that follows the documented entity_set_key rule.
- **Failure scenario:** A device-less template problem sensor is assigned to a device (or a sensor moves to another device). The mirror's device_id changes, changed=True with no op, reconcile returns entity_set_changed=False and only a refresh runs: no per-task entities are created on the new device (or they stay on the old device page) until some unrelated reload. A rename likewise leaves the device-page entity names stale. No home_keeper_task_updated fires, so integrations and automations that mirror tasks never see the new name, device or area.
- **Suggested fix:** Emit an 'updated' op with changed_fields from reconcile_problem_tasks, fire EVENT_TASK_UPDATED for it, and snapshot entity_set_key before the pure pass so a moved key returns entity_set_changed=True (mirror reconcile_declarative_companion_tasks).
- **Verifier reason:** problem_tasks.py:188-201 rewrites name/device_id/area_id as 'silent churn' with no op. store.reconcile_problem_sensor_tasks (store.py:1686-1708) fires only created/deleted/armed/cleared and sets entity_set_changed only for created/deleted, so problem_sync.py:204-213 does a plain refresh. The declarative reconciler snapshots entity_set_key and fires EVENT_TASK_UPDATED (store.py:1880-1887, 1931-1943), and .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md:577-585 says every path that mutates the task map fires its update events.
