Result: a re-homed mirror keeps its per-task entities on the old device (or gets none on the new one) until the next reload, and no home_keeper_task_updated fires. Re-homing a problem sensor is rare, and the next reload fixes the entities. Low.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 tmp-v-B18/b18_problem.py -> B18-4 ops: [] | changed: True | key before->after: dev1 Node 5 problem -> dev2 Sump pump problem

### B18-5 — All time-based behaviour depends on the coordinator having listeners and polling enabled

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/coordinator.py:90`
- **Defect:** The 5-minute DataUpdateCoordinator interval is the only clock, and Home Assistant schedules it only while an entity listens and only when the entry's 'Enable polling' preference is on.
- **Failure scenario:** A user turns off 'Enable polling for changes' in the Home Keeper entry's system options (it reads as network polling; Home Keeper is local), or disables the to-do and calendar entities while having no device-attached tasks, profiles or appliances. The interval refresh stops: no home_keeper_task_overdue/due_soon from time passing, 'Send when overdue/due soon' never sends, the one-off retention purge and use-task time backstop never run, and the to-do sweeps stop. Nothing logs an error.
- **Suggested fix:** Drive the time-based work from an entry-scoped async_track_time_interval that calls async_request_refresh (independent of listeners and the polling preference), or set the entry's pref_disable_polling guard aside explicitly.
- **Verifier fix note:** If fixed, register an entry-scoped async_track_time_interval (entry.async_on_unload) that calls coordinator.async_request_refresh; there is no supported way to override pref_disable_polling, so drop that alternative.
- **Verifier reason:** HA DataUpdateCoordinator (helpers/update_coordinator.py, dev): async_add_listener schedules the interval only for the first listener, the last listener's removal unschedules it, _schedule_refresh returns early when config_entry.pref_disable_polling is set, and after each refresh it reschedules only 'if self._listeners'. Home Keeper adds no listener and no timer of its own: no async_add_listener or async_track_time_interval in the component, only sensor_watcher.py:465 async_track_point_in_time for hold timers. The listener half is practically unreachable: the to-do entity (todo.py:42), the calendar entity (calendar.py:35) and the all-tasks count sensor (built unconditionally, sensor.py:134-150) are always coordinator listeners, so a user must disable all three. The polling half is reachable: the frontend system-options dialog shows 'Enable polling for changes' for every entry, although the manifest says local_push. Overstated in the finding: with polling off, every mutation still calls async_request_refresh, which runs _async_update_data independently of both conditions. Crossings, auto sends and the purge are therefore late until the next mutation or homeassistant.update_entity, not never. The user asked for no polling, so this is low.

### B18-6 — Edge state survives disabling and re-enabling the entry, so re-enable replays an overdue/due-soon storm

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/coordinator.py:119`
- **Defect:** The process-lifetime edge map is discarded only on entry removal, so an entry re-enabled days later skips the silent baseline and announces every crossing that happened while it was off.
- **Failure scenario:** User disables Home Keeper for a week and re-enables it without restarting HA. The new coordinator finds prior state, so _had_prior_edge_state=True suppresses the baseline; the first refresh after enable_transition_events fires overdue/due_soon for every task that crossed during the week and triggers every auto notification. An HA restart in the same situation baselines silently (docs/EVENTS.md:153-156).
- **Suggested fix:** Call discard_edge_state in async_unload_entry when entry.disabled_by is set, or store a timestamp with the map and re-baseline when it is older than a few refresh intervals.
- **Verifier fix note:** HA sets disabled_by before the unload (as the comment at __init__.py:2021-2023 notes), so discard_edge_state under 'if entry.disabled_by is not None' in async_unload_entry is enough.
- **Verifier reason:** Disabling the entry runs async_unload_entry (__init__.py:1995-2033), which keeps hass.data[home_keeper_edge_state]; only async_remove_entry calls discard_edge_state (__init__.py:2052). On re-enable the coordinator finds prior state (coordinator.py:119-121), so the setup refreshes do not baseline (coordinator.py:235-243). The first refresh after enable_transition_events (__init__.py:945) fires every crossing that happened while Home Keeper was off, and each auto notification sends once. docs/EVENTS.md:153-156 says only transitions observed while running fire. Needs a disable and re-enable with no HA restart in between. Low.

### B18-7 — due_in_hours is emitted as a float but declared int in the API surface

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transitions.py:73`
- **Defect:** The due-soon payload carries round(hours, 1) while api_surface declares the field int, and the generated API reference publishes int.
- **Failure scenario:** An integrator trusting the API reference validates or casts due_in_hours as an integer; the event carries 23.9 (simulated for a daily task), 71.5, etc. Strict consumers reject it and templates comparing it to ints behave unexpectedly.
- **Suggested fix:** Declare Field("due_in_hours", "float") (or emit an int if int is the intended contract).
- **Verifier reason:** transitions.py:73 emits round(hours, 1). api_surface.py:504 declares Field('due_in_hours', 'int'), and ci/generate_api_docs.py renders that type into the API reference. The test asserts 24.0 (tests/unit/test_transitions.py:52), and 'float' is already used for other fields in api_surface.py (:282, :386). Doc and contract mismatch only.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 tmp-v-B18/b18_1.py -> ('home_keeper_task_due_soon', 'Feed fish', 24.0)

### B18-9 — Problem-clear completion event carries a completed_at that differs from the recorded completion

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:1704`
- **Defect:** The reconcile records the completion at one now() and fires the event with a second dt_util.now() taken after the save, unlike complete_task which uses one timestamp for both.
- **Failure scenario:** An integration correlates home_keeper_task_completed with the task's last_completed or completions[].ts (the documented identity of a history entry) to de-dupe; for problem clears the two never match (they differ by the save latency).
- **Suggested fix:** Fire the event with task['last_completed'] (or capture now once and pass it to both).
- **Verifier reason:** store.py:1677 passes one dt_util.now() to the pure reconcile, which records the clear at that time (problem_tasks.py:215). store.py:1705 fires the event with a second dt_util.now() taken after 'await self._save()' (store.py:1684). complete_task uses one 'when' for both (store.py:1985-1986, 2033-2035). The event's completed_at therefore never equals last_completed or completions[].ts for a problem clear. The difference is only the save latency, and update_completion rejects synced problem tasks anyway (store.py _reject_synced_problem), so the only effect is correlation by timestamp.

### B19-2 — Profile ids are accepted verbatim, but to-do list sync keys split on ':', so a caller id with a colon makes every sync pass delete and re-add its items

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/profiles.py:122`
- **Defect:** normalize_profile keeps any caller-supplied id, but todo_list.sync_key/partition assume that profile ids never contain ':'. A profile id such as 'kids:chores' makes the planner treat its own bookkeeping as belonging to a deleted profile on every pass.
- **Failure scenario:** An automation (hand-written set_options calls with explicit profile ids are an expected use, see test_a_profile_sent_without_an_id_reads_as_a_removal) saves profiles: [{id: 'kids:chores', name: 'Kids', filter: {status: all}, sync: {entity_id: todo.family}}]. Pass 1 adds the item under key 'kids:chores:task1'. Pass 2 does key.partition(':') -> profile 'kids', task 'chores:task1', by_id.get('kids') is None, so it takes the 'profile was deleted' branch and plans RemoveOp. The desired loop then finds no tracked key and plans AddOp again. needs_pass stays True forever. Every task event, list state change and 5-minute sweep removes and re-adds the line (each up to the 4-pass budget), so the item flickers, loses any edits or comments on a cloud list such as Todoist, and floods the to-do integration with calls. I reproduced this with plan_sync: pass 2 gives remove=[('kids:chores:task1','u1')] and add=['kids:chores:task1'], while a uuid-style id settles with 0/0.
- **Suggested fix:** Enforce the invariant where ids are minted and accepted: in normalize_profile, replace an id that contains ':' (or anything outside [A-Za-z0-9_-]) with a fresh uuid, or reject it in SET_OPTIONS_SCHEMA. Also de-duplicate ids in normalize_profiles. Otherwise, split keys with rpartition, because task ids are uuid hex.
- **Verifier reason:** profiles.normalize_profile (profiles.py:122) keeps any caller id verbatim and SET_OPTIONS_SCHEMA (__init__.py:791) and the websocket take profiles as a bare list/dict, while todo_list.py:143-145 states the unenforced invariant 'Profile ids are uuid hex ... so the first : is unambiguous' and splits with partition (414, 660). A tracked key 'kids:chores:task1' resolves to profile 'kids', takes the deleted-profile branch (RemoveOp), and the want loop re-adds it, every pass; needs_pass stays True. Trigger needs a hand-written set_options with a colon in a profile id; the panel always mints uuid hex and the profiles field is not documented in services.yaml, so low.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B19 && python3 r2_profile_colon.py -> id 'kids:chores': pass 2 add=['kids:chores:task1'] remove=['kids:chores:task1'], pass 3 same, needs_pass True; id '3f2a0c': pass 2/3 add=[] remove=[], needs_pass False

### B19-3 — Profile-in-use guard matches only ids, but notifications resolve profile_id by id or name, so deleting or renaming a name-referenced profile silently stops the notification

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/options.py:303`
- **Defect:** profile_removals_in_use looks up notification profile_id only among removed profile ids. The notifier resolves profile_id through profiles.resolve_profile, which falls back to a name match, so a notification that names its profile works at send time but is invisible to the guard.
- **Failure scenario:** A hand-written set_options call stores notifications: [{name: 'Walk', profile_id: 'My chores', targets: [...], auto: {overdue: true}}]. It sends correctly, because resolve_profile matches the name. The admin later deletes or renames 'My chores' in the panel. The panel's notificationsUsingProfile and the backend guard both compare ids only, so the save goes through. From then on, _notification_profile returns (None, True) and the notification sends nothing, which is exactly the silent stranding that ProfileInUseError exists to prevent.
- **Suggested fix:** Pick one rule for stored references. Either resolve stored notification profile_ids by id only (keep the name fallback for the home_keeper.notify service argument), or canonicalize a name to its id in _normalize when notifications are written, so the guard and the notifier agree.
- **Verifier reason:** profile_removals_in_use (options.py:292-305) matches notification profile_id against removed ids only (its docstring: 'ids are matched, names are only reported'), while notifier._notification_profile (notifier.py:126-131) resolves through profiles.resolve_profile, which falls back to a name (profiles.py:146-158), and notifications.py:323 stores profile_id verbatim. So a stored name reference works at send time but a delete or rename of that profile passes the guard, after which auto notifications skip with only a debug log (notifier.py:297-304). Needs a hand-written set_options that stores a profile name; the panel stores ids. Low.

### B19-4 — A glue that self-registered and was then uninstalled suppresses its catalog suggestion until restart

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/companions_catalog.py:159`
- **Defect:** build_companion_list skips a catalog entry whenever its glue is in `registered`, even when that registered glue is no longer installed and its connected row was already dropped. The upstream then has no row at all.
- **Failure scenario:** The Battery Notes glue registers at startup, and the user then deletes the glue's config entry while Battery Notes (the upstream) stays installed. The registry keeps the stale registration (it never evicts). The registered loop drops the row because the glue is not installed, and the catalog loop hits `if glue in registered: continue`. The Companions list shows nothing for Battery Notes, no 'Suggested' row with the Install link appears, and reconcile never fires home_keeper_companion_suggested until HA restarts. I confirmed this with build_companion_list({GLUE: {...}}, {'battery_notes'}) -> [], while the same call with no registration gives a 'suggested' row.
- **Suggested fix:** Change the skip to `if glue in registered and glue in installed_domains: continue`, or drop registry entries whose domain no longer has a config entry during reconcile.
- **Verifier reason:** build_companion_list drops a registered-but-uninstalled glue from the connected rows (companions_catalog.py:152-154, whose comment covers exactly this uninstall case) and then skips its catalog entry because `glue in registered` (157-160); the registry never evicts (companions.py:95, 121-140). The upstream gets no suggestion row and no suggested event until a restart. Cosmetic and transient.
- **Repro:** cd SCRATCH/tmp-v-B19 && python3 r4_catalog.py -> registered+uninstalled glue, upstream installed: []; never registered, upstream installed: [('home_keeper_battery_notes', 'suggested')]

### B19-6 — Companion detection treats ignored and disabled config entries as installed

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/companions.py:145`
- **Defect:** _installed_domains uses config_entries.async_entries(), which by default includes entries with source 'ignore' and disabled entries. A disabled glue therefore reads as Connected, and a lingering ignored upstream entry keeps a glue suggestion alive.
- **Failure scenario:** The user disables the home_keeper_battery_notes entry. The Companions list still shows it as Connected with a Configure button, and no suggestion appears. In the other case, Battery Notes offers per-device discovery flows that users often ignore. After the main Battery Notes entry is removed, the ignored battery_notes entries remain, so Home Keeper keeps suggesting the glue for an upstream that is gone. A self-registered companion that the user disables mid-run also stays 'connected'.
- **Suggested fix:** Use async_entries(include_ignore=False, include_disabled=False), or filter on entry.source != SOURCE_IGNORE and entry.disabled_by is None.
- **Verifier fix note:** Excluding ignored entries is clearly right; excluding disabled ones changes a disabled glue from Connected to Suggested, so pick the wording deliberately.
- **Verifier reason:** companions.py:143-145 calls config_entries.async_entries() with defaults; HA 2026.9.0 config_entries.py:2204-2224 defaults include_ignore=True and include_disabled=True, so a disabled glue reads as Connected and an ignored battery_notes entry (the only catalog upstream today) keeps the glue suggestion alive. Wrong status text or a dismissable suggestion only; low.
- **Repro:** curl .../home-assistant/core/2026.9.0/homeassistant/config_entries.py -> `include_ignore: bool = True, include_disabled: bool = True`

### B20-3 — quality_scale action-setup is claimed done, but services register in async_setup_entry and vanish during every reload

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/quality_scale.yaml:11`
- **Defect:** HA's action-setup rule requires services registered in async_setup; Home Keeper registers them late in async_setup_entry and removes them in the unload half of every reload, so automations hit ServiceNotFound during routine reloads.
- **Failure scenario:** A 'washer finished -> home_keeper.complete_task' automation fires while Home Keeper reloads because a synced problem sensor appeared, options were saved, a companion reconciled, an appliance was deleted or the language changed. The unload removed every home_keeper service and setup re-registers them only after the store load, reconciles and platform forwarding, so the call fails with 'Action home_keeper.complete_task not found' and the completion is lost. When setup fails (SETUP_RETRY) every automation also reports an unknown action instead of the localized integration_not_loaded error the handlers already implement.
- **Suggested fix:** Register the services once in async_setup (the handlers already raise integration_not_loaded through _coordinator() when no entry is loaded) and stop removing them in async_unload_entry; update test_api_surface.py accordingly. If that is not wanted, set action-setup to todo so the platinum claim is honest.
- **Verifier fix note:** Registering in async_setup is the right fix; _coordinator() already raises the localized integration_not_loaded. Keep the removal out of async_unload_entry, and change test_unloading_removes_every_service to assert the services stay and answer integration_not_loaded while the entry is disabled. hass.services registrations made in async_setup persist for the HA run, so no teardown is needed.
- **Verifier reason:** async_setup only returns True (__init__.py:797-799); _register_services runs late in async_setup_entry (__init__.py:866) after store load, reconciles and platform forwarding; async_unload_entry removes every SERVICE_NAMES entry when no entry is loaded (__init__.py:2006-2008), which holds during every reload of this single-instance integration because the unloading entry is not LOADED. So every reload leaves a window with no home_keeper.* actions, and a failed setup leaves none at all. HA's own rule text for action-setup (developers.home-assistant.io rules/action-setup.md) names exactly this pattern (register in async_setup_entry, remove in async_unload_entry) as the one to avoid, so 'action-setup: done' in quality_scale.yaml:11-15 is wrong. tests/integration/test_api_surface.py:116-149 pins removal on disable only. Low: the runtime loss needs an automation call to land inside a reload window, and the Platinum claim is a self-assessment for a custom integration.

### B20-4 — Babel CLDR data first loads on the event loop via the shopping mirror; preload does not warm it, and quality_scale says there is no external dependency

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/backend_i18n.py:104`
- **Defect:** preload warms only the two JSON string tables, but assets.decimal_mark (Babel Locale.parse + get_decimal_symbol, which open locale-data files) is first called on the loop by the shopping-list pass at every HA start.
- **Failure scenario:** HA starts with a buy reminder for a spare part that has a stock unit ('ml') or restock_quantity > 1. async_at_started runs shopping_sync.async_initial_sync on the loop; _sync_once calls shopping.buy_tasks_by_part(lang=hass.config.language) before any target check, which reaches format_quantity -> decimal_mark -> babel.localedata.load -> open('.../babel/locale-data/root.dat'). HA logs 'Detected blocking call to open ... inside the event loop by custom integration home_keeper' pointing at assets.py:1128 - the same class of report as #150 and #247. It happens even when no shopping list is configured.
- **Suggested fix:** Warm assets.decimal_mark(hass.config.language) in the executor beside backend_i18n.preload in async_setup_entry (the language-change reload re-runs it). Correct the dependency-transparency and async-dependency comments to name Babel.
- **Verifier fix note:** Warm assets.decimal_mark(hass.config.language) in the executor next to backend_i18n.preload; the language-change reload re-runs setup, so that covers a new language. Fix the two quality_scale comments to name Babel.
- **Verifier reason:** assets.decimal_mark (assets.py:1117-1130, functools.cache) calls Locale.parse + get_decimal_symbol; format_quantity evaluates decimal_mark(lang) for every finite quantity, even a whole one (assets.py:1174). Its only caller chain is shopping.buy_tasks_by_part -> part_restock_label (shopping.py:161, assets.py:1180-1196), which ShoppingListSync._sync_once runs on the loop before any target check (shopping_sync.py:118-124), first at async_at_started (__init__.py:913-921) and on later passes. backend_i18n.preload warms only the JSON tables (backend_i18n.py:128-130). Babel opens global.dat, root.dat and <lang>.dat with builtins.open on first use, and HA's block_async_io wraps builtins.open (core block_async_io.py:137-143, non-strict: a logged warning for a custom integration). The integration test test_event_loop_blocking.py would catch it only if its seed had such a buy task. Precondition: a low part with a stock unit or restock_quantity > 1 that has a buy task. manifest.json requires Babel>=2.12, so quality_scale.yaml's 'No external requirements' (dependency-transparency) and 'No external dependency' (async-dependency) comments are wrong. Effect: one warning log and a few ms of file reads per language per run.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=pydeps python3 tmp-v-B20/babel_open2.py -> en '500 ml' | files opened: ['babel/global.dat', 'babel/locale-data/root.dat', 'babel/locale-data/en.dat']; de '500 ml' | files opened: ['babel/locale-data/de.dat']; second de call opens: []

### B20-5 — Static path re-registers on every entry reload; the RuntimeError guard never fires for a directory, so router entries accumulate

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/panel.py:58`  **Also reported as:** X13-3
- **Defect:** async_register_panel runs on every setup, and registering the same directory static path again appends another StaticResource instead of raising, so each reload leaks one router entry until restart.
- **Failure scenario:** An install whose entry reloads often (the #247 reporter saw a reload about every 10 seconds; problem-sensor and companion reconciles still reload the entry) adds a new aiohttp StaticResource for /home_keeper_panel on each reload - thousands per day - and memory grows until HA restarts. Routing stays correct because the first registered resource matches first.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep a hass.data flag (like _CARD_REGISTERED) and call async_register_static_paths only once per HA run; drop the RuntimeError assumption.
- **Verifier fix note:** Use a hass.data flag (like _CARD_REGISTERED) to register the static path once per HA run, and drop the RuntimeError branch.
- **Verifier reason:** panel.async_register_panel runs on every setup, including each reload (__init__.py:853), and calls hass.http.async_register_static_paths every time, relying on a RuntimeError that never comes (panel.py:57-64). HA's _async_register_static_paths (core http/server.py:377-397, dev) does router.register_resource(StaticResource) for a directory and, in current dev, also router.add_route('GET', url_path, ...); HA disables router freezing (server.py:534-538); aiohttp register_resource has no duplicate check for an unnamed resource, and add_resource makes a new PlainResource because a StaticResource never raw_matches. So each reload appends 1-2 resources. Routing stays correct (first registered match wins). card.py:159-162 already uses a hass.data flag for the same purpose. Impact is a small, bounded-by-reload-count memory growth.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=pydeps python3 tmp-v-B20/router_dup.py (aiohttp 3.14.3, freeze disabled like HA, HA dev registration sequence) -> register #1: no exception; resources for prefix = 2 / #2: = 4 / #3: = 6

### B21-3 — delete_archived_completion promises 'Its name works too' but resolves the name against live tasks, so it silently does nothing (or hits the wrong task)

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:1280`
- **Defect:** An archived completion belongs to a task that has been deleted (its history was moved onto the appliance), but handle_delete_archived_completion resolves task_id with _task_ref over coord.store.get_tasks(); a name therefore never matches the archived entry, and the call returns success without removing anything.
- **Failure scenario:** User follows the HA UI text 'Task name or ID ... Its name works too' and calls delete_archived_completion(asset_id='Furnace', task_id='Replace filter (Furnace)', ts=...). (a) No live task has that name -> NotFound -> the name is passed through -> assets.remove_archived_completion compares it to the stored uuid, matches nothing, returns False -> service succeeds, history unchanged. (b) The wear part was re-added so a new live task carries the same name -> the name resolves to the new task's id -> again no match, silent no-op. (c) Two live tasks share the name -> spurious task_ambiguous error about tasks the user did not mean.
- **Suggested fix:** Resolve task_id against the appliance's own task_history (id first, then the snapshotted task_name, raising task_ambiguous on duplicates) instead of live tasks, and raise a not-found ServiceValidationError when remove_archived_completion changes nothing; or drop the 'name works too' claim for this field.
- **Verifier fix note:** Resolve the name against the appliance's own task_history (task_id first, then the snapshotted task_name) and raise a localized not-found ServiceValidationError when nothing was removed; or remove the 'Its name works too' sentence from strings.json, translations/en.json, services.yaml and the 15 locales.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced. __init__.py:1279-1284 resolves task_id with _task_ref, which resolves over coord.store.get_tasks() (__init__.py:1042-1043), the live tasks. An archived history entry belongs to a deleted task (assets.find_archiving_asset is the delete path), so its name cannot match a live task: _ref returns the name unchanged on NotFound (__init__.py:1038-1039), assets.remove_archived_completion (assets.py:1855-1877) compares only entry['task_id'] and returns False, and store.delete_archived_completion (store.py:2396-2409) returns the asset without error. The service reports success and removes nothing. If a live task now has the same name, the name resolves to that task's id and it is again a silent no-op; two live namesakes give a task_ambiguous error about the wrong tasks. strings.json and translations/en.json (identical for this service) plus services.yaml:623-625 promise 'Its name works too'. Also a mistyped uuid or ts is a silent no-op. Downgraded to low: an admin clean-up service on archived history, rarely called by name, and no data is changed wrongly.

### B21-4 — translations/en.json has drifted from strings.json in 21 strings; HA shows the stale text while the API reference shows the new one

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/translations/en.json:385`
- **Defect:** A custom integration's runtime English (service names, field labels, descriptions, and backend_i18n exception text) comes from translations/en.json, not strings.json; test_en_matches_strings only compares key sets, so values have diverged and the HA action editor shows outdated wording.
- **Failure scenario:** In Developer tools > Actions the user sees 'Pull forward task' (en.json:385) where strings.json/API reference say 'Set task due today'; sign_document_url and sign_part_file_url fields are labelled 'Asset ID' / 'Document ID' / 'Part ID' with no mention that names are accepted (strings.json says 'Appliance name or ID ... Its name works too'); add_task recurrence_type omits that the type is inferred when omitted. api_surface.py:17-21 claims the reference and the HA UI 'read from one source and cannot say different things' - they do not.
- **Suggested fix:** Copy strings.json to translations/en.json (the HA develop-script convention) and change test_en_matches_strings to assert value equality, not just key equality.
- **Verifier fix note:** Copy strings.json over translations/en.json and make test_en_matches_strings assert value equality (translations/en.json == strings.json). The 15 other locales were translated from the older English for these keys, so check them for the same stale meaning (for example the sign_*_url labels that say 'ID' only).
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced with a flat value diff: the key sets are identical but 21 leaf values differ, all under services (add_task recurrence_type and active_season, update_task active_season, skip_task description and note, set_due_today name/description/task_id/origin, sign_document_url asset_id and document_id name+description, sign_part_file_url asset_id and part_id name+description, update_skip description and note, delete_skip, move_skip). Example: strings.json services.set_due_today.name 'Set task due today' vs translations/en.json 'Pull forward task'; sign_document_url.fields.asset_id.name 'Appliance name or ID' vs 'Asset ID'. A custom integration's runtime English comes from translations/en.json (strings.json is only a dev source), and service translations override services.yaml, so the HA UI shows the stale text while ci/generate_api_docs.py:121 builds the API reference from strings.json. tests/unit/test_translations_parity.py:250-257 compares key sets only, so nothing catches value drift.
- **Repro:** python3 flat diff of strings.json vs translations/en.json -> 'keys only in strings: [] / keys only in en: [] / 21 differing values' e.g. services.set_due_today.name S: 'Set task due today' E: 'Pull forward task'

### B21-5 — snooze_task text says Hours is 'ignored when Until is set', but the schema rejects a call that sets both

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/strings.json:346`
- **Defect:** hours and until are vol.Exclusive in the same 'deferral' group, so passing both fails validation; the translated field help (shown in the HA UI) tells the user the opposite.
- **Failure scenario:** A user in the action editor (or a script) fills Hours = 24 and later adds Until = next Tuesday 09:00 believing Hours is ignored; the call fails with 'two or more values in the same group of exclusion deferral' instead of snoozing.
- **Suggested fix:** Reword both descriptions to 'Use either Hours or Until, not both' (and the 15 locale files), or drop vol.Exclusive and let until win as documented.
- **Verifier fix note:** Keep vol.Exclusive (the handler comment explains why a schema default cannot be used) and reword the text in strings.json, translations/en.json, services.yaml and the 15 locales to 'Use Hours or Until, not both.'
- **Verifier reason:** __init__.py:250-251 `vol.Exclusive('hours', 'deferral')` and `vol.Exclusive('until', 'deferral')`, and the comment at __init__.py:240-242 and the handler comment at __init__.py:1355-1359 state that passing both is rejected. strings.json and translations/en.json say hours is 'Ignored when Until is set.' and until 'Setting this leaves Hours unused.'; services.yaml:691 and 701-703 say the same. A caller that sets both gets a voluptuous ExclusiveInvalid ('two or more values in the same group of exclusion deferral'), which contradicts the help text. voluptuous is not installed here, so not executed, but vol.Exclusive semantics and the code's own comments agree. Low: the error message names the conflict and the user can recover.

### F01-3 — In-panel Back leaves the panel after a deep link plus one drill-in (`_hasHistory` never resets)

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:718`
- **Defect:** `_hasHistory` flips true on the first push and stays true, so `_closeDetail`/`_closeSettingsSection` call `history.back()` even when the current entry is the deep-linked one with nothing of the panel behind it.
- **Failure scenario:** A notification opens `/home-keeper/tasks/A` (notifications.py:634), a fresh element with `_hasHistory=false`. The user taps the task's appliance or part chip: `_openDetail`/`_openPart` push, `_hasHistory=true`. They press Back (panel or Android back) and land on task A again. Now the panel Back button runs `history.back()`, which pops the entry before the deep link: the app/tab leaves Home Keeper, or, when the app was cold-started on the link, nothing happens and the button is dead. Expected: the fallback `_navigate({view, detail:null}, true)` to the task list. Same root cause in `_closeSettingsSection` when a section was reached by a replace (rail link, `openProfile`) after any earlier push.
- **Suggested fix:** Record the panel's own depth in history state: push `{hkDepth: (history.state?.hkDepth ?? 0) + 1}` in `_navigate` (and carry it on replace), then in `_closeDetail`/`_closeSettingsSection` call `history.back()` only when `history.state?.hkDepth > 0`, else navigate to the list/index with replace.
- **Verifier fix note:** The hkDepth idea works, but Home Assistant's dialog manager also writes history.state when a dialog with history opens (for example the date picker from the form), so carry the marker through and read it defensively, or track the depth per instance from the route changes the panel itself caused.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced. panel.ts:499 `_hasHistory = false` is set true at panel.ts:509 on the first push and no code clears it (grep finds only these two writes). `_openDetail`/`_openPart` push (panel.ts:662, 671, no replace flag). Path: a fresh element opens on a deep link D (notifications.py:634 builds `/home-keeper/tasks/<id>`), so `_hasHistory=false`; tapping the appliance chip pushes E and sets it true; Back pops to D; the panel Back at D then runs `_closeDetail` (panel.ts:718-727), which calls history.back() and pops the entry before D: the app leaves Home Keeper, or the button does nothing on a cold start. Without the earlier push the same button uses the replace fallback to the list, so the behavior is inconsistent. `_closeSettingsSection` (panel.ts:713-715) has the same flaw after a replace-reached section (rail link panel-settings.ts:2119, openProfile panel-settings.ts:1616). No test covers deep link + push + pop + panel Back: smoke.spec.ts:221-231 tests deep link only, smoke.spec.ts:246/310 test push then page.goBack. Severity lowered: navigation only, no data change, and the tab bar still reaches the lists.
- **Repro:** code trace: deep link D (fresh element) -> _openDetail push (panel.ts:662 -> 509 _hasHistory=true) -> Back to D -> panel #back-btn -> _closeDetail panel.ts:719 `if (this._hasHistory) history.back()` -> leaves the panel

### F01-6 — Renders that land after unmount re-arm the 45-minute re-sign timer and the media-query listener on the detached panel

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:1596`
- **Defect:** `_render` only checks `shadowRoot`, which survives disconnection, so an async path that finishes after `disconnectedCallback` rebuilds the detached tree and re-registers everything the disconnect just tore down.
- **Failure scenario:** On an appliance page with a manual, the user starts a large upload (or taps Done, or a refresh is mid-retry) and switches to a dashboard. `disconnectedCallback` clears `_resignTimer` and `_sheetQuery`. The upload's `finally` (panel-upload.ts:144) or the `_refresh` continuation calls `_render()`: `_syncDrawerModality` creates a new MediaQueryList listener (and re-adds `_drawerOnKey` if the drawer was open), `_hydrate` -> `_signFiles` -> `_armResign(true)`. That timer re-arms itself every 45 minutes forever, sending sign websocket calls from a panel nobody can see and keeping its whole shadow tree alive. An in-flight `_signFiles` at disconnect time re-arms it the same way.
- **Suggested fix:** Return early from `_render` and `_signFiles`/`_armResign` when `!this.isConnected` (re-render in `connectedCallback` already covers re-attachment), and abort `_uploadAbort`/clear `_uploadShowTimer` in `disconnectedCallback`.
- **Verifier fix note:** Guard `_armResign(true)` inside `_signFiles` with isConnected as well as `_render`, because a `_signFiles` already in flight at unmount re-arms the timer without any render.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced. `_render` checks only shadowRoot (panel.ts:1595-1596), never isConnected, and ends with `_hydrate` (1721) and `_syncDrawerModality` (1728). disconnectedCallback (panel.ts:525-548) nulls `_sheetQuery`, so a later `_syncDrawerModality` binds a new MediaQueryList listener (panel.ts:1846-1853) and, if the drawer was open, re-adds the document Escape handler (1866-1880). `_hydrate` -> `_signFiles` (1974) -> `_armResign(refs.length > 0)` (2085-2087) re-arms a timer that reschedules itself every 45 minutes (2098-2107, documents.ts:120) for as long as the detached element still shows an appliance with files. Paths that render after unmount: the `_refresh` continuation (844-857) after any action, `_init` after its up-to-4 s component wait (733-742), and an in-flight `_signFiles` at unmount. Only `_ensureMarkdown` (1542) guards with isConnected. Real but needs a race (leave the panel while a call is in flight): a memory leak plus a few sign calls per 45 minutes, nothing user-visible.
- **Repro:** code trace: disconnectedCallback clears _sheetQuery/_resignTimer -> pending _refresh resolves -> _render (no isConnected check) -> _syncDrawerModality re-binds matchMedia listener; _hydrate -> _signFiles -> _armResign(true) -> setTimeout(_signFiles, 45 min) repeats

### F01-7 — `narrow` changes never reach the toolbar's ha-menu-button, so the sidebar button is missing after a rotate or resize

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:129`
- **Defect:** `narrow` is a plain field copied into the menu button only when `_render` runs; `set hass` refreshes the button's `hass` but not its `narrow`.
- **Failure scenario:** An iPad in landscape (not narrow) shows the panel; the user rotates to portrait. Home Assistant sets `narrow = true` on the panel and collapses the sidebar into a hidden drawer, but `ha-menu-button` still has `narrow=false`, so it renders nothing (it shows only when narrow or the sidebar is always hidden). The panel has no way to open the HA sidebar until something triggers a full render (tab tap, opening a row).
- **Suggested fix:** Add a `narrow` setter that stores the value and updates the mounted `ha-menu-button` (`this.shadowRoot?.querySelector('ha-menu-button')`) in place.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced on the panel side. `narrow` is a plain field (panel.ts:129) with no setter; the only write to the menu button's narrow is in `_hydrate` (panel.ts:1992-2001); `set hass` (panel.ts:292-300) updates only `.hass` on live elements; the sheet media query handler calls `_syncDrawerModality`, not `_render` (panel.ts:1852), and its threshold is 1150px, not HA's narrow threshold. HA's ha-panel-custom sets `narrow` as a property on the panel element when it changes, and ha-menu-button renders only when its own narrow is true (or the sidebar is always hidden), so after a rotate or resize into narrow the button stays hidden until the next full render. No test sets narrow after mount (grep in tests/frontend). Low: any tab tap or row open re-renders and fixes it.

### F01-8 — The 'Counted' scope is saved to localStorage but `_loadPrefs` rejects it, so it resets to All on every reload

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:572`
- **Defect:** `TaskFilter` gained 'counted' in #321 and `_setFilter` persists it, but the restore allow-list was not extended.
- **Failure scenario:** User picks the Counted pill (persisted as `home-keeper.filter=counted`), reloads the page or reopens the panel: `_loadPrefs` does not match 'counted' and leaves `_filter='all'`, while every other scope (overdue, soon, shopping) is restored.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `f === 'counted'` to the check (or validate against a shared TASK_FILTERS list so the type and the restore cannot drift).
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced. panel.ts:571-573 restores the filter only for 'all', 'overdue', 'soon' and 'shopping'; TaskFilter (panel-types.ts:151) includes 'counted', the Counted pill is always offered (panel-controls.ts:96), and `_setFilter` (panel.ts:602-607) persists any value. A saved 'counted' falls back to 'all' on every reload.
- **Repro:** node tmp-v-F01/f8.js (evaluates the `_loadPrefs` condition taken from panel.ts for each TaskFilter member): all restored / overdue restored / soon restored / shopping restored / counted DROPPED -> all

### F02-1 — A hidden Last completed value leaks into a new one-off task, which is created already completed

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:1218`
- **Defect:** buildTaskPayload sends last_completed for every id-less task regardless of recurrence type, but the schema hides that field for one-off tasks, so a value typed before switching to Once is still sent and the backend records it as a completion of the one-off.
- **Failure scenario:** Add task (defaults to floating), fill Last completed with last week, then change Recurrence to Once and pick a due date next week, press Create. The field disappears but the edit state keeps last_completed; the payload carries it; build_task runs apply_completion on the one-off, which sets next_due=None. The new task is born Completed, never comes due or notifies, and is later purged by one_off_retention_days. The form's rule summary gives no hint. Same path in the dashboard card's create form.
- **Suggested fix:** Send last_completed only when the current type offers it: `if (!task.id && task.recurrence_type !== 'one-off')` (mirror the schema predicate, including locked fields). Add a test: one-off create with a stale last_completed must not carry it.
- **Verifier fix note:** Mirror the schema predicate exactly: send last_completed only when !task.id && task.recurrence_type !== 'one-off' && the field is not locked. Fix it in buildTaskPayload, not only in the panel, because card.ts:721 uses the same function.
- **Verifier reason:** The schema hides last_completed for a one-off (forms.ts:677), but buildTaskPayload sends it for every id-less task with no type check (forms.ts:1218-1221). The panel merges each section event into the edit state (panel-task-form.ts:365-370), so the typed value stays after the switch to Once, and _submitForm passes that state to buildTaskPayload (panel.ts:942). build_task applies any seed (models.py:956-977) and apply_completion sets next_due=None for a one-off (recurrence.py:542-556). The task is born complete. Rare: the user must fill Last completed and then change the type to Once, and the type is normally picked first.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types tmp-v-F02/repro.mjs -> 'F02-1 payload.last_completed = 2026-09-23T09:00:00.000Z recurrence_type = one-off'; python3 tmp-v-F02/f021.py (models.build_task on that payload) -> 'one-off next_due = None | completions = 1 | last_completed = 2026-09-23T09:00:00+00:00'

### F02-3 — Edit form always re-sends the stored meter baseline, defeating the backend re-baseline on entity change and reverting concurrent re-anchors

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:1091`
- **Defect:** taskFormData seeds sensor_baseline from task.sensor.baseline and the panel adopts it into the edit state, so every usage-task save sends an explicit baseline, which merge_update treats as a user choice.
- **Failure scenario:** (a) A usage task on sensor.plug_1_energy has baseline 12.3 kWh, target 50. The user swaps the plug and re-points the task at sensor.plug_2_energy, which reads 1480 kWh. merge_update documents that changing the entity re-baselines, but the payload carries baseline 12.3 explicitly (emptying the box cannot remove it: undefined falls back to task.sensor.baseline), so the new meter reads 1467.7 of 50 used and the task arms at once. (b) The drawer is open while someone completes the task on the dashboard or by NFC; completion re-stamps the baseline to the current reading, then Save sends the pre-completion baseline and the task comes due again immediately.
- **Suggested fix:** Send baseline only when the user changed it from the loaded value, or when it differs from task.sensor.baseline; drop it when sensor_entity_id differs from task.sensor.entity_id. Add a test: switching the entity omits the old baseline.
- **Verifier fix note:** Also correct the stale comment at models.py:1042-1044. It says the panel payload never carries the baseline, and that has not been true since the baseline box was added. Minimum fix: omit baseline when sensor_entity_id differs from task.sensor.entity_id and the user did not change the box.
- **Verifier reason:** taskFormData seeds sensor_baseline from task.sensor.baseline (forms.ts:869). The usage schema shows a baseline box on edit (forms.ts:591-594), so the panel adopts the value. buildTaskPayload sends it (forms.ts:1091-1093). merge_update re-baselines on an entity change only when 'baseline' is not in the new binding (models.py:1049-1058), so the panel can never ask for a re-baseline, and an emptied box falls back to the stored value. The box shows the old number, so the result is visible before save. Case (b) is the general stale-drawer overwrite that every field has.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types tmp-v-F02/repro2.mjs (usage task, entity switched to sensor.plug_2_energy) -> 'F02-3 payload.sensor = {"entity_id":"sensor.plug_2_energy","mode":"usage","target":50,"baseline":12.3}'

### F02-4 — Duplicate drops the active season, so a copy of a seasonal task is due all year

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:989`
- **Defect:** duplicateTaskSeed is an allowlist that omits active_season, so buildTaskPayload sees no season and sends active_season: null for the copy.
- **Failure scenario:** Duplicate 'Mow lawn, every week, active April 1 to October 31'. The seed has no active_season or season_on, seasonEnabled(seed) is false, the create payload carries active_season: null, and the copy comes due every week in winter. The docstring says the copy keeps the rule and must not behave differently from its original.
- **Suggested fix:** Carry `active_season: seasonWindows(task).map((w) => ({ ...w }))` (and nothing else of the flat season_* state) in duplicateTaskSeed; add active_season to the floating fixture of the payload-equality test.
- **Verifier reason:** duplicateTaskSeed is an allowlist with no active_season (forms.ts:989-1012). seasonEnabled(seed) is false (forms.ts:270-274), so buildTaskPayload sends active_season: null (forms.ts:1169-1183). The docstring (forms.ts:958-961) says the copy keeps the rule. The create drawer shows the season switch off before Create, so a careful user can see it. That makes the severity low.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types tmp-v-F02/repro.mjs -> 'F02-4 seed has active_season? false -> payload.active_season = null'

### F02-5 — Any panel save truncates a task's season windows to 6, deleting windows the API stored

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:1172`
- **Defect:** seasonCount caps at MAX_SEASON_WINDOWS and buildTaskPayload always rebuilds the whole list from that count, so windows 7+ written by a service call or import are silently removed on an unrelated edit.
- **Failure scenario:** A task has 8 season windows set with home_keeper.update_task or an import (the backend has no cap). The user opens Edit and only renames it. season_count is adopted as 6, payload.active_season holds 6 windows, merge_update stores them, and next_due is recomputed because active_season changed. Windows 7 and 8 are gone with no warning.
- **Suggested fix:** When the stored list is longer than the cap and the user did not touch the season, omit active_season from the payload (merge_update keeps it); or append stored windows beyond the cap; or show all stored windows.
- **Verifier fix note:** The simplest safe fix: when seasonWindows(task).length > MAX_SEASON_WINDOWS and the user did not touch a season field, omit active_season from the payload (merge_update keeps the stored value).
- **Verifier reason:** seasonCount caps at MAX_SEASON_WINDOWS = 6 (forms.ts:240, 282-286). buildTaskPayload rebuilds the whole list from that count on every save of a scheduled task (forms.ts:1169-1178). The backend has no length cap (models.py:632-645; __init__.py:175/213 vol.Any(None, dict, [dict])), and forms.ts:236-238 says a service caller can write more. So a rename truncates windows 7 and up with no warning. Rare: more than 6 windows needs a service call or an import.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types tmp-v-F02/repro.mjs -> 'F02-5 stored 8 windows -> payload sends 6'

### F02-7 — Threshold value and usage target boxes lack step 'any', unlike the declarative dialog's same fields

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:575`
- **Defect:** sensor_value and sensor_target use HA's default step 1, so a decimal threshold such as 20.5 is flagged invalid, and on iOS the target box gets a digits-only keypad with no decimal key.
- **Failure scenario:** A user makes a threshold task 'temperature >= 25.5' or a usage task 'every 2.5 m3'. ha-selector-number renders `step=1` with autoValidate, so the box shows a validation error for a value the backend accepts (_finite_float). For sensor_target (min 0) inputmode is 'numeric', which on iPhone offers no decimal separator. The declarative companion dialog uses step 'any' for the same threshold value and target.
- **Suggested fix:** Use `{ number: { mode: 'box', step: 'any' } }` for sensor_value and `selNumber(0, 'any')` for sensor_target, and pin both in sensor-form tests.
- **Verifier reason:** sensor_value has no step (forms.ts:575) and sensor_target uses selNumber(0) with no step (forms.ts:580). HA ha-selector-number sets .step to step ?? 1, uses autoValidate, and sets inputmode 'numeric' unless step is 'any' or not whole (tmp-F02/number.ts:114-137). A decimal therefore shows a step validation error. The number still reaches the payload, because _handleInputChange emits Number(value), so the save works. On iOS the target box gets a digits-only keypad.

### F02-8 — Fixed-schedule anchor is required by the backend but the form neither marks it required nor defaults it

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:666`
- **Defect:** A new fixed task with the 'First occurrence' box left empty sends anchor '' and the save fails with the raw backend error.
- **Failure scenario:** Add task, choose Fixed, set interval and frequency, press Create without picking a first occurrence. taskFormData seeds anchor '', the payload sends `haDateTimeToIso('') ?? ''` = '', and normalize_fields raises "missing required field: 'anchor'". A one-off due date is defaulted to now for the same situation, and the anchor field has no required marker or help text saying it is needed.
- **Suggested fix:** Mark anchor `required: true` in the schema, or default a new fixed task's anchor to now as the one-off due does.
- **Verifier reason:** The anchor field is not required (forms.ts:665-667). taskFormData seeds '' (forms.ts:842). haDateTimeToIso('') returns undefined (forms.ts:201-202), so payload.anchor falls back to '' (forms.ts:1163). _require treats '' as missing (models.py:435-437). The save fails with a visible error that the user can correct. No data is lost.

### F03-2 — A failed Add leaves an id-less blank row in _options; the next save resurrects it, and a retry creates two rows

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-settings.ts:1331`
- **Defect:** addProfile and addNotification write the blank row optimistically with no rollback. On failure there is no render, so the row is invisible but stays in p._options, and every later save of that list sends it to the backend, which assigns it a new id.
- **Failure scenario:** Rename a profile. About 600 ms later its autosave starts a config-entry reload. Press 'Add profile' during that reload. set_options answers not_loaded, the toast shows the error and no row appears, but p._options.profiles now ends with {id:''}. Press Add again: the list sent is [..., blank, blank], so two 'New profile' rows are created. Or edit any other profile: persistDebounced maps over p._options.profiles, the blank goes along, and a profile the user was told failed appears. Notifications behave the same, and the new row carries a real target. The rail count and the section summary also count the hidden row before that.
- **Suggested fix:** Pass rollbackOnFailure: true for both adds. There is no typed text to keep, so restoring the previous list is right. Also drop id-less rows from any list that is built from p._options.
- **Verifier fix note:** rollbackOnFailure: true on both adds is sufficient and matches the delete path; it is already guarded by isNewest.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced by reading. addProfile (panel-settings.ts:1331) and addNotification (:1734-1740) call persistOptionList without rollbackOnFailure. persistOptionList writes the list optimistically before the await (:1034), rolls back only with rollbackOnFailure (:1062), and renders only on success (:1051), so a failed add leaves an id-less blank in p._options off screen. Later saves build from p._options (:1168 profiles; listWith for notifications), and the backend gives the blank a fresh uuid (profiles.py:122, notifications.py:321); a second Add sends two blanks. Trigger is rare: set_options must fail (not_loaded while another save's reload runs, websocket_api.py:69, or a network error). Impact is a spurious empty row the user can delete; the blank notification has auto off (:1732), so nothing sends by itself. Downgraded from medium.

### F03-4 — Import file picker reads any file with no size check and swallows read errors

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-settings.ts:2056`
- **Defect:** The picker loads the whole file into memory and into the textarea before any size check. file.text() has no catch, so a failed read shows nothing and leaves an unhandled rejection.
- **Failure scenario:** The user picks a large file by mistake. accept is only a hint, so a multi-hundred-MB .json or backup is possible. The whole file is read and put into ha-textarea, and the render can freeze the tab. transfer.tooLarge shows only after Preview is pressed. If the read rejects (the file was moved, or the browser refuses to read it), the card stays unchanged with no error. The error path at panel.ts:1468-1476 is never reached.
- **Suggested fix:** Check file.size against MAX_IMPORT_WS_BYTES before reading, and set p._transfer.error to transfer.tooLarge when it is over the limit. Add a .catch that sets p._transfer.error and renders.
- **Verifier fix note:** Check file.size against the websocket limit before the read (set transfer.tooLarge), and add a .catch that sets p._transfer.error and renders.
- **Verifier reason:** panel-settings.ts:2052-2063: file.text() has no size check before the read and no .catch, so a read failure gives no message and an unhandled rejection, and an oversize file is loaded into the textarea before the only size gate (panel.ts:1468 on Preview). Impact is minor: an admin picks the file on purpose, and 'freeze the tab' is speculative.

### F04-2 — relativeDay counts rolling 24h windows, so a history row contradicts its own date

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/utils.ts:570`  **Also reported as:** X04-9
- **Defect:** relativeDay rounds elapsed milliseconds to days instead of comparing local calendar days, and panel-history renders it right beside the calendar date of the same completion.
- **Failure scenario:** Repro (TZ=America/Los_Angeles, copy of the function): now = Sep 30 08:00; completion Sep 29 23:30 -> row reads 'Sep 29, 2026 · today'. Now = Sep 30 20:00; completion Sep 29 07:00 -> 'Sep 29, 2026 · 2 days ago'; completion Sep 28 07:00 -> '3 days ago'. About half of all history rows are off by one depending on the time of day. dueLabel (utils.ts:950-957) already fixed the same bug for due dates ('Compare calendar days (local midnights), not rolling 24h windows').
- **Suggested fix:** Use the same startOfDay(local) difference as dueLabel: Math.round((startOfDay(now) - startOfDay(d)) / 86_400_000), keeping the <=0 -> today clamp; update the rounding test.
- **Verifier reason:** utils.ts:569-574 does Math.round((now - d) / 86400000), so at 08:00 on Sep 30 a completion at 23:30 on Sep 29 reads 'today' beside the date Sep 29, and 36 h rounds to '2 days ago'. dueLabel (utils.ts:950-957) already uses local midnights. Low: a label beside the correct date, no effect on data or behavior.

### F04-3 — parseRoute throws URIError on a malformed percent-escape, breaking the panel's route setter

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/utils.ts:1258`
- **Defect:** Every decodeURIComponent in parseRoute is unguarded, so a path segment such as '50%off' or a truncated '%E0' throws from the panel's `set route`.
- **Failure scenario:** A user opens a truncated or hand-typed link like /home-keeper/tasks/%E0 or /home-keeper/appliances/50%off. decodeURIComponent throws 'URIError: URI malformed' (verified in node) inside `set route` (panel.ts:311-314). The location is never applied; HA's custom-panel wrapper assigns `route` while creating the element and appends it only after the assignment returns, so a cold load of such a URL can leave the panel blank until a full reload.
- **Suggested fix:** Wrap decoding in a safe helper (try decodeURIComponent, fall back to the raw segment or to the list view) so parseRoute never throws; add a malformed-escape test.
- **Verifier fix note:** A safeDecode helper that returns the raw segment on URIError is enough. A test with '%E0' kills it.
- **Verifier reason:** Every decodeURIComponent in parseRoute (utils.ts:1241, 1253, 1258, 1261, 1267, 1270) has no guard, and panel.ts:311-314 'set route' calls parseRoute with no try/catch, so a malformed escape throws out of the setter. The browser URL parser keeps '%E0' and '50%off' in the pathname as typed. Not verified: whether HA's own router decodes the path first (its curPath may call decodeURIComponent). If it does, HA fails first on a malformed URL, and the panel failure moves to a literal '%25' that parseRoute decodes a second time. The 'panel blank' consequence was also not verified. Low: generated IDs never hold '%', so only a hand-typed or truncated URL gets here.
- **Repro:** node SCRATCH/tmp-v-F04/route.js -> '%E0 -> THROWS URIError: URI malformed', '% -> THROWS URIError', URL('http://h/home-keeper/tasks/%E0').pathname = /home-keeper/tasks/%E0

### F04-4 — Season range is assembled in hard-coded English 'Month Day–Month Day & …' order

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/utils.ts:687`
- **Defect:** recurrenceText builds the active-season range by concatenating a nominative month name and a bare day number, which is only correct in English.
- **Failure scenario:** A seasonal task {start:'04-15', end:'10-31'} reads 'April 15–Oktober 31' in German (should be '15. April–31. Oktober'), 'Avril 15–Octobre 31' in French (capitalised, wrong order), 'Kwiecień 15' in Polish (nominative, should be genitive '15 kwietnia'), '四月 15–十月 31' in Chinese. Every list row, the detail page and the form preview show it.
- **Suggested fix:** Format each boundary with Intl.DateTimeFormat(getLanguage(), {month:'long', day:'numeric'}) on a fixed leap-year date (new Date(2000, m-1, d)), and join windows with tlist() or a translated separator.
- **Verifier reason:** utils.ts:683-688 builds `${month} ${day}–${month} ${day}` from opt.month.N (nominative: de 'April', pl 'Kwiecień') and joins windows with ' & '. That order and case is correct only in English. Tests check English only.

### F04-5 — Backstop unit in the usage summary is never pluralised ('or every 1 months')

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/utils.ts:656`
- **Defect:** The also_every clause uses the plural-only `opt.unit.<unit>` label for any interval instead of the plural-aware `recurrence.unit.*` keys the main summary uses.
- **Failure scenario:** A usage task with a 1-month time backstop reads 'Every 300 h of use, or every 1 months'; in Polish 'co 1 miesiące' / 'co 5 miesiące' (correct: 'miesiąc' / 'miesięcy'). Same text on the list row, detail page (panel-detail.ts:248) and form preview (forms.ts:1426).
- **Suggested fix:** Build `every` with tn(`recurrence.unit.${unit.replace(/s$/, '')}`, interval) (or a plural template 'recurrence.every'), and add a test for interval 1.
- **Verifier reason:** utils.ts:656 uses t(`opt.unit.${unit}`), which is plural-only (en 'months', pl 'miesiące'), for any interval. The main summary uses tn(`recurrence.unit.${base}`, n) at utils.ts:664-665, so the backstop reads 'or every 1 months'.

### F04-7 — looksLikeMarkdown regex is quadratic and runs un-debounced on every keystroke

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/markdown.ts:116`
- **Defect:** The multiline alternatives starting with `^\s*` (and `\[[^\]]*\]`) backtrack across every following whitespace line, so a note with many blank lines or '[' characters freezes the editor.
- **Failure scenario:** Measured in node: 20,000 newlines -> 1.34 s, 40,000 -> 5.4 s per test() call; 40,000 '[' -> 1.6 s. update() calls looksLikeMarkdown before the debounce (markdown.ts:182), so each keystroke in such a note blocks the UI for that long. Notes have no backend length cap, and add_task/update_task can set one from any automation.
- **Suggested fix:** Use `^[ \t]*` instead of `^\s*` so a line-start match cannot span lines, bound the link scan (e.g. `\[[^\]\n]{0,200}\]`), and/or run the test inside the debounce.
- **Verifier fix note:** ^[ \t]* and [^\]\n] remove the cross-line backtracking. The MARKDOWN_HINT regex is on the Stryker surface (markdown.ts), so the change needs a test that kills the mutants it adds.
- **Verifier reason:** The quadratic backtracking is real: the multiline ^\s* alternatives cross blank lines, and \[[^\]]* scans to the end on an unclosed '['. looksLikeMarkdown runs before the debounce (markdown.ts:182). It needs pathological input, though: 2,000 blank lines cost 13 ms, a realistic 5 KB note costs 0.1 ms, and the freeze needs about 20,000 consecutive blank lines or 40,000 unclosed '['. Low.
- **Repro:** node SCRATCH/tmp-v-F04/re.js -> newlines x2000 12.6 ms; x5000 80.8 ms; x20000 1312.9 ms; x40000 5016.4 ms; brackets x40000 1325.0 ms; realistic 5KB prose 0.1 ms

### F05-4 — Card configured with a profile silently shows every task when the profile is missing or its fetch failed

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:596`
- **Defect:** _shaped falls back to the card's own filters (default 'all') when the configured profile id is not in _profiles, which happens after the profile is deleted or when getProfiles fails and is caught to [].
- **Failure scenario:** A kid's tablet card is set to profile 'Kid chores' (editor stores the id). An admin deletes that profile (ProfileInUseError checks notifications only, not dashboard cards), or a refresh lands in an entry-reload window so getProfiles rejects and is replaced by []. profile is undefined, so filterTasks runs with the card's own config (filter absent = 'all') and the card lists every task in the house, with no sign that the profile is gone. Also, setConfig adding a profile to an already-loaded card re-renders without fetching profiles, so the same fallback shows until the next refresh.
- **Suggested fix:** When config.profile is set but not found, show an alert (profile not found) and no rows instead of falling back; on a failed getProfiles keep the previous _profiles; fetch profiles in setConfig when the profile changes.
- **Verifier fix note:** Keep the suggestion; on a missing profile render an alert and no rows rather than the card filters.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced. card.ts:596-603 falls back to filterTasks(this._tasks, this._config, ...) when the profile id is not in _profiles; the stub config has filter 'all' (card.ts:410), so the card lists every task with no message. Profile delete is blocked only for notifications (options.py:263-306 profile_removals_in_use, raised at :335); the backend cannot see Lovelace card configs. card-filter.ts:59-61 documents that a set profile 'decides which tasks show'. setConfig (card.ts:413-418) re-renders without fetching profiles, so a newly chosen profile shows the fallback until the next refresh (editor preview). The getProfiles-fails branch (card.ts:563-565) is rare: getTasks runs first and would fail first in a reload window; get_profiles is not admin-gated (websocket_api.py:1252-1267), so non-admin users are not affected permanently. Visible, recoverable, no data effect, so low.

### F05-5 — A failed Done from the card is silent

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:657`
- **Defect:** _complete only logs to the console on error, while _setDueToday in the same file toasts error.actionFailed.
- **Failure scenario:** A non-admin taps Done while the store save fails, the task was deleted elsewhere, or the entry is reloading (not_loaded). Nothing on screen says the completion was not recorded; after the refresh the row looks unchanged, and the user may assume it worked or tap again with no idea why.
- **Suggested fix:** toast(this, t('error.actionFailed')) (or the server message) in the catch, as _setDueToday does.
- **Verifier reason:** card.ts:655-659 catches the completeTask error and only logs it, while _setDueToday (card.ts:676-679) toasts error.actionFailed. api.completeTask (api.ts:253-265) does not surface errors itself. Realistic triggers are a not_loaded reload window or a lost connection; a deleted task disappears on the refresh anyway.

### F05-7 — With show_disabled, a switched-off task passes the overdue/today filters and gets the red overdue rail

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:927`
- **Defect:** The card row and matchesFilter ignore enabled:false, unlike the panel row and scopeMatches, which were fixed for exactly this.
- **Failure scenario:** show_disabled: true, filter: overdue, sort: due. A pool task switched off in October with next_due in October heads the Overdue card with the red rail beside a 'Disabled' chip, above genuinely late work. The panel's Overdue pill excludes it and its row has no rail.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `task.enabled !== false &&` to the card rail and to the overdue/today branches of matchesFilter.
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced. With show_disabled: true, matchesFilter overdue/today (card-filter.ts:503, 508) has no enabled check, so a switched-off task with an old next_due passes Overdue and Today, and card.ts:927 gives its row the red rail (card.ts:1007, :185) beside the 'Disabled' chip (utils.ts:1006). panel-lists.ts:242 and panel-controls.ts:285 exclude disabled tasks on purpose, and card-filter.ts:501-502 says the card Overdue filter must match the panel pill. Needs the non-default show_disabled option.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types f7.mjs: show_disabled + filter overdue -> ['pool_off','late']; filter today -> ['pool_off','late']; statusBucket(pool_off)=disabled

### F05-8 — setConfig accepts a scalar for list options; `areas: kitchen` silently empties the card

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card-filter.ts:535`
- **Defect:** filterTasks builds `new Set(config.areas)` from a string, which is a set of its characters, so nothing matches and no config error is shown.
- **Failure scenario:** YAML `areas: kitchen` (or `labels: dog`, `recurrence_types: fixed`, `devices: <id>`): 'kitchen'.length is truthy, new Set('kitchen') = {k,i,t,c,h,e,n}, no task area id is in it, and the card shows 'No tasks match this filter.' HA cards normally throw in setConfig so the user sees a config error.
- **Suggested fix:** In setConfig, normalize a string to a one-item list (or throw) for areas/devices/labels/recurrence_types, and throw on unknown filter/sort/group_by values.
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced. card.ts:413-417 accepts any object; card-filter.ts:535-539 builds new Set(config.areas) etc., and a YAML scalar string becomes a set of its characters, so the card shows 'No tasks match this filter.' with no config error. Only a one-character id still matches. YAML-only misconfiguration; the GUI editor always writes lists.
- **Repro:** node --experimental-strip-types f8.mjs: areas: ['kitchen'] -> ['a']; areas: 'kitchen' -> []; recurrence_types: 'fixed' -> []

### F05-9 — Card editor cannot select the shopping/counted filters or the 'use' recurrence type

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:137`
- **Defect:** FILTER_OPTS lists all/overdue/today/soon/no_due only, and RECURRENCE_OPTS omits 'use', although CardFilter and RecurrenceType include them.
- **Failure scenario:** A user who wants a shopping-list card or a counted-wear card in the GUI editor has no option for it; a YAML config with filter: shopping opens in the editor with a blank Filter field.
- **Suggested fix:** Add the shopping and counted filter options and the use recurrence option to the editor lists.
- **Verifier reason:** card.ts:137-143 FILTER_OPTS has no 'shopping' or 'counted' though CardFilter (card-filter.ts:22-29) and matchesFilter support them, and card.ts:156-162 RECURRENCE_OPTS omits 'use' (types.ts:9-17). CHANGELOG says 'Use the Shopping filter beside it' for the card and documents filter: shopping as YAML; the same gap in the recurrence list was fixed before (CHANGELOG 'recurrence filter was missing half the task types'). The blank-field claim for a YAML value outside the options depends on HA's select selector and was not checked.

### F06-3 — Live preview shows a red validation error for draft states the dialog itself creates (blank name on Add, empty value/target after a mode switch)

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/websocket_api.py:1519`
- **Defect:** The preview normalizes the draft with the full save validation, relaxing only an empty template, so it refuses to list matches while the name, threshold value, usage target or state box is still empty.
- **Failure scenario:** Admin clicks Add: the blank draft has name '' so the preview area shows the red alert 'name is required' instead of matches. Admin types a name, then switches the trigger to Threshold or Usage: triggerForMode deliberately leaves value/target empty, so the preview becomes 'sensor.value must be a number' / 'sensor.target must be a number' and the match list, overlap warning and Exclude buttons disappear until a number is typed. The backend already made this exception for an empty template for exactly this reason.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the preview a draft-tolerant normalization (placeholder name, and skip trigger value/target/state requirements, as allow_missing_template does), or have the panel substitute placeholders before calling preview.
- **Verifier fix note:** Either route is fine. The narrowest is a draft flag in normalize_declarative_companion that substitutes a placeholder name and lets normalize_sensor skip value/target/state, as allow_missing_template already does; the preview only needs selection + trigger mode to list matches.
- **Verifier reason:** The Add button opens a blank draft (panel-declarative.ts:309 -> openDeclarativeForm(p, null); emptyDeclarativeCompanion at :148 has name ''), and the dialog schedules a preview unconditionally (panel-declarative.ts:965). ws preview (websocket_api.py:1519-1521) relaxes only the template; name is required=True (declarative_companions.py:316-318, DeclarativeCompanionValidationError subclasses TaskValidationError, so the handler returns invalid_declarative_companion). refreshPreview's catch (panel-declarative.ts:1199-1200) replaces the match list with a red ha-alert. The same happens after a switch to threshold/usage, because TRIGGER_DEFAULTS leaves value/target empty on purpose (panel-declarative.ts:114-118). Severity lowered: no data effect, and the preview comes back on the next change once the box is filled, because every section change calls schedulePreview.
- **Repro:** python3 tmp-v-F06/repro3.py -> blank Add draft -> DeclarativeCompanionValidationError name is required; named, threshold w/o value -> sensor.value must be a number; named, usage w/o target -> sensor.target must be a number; named, state blank -> sensor.state is required; named, template blank -> OK

### F06-4 — Key editor hides task_names whose key is not in selection.translation_keys, then deletes them on the first key edit

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/declarative-filters.ts:105`
- **Defect:** keyRows builds rows only from translation_keys and applyKeyRows rebuilds task_names only from those rows, but the backend applies task_names to any matched entity by key, with or without a translation_keys filter.
- **Failure scenario:** A companion is created by the documented service/websocket contract with selection {target_integration:'roborock', domain:'sensor'} and task_template.task_names {main_brush_time_left:'Replace main brush', filter_time_left:'Replace filter'} and no translation_keys (all sensors match; the named ones get friendly task names). Admin opens Edit: the key editor shows no rows and the summary says no filters. Admin clicks one key in the key list (or Add key) to restrict the selection: onChange -> applyKeyRows(rows) sets draft.task_template.task_names to only that row's (blank) name, so both stored names are deleted on save and the tasks are renamed back to the entity names.
- **Suggested fix:** Show rows for the union of translation_keys and task_names keys (marking the ones that do not filter), or carry task_names entries whose key has no row through applyKeyRows unchanged.
- **Verifier fix note:** Carrying unmatched entries through applyKeyRows is not enough on its own: also seed a new row's name from the stored table (toggleKeyRow / Add key), or a picked key still loses its name. When translation_keys ends up non-empty, names for keys outside it cannot apply, so pruning them then is acceptable.
- **Verifier reason:** keyRows (declarative-filters.ts:101-106) builds rows only from translation_keys, and the key editor's onChange (panel-declarative.ts:771-774) replaces draft.task_template.task_names with applyKeyRows(rows).task_names (declarative-filters.ts:113-127). The backend accepts task_names with no cross-check against translation_keys (declarative_companions.py:197-226) and applies them by key to every match (task_name_for, :229-244); services.yaml:1557-1559 and docs/INTEGRATING.md:727-729 document task_names as a plain key->name table. So with an empty translation_keys list, names that do apply are hidden, and the first key-editor interaction deletes them. Worse than the finder states: clicking a key that already has a stored name adds a row with name '' (toggleKeyRow, :150-153), so even the name for the key now in the list is lost. Severity lowered: the panel and the presets never produce this shape (panel writes names only for rows; preset task_names keys equal translation_keys), so it needs a spec made through the service, websocket or import, and then a key edit. The draft keeps task_names intact when the key editor is not touched (onChange fires only on input/click in renderKeyEditor).
- **Repro:** node tmp-v-F06/repro45.mjs (verbatim logic of keyRows/applyKeyRows/toggleKeyRow) -> F06-4 rows shown: []; F06-4 after one key click: {"translation_keys":["main_brush_time_left"],"task_names":{}}

### F06-5 — Key list toggle compares an untrimmed key, so a typed key with a space shows as picked but clicking it adds a duplicate row

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/declarative-filters.ts:151`
- **Defect:** drawOptions marks an option picked by the trimmed row key, but toggleKeyRow matches the raw row key, so the option cannot remove the row it shows as picked.
- **Failure scenario:** Admin pastes 'filter_life ' (trailing space) into a key row. The key list shows filter_life with a check mark (aria-pressed=true).
