Clicking it to remove adds a second row {key:'filter_life'}; the draft is unchanged (applyKeyRows keeps the first), and a second click removes only the new row. The admin cannot unpick the key from the list.
- **Suggested fix:** Compare `r.key.trim() === key` in toggleKeyRow (both in some and filter).
- **Verifier fix note:** Suggested fix is right: compare `r.key.trim() === key` in both the some() and the filter().
- **Verifier reason:** The key input stores the raw value (panel-declarative.ts:1020-1023, no trim). drawOptions marks picked by trimmed key (:1080), and the click handler calls toggleKeyRow(rows, opt.key) (:1098), which compares the untrimmed key (declarative-filters.ts:150-153). A key typed with a space therefore shows as picked, and a click adds a duplicate row instead of removing it. The draft stays correct because applyKeyRows trims and de-duplicates, and the row's own remove button still works, so this is cosmetic. It cannot survive a reopen, because the stored keys are trimmed.
- **Repro:** node tmp-v-F06/repro45.mjs -> F06-5 picked shows: true; after click: [{"key":"filter_life ","name":""},{"key":"filter_life","name":""}] -> {"translation_keys":["filter_life"],"task_names":{}}; after 2nd click: [{"key":"filter_life ","name":""}]

### F07-1 — Appliance History tab renders skip rows whose edit/move/delete buttons are never wired (no icon, no handler)

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-detail.ts:1145`
- **Defect:** wireSkipHistoryRows is called only in the task branch of wireDetailActions, but the appliance History tab also renders skip rows for every related task.
- **Failure scenario:** Skip (enabled by default) a task that is attached to an appliance's device. Open the appliance > History. The skip row shows three blank ha-icon-buttons (no path set). Tapping delete/edit/move does nothing, so the skip cannot be undone or re-dated from the appliance page. The same row on the task page works.
- **Suggested fix:** Call wireSkipHistoryRows(p, root) from wireDetail for both kinds (next to wireHistory), not from the task-only branch.
- **Verifier fix note:** Suggested fix is right: call wireSkipHistoryRows(p, root) next to wireHistory in wireDetail (both kinds) and drop the task-branch call so it is not bound twice.
- **Verifier reason:** wireSkipHistoryRows is called only in the task branch of wireDetailActions (panel-detail.ts:1145); the only other definition/use is panel-defer.ts:148. wireDetail calls wireHistory for both kinds (panel-detail.ts:1041), but wireHistory (panel-history.ts:337-369) sets icons and handlers only on .hk-hist-del/.hk-hist-edit/.hk-hist-move. The asset groups carry skips (panel-history.ts:62-67), historyGroup renders .hk-hist-skip-edit/-move/-del for live-task groups (panel-history.ts:222-233, editTask = taskId when not archived). So on the appliance History tab each skip row has three iconless, handler-less ha-icon-buttons. The only e2e (skip-snooze.spec.ts:172-178) drives the task page. Re-rated low: no data effect, and the same actions work on the task page.
- **Repro:** grep -rn 'hk-hist-skip' src/*.ts -> only panel-defer.ts:149/157/167 wire them; only caller panel-detail.ts:1145 inside `if (d.kind === 'task')`

### F07-10 — Tree view search leaves matching sub-appliances hidden under a collapsed parent

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-lists.ts:196`
- **Defect:** renderGroups forces sections open while a query runs, but tree nodes still honour _treeCollapsed, so a match inside a collapsed parent that also matches stays display:none.
- **Failure scenario:** Collapse 'HVAC' in tree view, search 'hvac'. Parent and child 'HVAC filter' both match; the child is nested under the collapsed node and is not visible.
- **Suggested fix:** Use `const isOpen = !!p._query || !p._treeCollapsed.has(entry.item.id)`.
- **Verifier reason:** assetsList filters by query before buildAssetTree (panel-lists.ts:165-169, 183-185), and _applyQuery re-renders the list through assetsList (panel.ts:438). The tree node open state ignores the query (panel-lists.ts:196), and CSS hides children of a non-open group (panel-styles.ts:1145). When a collapsed parent and its child both match, the child stays hidden; a child whose parent does not match becomes a root and shows. The grouped sections force open during search (panel-controls.ts:450). The parent row stays visible with its chevron, so the user can expand it.

### F07-11 — Task 'Linked consumable' row renders an empty appliance link for a blank-named appliance

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-task-form.ts:233`
- **Defect:** consumableLinkLabel (rendered by taskDetail at panel-detail.ts:450) uses asset.name with no device-name fallback, so the anchor that opens the part has no text.
- **Failure scenario:** Existing-device appliance saved without a name holds consumable 'Rinse aid'; a task linked to it shows ' · Rinse aid · In stock: 3' with an invisible, untappable appliance link.
- **Suggested fix:** Use the title chain asset.name || deviceName(p._hass?.devices, asset.device_id) || t('appliance.fallbackName').
- **Verifier reason:** consumableLinkLabel renders escapeHTML(asset.name) as the anchor text with no device-name fallback (panel-task-form.ts:232-236), shown on the task page (panel-detail.ts:450). A blank-named existing-device appliance (assets.py:1398) gives an empty link. Same blank-name root cause as F07-6 on a different surface, so not a duplicate.

### F07-2 — Auto-buy reminder's detail page offers Delete that the backend always refuses, after navigating away

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-detail.ts:364`
- **Defect:** sourceOwnedTask does not cover source.buy tasks and they have no managed_by, so the page renders a live Delete, but store.delete_task raises for every buy task.
- **Failure scenario:** A part goes low, the reconciler mints 'Buy X' (source.buy, no managed_by). Open its page, press Delete, confirm 'cannot be undone'. The panel first replaces the route with the task list (panel-detail.ts:1171), then _delete fails with 'This is an auto-created buy reminder...' toast. The task still exists; the user was thrown off its page. The panel already knows this case (_canDuplicate blocks isBuyTask) but the Delete path does not.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat isBuyTask(task) like a source-owned task for Delete (show an explaining caption, e.g. restock or turn off auto-buy), or use openBlockedDialog.
- **Verifier fix note:** Gate only Delete on isBuyTask (caption or greyed button with the restock / auto-buy reason). Do not add isBuyTask to sourceOwnedTask: that also withholds Edit, and the backend allows editing a buy task (store.py:544-553 blocks only a consumable link).
- **Verifier reason:** A buy task has source.buy and no managed_by (reconcile.py:886-900). sourceOwnedTask checks only source.part and source.problem_sensor (panel-chips.ts:68-79), so taskDetail takes the !sourceOwned branch and, with mb undefined, renders a live .d-del (panel-detail.ts:364-371). Its handler confirms, navigates to the list, then _delete (panel-detail.ts:1167-1173, panel.ts:1260-1269). store.delete_task always raises for _buy_source (store.py:837-843). Result: the user is moved off the page and gets an error toast; the task stays. _canDuplicate already special-cases isBuyTask (panel.ts:1198). Re-rated low: no data loss and the toast explains the fix (restock or turn off auto-buy).

### F07-7 — Greyed Done (role=button, tabindex=0) has no keyboard activation on list rows or the detail page

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** accessibility
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-lists.ts:496`  **Also reported as:** X11-2
- **Defect:** The blocked Done wrapper is focusable with role=button but only a click listener is attached, so Enter/Space does nothing.
- **Failure scenario:** Keyboard user tabs to the greyed Done of a scan-locked task and presses Enter: no toast explaining 'scan the tag'. The greyed Duplicate beside it does respond, because its keydown is wired by hand.
- **Suggested fix:** Add the same Enter/Space keydown handler to .done-blocked-wrap and .d-done-blocked-wrap.
- **Verifier reason:** _blockedButton renders span role=button tabindex=0 (panel.ts:1162); the Done variant (_blockedDone, panel.ts:1172-1175) is wired by click only on list rows (panel-lists.ts:496-501) and the detail page (panel-detail.ts:1141-1143). A span with role=button gets no synthetic click on Enter/Space, so a keyboard user gets no explanation. The greyed Duplicate beside it has a keydown handler (panel-detail.ts:1151-1165). The title attribute still carries the reason on hover.

### F07-8 — Appliance History badge counts related tasks, not history entries

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-detail.ts:625`
- **Defect:** history count = completionGroupsFor(...).length, one group per related task even with no entries, while historyBody hides empty groups.
- **Failure scenario:** Appliance with 3 related tasks, none completed: badge 'History 3', body 'No completions recorded yet.' An appliance with 1 task and 40 completions shows 'History 1'. The task page badge counts entries.
- **Suggested fix:** Sum completions (+ skips) across the groups, as taskSubtabs does.
- **Verifier reason:** history badge = completionGroupsFor(p, 'asset', id).length (panel-detail.ts:625), one group per related task plus one per archived entry (panel-history.ts:62-72), while historyBody drops groups with no completions and no skips (panel-history.ts:165-170). So 3 tasks with no history read 'History 3' over an empty-state body, and one task with 40 entries reads 1. The task page badge counts entries.

### F07-9 — Usage meter note reads a negative '-N to go' once the target is passed

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-detail.ts:237`
- **Defect:** remaining = round1(target - consumed) is not clamped, while the bar is clamped to 100%.
- **Failure scenario:** Usage task target 300 h, baseline 1000, reading 1350 (due, not yet done): the Schedule tab shows a full bar with '-50 h to go' and the same text as the progressbar aria-label.
- **Suggested fix:** Clamp at 0, or switch to an 'over by N' string when consumed > target.
- **Verifier reason:** sensorProgressBar clamps pct to 0..100 but prints round1(target - consumed) unclamped (panel-detail.ts:234-239) into '{remaining} to go' (en.json:537), for both the note and the progressbar aria-label. A usage task stays due until completed while the meter keeps rising, so consumed > target is the normal due state.
- **Repro:** node r.js: F07-9 remaining= -50 pct= 100

### F08-4 — Part-file upload and removal results are applied by the row index from render time, not by part id

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-asset-editors.ts:919`
- **Defect:** uploadPartFile and removePartFile write the file_* fields into list[i], where i was captured at render time. The api.ts contract says the caller applies them by id, and the part Remove button stays enabled during an upload.
- **Failure scenario:** The parts are [Filter, Anode]. The user attaches a file to Anode (i=1), and during the upload removes Filter, which leaves [Anode]. When the upload ends, list[1] = {...undefined, file_name, ...} adds a new part row with no name, and Anode shows 'Attach file' again. A second upload then replaces the stored file. If the user removes the uploading part itself, the next part shows the file card, and its Open cannot sign.
- **Suggested fix:** At completion, find the row with `list.findIndex((x) => x.id === partId)` and do nothing if it is gone.
- **Verifier reason:** uploadPartFile writes list[i] with the render-time index (panel-asset-editors.ts:918-925), against the api.ts:772-775 contract ('grafts ... by id'). The part Remove (:813-838) stays enabled during an upload, and Filter's row can be opened while Anode's upload runs. The result is a nameless ghost row that carries the file, and Anode shows 'Attach file' again. No stored data is corrupted: Save drops nameless parts (panel.ts:1333) and the backend _merge_parts always restores file_* from storage (assets.py:785). The effect is a confusing draft for the rest of the session, plus a possible needless re-upload. removePartFile has the same index pattern, but its window is one WS round trip. Rare (a part must be removed during an upload), so low.
- **Repro:** node tmp-v-F08/idx.mjs (mirrors :918-925 after removing parts[0]) -> [{"id":"p2","name":"Anode"},{"file_name":"anode.pdf","file_content_type":"application/pdf","file_size":10}]

### F08-5 — Clearing a link's URL in Edit saves a link with no URL, which then disappears from the appliance page and the card

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-asset-editors.ts:258`
- **Defect:** The add path refuses an empty URL, but the inline edit sends url '' and the backend accepts it. isDisplayableDocument then hides the document on every read surface.
- **Failure scenario:** The user edits a link, clears the URL (for example to paste a new one), and presses Save. update_document stores url ''. The document is no longer shown on the appliance page or the dashboard card, and it stays in the editor as a row with no Open action.
- **Suggested fix:** Apply the same non-empty and isHttpUrl check to the edit Save, and make the backend reject an empty URL on a link.
- **Verifier fix note:** The backend check for an empty link URL goes in _normalize_document_entry for kind 'link'. That covers add, update, create-seeding and import together.
- **Verifier reason:** The inline edit Save sends {name, url: ''} with no check (panel-asset-editors.ts:257-259). Only the add path refuses an empty URL (:426). ws update_asset_document takes changes as a plain dict (websocket_api.py:976-980). assets.update_document merges the url (assets.py:392-394), and _normalize_http_url returns '' for an empty value (:188-189), so a link with url '' is stored. isDisplayableDocument then hides it on the dashboard card (card.ts:869) and in the task-form document list (panel-task-form.ts:186). Correction to the finding: the appliance detail page still lists it, as inert plain text (panel-detail.ts:671-677). In the editor it stays as a card with no Open (:190). The user must clear the URL and press Save, so low.

### F09-3 — Card live-refresh relies on last_updated bumping on every coordinator refresh, which HA does not do; edits from other surfaces do not reach an open card

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:508`
- **Defect:** _stateSignal fingerprints the count and newest last_updated of entities whose id contains 'home_keeper', on the stated premise that todo/calendar re-write their state and bump last_updated on every coordinator refresh; HA only changes last_updated when state or attributes change, so most task edits leave the fingerprint unchanged.
- **Failure scenario:** The card is open on a tablet. From the panel, an admin renames a task, edits its notes, snoozes a task due in 10 days by two weeks, or completes a floating task early that is not the next one up. todo.home_keeper_tasks state is the count of needs_action items (unchanged: the task stays on the list), the calendar only exposes the current/next event (unchanged), and the all-tasks count sensor's counts and next-up pointer are unchanged. last_updated stays the same, the fingerprint matches, and the card keeps showing the old name/due date until the page is reloaded. Only completions arrive through the event subscription, and for non-admin users not even those (F09-1).
- **Suggested fix:** Drive refresh from a Home Keeper-owned signal: a websocket subscription for task_created/updated/deleted/snoozed/skipped/completed (the same command F09-1 needs), or expose a monotonically changing attribute (e.g. a store revision counter) on todo.home_keeper_tasks and fingerprint that.
- **Verifier fix note:** Prefer the subscription command from F09-1. If a revision attribute on todo.home_keeper_tasks is used instead, add it to _unrecorded_attributes so each mutation does not write a recorder row with a meaningless attribute.
- **Verifier reason:** The premise in the card.ts:500-507 comment is false. HA core StateMachine.async_set_internal (SCRATCH/tmp-v-B02/src/homeassistant/core.py:2455-2492): when state and attributes are both unchanged it only updates last_reported and fires EVENT_STATE_REPORTED, then returns; last_updated is untouched and no state_changed reaches the frontend's hass.states. card.ts:508-519 fingerprints only count + newest last_updated of entity ids containing 'home_keeper': todo.home_keeper_tasks (state = NEEDS_ACTION item count, todo.py:61-103, no attributes), calendar.home_keeper_upcoming_tasks (only the single next event, calendar.py:63), and the count sensors (sensor.py:327-332; task_counts.py:106-119 attributes are counts plus next_due/next_task_name/next_task_id). Per-task entity ids come from the device name plus a task label (task_entities.py:1-11, 53-68), so they do not contain 'home_keeper'. No timer refresh exists in card.ts (only the setTimeout at 481 and 1089, neither a refresh). So a rename, a notes edit, or a snooze/early completion of a task that is not next-up and does not move a count leaves the open card stale. Downgraded to low: the staleness ends at the next count or next-up change (at the latest when a task falls due) or a page reload, and completions reach admin viewers through the event subscription.

### F10-2 — Snooze can move the due date earlier: presets count from now, custom accepts a past date

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/defer.ts:180`
- **Defect:** Snooze is offered on every non-dormant task and resolves its presets from the current time, not from next_due, so on a task that is not yet due the default preset pulls the due date forward, the opposite of what the guide says Snooze does.
- **Failure scenario:** A task is due in 30 days. The user opens Done > Snooze (default preset '1w') and presses Snooze. snoozeTarget returns now+7d, the panel sends until=now+7d, and store.snooze_task sets next_due to it: the task is now due 23 days earlier. The same happens with '1h'/'1d' on any future task, and with a custom date in the past (the websocket and store accept it, so the task becomes overdue immediately). docs/guide/tasks/snooze-and-skip.md:4-5 states 'Snooze moves the due date later'. deferVerbs already hides Due today on overdue tasks for the mirror-image reason, but has no equivalent guard for Snooze.
- **Suggested fix:** Resolve presets from max(now, next_due) (or hide/disable presets whose target is not later than next_due), and reject or warn on a custom date earlier than the current due date (and earlier than now) in the dialog and in snooze_task.
- **Verifier fix note:** The same from-now base is used by the service (__init__.py:1361 `dt_util.now() + timedelta(hours=...)`) and the notification action (notifier.py:503), so a fix only in defer.ts leaves the service contradicting its own description. Use one base, max(now, next_due), in all three places (for a due or overdue task, which is the notification case and #248's problem-sensor case, this is still now), and reject or warn on a custom `until` earlier than the current next_due. Show the current due date next to the preview line in the dialog.
- **Verifier reason:** No guard anywhere: defer.ts:64 offers Snooze on every non-dormant task; defer.ts:179-180 resolves presets from `now`; utils.ts resolveSnoozePreset adds the offset to `from`; websocket_api.py:519-530 parses `until` with no lower bound; store.py:657-666 checks only dormancy, then writes next_due = until. So a task due in 30 days with the default '1w' preset becomes due 23 days earlier, and '1h'/'1d' on a due-soon task (due tomorrow) moves it earlier too. A custom past date is accepted and makes the task overdue at once. This contradicts docs/guide/tasks/snooze-and-skip.md:4-5 ('Snooze moves the due date later'), the menu hint defer.snoozeHint 'Move the due date later', settings.skipsnooze_help, and services.yaml snooze_task ('Defer a task's due date by a number of hours'). Rated low: the dialog previews the resulting date (defer.snoozeResolves 'Due date moves to {date}.'), nothing is lost, the recurrence is untouched and a second snooze reverses it; the harm is an early reminder.

### F10-3 — Async dialog submit closes whatever dialog is open when the request returns, and loses its error

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/defer-dialogs.ts:235`
- **Defect:** submitSnooze/submitSkip/submitCompletion/submitMoveCompletion capture the state object before the await, then call the host-level close (which resets the current panel state) or write the error into the captured, possibly stale, object.
- **Failure scenario:** On a slow connection the user presses Snooze for task A, then presses Escape and opens Snooze (or Skip) for task B. When A's request resolves, close() runs closeSnooze(p), which replaces p._snooze with an empty state, and B's dialog vanishes mid-edit. If A's request fails instead, the message is written to A's discarded state object and never shown, so the user gets no sign that A was not snoozed.
- **Suggested fix:** After the await, only close if the host's current state is still the captured object (e.g. `if (p._snooze === s) close()`), and surface a toast when the error lands on a state that is no longer shown.
- **Verifier fix note:** Guard with identity, e.g. `if (p._snooze === s) close()` (pass a predicate or the state getter through DeferDialogHost), and the same for skip/completion/move. A lost error on a dismissed dialog is better surfaced as a toast than dropped; alternatively disable the primary button while the request is pending, which also prevents a double submit.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced: defer-dialogs.ts:214 wires the Snooze button to submitSnooze(host, s, close) with close = () => closeSnooze(p) (panel-defer.ts:116); submitSnooze awaits api.snoozeTask then calls close() (defer-dialogs.ts:233-236), and closeSnooze (panel-defer.ts:51-54) replaces p._snooze unconditionally. Same for submitSkip (defer-dialogs.ts:319-323 -> closeSkip) and submitCompletion (panel-dialogs.ts:117-123 -> closeCompletionDialog). No busy flag or disabled button during the await (no busy/pending/disabled in defer-dialogs.ts), so Escape and a new open of the same dialog kind are possible while the request is in flight. On failure after dismissal, the error goes to the discarded state object. Needs user actions inside one websocket round trip (caret, menu, dialog), so it matters only on a slow remote link; low.

### F10-4 — Skip and Snooze dialogs are not counted as editing, so a background re-render steals focus mid-typing

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:1559`
- **Defect:** _editingOpen() lists the task/asset forms, the note editor, and the completion and move dialogs, but not _snooze.open or _skip.open, so the deferred Markdown-upgrade render rebuilds the skip dialog while the user types a note.
- **Failure scenario:** The user opens Skip right after the panel loads and starts typing a note. The lazy Markdown chunk resolves, _editingOpen() is false, and _render() rebuilds the dialog host. The text survives in s.data, but focus drops to <body>, and Home Assistant's one-letter shortcuts (e, c, a, m, d) then act on the rest of the typed word, which is the exact failure the guard exists to prevent.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `this._snooze.open || this._skip.open` (and _declDialog.open if it has text fields) to _editingOpen().
- **Verifier fix note:** Add `this._snooze.open || this._skip.open || this._declDialog.open` to _editingOpen(); the completion and move dialogs are already there, so this matches the existing intent.
- **Verifier reason:** panel.ts:1559-1566 _editingOpen() omits _snooze.open and _skip.open (and _declDialog.open), and its only caller is the markdown upgrade at panel.ts:1549. _render() calls _ensureMarkdown() on every render (panel.ts:1600), ensureMarkdown waits up to 4 s (markdown.ts:54-69), and the resulting _render() rebuilds shadowRoot.innerHTML (panel.ts:1697) and re-creates the skip dialog (panel.ts:1981). _focusKey (panel.ts:1740-1753) restores focus only for nav segments or elements with an id, so a focused ha-form field in the dialog is not restored and focus falls to body. The text survives in s.data (defer-dialogs.ts:285-296). The window is narrow: once per page session (markdownReady short-circuits afterwards), within about 4 s of the first render, with the skip dialog open and focused. Snooze has no free-text field, so the typed-shortcut harm applies mainly to the skip note.

### X01-4 — Spares number entity lets any user adjust part stock, bypassing the admin gate on adjust_part_stock

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/number.py:154`
- **Defect:** The per-part stock number entity writes through store.adjust_part_stock, the operation SECURITY.md lists as admin-only and that both the websocket command and service gate, but HA entity services carry no admin check.
- **Failure scenario:** A non-admin calls number.set_value on number.<appliance>_<part>_stock with value 0 (or 9999). async_set_native_value computes the delta and calls store.adjust_part_stock, firing low/out/restocked events and creating or removing Buy tasks; any automation keyed on those events (reorder, notify) runs. The same user gets 401 from home_keeper.adjust_part_stock, so the documented gate is decorative.
- **Suggested fix:** Either check the calling user in async_set_native_value (self._context / hass.auth user from the service context; raise Unauthorized for a non-admin) or document the entity as the deliberate open path and drop the service/websocket gate; add an integration test either way.
- **Verifier fix note:** Decide the policy first. The maintainers' own framing (stock is not administration) favors documenting the entity as the open path and dropping 'Spare-part stock adjustments' from the admin table (or un-gating adjust_part_stock). If the gate stays, check self._context.user_id in async_set_native_value (HA sets the call context on the entity before the method runs) and raise Unauthorized; note this also disables the dashboard number control for non-admins.
- **Verifier reason:** number.py:140-156 async_set_native_value calls store.adjust_part_stock with no user check (no context/user_id use in number.py). HA core helpers/service.py:690-697 only checks entity permissions for a non-admin (admin_only is not set for number.set_value), and auth/permissions/system_policies.py USER_POLICY = {entities: True}, so any non-admin passes. The same user gets Unauthorized from the service (__init__.py:1526) and websocket (websocket_api.py:876), and SECURITY.md:37 lists adjust_part_stock as admin-only, so the documented gate has a third ungated door. Low: SECURITY.md:66-67 and tests (test_assets.py:1628, test_admin_gates.py:588) say stock is not private or administration, INTEGRATING.md:887-891 documents that setting the number moves the count, and any user can already draw stock down by completing a consumable-linked task.
- **Repro:** curl HA core dev: helpers/service.py L694-697 `if not user.is_admin: if admin_only: raise Unauthorized ... entity_perms = user.permissions.check_entity`; system_policies.py `USER_POLICY = {CAT_ENTITIES: True}`

### X02-1 — Upload that spans an entry reload writes its metadata into the replaced store, so the document silently disappears

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/manuals.py:552`
- **Defect:** Both upload views take the coordinator before they stream the body (up to 100 MB) and use that object afterwards. If the entry reloads during the stream, the metadata goes into the old HomeKeeperStore, which the new store never sees, and the old store's _save() writes its whole stale snapshot over the file.
- **Failure scenario:** An admin uploads a 40 MB manual over remote access (slow uplink, 30-90 s). During the stream the entry reloads. Reloads are routine: a buy task changes, a problem sensor appears, a declarative reconcile runs, options are saved in another tab, or a one-off is purged. The new setup builds a new HomeKeeperStore and loads the file. When the stream ends, post() calls the captured coord.store.add_asset_document, which adds the document to the OLD store's memory and saves that old snapshot. The response returns the asset with the document, so the panel shows success. The live coordinator (new store) has no such document, so a panel refresh does not show it. The next save by the new store rewrites the file without it, and the blob under home_keeper/documents/ is orphaned. The old snapshot also overwrites on disk whatever the new store saved since it loaded. The next reload reads from disk, so those writes are lost. HomeKeeperPartFileView.post has the same flaw through coord.store.set_part_file.
- **Suggested fix:** Resolve the coordinator again after _parse_upload returns (find_coordinator), and fail with integration_not_loaded when it is gone. Better: give HomeKeeperStore a closed flag that async_unload_entry sets, and make _save() raise when the flag is set, so no replaced store can write the shared file.
- **Verifier fix note:** A second find_coordinator after _parse_upload makes the window smaller but does not close it: a reload can still start during the executor move or the save. The real guard is a closed flag on HomeKeeperStore, set in async_unload_entry, that makes _save refuse. The same flag also covers X02-2 and X02-4.
- **Verifier reason:** _begin_upload captures the coordinator (manuals.py:552, 560). post() then streams the body (manuals.py:595) and after that writes through the captured object (manuals.py:628 add_asset_document; the part-file view does the same with set_part_file). Each setup builds a new HomeKeeperStore on the same storage key (__init__.py:810-811). store.py has no closed or unloaded flag, and _save writes the whole in-memory snapshot (store.py:316-326). Unload does not stop the old store. If a reload runs during the stream, the document goes into the old store only, and the new store drops it at its next save. Reloads are routine (options.py:339, problem_sync.py:211, declarative_companion_sync.py:452, coordinator.py:188/306, websocket_api.py:286/315/340). The full path is traced. It is rare, because a reload must start during one upload.

### X02-2 — A replaced HomeKeeperStore keeps writing after a reload: fire-and-forget passes are not tracked by the entry

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:316`
- **Defect:** Store-writing work starts with hass.async_create_task, not entry.async_create_task. Examples are sensor evaluation, the problem-sensor reconcile, tag-scan completion, notification-tap handling, to-do and shopping sync passes, and the declarative reconcile. HA's unload therefore neither waits for this work nor cancels it. A pass in flight during a reload finishes against the old coordinator, and its whole-document _save() lands after the new store has loaded.
- **Failure scenario:** A threshold task watches a salt-level sensor. The sensor crosses its limit while a reload runs, for example from an options save or a buy-task settle. _handle_state_change has already started async_evaluate on the old watcher. The evaluation awaits a first write (a declarative notes refresh, or a baseline or arm of an earlier task) and then calls old_store.trigger_task, whose save reaches the executor after the new setup has run store.load(). The new store never has the arm. SensorTaskWatcher.async_baseline in the new setup then reads the live value, which is already past the threshold, and records condition_met=True with crossed_at=None. The task never arms, and the next save by the new store erases the old arm from disk. The same flow applies to a to-do sync pass inside _complete_tasks / async_set_todo_list_items (there is no _stopped check after _apply). It also applies to async_settle_buy_tasks: its settle_use_tasks and reconcile_buy_tasks run on the captured store after a slow shopping-list read, even when the sync pass itself bailed on _stopped. Each such write also overwrites on disk whatever the new store saved since it loaded.
- **Suggested fix:** Start these passes with self._entry.async_create_task(hass, ...) so that unload waits (up to 10 s) before the new store loads. Also close the old store on unload (a flag checked in _save) so that a late writer fails loudly instead of overwriting the file.
- **Verifier fix note:** Do not use entry.async_create_task for a task that itself awaits async_reload of the same entry (coordinator._async_reload_for_buy_tasks, problem_sync._async_reload, declarative _async_reload). Unload would wait up to 10 s on its own caller and log 'did not complete in time'. Track only the store-writing passes, and add the store closed flag from X02-1 as the main guard.
- **Verifier reason:** HA 2026.9 _async_process_on_unload waits only for entry._tasks and entry._background_tasks (fetched config_entries, ce-2026.9.0.py:1249-1263). The passes start with hass.async_create_task and are not tracked (sensor_watcher.py:478/497, problem_sync.py:182/196, tag_listener.py:67, notifier.py:554, todo_sync_driver.py:137, todo_list_sync.py:124/152, shopping_sync.py:109, declarative_companion_sync.py:433/438). The sensor watcher and the store have no stop check. todo_list_sync.py:199-206 writes async_set_todo_list_items after its last _stopped check, and async_settle_buy_tasks calls settle_use_tasks and reconcile_buy_tasks on the captured store (coordinator.py:180-181). A pass that spans unload and the new store.load() therefore writes the old snapshot over the file. The mechanism is traced. The lost-arm result needs a narrow race, so the severity is low.

### X02-4 — An evaluation in flight during unload re-creates hold timers (and can re-subscribe a state listener) on the replaced SensorTaskWatcher

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_watcher.py:465`
- **Defect:** _cancel_hold_timers runs once, at unload. An async_evaluate still running after that point goes on to call _schedule_hold for later tasks and creates new async_track_point_in_time timers that nothing cancels. When such a timer fires, it runs a full evaluation of the dead watcher against the stale store.
- **Failure scenario:** Declarative-companion tasks with for_seconds holds are pending (a hold timer is set again on every evaluation), and a state change starts an evaluation. The evaluation awaits a store write for an earlier task. During that await the entry unloads (reload), and _unsubscribe_state and _cancel_hold_timers run. The evaluation then resumes, and _apply_edge -> _schedule_hold creates timers on the old watcher. When a timer fires, _handle_hold_due starts async_evaluate on the old watcher, which arms tasks in the old store and saves the old snapshot over the live file (see X02-2). That evaluation first calls _resubscribe_state(). _unsubscribe_state never reset _tracked, and a declarative reconcile creates tasks just before its reload, so the old store's bound-entity set can differ from _tracked. In that case a new async_track_state_change_event listener attaches to the dead watcher. That listener is never removed, because on_unload already ran, and it keeps writing stale snapshots on each state change until HA restarts.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a _stopped flag that the unload callbacks set. Make _schedule_hold, _resubscribe_state and async_evaluate return early when it is set, and reset _tracked in _unsubscribe_state.
- **Verifier fix note:** Add a _stopped flag in an unload callback. Check it in _schedule_hold, _resubscribe_state and at the start of async_evaluate, and reset _tracked in _unsubscribe_state. The store closed flag from X02-1 also stops the stale write.
- **Verifier reason:** async_start_listeners registers _unsubscribe_state and _cancel_hold_timers once with entry.async_on_unload (sensor_watcher.py:424-425). _schedule_hold has no liveness check (sensor_watcher.py:454-467). It is called from _apply_edge (sensor_watcher.py:763), after awaits on store writes in the same pass (:595/:598/:742). An evaluation that resumes after unload therefore creates timers that nothing cancels. When a timer fires, _handle_hold_due starts async_evaluate on the dead watcher (:469-478), which writes to the old store. _unsubscribe_state keeps _tracked (:428-432), so _resubscribe_state (:480-491) attaches a listener that never goes away only when the old store's bound-entity set changed after the in-flight pass subscribed. That part is narrower than the timer part. The timing is rare.

### X02-5 — Notification taps and tag scans that arrive during a reload are silently dropped

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/__init__.py:936`
- **Defect:** The mobile_app_notification_action and tag_scanned listeners stop at unload and start again only at the very end of setup, after store load, the reconciles, platform forwarding, device pruning and the sensor baseline. An event in that window reaches no handler, and nothing replays it.
- **Failure scenario:** A walk notification shows an auto-buy reminder, and the user taps Mark done. _handle completes it, and async_settle_buy_tasks starts an entry reload (eager). The walk then immediately sends the next card, while the reload is still running. If the user taps Mark done on that card before setup reaches line 936, the event has no listener. The phone dismisses the card, the user believes the task is done, and Home Keeper records nothing. A tag scan during any reload is lost the same way, including for require_tag_scan tasks, which accept no other completion.
- **Suggested fix:** Register the two bus listeners once per HA run (on hass.data) and resolve the live coordinator per event, queueing the event or retrying briefly while find_coordinator is None. Or at least register them before the slow setup steps.
- **Verifier fix note:** If the registration moves earlier in setup, the window only gets smaller, because unload still removes the listener. The full fix is one listener for each HA run that finds the live coordinator for each event and retries for a short time while find_coordinator returns None.
- **Verifier reason:** The notification-action and tag listeners are removed at unload and added again only at the end of setup (__init__.py:936, 940-942). That is after store.load (:811), platform forwarding, device pruning and the sensor baseline. notifier.py:526 can start a reload (coordinator.py:188) and then sends the next walk card on the old coordinator (notifier.py:535). A tap or a tag scan in the setup window reaches no handler, and nothing replays it. The gap is real but short, a few seconds on a slow host, so the severity is low.

### X03-11 — settle_use_tasks arms tasks from a decision made before its awaits

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:1655`  **Also reported as:** B01-6, B09-7
- **Defect:** to_arm is computed once, then each trigger_task awaits a save; a task removed in between raises KeyError out of async_settle_buy_tasks, and the claimed dormancy check does not exist.
- **Failure scenario:** A use completion puts replacement task R in to_arm; during the trimmed save an update_asset removes the part and reconcile_part_tasks deletes R. trigger_task(R) raises KeyError; handle_complete_task (outside _store_errors) fails after the completion is already saved, and the buy-task reconcile and shopping sync after it are skipped. Two overlapping settles also arm R twice, firing two triggered events and moving next_due.
- **Suggested fix:** Re-check each id before arming (skip missing or already-armed tasks) instead of relying on trigger_task.
- **Verifier reason:** Same defect as B01-6 / B09-7. store.py:1646-1655 decides to_arm, awaits _save when trimmed, then awaits trigger_task per id; the comment claims a dormancy check, but trigger_task (store.py:613-627) has none and raises KeyError for a task removed in between. Needs a concurrent delete/re-arm inside the save window, so low.

### X03-6 — Import overwrites task changes made while appliances are provisioned

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer_runner.py:129`
- **Defect:** Task records are planned (merged from the stored task) before the appliance write and device provisioning awaits, then written wholesale, so any task mutation in that window is lost.
- **Failure scenario:** Import a document with several new appliances plus updates to existing tasks. While async_reconcile_assets awaits one save per provisioned device, a to-do sync or notification action completes task T (or the sensor watcher arms it). async_import_records then assigns the planned record for T, dropping the new completion and reverting next_due; if T was deleted meanwhile it is re-created (existing is None -> created event).
- **Suggested fix:** Re-plan (or re-merge update records against the live task) after provisioning, or write tasks before provisioning and patch only the placeholder device_id afterwards.
- **Verifier fix note:** Re-plan (or re-merge update records against the live task) right before the task write, which is synchronous up to its save; the placeholder device_id resolution can stay where it is.
- **Verifier reason:** Traced: transfer_runner.py:62-74 plans update records from a copy of the stored task (transfer.py:1510 merge_update copies stored[matched]); then line 81 awaits async_import_records (store._save -> HA Store.async_save, which awaits an executor write and yields) and line 89 awaits async_reconcile_assets (devices.py:504 set_asset_device_id -> _save per newly provisioned appliance; store.py:1353-1355); then line 129 writes the planned tasks wholesale (store.py:1011-1013 `self._tasks[task_id] = record`). No lock in store/coordinator/runner (grep). A completion, snooze, or sensor arm landing in that window is overwritten, and a task deleted in the window is re-created. Low: needs an admin import that carries appliances plus a concurrent task mutation during a few save round-trips.

### X03-9 — Export drops a non-reserved source that import accepts, so glue tasks duplicate after restore

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/transfer.py:73`
- **Defect:** is_portable_task exports a task whose source is an integration's own namespace, but EXCLUDED_TASK_KEYS strips source on export, contrary to its stated reason.
- **Failure scenario:** A glue (or automation) adds a task with source {pawsistant: {...}} and no managed_by. Export writes it without source; restore on a new install creates a plain task with the history; the glue cannot find its task by its own source copy and creates a second one. The documented reason ('a task carrying one is not exported') is false for these tasks.
- **Suggested fix:** Export the non-reserved namespaces of source (drop only reserved keys), or refuse such tasks as non-portable and correct the exclusion reason.
- **Verifier fix note:** Export the non-reserved namespaces (import already accepts them, test_transfer.py:899-905), then correct the EXCLUDED_TASK_KEYS reason; test_transfer_roundtrip.py should cover a task with a non-reserved source.
- **Verifier reason:** Reproduced: build_task with source {pawsistant:{pet:'7'}} and no managed_by is portable (transfer.py:305-308 rejects only reserved sources and managed_by), but build_document strips source (EXCLUDED_TASK_KEYS transfer.py:73), and the import plan carries source None. The stated reason 'a task carrying one is not exported' is false for these tasks. INTEGRATING.md:21, 110-126 tell integrations to find their tasks by their own source namespace, so a restore onto a new install loses the link and a source-matching glue adds a duplicate. Low: the task id survives the restore, and an integration that sets managed_by (recommended) is excluded consistently.
- **Repro:** python3 tmp-v-X03/repro_source.py -> 'is_portable_task: True' / 'source in export: False' / 'plan.ok: True imported source: None'

### X04-8 — Sensor hold time is measured in wall-clock time, so DST shortens or lengthens it

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor_tasks.py:561`
- **Defect:** crossed_at and now are both dt_util.now() values with the same ZoneInfo, and Python subtracts/adds same-tzinfo datetimes as naive wall-clock values.
- **Failure scenario:** State task with for_seconds 3600 crosses at 01:50 PST on 2027-03-14; at 03:05 PDT (15 real minutes later) the evaluation arms it (reproduced). On the autumn change a 600 s hold that crossed at 01:50 PDT is still pending 30 real minutes later, and hold_due_at books 02:00 PST (70 real minutes).
- **Suggested fix:** Do the hold arithmetic in UTC: `(dt_util.as_utc(now) - dt_util.as_utc(crossed_at))` and `as_utc(crossed_at) + timedelta(...)`.
- **Verifier reason:** crossed_at is kept in coordinator memory as the datetime from dt_util.now() (sensor_watcher.py:297, 511, 760). It is never round-tripped through ISO, so both operands share the same ZoneInfo object, and Python does same-tzinfo arithmetic on wall-clock values. sensor_tasks.py:561 (held) and sensor_tasks.py:466 (hold_due_at) are both affected. Low: it needs a hold that spans the DST change at night, twice a year.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-X04/t.py -> spring: now 2027-03-14T03:05:00-07:00 held = 4500.0 s (real 900); fall: held = -1800.0 s (real 1800); hold_due_at 2026-11-01T02:00:00-08:00 real secs after crossing: 4200.0

### X06-1 — Dependabot auto-merge waits on its own check run and never merges

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** ci
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml:33`
- **Defect:** `gh pr checks --watch` lists the running `dependabot` job as a pending check, so the watch loop never exits: the job times out at 20 minutes, and GitHub reports the timeout as cancelled, not failed.
- **Failure scenario:** Dependabot opens a PR. Every other check passes in about 5 minutes. The auto-merge job keeps polling because its own check stays `pending`. At 20 minutes the job is cancelled, so `gh pr merge` never runs. This happens on every Dependabot PR. The PRs stay open, and the maintainer merges them by hand in combined PRs (#328, #384), security bumps included. The run reads 'cancelled' (grey), so nothing looks red.
- **Suggested fix:** Leave the job's own check out of the wait. For example, poll `gh pr checks "$PR_URL" --json name,workflow,bucket` in a loop until every entry whose workflow is not 'Dependabot auto-merge' is done, and fail on any `fail` bucket. Or use `--required` with a complete required-check list. Also pass `--match-head-commit ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha }}` to `gh pr merge`, so that a later push cannot be merged without its checks.
- **Verifier fix note:** Excluding the job's own check is not enough on its own: the fix must also drop or skip the `notify / notify` check (notify-discord.yml fails on every Dependabot PR for lack of secrets), or the loop exits red at 45 s. `gh pr checks --json name,workflow,bucket` supports the filter the finder suggests. Keep --match-head-commit on the merge.
- **Verifier reason:** dependabot-auto-merge.yml:33 runs `gh pr checks --watch --fail-fast` from inside a job that is itself a check on the PR, and gh does not exclude the calling job. Live evidence (read-only API): job 96961624897 (Dependabot PR github-script 8->9) started 01:58:13, every poll lists `dependabot pending` (itself) while all other checks are pass/skipping, and it ends at 02:18:26 with 'The operation was canceled.' and conclusion 'cancelled' (the timeout-minutes: 20 at :29). So the merge at :34 can never run on an all-green PR. Today the job dies even earlier: runs 36395412356 and 36395403322 (jsdom, rollup bumps, 2026-09-28) exit 1 after ~45 s because `notify / notify` (notify-discord.yml, which needs secrets.DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL that a Dependabot run does not get) fails and --fail-fast stops. Dependabot PRs #386 and #387 are still open, which matches. Severity lowered: the job fails closed (nothing bad merges), so the cost is maintainer toil and delayed dependency and security bumps, not user impact.
- **Repro:** get_job_logs 96961624897: '02:18:17 dependabot\tpending\t0 .../job/96961624897' ... '02:18:26 ##[error]The operation was canceled.'; get_workflow_job: started_at 01:58:13, completed_at 02:18:28, conclusion cancelled. Run 36395412356 log: 'notify / notify\tfail\t3s' then '##[error]Process completed with exit code 1.'

### X06-2 — Notify dry run still tags, publishes and deploys a real release from the dispatched branch

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** ci
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:110`
- **Defect:** On workflow_dispatch, the `release` job runs in full. Its tag and release steps check only `github.event_name != 'pull_request'`, so a 'notify_dry_run' rehearsal on any ref whose manifest version has no tag creates a real release. When that version is stable, the run also deploys the docs.
- **Failure scenario:** The maintainer opens the 'Release 0.27.0' PR. Before the merge, the maintainer rehearses the notify step as RELEASE.md says (Run workflow, notify_dry_run checked, notify_version 0.27.0) and picks the PR branch. The release job finds no tag v0.27.0, tags the unmerged branch head, publishes a stable GitHub Release that HACS offers to every user, and deploy-docs publishes the site from that tag. The notify step posts nothing because it is a dry run. When the PR merges, the push run sees that v0.27.0 exists and sets release=false. notify-issues is skipped, so no issue that 0.27.0 fixes gets a comment or closes.
- **Suggested fix:** Put `github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'` on the tag, release and deploy-docs steps. Or skip the build and publish steps when `inputs.notify_dry_run` is true. At minimum, make a dispatch fail when it would create a tag.
- **Verifier fix note:** Gate the tag, release and deploy-docs steps on `github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`, or skip the whole build/publish path when inputs.notify_dry_run is true. A dispatch that is meant to finish a failed push-run release should be a separate, explicit input.
- **Verifier reason:** notify_dry_run gates only the notify-issues job (release.yml:158-160) and its posting (:268, :311). The release job has no `if`; on workflow_dispatch `github.event_name` is 'workflow_dispatch', so Create and push tag (:110), Create GitHub Release (:120) and deploy-docs (:137-140) all run whenever the dispatched ref's manifest version has no tag (:50-54). docs-deploy.yml has no branch guard of its own (:46 checks out inputs.ref). Harm needs an untagged manifest version on the dispatched ref: a non-main branch picked in the Run workflow dropdown, or main after a push run that failed or has not tagged yet. The documented rehearsal (RELEASE.md:118-121: on the default branch, notify_version set to a past release) is harmless, because every push run on main tags the manifest version, so check sets release=false. Real but needs an unusual operator choice, so low.

### X06-3 — A new top CHANGELOG section with no manifest.json bump passes every gate and never ships

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** ci
- **Location:** `ci/check-changelog-release-gap.py:77`
- **Defect:** The gap guard reads a changed CHANGELOG heading as a version bump without reading manifest.json. release.yml exits at the 'tag already exists' check before it compares the CHANGELOG or PANEL_VERSION.
- **Failure scenario:** The top section 0.28.0b2 is released. A feature PR renames it to '## [0.28.0b3]' (and maybe bumps const.py) but does not bump manifest.json. check-changelog-release-gap sees base_version 0.28.0b2 != head_version 0.28.0b3 and returns None. The PR run of release.yml reads manifest 0.28.0b2, finds tag v0.28.0b2 and exits 0, so it never checks the changelog section or the const.py mismatch. Every check is green. After the merge, the push run skips too, so the feature sits on main unreleased. That is the #236 failure mode that this guard was written to stop.
- **Suggested fix:** In check-changelog-release-gap.py, read manifest.json and const.py PANEL_VERSION at HEAD. Fail when the CHANGELOG top version differs from the manifest version and has no tag, and fail when manifest and PANEL_VERSION differ. Or, in release.yml, run the CHANGELOG and PANEL_VERSION consistency checks before the 'tag exists' early exit.
- **Verifier reason:** check-changelog-release-gap.py:75-78 returns None as soon as the top CHANGELOG heading differs between merge-base and HEAD, and main() never reads manifest.json or PANEL_VERSION. release.yml:50-54 exits with release=false at the 'tag already exists' check, before the CHANGELOG check (:56) and the PANEL_VERSION check (:61-65), on both the PR run and the push run. No other workflow or test compares manifest.json with the CHANGELOG top or with PANEL_VERSION (grep of tests/, ci/, .github/: only the PR template checkbox). So a PR that opens '## [0.28.0b3]' but leaves manifest.json at the released 0.28.0b2 is green and never ships. Lowered to low: it needs a human slip, and the code ships on the next manifest bump or stable; the lost part is the beta build and the beta issue notifications for that section.
- **Repro:** python3 tmp-v-X06/repro3.py (real check() with the real release-issues section()): base top '## [0.28.0b2]' (tagged), head adds '## [0.28.0b3]' above it -> check(...) returns None

### X06-7 — Mutation scope filter `x_<name>*` also scores every function whose name starts with the changed one

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** ci
- **Location:** `ci/mutation_scope.py:165`
- **Defect:** The trailing `*` comes directly after the function name, so the filter for `_parse` also matches `_parse_mmdd`. mutmut and mutation_report.py both apply it with fnmatch, so the PR is scored on untouched functions.
- **Failure scenario:** A PR edits one line inside `recurrence._parse` (line 342). The scope is `custom_components.home_keeper.recurrence.x__parse*`. That filter matches `x__parse_mmdd__mutmut_N`, so mutmut runs, and the report scores, every mutant of the untouched `_parse_mmdd` (line 81). Surviving mutants there can fail the 80% gate for debt that the PR did not create, which the diff scoping exists to prevent. The current surface has 10 such prefix pairs, for example assets `_normalize_part` -> `_normalize_part_action`, `_normalize_part_tag_id`, `_normalize_parts`; `normalize_profile` -> `normalize_profiles`; `validate_upload` -> `validate_upload_stream`.
- **Suggested fix:** Emit `{module}.x_{name}__mutmut_*` and `{module}.xǁ{cls}ǁ{method}__mutmut_*` (mutmut's mutant suffix). Add a unit test for the scope script with a prefix pair.
- **Verifier fix note:** `{module}.x_{name}__mutmut_*` is the pattern mutmut itself uses for a function (mutmut/__main__.py:1977 retest `...__mutmut_*`). One change in mutation_scope.py fixes both mutmut run and the report, since both read the same scope file.
- **Verifier reason:** mutation_scope.py:165 emits `{module}.x_{name}*` (and :172 for methods). mutmut 3.7.0, the version mutation.yml:59 pins, filters with fnmatch (mutmut/__main__.py:1245 `any(fnmatch.fnmatch(key, mutant_name) ...)`) and names mutants `x_<func>__mutmut_<n>` (:569-571). mutation_report.py:106 applies the same scope with fnmatch. So a change inside `_parse` also runs and scores every `_parse_mmdd` mutant. The effect is a stricter gate than intended (possible false red for older debt), never a bypass, so low. The real surface has exactly 10 prefix pairs, as claimed.
- **Repro:** python3 tmp-v-X06/repro7.py (imports the real ci/mutation_scope.py): python_filters({recurrence.py: [(342,342)]}) -> ['custom_components.home_keeper.recurrence.x__parse*']; fnmatch on 'recurrence.x__parse_mmdd__mutmut_1' -> True; 10 prefix pairs on the only_mutate surface (recurrence _parse/_parse_mmdd, assets x5, notifications x2, profiles, documents).

### X07-3 — To-do entity tests only complete items with an unchanged summary, so a rename or notes edit saved together with the check-off is silently dropped

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** test-gap
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo.py:122`
- **Defect:** async_update_todo_item returns straight after complete_task when status is COMPLETED. Every COMPLETED test in test_todo.py passes summary=task['name'], so no test shows that the edit made in the same save is lost.
- **Failure scenario:** In Home Assistant's to-do item dialog, a user changes the item summary (or its description) and ticks the checkbox, then presses Save. HA's todo.update_item merges rename, status and description into one TodoItem and calls async_update_todo_item once. Home Keeper completes the task and returns, so the rename and notes edit are never written, even though the entity declares UPDATE_TODO_ITEM and the docstring promises that a rename persists.
- **Suggested fix:** Apply the summary/notes diff before completing (or in the same branch). Add a test that sends COMPLETED with a new summary and a new description and asserts both the completion and the update_task call.
- **Verifier fix note:** Apply the summary/notes diff in the COMPLETED branch too. Apply it after complete_task succeeds (a problem-sensor task rejects completion, and the rename should not half-apply there), and also on the one_off_completed early return path if the rename is to persist for a finished do-once task.
- **Verifier reason:** todo.py:122-135 completes the task and returns before the summary/notes block at todo.py:137-146, so a rename or notes edit that arrives with status COMPLETED is dropped with no error. The path is reachable: Home Assistant's todo.update_item merges rename, status and description into one TodoItem and calls async_update_todo_item once, and the HA to-do item dialog has a checkbox beside the summary field and saves summary, status and description in one update_item call. Every COMPLETED test in tests/unit/test_todo.py (297, 309, 323, 340, 384) passes summary=task['name']. No comment or doc says the drop is intended. Low: the check-off works and the user sees the old name and can rename again.

### X07-4 — Admin gate of services is checked by text only and at runtime for 9 of 20 admin-only services: an un-awaited or late _verify_admin passes every test

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** test-gap
- **Location:** `tests/unit/test_api_surface.py:221`
- **Defect:** test_admin_only_services_verify_admin passes when the substring '_verify_admin' appears anywhere in the handler source. tests/integration/test_admin_gates.py exercises only a hand-listed subset, so a gate that never runs is not caught for archive_asset, restore_asset, adjust_part_stock, remove_part_file, remove_asset_document, update_asset_document, or the three declarative-companion services.
- **Failure scenario:** A refactor writes `_verify_admin(call)` without await in handle_adjust_part_stock, or moves it below the store mutation. The coroutine is created and dropped (a RuntimeWarning only), so a non-admin can change stock. The text check still sees '_verify_admin', and no integration test calls adjust_part_stock as a non-admin, so CI stays green. The privilege model in docs/SECURITY.md depends on this gate.
- **Suggested fix:** Parametrize the non-admin refusal test over every ServiceSpec with admin_only=True read from api_surface. Make the unit check AST-based: require an `await _verify_admin(call)` expression as the first statement of the handler.
- **Verifier fix note:** Drop the un-awaited case from the rationale (mypy catches it). The parametrized runtime test over every admin_only ServiceSpec is the part that adds real protection; the AST check (first statement is `await _verify_admin(call)`) covers the placement case.
- **Verifier reason:** test_api_surface.py:221 is a substring check (`"_verify_admin" in bodies.get(handler, "")`), and tests/integration/test_admin_gates.py:138-146 and 167-177 exercise only 9 services at runtime. Correction: api_surface.py:186-208 declares 18 admin_only services, not 20, so 9 of 18 (archive_asset, restore_asset, adjust_part_stock, remove_part_file, remove_asset_document, update_asset_document, and the 3 declarative-companion services) have only the text check. The failure example is partly caught: _verify_admin is async (__init__.py:1075), and mypy's default-enabled unused-coroutine code (not disabled in pyproject [tool.mypy]) fails lint.yml on an un-awaited call. A gate moved below the store mutation, placed in a branch, or present only in a comment still passes every test. No gate is broken today.
- **Repro:** grep: no non_admin test in tests/ names any of the 9 services; api_surface.py admin_only=True count = 18

### X08-2 — Every mutation serializes and rewrites the whole storage document on the event loop, and bulk loops do it once per item

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:316`
- **Defect:** _save() calls Store.async_save with the full document (tasks with up to 500 completions each, assets, bookkeeping); the Store is built with the default serialize_in_event_loop=True and async_delay_save is never used, so each mutation JSON-encodes megabytes in the event loop and writes the whole file, and loops such as the one-off purge and settle_use_tasks multiply it per task.
- **Failure scenario:** Measured with a synthetic document of 500 tasks (100 daily tasks at the MAX_COMPLETION_HISTORY=500 cap, 400 with 30 completions) and 100 appliances: 7.17 MB compact JSON, stdlib json.dumps 68 ms, 16 MB when indented. HA serializes with orjson (faster, but still tens of ms at this size) in the loop, then writes the entire file in the executor, for every tap in the panel. Bulk paths do this per item: coordinator._purge_expired_one_offs calls store.delete_task once per expired one-off (a user who first sets one_off_retention_days with 200 completed one-offs gets 200 full serializations and ~200 x 7-16 MB of writes inside one coordinator refresh, plus 200 events); settle_use_tasks calls trigger_task per armed task (each saves); todo_list_sync._complete_tasks calls complete_task per ticked item (each saves). On a Raspberry Pi with an SD card this stalls the loop and wears the card.
- **Suggested fix:** Build the Store with serialize_in_event_loop=False (the data dicts are not mutated from other threads) or switch _save to async_delay_save(self._data_to_save, delay=1) for ordinary edits with an explicit flush on unload; give the purge loop and the settle loop a batch path that saves once.
- **Verifier fix note:** Do NOT flip serialize_in_event_loop=False as suggested: HA's docstring (storage.py:249-256) allows it only when the data is thread safe and not mutated by others, and this store mutates self._tasks and the nested completions lists in place on the loop while the executor would be iterating them (dict-changed-size errors or a torn snapshot). Use async_delay_save(self._data_to_save, delay=1) with a data_func (HA flushes pending delayed writes at stop), or keep async_save and give the purge loop a batch delete that saves once.
- **Verifier reason:** Mechanism is real: store.py:200 builds Store(hass, STORAGE_VERSION, STORAGE_KEY) with the default serialize_in_event_loop=True (HA helpers/storage.py:240), _save (store.py:316-326) passes the whole document to async_save, which serializes it in the loop via prepare_save_json/orjson and writes the full file in the executor (storage.py:464-478, 591-598). There are 45 _save() call sites and no async_delay_save. coordinator.py:287-290 purges expired one-offs with one delete_task (one save, one event) per task; store.py:1651-1655 arms each use task through trigger_task (one save each). But the cost at a realistic size is milliseconds: a 60-task document with 50 completions each is 0.25 MB and serializes in 2.3 ms with the stdlib C encoder (orjson is faster). Only the finder's extreme (100 daily tasks at the 500-completion cap = 1.4 years of daily ticks on 100 tasks) reaches ~5 MB / ~50 ms stdlib. The purge burst happens once, when a user first enables one_off_retention_days with a backlog; after that each tick purges 0-1 tasks. The settle and to-do completion loops usually carry 0-3 items. Real but small: low.
- **Repro:** python3 scratchpad/tmp-v-X08/bench2.py -> 'typical 60x50: 0.25 MB compact, stdlib C encoder 2.3 ms' / 'extreme 100x500+400x30: 4.94 MB compact, stdlib C encoder 52.1 ms'

### X08-3 — To-do list sync runs a full enrich-and-plan pass for every task event even when no sync is configured, so bulk operations are O(n^2)

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list_sync.py:120`
- **Defect:** _handle_task_event schedules async_sync for every one of 8 task event types unconditionally, and _sync_once copies and enriches every task before it checks whether anything is configured or tracked; async_schedule_sweep has that early-out but the event path does not.
- **Failure scenario:** A user with no to-do list sync configured imports 500 tasks (async_import_records saves once but fires one home_keeper_task_created per task), or the declarative/problem/buy reconcilers create or delete many tasks. Each event creates a task that runs _sync_once: notifier.effective_filter_tasks builds {**task, labels, area_id} for all 500 tasks with device and area registry lookups, then desired_by_sync. Because a pass that needs no list read never suspends, the _running/_pending coalescing never engages and all 500 passes run one after another: 500 x 500 = 250,000 task-dict copies. Measured 0.55 ms per enrichment pass with dict-mocked registries, so about 0.3 s of event-loop time for the copies alone for a feature the user does not use; it grows with the square of the task count.
- **Suggested fix:** Early-return in _handle_task_event (or at the top of _sync_once) when no profile has a sync target and nothing is tracked, and debounce the event path (for example async_call_later 0-1 s or a Debouncer) so a burst of events runs one pass.
- **Verifier fix note:** The early return is correct and cheap: at the top of _handle_task_event (or _sync_once when not force) return when profiles.synced_profiles(self._configured_syncs()) is empty and store.get_todo_list_items() is empty, the same test async_schedule_sweep uses. Move the own_todo_entity_ids scan after that check. A debounce is optional.
- **Verifier reason:** todo_list_sync.py:114-124 subscribes to all 8 task events unconditionally and _handle_task_event schedules async_sync with no guard. _sync_once (todo_list_sync.py:155-168) calls _resolve_syncs, which runs own_todo_entity_ids (shopping_sync.py:51-56, a scan of the whole entity registry), and notifier.effective_filter_tasks over every task (notifier.py:97-109, one dict copy per task) BEFORE todo_list.needs_pass, which returns False when nothing is configured or tracked (todo_list.py:659-680). Only async_schedule_sweep has the early-out. Because a pass that stops at needs_pass never awaits, the _running/_pending coalescing (todo_sync_driver.py:147-160) never engages, so a burst of N events runs N full passes. Cost is small: the enrichment is 0.03 ms for 60 tasks and 0.55 ms for 500 tasks per event, plus one registry scan; the quadratic case needs a bulk import or reconcile burst, and 500 imported tasks cost about 0.3 s in total, split into 500 separate loop slices. Milliseconds per event: low.
- **Repro:** python3 scratchpad/tmp-v-X08/bench.py -> 'enrichment pass over 60 tasks: 0.031 ms' / 'enrichment pass over 500 tasks: 0.553 ms'

### X08-4 — Each count sensor computes its whole count twice per state write

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/sensor.py:326`
- **Defect:** native_value and extra_state_attributes each call _counts(), which enriches every task (dict copy plus device and area registry lookups) and runs count_tasks, so every write of every count sensor does two full passes over the task map.
- **Failure scenario:** With 500 tasks and 5 profiles there are 6 count sensors; each coordinator refresh (every 5 minutes and after every mutation, see X08-5) runs 12 full enrich+count passes instead of 6. The dict copies in effective_filter_tasks measured 0.55 ms per pass with mocked registries before count_tasks runs, so the duplicate work is a fixed 2x on the hottest aggregate path.
- **Suggested fix:** Compute the counts once in _handle_coordinator_update and store them in _attr_native_value / _attr_extra_state_attributes before super()._handle_coordinator_update(); also compute the enriched task list once per refresh on the coordinator and share it across the count sensors.
- **Verifier fix note:** Compute once in _handle_coordinator_update into _attr_native_value / _attr_extra_state_attributes, and also compute once in async_added_to_hass so the first write has values.
- **Verifier reason:** sensor.py:309-324 _counts enriches every task and runs count_tasks; native_value (sensor.py:326-328) and extra_state_attributes (sensor.py:330-332) each call it, and HA's async_write_ha_state reads both once per write, so each count sensor does two full passes per write. At 60 tasks this is well under 1 ms per refresh; at 500 tasks and 6 count sensors it is about 12 x 0.55 ms of enrichment plus count_tasks. Pure 2x waste on a small number: low.

### X08-5 — async_settle_buy_tasks writes every coordinator entity twice per mutation

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/coordinator.py:194`
- **Defect:** The no-reload branch calls async_update_listeners() and then async_request_refresh(); the refresh debouncer runs immediately on a first call and, with always_update left at its default, calls every listener again, so each completion, stock change or to-do tick writes all ~2000 entities twice.
- **Failure scenario:** At 500 device-attached tasks (button, next-due sensor, overdue binary sensor each) plus part entities, one tap on Done in the panel or on the to-do card (13 call sites) causes about 4000 state writes. Each NextDueSensor write recomputes max() over up to 500 completions and the usage stats; each count sensor write does two full passes (X08-4). Only the part entities (spares number, low-stock sensor) needed the early write, per the code comment. The same always_update default also means the 5-minute tick rewrites every entity even when nothing changed; note that flipping always_update=False alone would never notify, because data is the same mutable store dict each time.
- **Suggested fix:** Send a dispatcher signal that only the part number/binary_sensor entities listen to instead of async_update_listeners(), or skip the explicit call when the debouncer is not in cooldown; longer term, return a new mapping (or a version counter) from _async_update_data so always_update=False can skip unchanged refreshes.
- **Verifier fix note:** The dispatcher-signal fix for the part entities is the clean one. The finder is right that always_update=False alone would never notify, because _async_update_data returns the same mutable store dict (coordinator.py:217, 262).
- **Verifier reason:** coordinator.py:189-195 calls self.async_update_listeners() and then await self.async_request_refresh(). HA's DataUpdateCoordinator defaults always_update=True (update_coordinator.py:84) and calls async_update_listeners after every refresh when always_update is set (update_coordinator.py:594-596); its Debouncer is immediate=True with a 10 s cooldown (update_coordinator.py:35-36, 140). So the first mutation outside the cooldown writes every coordinator entity twice back to back, and inside the cooldown the second write comes at the cooldown end. One settle per mutation (for example websocket_api.py:423, 495, 903). The state machine drops a write whose state and attributes are unchanged (no state_changed event, no recorder row), so the duplicate costs only property recomputation: a few ms at realistic entity counts. Low.

### X08-6 — Panel and card refetch the full task store, with all completion histories, after every change

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:781`
- **Defect:** Every panel mutation awaits _refresh(), which re-runs 9 websocket calls including get_tasks, and get_tasks returns the raw task dicts with every completions/skips entry; the panel then replaces its entire shadow tree; each dashboard card does the same fetch on every task_completed event and on any Home Keeper entity last_updated change.
- **Failure scenario:** At the X08-2 scale the get_tasks payload is the same ~7 MB as the storage document (the tasks dominate it). A phone user taps Done: the backend serializes 7 MB into the websocket, the browser parses it on the main thread, and the whole panel re-renders all 500 rows. Every open dashboard with the card (non-admin users too) also fetches the full 7 MB on each completion anywhere in the house, once per card instance.
- **Suggested fix:** Give get_tasks a summary mode that trims completions/skips to what the list needs (last entry and a count) and fetch the full history only on the task detail page; after a single-task mutation, patch that task from the websocket result instead of reloading everything.
- **Verifier fix note:** A summary mode on get_tasks (last completion plus a count) is a websocket shape change only (internal, not a one-way door); keep full history on the detail fetch.
- **Verifier reason:** panel.ts:845-857 _refresh runs _reload (panel.ts:767-796: 9 websocket calls including get_tasks) and a full _render after every mutation; websocket_api.py:263 returns store.list_tasks() (store.py:400-401), the full dicts with all completions and skips. The card refetches get_tasks on home_keeper_task_completed (card.ts:533-536) and on any change of the Home Keeper state fingerprint (card.ts:446-451, 508-520). Correction: non-admin users cannot subscribe to a custom event (HA subscribe_events allowlist), so their cards take only the state-signal path (the catch at card.ts:546 covers that); they still refetch once per change. At a realistic size the payload is about 0.25 MB, not 7 MB, so this is a design simplification that costs tens of ms on a phone: low.

### X09-2 — Panel notification Test goes through call_service, so its errors toast as English 'Validation error: ...' text in every language

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-settings.ts:1713`
- **Defect:** runNotification sends home_keeper.notify through the generic call_service websocket command; HA core answers a ServiceValidationError with message f'Validation error: {err}' where str(err) is always the English template, and testNotification toasts err.message verbatim, bypassing the _err/backend_i18n localization every other panel error uses.
- **Failure scenario:** HA language is German. An admin adds a notification, picks a profile, has not chosen a 'Send to' device yet, and presses Test (enabled regardless of targets). notifier returns notify_no_targets; the panel toasts 'Validation error: This notification has no target to send to. Add a "Send to" device in Settings -> Notifications, or pass target: when calling the service' in English, with HA's prefix and advice about a service field the panel user never sees. Same for notify_profile_not_found (a notification whose profile id dangles, a state options.py calls designed and reachable from a backup).
- **Suggested fix:** Either route the panel's Test through a home_keeper/* websocket command that calls notifier.async_run_notify and answers errors with _err (localized to hass.config.language, no 'Validation error:' prefix), or read err.translation_key/translation_placeholders in testNotification and map them to panel locale keys; also disable Test (with a hint) when the notification has no targets.
- **Verifier fix note:** Disabling Test (with a hint, the _blockedButton pattern) when current.targets is empty removes the common case cheaply. For full localization prefer a home_keeper/* websocket twin that calls notifier.async_run_notify and answers with _err (the service stays, as AGENTS.md requires). Mapping err.translation_key to panel locale keys duplicates strings.json text in the frontend locales, so it is the weaker option.
- **Verifier reason:** Full path traced. api.ts:133-145 runNotification sends home_keeper.notify through the generic call_service websocket command (the only call_service in the panel). handle_notify (__init__.py:1419-1427) raises ServiceValidationError(translation_key=notify_no_targets / notify_profile_not_found / ...). HA websocket_api/commands.py handle_call_service (lines 324-334 in the HA copy under scratchpad/tmp-v-B02/src) answers send_error(..., f"Validation error: {err}", translation_key=...), and HA exceptions.py:60-66 says str(err) is 'in English, regardless of the configured language'. testNotification (panel-settings.ts:1707-1714) toasts err.message verbatim and never reads translation_key (no frontend file reads it), so a German user gets 'Validation error: This notification has no target to send to. ... or pass target: when calling the service.'
