Every home_keeper/* websocket error goes through websocket_api.py:50 _err, which resolves the text in hass.config.language (backend_i18n.py:1-18 says the panel has no lazy lookup), so this path is the exception. Test stays enabled with no target: itemCard's Test button (panel-settings.ts:868-891) is disabled only during the round trip and never checks current.targets. Reachable: addNotification (1724) gives targets [] when available_targets (notifier.py:55-62, mobile_app_* only) is empty, or the user clears Send to; notifier.py:417-421 then returns notify_no_targets. Mitigation: when no mobile_app service exists, the section shows the localized notify.no_targets alert (panel-settings.ts:1393-1397), so only the toast is English. No test covers the panel error toast. Cosmetic i18n defect.

### X11-4 — Appliance tree expand/collapse chevron is a bare span: not focusable, no name, no aria-expanded, 24px target on phone

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** accessibility
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-lists.ts:380`
- **Defect:** The Tree view's group toggle is an empty <span class=hk-chevron> with a click listener only. Keyboard and screen-reader users cannot expand or collapse sub-appliance groups, and a collapsed group hides its children with display:none.
- **Failure scenario:** A user collapses 'Living room shades' in Tree view with the mouse. The choice persists in localStorage (LS_TREE_COLLAPSED). In a later keyboard-only or screen-reader session, the sub-appliances are display:none and the toggle can be neither reached by Tab nor activated. Its arrow is drawn by ::after, so it has no accessible name, and no aria-expanded exposes its state. The children are only reachable by opening the parent's page. On a phone the toggle stays 24x24px, under the 44px --hk-tap minimum this stylesheet applies to the other phone controls.
- **Suggested fix:** Render the toggle as <button type=button class=hk-chevron aria-expanded=... aria-label='Expand/Collapse <appliance name>'>, set aria-expanded in the click handler, and give it min-width/min-height var(--hk-tap) in the max-width:700px block.
- **Verifier reason:** panel-lists.ts:379-381 renders an empty span.hk-chevron with no role, tabindex, aria-label or aria-expanded; panel-lists.ts:459-470 binds click only. panel-styles.ts:1146-1156 makes it 24x24px, and the phone block (panel-styles.ts:1935-1938) only changes pointer-events/position, not size. Collapsed groups hide children with display:none (panel-styles.ts:1145) and the collapse persists in localStorage. Severity lowered: Tree is opt-in (default _assetView is 'flat', panel.ts:215), groups start expanded (panel-lists.ts:196), and the flat view or the parent's page still reaches every child.

### X11-5 — Defer menu claims role=menu but never moves focus into it, has no arrow keys, and Escape drops focus to <body>

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** accessibility
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/defer-dialogs.ts:106`
- **Defect:** The Snooze/Skip/Due today split-button caret opens a role=menu of role=menuitem buttons, but opening leaves focus on the caret, no arrow-key navigation exists, and Escape hides the menu without returning focus to the caret.
- **Failure scenario:** A screen-reader user presses Enter on the caret ('More, menu button, collapsed'). It says expanded, but focus stays on the caret. Screen readers switch to focus mode inside role=menu and expect Up/Down, which do nothing. A user who Tabs into the menu and then presses Escape: close() sets menu.hidden, the focused menuitem becomes display:none, and focus falls to <body>. The user loses their place in the task list and must Tab from the top of the page.
- **Suggested fix:** On open, focus the first menuitem. Handle ArrowUp/ArrowDown/Home/End with roving focus. On Escape, and on close after an item is chosen, focus the caret again. Or drop role=menu/menuitem and expose a plain disclosure (aria-expanded with ordinary buttons), which matches the current Tab-only behaviour.
- **Verifier fix note:** The cheaper fix in the suggestion (drop role=menu/menuitem, keep a disclosure with aria-expanded) matches the current Tab behaviour; either way, focus the caret on close when focus was inside the menu.
- **Verifier reason:** defer.ts:122-126 declares aria-haspopup=menu on the caret and role=menu on the list; defer.ts:73 uses role=menuitem. defer-dialogs.ts:106-121 _openMenu shows the menu and sets aria-expanded but never moves focus; its keydown handler knows only Escape. No ArrowUp/ArrowDown handling anywhere in src. close() (defer-dialogs.ts:54-67) sets menu.hidden and never focuses the caret, so Escape with focus on a menuitem hides the focused node and focus falls to body. The menu is still usable with Tab, so impact is low.
- **Repro:** grep -rn 'ArrowDown|ArrowUp' src/*.ts -> no match

### X11-6 — Notification badge hard-codes a white glyph on a user-picked or secondary-text fill: illegible in dark mode and on light colors

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** accessibility
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-styles.ts:646`
- **Defect:** .hk-notify-chip always paints its icon #fff. The fill is either any color from a free color_rgb wheel or var(--secondary-text-color) as the fallback, which is a light grey in HA's dark theme.
- **Failure scenario:** (a) Dark theme, notification with an icon but no color: the tile is --secondary-text-color (about #9b9b9b in HA's default dark theme) under a white glyph, about 2.7:1, below the 3:1 non-text contrast minimum. (b) Any theme: a user who picks white or yellow on the wheel (forms.ts:2577 color_rgb) gets a white glyph on a white or yellow tile, which is invisible. The CSS comment says 'every color the picker offers stays legible', but a free wheel cannot promise that with a fixed foreground.
- **Suggested fix:** In notifyRowChip, compute the relative luminance of the validated hex and emit an inline color of #000 or #fff, whichever contrasts more. For the no-color fallback, use a fill that stays dark in both themes, or draw the glyph in var(--hk-ink-2) on var(--hk-surface) with a border instead of a filled tile.
- **Verifier reason:** panel-styles.ts:644-647 fixes color:#fff on .hk-notify-chip; utils.ts:55-58 fills it with the stored hex or var(--secondary-text-color). HA's default dark theme secondary text is #9b9b9b, and white on #9b9b9b is about 2.8:1. The picker is HA's free color_rgb selector (forms.ts:108), so a white or yellow fill gives a white-on-white or white-on-yellow glyph. Cosmetic only: the chip is a badge beside the notification's name in Settings, so the name still identifies the row.
- **Repro:** contrast(#fff, #9b9b9b): L=0.328 -> 1.05/0.378 = 2.78

### X11-7 — Stock stepper and note editor skip the phone rules: 28px +/- targets and sub-16px inputs that zoom the companion app

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** accessibility
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-styles.ts:958`
- **Defect:** The part stock stepper's +/- ha-icon-buttons are 28px at every width, and the stock number input and the notes textarea inherit the body font. Both miss the 44px --hk-tap and 16px-input rules the phone block applies to every other control.
- **Failure scenario:** On a phone, a user taps the 28px '-' beside a part's stock. The target is far under the 44px --hk-tap this file uses for thumb controls, so neighbouring taps miss or hit '+'. Focusing the stock input or the Notes textarea in the iOS companion app (a WKWebView) zooms the whole page, because the font is inherited (HA's 14px body size) rather than 16px. The stylesheet fixes this for the search field and the declarative inputs, with the comment 'A WKWebView zooms the whole page when the field taking focus is smaller than that'.
- **Suggested fix:** In the max-width:700px block add `.hk-stock ha-icon-button { --mdc-icon-button-size: var(--hk-tap); }` and `.hk-stock-input, .hk-note-input { font-size: 16px; }`.
- **Verifier reason:** panel-styles.ts:958 sets --mdc-icon-button-size:28px on the stock stepper with no override in either max-width:700px block (1736, 2173). .hk-stock-input (959-963) and .hk-note-input (1538-1543) use font:inherit with no 16px rule on phone, while the search field (1778-1782) and declarative inputs (2178) get font-size:16px with the stated WKWebView zoom reason. The inherited size is HA's body text (14px), so focus zooms the companion app. Tap target and zoom are usability defects, not blockers.

### X12-7 — Panel never reads data again after the first load (no live updates, no refresh after a reconnect or restart), so stale rows lead to double completions

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:299`  **Also reported as:** F01-5
- **Defect:** The panel loads only on the first set hass, after its own mutations, and from the error retry button. It does not subscribe to events and does not react to entity-state changes or to a websocket reconnect.
- **Failure scenario:** The panel is open on a tablet or in a desktop tab. Someone completes 'Change filter' from the dashboard card, the to-do list, an NFC tag scan or a notification action. The panel still shows the task as due, and a tap on Done records a second completion (the backend does not dedupe, see X12-3). After a Home Assistant restart, the panel keeps the data from before the restart. That includes one-offs purged or tasks reconciled at startup, and actions on them then fail with not_found errors.
- **Suggested fix:** Reuse the card's approach: fingerprint the Home Keeper entity states in set hass (or subscribe to the home_keeper_* events for admins, since the panel is admin-only), and call the coalescing _refresh when the fingerprint changes. Skip the refresh while a form or dialog is open.
- **Verifier reason:** panel.ts:292-300 set hass refreshes only on the first update; _init (panel.ts:731-743) gates on !_loaded; no subscribeEvents, visibility or reconnect hook in panel*.ts; other _refresh calls are after the panel's own writes or the retry button. Downgraded to low: the panel is the admin surface, HA re-creates a custom panel element when the user navigates back (so staleness is limited to a panel left open), and the harm needs a completion from another surface plus a Done on the stale row.

### X12-8 — An upload continues after the appliance form closes and then writes into whichever draft is open

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-asset-editors.ts:484`  **Also reported as:** F08-2
- **Defect:** Closing the form, navigating away and unmounting do not abort _uploadAbort. When the upload ends, uploadDocument and uploadPartFile write into the current p._assetEdit, which can be null or a different appliance.
- **Failure scenario:** The user starts a 15 MB manual upload for appliance A, closes the form, and presses Add appliance before the upload ends. setEditDocuments replaces the new draft's documents with A's full list. On Create, _submitAssetForm sends that list for a new appliance, so A's link documents are copied into the new appliance, and any links typed into the new draft are lost. For a part-file upload with the form closed, `p._assetEdit.asset!.parts = list` throws a TypeError on null, which is an unhandled rejection. The new form's upload buttons are enabled again, so a second upload can start, and the first upload's finally block then clears the second one's _uploadAbort and progress state.
- **Suggested fix:** Abort p._uploadAbort in _closeAssetForm, in _applyLocation when it closes the form, and in disconnectedCallback. After runUpload, graft the result only if p._assetEdit.asset?.id is still the assetId that the upload started for.
- **Verifier reason:** _closeAssetForm (panel.ts:1317-1320) replaces _assetEdit without aborting _uploadAbort; Close/Cancel stay enabled during an upload (only #a-save is disabled, panel-asset-form.ts:104). After runUpload, uploadDocument (panel-asset-editors.ts:476-485) calls setEditDocuments, which writes the old appliance's documents into whatever draft is open (386-388); a new draft then sends those link documents on Create (panel.ts:1342-1344). uploadPartFile (905-925) dereferences p._assetEdit.asset! and throws when the form is closed. panel-upload.ts:140-144 finally clears the shared _uploadAbort/upload state of a second upload. Low: needs a long upload, close, and a new draft before it ends; the upload itself persists correctly.

### X13-2 — find_coordinator returns a dead coordinator after a failed setup, because HA keeps runtime_data on a SETUP_ERROR entry

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** lifecycle
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/coordinator.py:430`
- **Defect:** runtime_data is assigned at __init__.py:830, before about 30 lines of setup that can raise. HA deletes runtime_data only when it unloads a LOADED entry, so a failed setup leaves a shut-down coordinator on the entry. find_coordinator checks isinstance only, not entry.state, so websocket commands, services, device triggers and document views keep using it, and do not answer integration_not_loaded as the unload comment says.
- **Failure scenario:** A reload (options save, synced problem sensor, asset change) unloads cleanly and then fails in setup after line 830, for example in devices.async_reconcile_assets, problem_sync.async_initial_reconcile or async_forward_entry_setups. HA runs _async_process_on_unload, so the coordinator is shut down, but entry.runtime_data still holds it. The sidebar panel stays up on purpose (#247). The user keeps editing: websocket writes go to the orphan store and persist, coordinator.async_request_refresh is silently dropped (Debouncer: 'call ignored as shutdown has been requested'), and no entity, event, to-do or calendar changes. The panel shows no 'not loaded' state. When the user reloads again, the new HomeKeeperStore loads the file at __init__.py:810. Until line 830 replaces runtime_data (the heal pass and first refresh are awaits in between), find_coordinator still returns the old coordinator. A panel write in that window saves through the old store, and the new store's next save overwrites it: the write is lost.
- **Suggested fix:** In find_coordinator, return the coordinator only when entry.state is ConfigEntryState.LOADED (allow SETUP_IN_PROGRESS only if a caller needs it during setup). Alternatively, wrap the post-assignment part of async_setup_entry in try/except that runs `del entry.runtime_data` and re-raises. Add a unit test for a failed setup.
- **Verifier fix note:** Prefer the entry.state check in find_coordinator (return only for ConfigEntryState.LOADED). It covers every failure path, and no setup step uses find_coordinator (platforms read entry.runtime_data, and the syncs get the coordinator passed in). device_trigger.py:80-82 already tolerates None. The try/except that deletes runtime_data and re-raises is an acceptable alternative, but it must wrap everything from :830 to the end of setup.
- **Verifier reason:** Full path traced in the 2026.9.4 source. entry.runtime_data is set at __init__.py:830, before about 35 lines of unguarded setup (reconcile_assets :836, part/buy tasks :837-840, problem and declarative sync :842-849, forward :859, detach/prune :863-864, services :866). When async_setup_entry raises, HA logs it, runs _async_process_on_unload in the finally block (config_entries.py:912-914), which calls the coordinator's async_shutdown (update_coordinator.py:149), and sets SETUP_ERROR. It never deletes runtime_data: the only delete is after a successful async_unload_entry (config_entries.py:1053-1055), and unload of a non-LOADED entry returns early (:1029-1032). find_coordinator (coordinator.py:421-434) checks isinstance only, not entry.state, so websocket commands (websocket_api.py:46-47, 154-157, still registered from the earlier setup), device triggers (device_trigger.py:80) and document views (manuals.py:287-291) keep using the shut-down coordinator. This contradicts __init__.py:2026-2031, which says the panel answers integration_not_loaded after a failed reload setup. Writes still persist through the orphan store, but async_request_refresh is dropped by the shut-down Debouncer (debounce.py:95-96), so entities, events, to-do and calendar do not change. The lost-write window on the next reload (new store loaded at :811, old coordinator still found until :830) is real but narrow. No test covers find_coordinator. Low: it needs a setup failure after line 830, which today takes another defect (it becomes common only in the X13-1 2027.8 scenario), and the harm is a misleading panel plus a narrow lost-write window.
- **Repro:** grep in 2026.9.4 config_entries.py -> :912-914 'finally: if not result and domain_is_integration: await self._async_process_on_unload(hass)'; :1029-1032 'if self.state is not ConfigEntryState.LOADED: ... NOT_LOADED ... return True'; :1054-1055 only runtime_data delete; coordinator.py:430-433 no state check; grep -rn find_coordinator tests/ -> no hits.

### X13-4 — To-do and calendar entities have hard-coded English names while the quality scale claims has-entity-name and entity-translations

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** CONFIRMED  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo.py:52`
- **Defect:** HomeKeeperTodoListEntity and HomeKeeperCalendarEntity set _attr_has_entity_name = False with literal English names. strings.json has no entity.todo or entity.calendar keys, but quality_scale.yaml marks has-entity-name and entity-translations as done (platinum).
- **Failure scenario:** On an HA configured for German or any of the 16 shipped locales, the to-do sidebar list and the calendar show 'Home Keeper Tasks' and 'Home Keeper Upcoming tasks' in English, while every per-task sensor, binary_sensor, button and number is translated. hassfest/quality-scale review would reject the has-entity-name claim for these two entities.
- **Suggested fix:** Set _attr_has_entity_name = True with _attr_translation_key ('tasks', 'upcoming_tasks') and add entity.todo / entity.calendar names to strings.json and the translations. Existing entity_ids stay the same because the registry keys them on unique_id. Otherwise mark the quality-scale rules as not fully done.
- **Verifier fix note:** The suggested fix changes new installs' entity_ids to todo.tasks / calendar.upcoming_tasks (the reason for todo.py:50-51), and tests, docs, card.ts and shopping.py name todo.home_keeper_tasks. Put both entities on the existing service device (devices.service_device_info(), named 'Home Keeper') with has_entity_name=True and a translation_key, so the name composes to 'Home Keeper Tasks' and the new entity_id stays todo.home_keeper_tasks. Or set self.entity_id explicitly. Add entity.todo / entity.calendar to strings.json and all 16 translations. Otherwise mark both rules as partly done in quality_scale.yaml.
- **Verifier reason:** todo.py:52-53 and calendar.py:54-55 set _attr_has_entity_name = False with literal English names; strings.json 'entity' has only sensor, binary_sensor, button and number; quality_scale.yaml:49-51 (has-entity-name: done) and :144-146 (entity-translations: done, '16 locales') overstate coverage. So on a non-English HA, the to-do list title and the calendar name stay English while the per-task entities translate. Two claims in the finding are overstated: the has-entity-name comment already scopes itself to 'Per-task and per-asset entities', and hassfest does not check has_entity_name in code for a custom integration (test.yml hassfest passes; custom integrations get no quality-scale review). The False setting is deliberate (todo.py:50-51: with no device, has_entity_name=True would give todo.tasks), but the i18n gap and the inaccurate self-assessment are real. Low: cosmetic, two entities.
- **Repro:** python3 -c "import json;print(list(json.load(open('custom_components/home_keeper/strings.json'))['entity']))" -> ['sensor', 'binary_sensor', 'button', 'number']

## Plausible (not proven) (21)

### B01-2 — Declarative spec that fails load-time validation is erased from disk by the next unrelated save, leaving its tasks undeletable and locked

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** data-integrity
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/store.py:268`
- **Defect:** load() drops a spec that normalize_declarative_companion rejects and keeps no copy; _save() then writes the filtered map, so the next save of any other change deletes the spec permanently, contrary to the 'losing one spec is recoverable' comment.
- **Failure scenario:** A beta tester creates a companion with a sensor mode that only the beta knows, then rolls back to stable through HACS (or a later release tightens a validation rule). Stable's load() raises 'invalid sensor mode' for it and drops it. The first save after that (any task completion) rewrites .storage/home_keeper without the spec. Reproduced: 'in memory after load: []', 'on disk after next save: []'. The spec's materialized tasks remain with managed_by {deletion_protected: True, config_entry_id: <Home Keeper's own, loaded entry>, locked_fields, completion_blocked}, so delete_task refuses them ('managed by <name>') without force, their name/device/sensor are locked, and a clear_on_recover task cannot be completed by hand. Re-upgrading does not bring the spec back.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep rejected raw specs in a separate map and write them back unchanged in _save (or under a 'declarative_companions_invalid' key), and surface a repair issue. At minimum, do not let an unrelated save drop them.
- **Verifier fix note:** Keeping the raw spec is one option. The smaller fix: let load or the reconciler release (or delete) managed tasks whose spec_id is not in the map, so a dropped spec does not strand locked tasks. Raise a repair issue for the drop.
- **Verifier reason:** The mechanics hold: store.py:262-279 drops a spec that fails normalize, and _save (316-326) writes the filtered map (the repro reproduces this). But the drop is the stated design: store.py:263-266 accepts losing one spec so that HA still starts. A store written by the same version never trips it: all 112 shipped presets survive normalize -> JSON -> normalize unchanged. It needs a hand-edited file, a beta-to-stable downgrade, or a future release that makes validation stricter. The spec's tasks are not undeletable: the delete_task service and its websocket twin take force=True (__init__.py:316-321, 1213; websocket_api.py:325, 337), and models.deletion_blocked honours it (models.py:834). The real residue: _reconcile_all walks only the stored specs (declarative_companion_sync.py:333-362), so the tasks of a dropped spec stay locked and orphaned, and a recreated spec makes duplicates of them.
- **Repro:** python3 SCRATCH/tmp-v-B01/repro_spec_drop.py -> 'in memory after load: []', 'on disk after next save: []'. Round-trip of declarative_presets.CATALOG_PRESETS through normalize/json/normalize -> 'presets: 112 reload rejects: 0 changed: 0'.

### B03-4 — sign_part_file_url is open to non-admins although no non-admin surface uses it, handing out part attachments the narrowed view withholds

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/websocket_api.py:1081`
- **Defect:** card_projection omits part file_name, cost, vendor and part_number, and the card signs only document files, yet any signed-in user can mint a 1-hour signed URL for any part's attached file using part ids from get_assets.
- **Failure scenario:** A guest calls get_assets, which returns each part's id in the projection, then loops home_keeper/sign_part_file_url over every (asset_id, part_id). A part with no file answers unknown_part_file. A part with one returns a signed URL that downloads the file without a login, for example an invoice or receipt showing the part cost and vendor that SECURITY.md lists as withheld. SECURITY.md justifies open signing only because 'the card needs one to open a document on a task', but the card never signs part files (card.ts:833-847 builds only kind 'document' refs). The service twin is also ungated.
- **Suggested fix:** Make home_keeper/sign_part_file_url require_admin, gate handle_sign_part_file_url with _verify_admin, and set admin_only=True in api_surface.py. Or, if a non-admin surface is planned, refuse unless the part file is referenced by something the projection exposes. Update SECURITY.md to say which signed URLs are open.
- **Verifier fix note:** Requiring admin on sign_part_file_url alone is ineffective. If part files are meant to be admin-only, the gate belongs in HomeKeeperPartFileView.get, and it must still admit the signed-URL content user (a signed request authenticates as the 'Home Assistant Content' system user, manuals.py:341-353), so check the signer, not only is_admin. The higher-value gap in this area is the upload POSTs: _begin_upload (manuals.py:532-560) never checks is_admin, so a non-admin can add documents and replace part files and receives the full asset in the reply (manuals.py:664-666, 766-767); see the B03-1 fix note.
- **Verifier reason:** True that ws_sign_part_file_url (websocket_api.py:1074-1096) and handle_sign_part_file_url (__init__.py:1619-1642) have no admin gate and that no non-admin surface signs part files (card.ts:833-847 pushes only kind 'document'). But the command is not what exposes the file: HomeKeeperPartFileView.get (manuals.py:680-697) has requires_auth = True and no admin check, so any signed-in user already downloads GET /api/home_keeper/part_document/<asset_id>/<part_id> with their own token, using the ids card_projection hands out. Gating the sign command changes nothing a non-admin can read. SECURITY.md:87-106 documents file reads and signing as open to signed-in users and lists only document/part-file changes as admin-only (SECURITY.md:36). What remains is a least-privilege nit (an unneeded open command that mints bearer links) plus an inconsistency between the projection hiding part file_name and the view serving the file.

### B06-5 — Multipart 'name' field is read into memory with no size cap

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/manuals.py:433`
- **Defect:** part.text() buffers the whole name part; request.multipart() does not enforce _client_max_size, so the documented backstop does not exist and a huge name part can exhaust HA's memory.
- **Failure scenario:** An authenticated user (any user today, see B06-1) POSTs multipart to /api/home_keeper/document/<asset>/<uuid> with a multi-GB part named 'name'. `await part.text()` accumulates it in a bytearray and then decodes it to a str (about 2-3x the size) before the 200-char name limit is ever checked; Home Assistant is OOM-killed. The `except web.HTTPRequestEntityTooLarge` backstop never fires because aiohttp checks client_max_size only in Request.read()/post(), not in the streaming multipart reader.
- **Suggested fix:** Read the name part with a bounded read_chunk loop and return 400 once it passes a small limit (for example 4 KB); drop or correct the backstop comment, and enforce a total-body budget across all parts.
- **Verifier fix note:** Keep the HTTPRequestEntityTooLarge backstop and its comment, which are accurate on aiohttp >= 3.14.1. Read the name part with a bounded read_chunk loop (for example 4 KB) so older HA is bounded too and current HA does not buffer 100 MB.
- **Verifier reason:** Partly refuted. HA 2026.7+ pins aiohttp >= 3.14.1 (2026.7.0: 3.14.1; 2026.8.0/2026.9.0/master: 3.14.3). There, Request.multipart() passes client_max_size=self._client_max_size and max_size_error_cls=HTTPRequestEntityTooLarge (aiohttp web_request.py:723-732), and BodyPartReader.read(), which text() uses, raises once a part passes the cap (multipart.py:328-347). _begin_upload raises _client_max_size to MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES (manuals.py:547) before multipart() runs, so the name part is capped at 100 MB and the backstop at manuals.py:448-452 does fire (413). The 'multi-GB name part, OOM' scenario holds only on HA <= 2026.6 (aiohttp <= 3.13.5, which pass no cap). hacs.json declares no HA floor, so such users exist. The residual on current HA is a 100 MB name part held in memory (plus a decoded copy), which goes against the fixed _FLUSH_BYTES memory design. The caller must be signed in (any user today because of B06-1).
- **Repro:** Downloaded aiohttp sdists: 'async def multipart' passes client_max_size in 3.14.1 and 3.14.3; not in 3.11.18, 3.12.15, 3.13.0, 3.13.3 or 3.13.5. HA package_constraints: 2026.6.0 aiohttp==3.13.5, 2026.7.0 ==3.14.1, 2026.9.0 ==3.14.3.

### B06-8 — CSV report has no UTF-8 BOM, so localized headers and names open as mojibake in Excel

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** i18n
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/appliance_report.py:171`
- **Defect:** The report localizes headers into non-Latin languages but is saved as BOM-less UTF-8, which Excel decodes with the ANSI code page when the file is double-clicked.
- **Failure scenario:** A Russian, Czech, Polish or Chinese admin exports the report and opens it in Excel for an insurance claim: headers such as 'Название' and appliance names with non-ASCII letters appear as 'ÐÐ°Ð·...'. The report is explicitly meant to open in a spreadsheet.
- **Suggested fix:** Prepend '\ufeff' when the panel saves a text/csv download (keeps the service's csv field unchanged), or add it in report_to_csv behind a flag.
- **Verifier fix note:** Add the BOM in the panel download for text/csv only, so the service's csv field stays unchanged.
- **Verifier reason:** Code confirmed: report_to_csv (appliance_report.py:154-190) writes no BOM, no '\ufeff' exists anywhere in the frontend, and panel.ts _downloadFile builds a Blob with type 'text/csv;charset=utf-8', which does not write a BOM into the saved file. Mojibake is the known behavior of Excel on Windows when it opens a BOM-less UTF-8 CSV with a double-click. It was not reproduced, and LibreOffice, Numbers and Google Sheets read the file correctly.

### B10-4 — A stale read right after our own rename marks the line user-named, so the title never syncs again

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list.py:551`
- **Defect:** user_named is inferred when the live summary is neither what we last wrote nor the current name; a read that still shows the pre-rename title (CalDAV refreshes its cache in a fire-and-forget task) trips it, and the flag is sticky.
- **Failure scenario:** Profile synced to a CalDAV list. User renames a task in the panel. store.update_task fires task_updated -> pass 1 renames the line and records summary=new. ws_update_task then calls coord.async_request_refresh(); its sweep calls async_sync(force=True) while pass 1 is still awaiting update_item, which sets _pending, so pass 2 runs immediately and reads the not-yet-refreshed cache (old title). live=old, written=new, name=new -> user_named=True, stored summary=old. From then on every Home Keeper rename is skipped for that line, although nobody renamed it on the list. Verified with the real planner (later rename -> no UpdateOp).
- **Suggested fix:** Record the previous written title (or a 'rename pending' stamp) with the entry when a rename is sent, and do not infer user_named while the live title still equals the pre-rename title within a grace like UNCONFIRMED_GRACE; only infer it from a title that matches neither the old nor the new written value.
- **Verifier reason:** The planner inference is as described (todo_list.py:551-553, 577-579): after our rename the entry records summary=new, and a read still showing the old title sets a sticky user_named. The trigger chain exists: ws update_task calls coord.async_request_refresh (websocket_api.py:316), the refresh sweeps (coordinator.py:260-261) with force, and a poke during the task-event pass sets _pending so a second pass runs at once (todo_sync_driver.py:147-160). The stale read depends on CalDAV refreshing its cache fire-and-forget (acknowledged in todo_list.py:48-53) and on timing, which I did not reproduce. Effect is cosmetic: later title renames stop reaching that one line.

### B10-5 — A pass in flight during an entry reload keeps writing but never records what it wrote; the new instance can add the item again

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/todo_list_sync.py:199`
- **Defect:** _async_stop only sets a flag; _apply has no stop check, so the old pass finishes its service calls, then returns before persisting, while the reloaded entry (new store loaded from disk, new TodoListSync running its initial pass at once) plans from bookkeeping that lacks those writes.
- **Failure scenario:** Profile with status 'all' synced to a CalDAV list. User adds a device-linked task in the panel: store.add_task fires task_created -> old pass reads the CalDAV cache and calls add_item; ws_add_task then reloads the entry. Unload sets _stopped; the old add_item still lands but the pass returns at line 200 without persisting. The new setup loads the store from disk, and async_at_started runs the new instance's pass immediately; the CalDAV cache has not refreshed yet, so it adds the item a second time. Both copies exist; the entry binds one, the other is a permanent orphan (the exact duplicate UNCONFIRMED_GRACE was built to prevent). No test covers a stop during _apply or two instances overlapping.
- **Suggested fix:** Track the running pass task and await it (with a timeout) in async_unload_entry so it persists before the store is reloaded, or keep the re-entrancy guard/lock in hass.data per entry so a new instance waits for the old pass; alternatively persist bookkeeping per successful op rather than once at the end.
- **Verifier reason:** _async_stop only sets a flag (todo_sync_driver.py:126-128); _apply has no stop check and the pass returns before async_set_todo_list_items when stopped (todo_list_sync.py:199-201). ws add_task reloads for device-linked tasks (websocket_api.py:285-286) and the new instance runs at once via async_at_started (__init__.py:926-933) on a store loaded from disk. A duplicate needs the old add to be in flight at unload AND the new read to miss the added line (CalDAV-style lag); local_todo/Todoist await their refresh, so find_open adopts the line. Race not reproduced.

### B12-5 — Reconcile cost is O(specs x all entities) per pass with a full registry projection each time, run eagerly inside registry events

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** performance
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/declarative_companions.py:555`
- **Defect:** expand_spec scans every registry entity for every spec (no platform index, unlike count_matches), and the snapshot projects the whole registry on every pass, every preview keystroke and every picker open.
- **Failure scenario:** Measured with the real expand_spec on synthetic registries and 50 integration-preset specs (the picker offers 112, cap is 50): 52 ms at 5k entities and 222 ms at 20k entities on a dev CPU, before projection, Jinja and per-spec store writes; several times that on a Raspberry Pi. The pass runs at least twice per registry burst and its first half runs synchronously inside the registry event (eager task), stalling the event loop during integration loads on large installs.
- **Suggested fix:** Build the snapshot once per pass indexed by platform/domain (as count_matches does) and filter the indexed subset per spec; cache the projection between registry events for the preview/key-list endpoints.
- **Verifier reason:** expand_spec walks every entity per spec (declarative_companions.py:555-576) and _registry_snapshot projects the full registry per call (declarative_companion_sync.py:197-210). The _reconcile_all docstring (:321-328) documents this O(specs x entities) cost as an accepted trade-off with an 'index if users report slowness' note. Finder numbers (52 ms at 5k entities with 50 specs) imply a few ms for realistic loads (<=10 specs); only extreme installs approach a noticeable stall.

### B17-2 — A duplicate tag_scanned event completes every bound task twice

- **Severity:** low (finder: high)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/tag_listener.py:57`
- **Defect:** The tag listener has no debounce or current-occurrence guard, so a double read (common with ESPHome readers and phone NFC) records two completions; a fixed task skips a whole occurrence, a wear-part task draws two spares, and a counted-wear use task records two uses.
- **Failure scenario:** Monthly fixed task 'Test smoke alarms' bound to a tag. The user taps the phone, sees no feedback, taps again 2 s later (or the reader re-reads the tag). Scan 1: next_due moves from N0 to N1. Scan 2: _advance_fixed_schedule advances past max(now, N1) = N1, so next_due becomes N2 and the next month's occurrence is silently lost. For a task with a part link, _stamp_part_replacement consumes stock twice; for a use task, 2 uses are counted toward the replacement. Each scan also fires home_keeper_task_completed, so contributors act twice. The notification path guards exactly this ('a second card ... is a silent no-op rather than a double-advance (each completion advances next_due a full interval)'); the tag path does not.
- **Suggested fix:** Ignore a scan for a task completed by ORIGIN_TAG_SCAN within a short window (e.g. 30-60 s, tracked per task id in the listener), or apply the notifier's rule: complete only when the task's next_due is still the one it had when the previous scan was handled. Keep the check and the completion without an await between them, as the notifier comment requires.
- **Verifier fix note:** If wanted, a short per-task debounce in tag_listener (ignore a second ORIGIN_TAG_SCAN completion of the same task within a few seconds) covers reader re-reads. Decide first whether it may also apply to use tasks, where each scan is meant to count.
- **Verifier reason:** Home Assistant does not fire tag_scanned twice for one scan: tag.async_scan_tag fires EVENT_TAG_SCANNED exactly once per call and has no dedupe (core tag/__init__.py:325-348), and the companion app and ESPHome each call it once per read they report. A double completion therefore needs 2 reports: a second tap, or a reader that loses the tag for one poll and reports it again. The listener is registered once per entry (__init__.py:940-942 via async_on_unload), so a reload adds no second listener. Given 2 reports the effect is real: complete_task has no current-occurrence guard (store.py:1979-2037), a fixed task advances past max(now, next_due) (recurrence.py:483-486), and a part-linked task draws stock twice. But the device button and the complete_task service have no such guard either. The notifier guard (notifier.py:447-483) is for stale cards, not repeated live actions. For a counted-wear use task, 1 scan = 1 use is the documented contract (appliances.md:192). This is a missing debounce, not a tag-path defect.
- **Repro:** Read HA core dev homeassistant/components/tag/__init__.py (fetched): async_scan_tag -> one hass.bus.async_fire(EVENT_TAG_SCANNED, ...), no debounce.

### B18-3 — Problem-sensor state changes between the initial reconcile and listener start are lost, with no catch-up pass

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/problem_sync.py:84`
- **Defect:** Setup reconciles once, then awaits panel/card/platform setup before subscribing to state changes, and subscribing does not reconcile; nothing else ever re-reads the sensors, so a flip in that window leaves the mirror wrong until the sensor changes again.
- **Failure scenario:** HA restarts with a mirror stored armed. The sensor's integration sets up concurrently and writes its first state 'off' while Home Keeper awaits async_forward_entry_setups. No listener exists yet, so the clear is missed; the mirror stays overdue indefinitely (problem sensors rarely change), counts as overdue on every surface and feeds notifications. Symmetrically, a problem that began while HA was down and is first written 'on' in the gap stays dormant and is never announced. The same gap exists on every entry reload (options save, new synced sensor).
- **Suggested fix:** Schedule one reconcile right after subscribing in async_start_listeners, and another from async_at_started; or run the problem reconcile on the coordinator tick as a backstop.
- **Verifier reason:** The gap is real: the initial reconcile runs at __init__.py:842, then setup awaits the refresh, panel/card registration, the temp-upload cleanup and async_forward_entry_setups (__init__.py:859) before problem_sync.async_start_listeners at :897. async_start_listeners only subscribes (problem_sync.py:92-103), and nothing else re-reads problem sensors (coordinator._async_update_data, coordinator.py:203-262, never calls problem_sync). At startup a sensor whose integration writes its first state in that window is missed. It is only wrong when that state differs from the stored mirror (a problem that cleared or began while HA was down), and any later state change of any tracked sensor or a binary_sensor registry event runs a full reconcile and fixes it. Not reproduced; depends on startup timing. Low.

### B18-8 — A problem already active when its mirror is created never fires home_keeper_task_triggered

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** contract
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/problem_tasks.py:183`
- **Defect:** A newly built mirror that starts armed emits only a 'created' op, although EVENTS.md says a synced task is 'triggered when the sensor reports a problem'.
- **Failure scenario:** The user turns on Sync problem sensors, or a new device joins with its problem sensor already on. An automation on home_keeper_task_triggered filtered to source.problem_sensor never runs for these problems; only task_created (and later task_overdue) fires.
- **Suggested fix:** Also emit an 'armed' op (EVENT_TASK_TRIGGERED) when a freshly created mirror is armed, or document that the initial state arrives as created only.
- **Verifier fix note:** Prefer the documentation fix: say the initial state arrives with task_created (next_due set when armed), followed by task_overdue.
- **Verifier reason:** Mechanism confirmed: a mirror created while the sensor is on is built armed and gets only a 'created' op (problem_tasks.py:173-185), so store.py:1687-1691 fires only task_created. Whether this is a defect is unclear. It matches the model elsewhere: add_task of an armed triggered task also fires only created, and task_triggered comes only from a dormant-to-armed transition (store.trigger_task, store.py:600-626). At runtime home_keeper_task_overdue still fires on the next refresh, because next_due is now. Firing triggered for every sensor already on when the sync is enabled would be the burst the silent baseline avoids. At most a wording gap in docs/EVENTS.md:138-140.
- **Repro:** PYTHONPATH=../pylib python3 tmp-v-B18/b18_problem.py -> B18-8 ops: ['created'] | armed next_due: 2026-09-30T12:00:00+00:00

### B19-5 — Companion registry cap counts never-installed domains and never evicts, and register_companion has no admin check, so any signed-in user can fill it or overwrite a real companion's row

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** security
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/companions.py:121`
- **Defect:** register() refuses new domains once 50 are stored, but it stores domains that have no config entry and never removes them. The service handler does not call _verify_admin, so a non-admin user can use up the cap or replace an installed companion's name, description and docs_url in the admin-only panel.
- **Failure scenario:** A guest (non-admin) account calls home_keeper.register_companion 50 times with junk domains. None of them show, because they are not installed, but each one takes a slot. A companion installed later (for example Pawsistant) is refused ('limit is reached') and never appears until HA restarts. The same user can also re-register an installed domain, for example home_keeper_battery_notes, with description 'Action needed: re-authorize' and docs_url https://attacker.example. Updates always apply, so the admin's Settings > Companions row shows that text and a Docs button to the attacker's site. Gating with _verify_admin would still let real companions register, because they call at setup with no user_id, which _verify_admin trusts.
- **Suggested fix:** Accept a registration only when its domain has a config entry (or evict uninstalled domains before the cap check). Call _verify_admin(call) in handle_register_companion, because no-user integration calls stay trusted. Document the decision in SECURITY.md.
- **Verifier fix note:** Gating with _verify_admin is safe for real companions: docs/INTEGRATING.md:646-685 has them call at setup and from an event listener with no user context, which _verify_admin trusts. Record the decision in docs/SECURITY.md either way.
- **Verifier reason:** Technically accurate: handle_register_companion (__init__.py:1851-1861) has no _verify_admin, register() counts every stored domain toward MAX_COMPANIONS including never-installed ones and never evicts (companions.py:121-140), and an update overwrites name/description/docs_url. But .amazonq/rules/architecture-and-code.md lists 'companions' under 'Usage stays open', so the missing gate matches a written decision, while docs/SECURITY.md does not list registration as open; whether that is intended cannot be settled from the repo. Impact needs a hostile signed-in user, the panel escapes every field and allows only http(s) docs_url, and the in-memory registry clears on restart. Low.

### F01-4 — Drawer's document-level Escape handler also fires for an Escape aimed at a nested Home Assistant dialog, discarding the form

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:1868`
- **Defect:** `_drawerOnKey` closes the open form on any Escape keydown that reaches `document` while the drawer is open, without checking `defaultPrevented` or whether the event came from a surface layered on top of the drawer.
- **Failure scenario:** User edits a task in the drawer, changes several fields, then clicks the `anchor`/`due`/`last_completed` date field (datetime selector, forms.ts:666-678), which opens Home Assistant's date-picker dialog in the app root. Pressing Escape to dismiss the picker: HA's dialog handles Escape on its own document/native-dialog path (which does not stop our listener, registered earlier on the same target), and `_drawerOnKey` runs `_closeForm()`. Both overlays close and every unsaved edit in the drawer is lost. The panel already treats 'one Escape closes two overlays' as a bug for its own confirmation (teardownOverlay removes this handler while that dialog is up) but not for HA dialogs opened from inside the form.
- **Suggested fix:** Ignore the key when `e.defaultPrevented`, or when the event's composed path does not include the drawer (`!e.composedPath().includes(drawer)` and focus is outside the panel), or when another dialog is open above the drawer.
- **Verifier fix note:** Checking e.defaultPrevented alone will not help: MDC and native dialogs do not preventDefault the Escape keydown. Return early when e.composedPath() does not include the open drawer (HA dialogs live outside it). Menus opened inside the drawer (ha-select) are inside the path and need their own check. Confirm in a browser before and after.
- **Verifier reason:** Panel side traced: panel.ts:1866-1879 registers the drawer's Escape handler on document; it checks only that a drawer is open, not defaultPrevented, composedPath or an open dialog above it, and `_closeForm`/`_closeAssetForm` (panel.ts:929-932, 1317-1320) discard the edit with no dirty check. The only suppression is teardownOverlay for the panel's own confirm (panel-dialogs.ts:139-152, 179-183). The date fields use selDateTime (forms.ts:666-678), whose picker opens as a Home Assistant dialog in the app root. The known HA dialog implementations (mwc ha-dialog registers its Escape handler on document after ours; native dialog close requests run after keydown) do not stop the keydown before document, and our listener was added first, so both overlays close. Not verified in a browser (no HA frontend source here, no e2e allowed). Severity lowered: loss of unsaved form input on a keyboard-only path; nothing stored is damaged.

### F02-6 — Emptied dropdowns send '' that the backend refuses, failing the whole save (part type, backstop unit, season month)

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/forms.ts:1982`
- **Defect:** The file itself notes that an emptied picker sends '', which survives ??, but three readers still use ?? on a non-required select.
- **Failure scenario:** On a counted wear part, clear the 'or every' unit dropdown and save: mergePartForm stores unit '' and assets._normalize_replace_also_every raises 'invalid replace_also_every.unit' for the whole appliance. Clearing the part Type dropdown gives type '' and 'invalid part type'. In the task form, clearing a season month gives Number('') = 0, mmdd '00-dd', and 'active_season[0] start month must be 1-12: 0'.
- **Suggested fix:** Use || for these three reads, as mergePartForm already does for action, or mark the selects required.
- **Verifier reason:** The three reads use ?? (forms.ts:309, 1937, 1982), and the file itself states that an emptied picker sends '' (forms.ts:1952-1955). The backend then refuses the value (assets.py:609-611, 646-648; models.py:621-622). I did not read the HA select source, so the '' claim rests on the project comment. The result is a visible save error that the user fixes by picking a value again. There is no silent data change.

### F03-3 — Test notification overwrites _options with its answer outside the newest-save guard

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-settings.ts:1709`
- **Defect:** testNotification assigns the set_options answer to p._options wholesale without claimSave/isNewest. This reopens the out-of-order-answer race that persistOptionList was hardened against (#255).
- **Failure scenario:** Press Test on notification A while a debounced save of row B is in flight. If A's answer arrives after B's newer answer, p._options.notifications goes back to the state before B's edit. The next debounced save of any other row builds its list from p._options, so B's edit is written back out to disk, although B's card said Saved. saveOptions has the same class of issue: its await p._reload() at :705 also replaces p._options from get_options with no ordering check.
- **Suggested fix:** Route the Test save through claimSave('notifications'), or through persistOptionList itself, and assign only when it is still the newest.
- **Verifier fix note:** Routing the Test save through claimSave('notifications') is cheap and consistent; low priority.
- **Verifier reason:** True that testNotification assigns the set_options answer to p._options with no claimSave/isNewest (panel-settings.ts:1709), unlike persistOptionList (:1031,:1040). But the harmful ordering needs two set_options calls that both change options to answer out of order. Each changing call awaits async_reload (options.py:338-339); a call landing during that reload most likely gets not_loaded from _with_coordinator, and a no-change call returns early with a merged copy that already includes the earlier write. So the race is at most rare; not reproduced. A debounce that is still armed composes correctly, because buildList runs late (:1098).

### F05-10 — Card title cannot be hidden from the GUI editor; config doc says omitting hides the header

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** correctness
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:750`
- **Defect:** An omitted title renders the default 'Tasks', and HA's text selector turns a cleared field into undefined, so the editor can never store the '' that hides it.
- **Failure scenario:** A user clears Title in the card editor to drop the heading; the config is saved without title and the card still reads 'Tasks'. The header row also stays whenever show_add is on, contrary to the 'hide the header entirely' doc comment.
- **Suggested fix:** Add an explicit show_title (or show_header) boolean, or treat a present-but-empty title key as hidden and fix the doc comment.
- **Verifier fix note:** Fixing the doc comment alone is a valid minimal fix.
- **Verifier reason:** card.ts:750 title ?? t('tab.tasks') renders 'Tasks' when title is omitted, which contradicts the doc comment at card-filter.ts:47 ('omit or set to empty to hide'); only title: '' with show_add: false hides the header (card.ts:762-763). The editor (card.ts:1290 selText, merge at card.ts:1346) would need to store '' to hide it; HA's text selector usually reports a cleared optional field as undefined, which was not verified against HA source. User docs (docs/guide/views/dashboard-card.md) promise nothing about hiding the title, so the defect is a wrong code comment plus a missing editor affordance.

### F05-2 — Overlapping refresh is dropped and the newer state signal is recorded, so the card keeps stale rows (and invites a second completion)

- **Severity:** low (finder: medium)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:554`  **Also reported as:** X12-5
- **Defect:** _refresh returns early while one is in flight, and the in-flight refresh stores the current hass signal at its end, so a change that lands after its get_tasks reply is never fetched.
- **Failure scenario:** Remote dashboard (~200 ms RTT). User taps Done on X: its state change starts refresh A (getTasks, getOptions, getAssets, signing = 3-5 sequential RTTs). 400 ms later the user taps Done on Y. Y's state change hits set hass while A is in flight: _signal is set to S2 and _refresh() returns immediately; _complete(Y)'s own `await this._refresh()` also returns immediately. A's getTasks was answered before Y was stored, and A ends with `this._signal = this._stateSignal(this._hass)` = S2. The card renders Y as still due, and no later refresh fires until another Home Keeper entity changes. _completing no longer holds Y, so a second tap records a second completion (duplicate history row, duplicate event, a part-linked task consumes a second spare). The same drop can leave the card on the load-error alert if the refresh that failed during an entry reload is still in flight when the re-added entities report in.
- **Suggested fix:** Capture the signal before the first await and store that value, and on a request during an in-flight refresh set a dirty flag and run one more refresh when the current one ends (return a promise so `await this._refresh()` waits for fresh data).
- **Verifier reason:** Same defect as X12-5. Code path is real: card.ts:554 returns while _refreshing; card.ts:575 stamps _signal with the current hass at the end, so a change seen mid-refresh is consumed; the task_completed event handler (card.ts:535-538) and _complete's own await this._refresh() (card.ts:662) hit the same guard. Staleness is bounded: the next coordinator write (async_request_refresh, HA debouncer cooldown about 10 s) changes last_updated and a new signal triggers a refresh. The double-completion consequence needs a second tap inside that window. Not reproduced.

### F05-6 — No not_loaded retry: a card refresh that lands in Home Keeper's own entry reload replaces the list with 'Couldn't load tasks'

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/card.ts:576`
- **Defect:** The card treats the transient not_loaded error as a hard failure, although api.isNotLoaded documents it as always temporary and the panel retries it.
- **Failure scenario:** Any add/edit/delete of a device-linked task, a buy-task settle or an options save reloads the entry. The entity removal changes the card's signal, the refresh hits not_loaded, and every dashboard showing the card flips to the error alert until a later state change (see F05-2 for the case where that later refresh is dropped and the alert stays).
- **Suggested fix:** On api.isNotLoaded(err) keep the current rows and retry after a short delay (as panel._reload does) instead of setting _error.
- **Verifier reason:** card.ts:576-586 sets _error for any failure, including api.isNotLoaded (api.ts:35-37), and panel.ts:828 retries that case but the card does not. The alert is transient: find_coordinator reads entry.runtime_data (coordinator.py:421-434), which setup sets at __init__.py:830 before platforms forward at :859, so the refresh fired by the re-added entities succeeds. The error shows only when a refresh lands between unload and line 830; the stuck case needs F05-2 overlap, which is unlikely because a not_loaded getTasks fails at once.

### F08-6 — Cancel upload during the 'Finishing...' phase does not cancel the upload, although the code comment says cancel is safe at any point

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** error-handling
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel-upload.ts:277`
- **Defect:** The Cancel button stays visible after the whole body has been sent. At that point the server has the file and continues to store it, but the panel treats the abort as a cancellation and drops the result.
- **Failure scenario:** During a large upload the label reads 'Finishing...' (state.sent), and the user presses Cancel upload. The XHR aborts, the panel says nothing, and the draft keeps its old document list. The server has already parsed the body, so it stores the blob and adds the document, and the user later finds the 'cancelled' file on the appliance page. If Home Assistant cancels the handler on disconnect between async_store_document and add_asset_document, an unreferenced blob stays on disk instead.
- **Suggested fix:** Hide or disable Cancel once state.sent is true. Or, after an abort in the sent phase, reload the asset so the form shows what the server did.
- **Verifier fix note:** Disabling Cancel once state.sent is set is the simplest correct fix. A reload-after-abort does not cover the orphan-blob case.
- **Verifier reason:** Front-end path confirmed: renderUploadStatus keeps Cancel in the 'Finishing...' state (panel-upload.ts:239, 274-279). An abort resolves to undefined, so the result is dropped silently (:131-138). The claim that 'the server has the file' at that point is overstated. xhr.upload 'load' (api.ts:628-630) fires when the browser has written the last byte to the socket, and on a slow uplink the socket buffer can still hold a lot of data. An abort in that part of the phase cuts the body, _parse_upload fails, and nothing is stored, as the comment says. The window where the server has read the whole body is short: validate, rename, store save, and the response (manuals.py:601-666). An abort in that window can leave the document stored while the panel shows nothing. If HA cancels the handler on disconnect, it can instead leave an orphan blob between async_store_document and add_asset_document. The race is real but narrow and timing-dependent, and I could not check it without running HA.

### X06-6 — Preview release is never deleted for a PR closed while it has merge conflicts

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** ci
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/preview-release.yml:157`
- **Defect:** Cleanup runs on `pull_request: closed`. GitHub does not run pull_request workflows while the PR has a merge conflict, and in this repository concurrent feature PRs nearly always conflict.
- **Failure scenario:** Feature PRs #396 and #397 both bump manifest.json, const.py and the top CHANGELOG section to 0.28.0b3. One of them merges, and the other now conflicts. The maintainer closes the conflicting PR without a rebase. No pull_request workflow runs, so the `v0.28.0.dev<N>` pre-release and its tag stay in Releases and in the HACS version list for beta users. The walkthrough and docs previews for that PR also stay on gh-pages.
- **Suggested fix:** Move the cleanup job to `pull_request_target: types: [closed]`. This is safe because the job checks out and runs no PR code. Or add a scheduled sweep that deletes 'Preview · PR #N' releases whose PR is closed.
- **Verifier fix note:** pull_request_target: [closed] for the cleanup job only is safe (it checks out nothing). Note that #351 also shows the overflow item: 0.25.0.dev351 and 0.26.0.dev351 both remain on one open PR.
- **Verifier reason:** The mechanism matches GitHub's documented rule that pull_request workflows do not run while the PR has a merge conflict, and the cleanup (preview-release.yml:157-162) runs only on pull_request closed. But no leak was found in the repository: the 4 remaining 'Preview · PR #N' releases (#393, #351 x2, #304) all belong to PRs that are still open (search is:open: 397, 396, 393, 387, 386, 351, 326, 304, 284), and a conflicting PR is usually rebased and merged, which triggers the cleanup. Only a PR abandoned while conflicted leaks, so low.
- **Repro:** list_releases: Preview · PR #393/#351/#304 present; search_pull_requests is:open -> 393, 351 and 304 are open (draft).

### X06-8 — Mutation gate does not check its scope script or the mutmut pin, so a break reads as 'nothing to test'

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** test-gap
- **Location:** `ci/test-mutation-frontend.sh:37`
- **Defect:** The TypeScript toolchain smoke list leaves out ci/mutation_scope.py and mutation.yml, and the Python lane has no smoke run at all. An empty scope or a filter that matches no mutant passes as 'nothing to test'.
- **Failure scenario:** A PR bumps `mutmut==3.7.0` in mutation.yml to a release that renames mutants, or it changes mutation_scope.py so that the script prints nothing. The PR changes no Python or TypeScript, so both jobs print 'No mutable ... changed' and pass. Later PRs get filters that match no mutant. `mutmut results` then gives an empty tally, which mutation_report.py passes by design (:290-292), so the 80% gate stops gating with every check green.
- **Suggested fix:** Add ci/mutation_scope.py and .github/workflows/mutation.yml to TOOLCHAIN. Give the Python lane the same smoke run, for example a fixed small function, when requirements-test.txt, pyproject [tool.mutmut], mutation.yml, mutation_scope.py or mutation_report.py changes. Add unit tests for mutation_scope.py.
- **Verifier fix note:** Adding mutation_scope.py and mutation.yml to the smoke trigger and adding a unit test for python_filters (including a prefix pair, see X06-7) covers the real residual. The 'empty tally passes' path only matters when mutmut's results output changes format, not when filters stop matching.
- **Verifier reason:** The central step of the scenario is wrong for the pinned mutmut: with filters that match no mutant, mutmut 3.7.0 raises `assert filtered_mutants, 'Filtered for specific mutants, but nothing matches'` (mutmut/__main__.py:1247). mutmut exits non-zero, and test-mutation-python.sh:66-69 fails the job, so a naming change fails loudly on the next Python PR and does not pass as 'nothing to test'. What remains is true but speculative: TOOLCHAIN (test-mutation-frontend.sh:37-38) leaves out ci/mutation_scope.py and mutation.yml, the Python lane has no smoke run, and no unit test covers mutation_scope.py (only a docstring mention in test_options_write.py). So a pin-bump or scope-script PR is itself untested, a scope regression that prints nothing would silence both lanes, and a change to the mutmut results output format would give an empty tally that passes (mutation_report.py:284 needs a non-empty tally to fail).
- **Repro:** grep mutmut-3.7.0 wheel: __main__.py:1245-1247 fnmatch filter + assert 'Filtered for specific mutants, but nothing matches'

### X12-9 — Panel _refresh joins an in-flight load that can have read the store before the caller's change, and direct _reload calls skip the coalescing

- **Severity:** low (finder: low)  **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE  **Category:** concurrency
- **Location:** `custom_components/home_keeper/frontend/src/panel.ts:849`
- **Defect:** `await this._refresh()` after a change returns the promise of a load that may have started before the change, so the render can show the old state. saveOptions and _runImport call _reload() directly, so two loads can run at once with no ordering guard.
- **Failure scenario:** A refresh R1 is in flight, and its 9 parallel reads were sent before the user's next change was applied. When the change resolves, `_refresh()` returns R1, which renders the task list from before the change. Because the panel never refreshes by itself (X12-7), the stale row stays on screen. On one socket, the window is limited to the time before R1's slowest read comes back. The not_loaded retry wait in _reload stretches it to about 1s.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep a generation counter: a _refresh requested while one is in flight sets a dirty flag, and the in-flight promise runs _reload again before it resolves. Send the direct _reload callers through _refresh.
- **Verifier reason:** panel.ts:849 returns the in-flight promise, whose reads may predate the caller's write, and panel-settings.ts:705 and panel.ts:1449 call _reload directly outside the coalescing. The window is small: the panel only refreshes after its own writes, and reads are answered in socket order, so it needs a second write that resolves while the first refresh's slowest read is still pending. Not reproduced.

- **B08-3** Logging a completion older than the latest one rewinds last_completed and next_due — see B07-3
- **B08-4** Deleting or moving an older completion recomputes next_due and undoes a skip, snooze or early fixed completion — see B07-2
- **B17-3** Import iterates registry.devices directly and crashes on Home Assistant before 2026.9 — see B04-1
- **B18-1** Automatic notification triggers fire on any task's transition, not only on tasks in the notification's profile — see B16-1
- **B20-2** Diagnostics dump appliance free-text custom fields (metadata), costs, vendors, part numbers and document share links — see B20-1
- **B21-1** Diagnostics dump leaks completion/skip notes, costs and appliance metadata that the declared surface says are redacted — see B20-1
- **B21-6** set_options accepts an unbounded one_off_retention_days; a huge value overflows in the coordinator and keeps the entry in setup retry — see B19-1
- **F01-1** Panel Done has no in-flight guard: a double tap records two completions and draws two spares — see X12-3
- **F01-2** Drawer Create/Save has no in-flight guard: a double click creates two tasks or two appliances — see F05-3
- **X01-3** Uploading with an existing document_id overwrites the existing file, then renames it away, destroying the original document — see B06-2
- **X03-2** Upload with an existing document_id overwrites that file's blob, then moves it away — see B06-2
- **X03-3** Import history on an existing task rewinds last_completed and next_due — see B04-4
- **X03-4** Restore attaches tasks of same-named appliances all to the first one — see B04-3
- **X04-2** Fixed task: Due today then Done (or Skip) hands back the occurrence the user just did early — see B07-5

- **X04-4** Logging a back-dated completion older than the latest one rewinds last_completed and next_due — see B07-3
- **X04-5** Calendar ignores next_due for fixed tasks, so snoozed, skipped, done-early and due-today tasks show at the wrong time — see B11-2

- **X12-4** Create/Save in the task and appliance forms can be submitted twice and create duplicate tasks or appliances — see F05-3
- **B01-6** settle_use_tasks can re-arm a replacement task and fire task_triggered twice; the promised dormancy check does not exist — see X03-11
- **B09-7** settle_use_tasks can arm the same replacement task twice, or raise KeyError, across its awaits — see X03-11
- **B10-7** List due date is the date in the stored offset, not the household's local date — see X04-1
- **B17-4** Split heal repoints task and asset device_id but leaves other stored device ids on the dead id — see X03-8
- **B21-2** Declarative-companion services let TaskValidationError/KeyError escape unlocalized; their websocket twins map them — see B02-3

- **F08-2** Closing the drawer during an upload does not abort it, and the upload result is written into whichever appliance draft is open next — see X12-8
